# Evaluator, hosting, and pipeline rules

The full statement of the rules that AGENTS.md carries in compact form — consequences, examples, the implementation map, and the pinning tests — moved here verbatim from AGENTS.md in September 2026. These rules are as binding as AGENTS.md; the compact bullet there points at the section here. Keep both in step: a rule change edits the compact bullet and the section together.

## Elaboration boundary

The individual elaboration passes are INTERNAL implementation stages: `ParameterDetector`, `ImplicitArgumentResolver`, `PropertyExposureResolver`, `FrontEndPipeline`, and `Parser.ParseSyntax` are not host API, and hosts must not be able to compose partial elaboration — a Detect→Resolve tree without exposure resolution leaves every `Property.Exposure` at the default `Exported`, which the evaluator enforces — a falsely exported capture is refused at run time, with a different error than the engine's (pinned by `FrontEndElaborationBoundaryTests` and the non-friend `PublicElaborationSurfaceTests`). The supported public layers are `Parser.Parse`/`ParseAsync` and `KatLangEngine` (complete pipeline), `SemanticModelBuilder` over `ParseResult`, and `Evaluator.Run*` direct evaluation of host-built ASTs (as-is, preflight only, no elaboration). Do not re-publicize a stage or add a partial facade; exposure/ownership metadata is trustworthy only after the complete pipeline finishes. (`ModuleLoader` is internal too — load elaboration alone is even more incomplete: a loader-only tree has had no parameter detection, so spliced module algorithms cannot even bind their arguments; hosts get module loading through `Parser.ParseAsync`/`KatLangEngine.RunAsync` with `RunOptions.DownloadCode`/`AllowedHosts`.)

## Source loading and deferred modules

A deferred placeholder's body is provisional and is validated at materialization. A complete host-built `ScopeCtx` attached as its parent is still covered by the whole-tree pre-evaluation gate, even when the body is undemanded. Alias reachability and target-head metadata observe the same boundary: skip provisional body content, include the attached parent scopes (`FourFixInteractionReviewTests`).

Deferred source is loaded only when KatLang needs that source to continue the computation or resolve required program information. Host source limits are applied when that source is loaded; unused deferred source is not loaded. They are the run's inherited limits, judged as eager elaboration would judge them: a deferred branch's loads are refused by `MaxModuleDepth` at the import depth of the module that contains the branch (November 2026, audit F3: the loader records the live import depth on the region at deferral — since 2026-11-15 as the length of the recorded import chain, `DeferredModuleRegion.ImportDepth` derived from `ImportChain` — and the materialization's walk-context scope resumes it and restores the displaced depth on every exit path), so a refusal names the same depth as eager, and nested deferrals accumulate.

DEFERRED LOADS AGREE WITH EAGER LOADING ON LOAD CYCLES (owner decision 2026-11-15, from the adversarial audit of 2026-11-14 §1.6, "self-reaching deferred regions"; C# host policy, no Lean counterpart — Lean has no deferred-region model, Q-63's descriptive C# load boundary). A selected deferred unit whose loads would close a module load cycle is refused with the existing `LoadCycle` diagnostic (`load cycle detected: <url>`, naming the module whose load closes the cycle) at its FIRST selection. Before, the materialized body could reach back to the module that contains its region — by opening that module, through a module that opens it back (A→B→A), or through a second region (A's branch loads B and B's branch loads A) — the load was a cache hit that spliced the module's shared view, that view contains the region's own placeholder, and the composed program graph was CYCLIC: `Evaluation.DeferredBodyAdmission` read each consumption as deeper nesting, so `rep.kat`, whose `H(0)` branch opens `rep.kat`, ran under `Step(0).repeat(72)` and failed with `AstDepthLimitExceeded` at `repeat(73)`. Such programs are now refused at their first selection, as decided; a back-edge rule letting deferred loading recurse into its own module was rejected.
- THE CHAIN. A region records its IMPORT CHAIN at deferral (`DeferredModuleRegion.ImportChain`): the canonical URLs of the modules in progress, outermost first, including the chain inherited from the materialization that walked its text. Its own module is always on it, and the chain is EXACT: every module in progress at deferral splices the next one, so each of their views contains the region, and reaching any of them reaches the region. The import depth is the chain's length (`ImportDepth` is derived from it; the loader asserts the agreement when it defers a branch).
- THE STATIC IMPORT GRAPH. Its edges are every load WRITTEN in a module's text, processed eagerly or written inside one of its own deferred branches (`ModuleLoader.CachedModule.LoadTargets`, recorded with the cache entry from the load-bearing pre-scan, which already reaches every load; the canonical URL comes from the one admission check without a diagnostic, a download or a budget charge, and a target without a literal URL or that fails admission contributes no edge).
- THE RULE, applied at materialization only. (1) The materialization's walk-context scope installs the region's chain as the loader's modules in progress for the duration of its walk and restores the displaced ones on every exit path — success, a refused load, a downloader exception, cancellation, `FrontEndStackExhaustedException` — with the same owner-held, order-checked protocol as the import depth (`ModuleLoader.InProgressModules`, `WalkContextScope`; materializations stay serialized by the loader's one gate). The ordinary step-4 cycle check then reports a load of a chain module exactly as eager loading does. (2) Every module the materialization splices is checked BEFORE it is spliced — a cache hit, or a cache miss once it has been fetched, elaborated and cached: an iterative walk with a visited set over the recorded targets of every module the loader knows (`ModuleLoader.RefusesCycleClosingSplice`, bounded by the known modules) refuses the load with `LoadCycle` at its site when it reaches a URL on the installed chain. Its verdicts are kept for the rest of the materialization — reaching the chain is final, because known modules only grow; not reaching it holds until the next module is cached — so a body that splices the same modules at many load sites walks their graph once per cache write, not once per site. A refused miss stays cached (the module is valid; only the splice that closes the cycle is refused) and its download stays charged; a module whose own elaboration failed is not cached and already fails the attempt. (3) A cache miss is fetched as before: it joins the modules in progress, its own eager loads go through (1) and (2), and its deferred branches record chains that include the installed one. (4) Outside a materialization nothing changes: the initial eager walk runs no reachability check, so a program whose cycle passes only through branches that are never selected stays accepted.
- WHY DEFERRED EDGES COUNT. Otherwise the verdict would depend on load order and cache state. Open both modules of the two-region case eagerly at the root, `ma2` and then `mb2`: `H(0)`'s region records the chain [`ma2`] and `G(0)`'s records [`mb2`]. When `G(0)` materializes, `ma2` is a cache hit whose EAGER imports are empty, yet its view contains `H(0)`'s region, whose body reaches `mb2` again; only `ma2`'s deferred edge to `mb2` reveals the cycle. Open only `mb2` and select `G(0)` instead, and `ma2` is a cache MISS, which is why fetched modules are checked too: checking only cache hits would accept this program while refusing the previous one. The verdict is exactly the cycle eager loading would report if every branch were loaded eagerly, and it depends only on the static import graph of the modules the loader knows. It also refuses a selected unit whose cycle passes through a branch that has not been selected YET — intended: the unit takes part in a load cycle.
- REFUSAL SEMANTICS are those of every required deferred refusal: the materialization fails and publishes nothing (the region's body is never compiled), the failure is not cached, so every reselection refuses again, and downloads stay charged. The host sees the existing materialization-failure `EvalFailure` carrying `LoadCycle`, positioned like the region's other loader diagnostics (at the current document's import site for a load written in module text); it is outside `IsResourceLimit` and never a clause non-match (Q-71). With the loader rule, composed graphs are acyclic again, so `DeferredBodyAdmission` needs no refusal of its own and repeated selection of a materialized region costs one lookup. The B16 reentrant-selection `LoadCycle` refusal (a selection from the downloader's own execution context, `DeferredModuleRegion.ReentrantMaterializationRejected`) is a different mechanism that only shares the code. `DeferredLoadCycleTests`; `BranchLazyModuleLoadingTests.WalkContextScopes_*` also checks the installed and restored chain.

A DEFERRED BRANCH IS CLASSIFIED FROM ITS PROVISIONAL BODY, RESOLVED AS IF ITS ON-DEMAND MODULES WERE EMPTY, AND A MATERIALIZED BODY THAT REQUIRES MORE IS REFUSED (owner decisions 2026-11-15, option (C) "refuse at load", and 2026-11-16, option 3 "root-cause fix", from the adversarial audit of 2026-11-14 §1.2, "deferred-branch exposure"; C# host policy, no Lean counterpart — Lean has no deferred-region model, Q-63's descriptive C# load boundary). Three steps. (1) RESOLVE THE PROVISIONAL BODY: the eager front end elaborates a load-bearing clause's provisional body completely — detection decides its written names, and implicit-argument resolution then analyzes, selects and rewrites it exactly as any other branch body, with nothing reported. The modules the branch loads on demand provide NOTHING to it: a name only they could bind stays as written, and every reference the owner walk, the prelude or an open of a known provider at any level decides is lifted and forwarded exactly as in an eager branch. (2) CLASSIFY FROM IT: exposure classifies the family, and every declaration that reads it, from that resolved body, so those forwardings are charged. (3) REFUSE EXACTLY THE UNCHARGED: when the unit is selected and elaborated, its body may require nothing of an enclosing owner beyond what the family charged; otherwise the materialization is refused with `DeferredBranchForwardsEnclosingParameter` (`'X' would receive the enclosing parameter 'y' implicitly through a module this branch loads on demand. A branch that loads its module on demand passes enclosing parameters to what its module provides only explicitly: write X(y).`). WHY: exposure is static and decided before evaluation, and MOD-10 validates a deferred unit when it is selected, not before. Until step (1) existed the eager front end lifted NOTHING in a provisional body, so no forwarding written in a load-bearing clause was charged to any enclosing declaration, whatever the callee. With the module `public X = y * 10`, `G(y) = { F(0) = 0 ⏎ F(n) = { open 'u' ⏎ X } ⏎ R = F(1) ⏎ R }` left `R` `Exported` with ONE run-wide cache entry, so `G(1), G(2)` gave `(10, 10)` where the eager twin (the holder declared at the root and opened in the branch) gives `(10, 20)`; `G.R` failed with `UnknownName`; and `H(y) = G.F(1)` / `H(3)` gave `30`, the caller's `y`. A callable the program declares itself had the same hole: `W = y + 1` at the root with `F(n) = { open 'u' ⏎ W + Z }` gave `(7, 7)` where the eager twin gives `(7, 8)`. Reading the modules as empty adds no lookup rule: provisional detection and the exposure summary already read the provisional body that way for written names. After step (1) an uncharged requirement can arise only where a loaded module changed what a reference denotes or needs — a member of it that receives an enclosing parameter, directly or through a declaration of the branch — so step (3) refuses exactly those programs, and nothing asks which callee received the binding (an earlier form of the rule did, with a second hand-written lookup, and was wrong in both directions: it missed `open P` over an in-branch alias `P = M` of the holder, and blamed the module for a callable an inner `open L` provided). Making the provisional summary conservative was rejected (2026-11-15): it would make self-contained deferred branches local-only and break their parity with the eager twin.
- THE PROVISIONAL RESOLUTION (`ImplicitArgumentResolver.ResolveProvisionalBranch`, reached from `ProcessConditionalProperty` where the rewrite meets a placeholder, in the eager run and in a materialization's run alike) has ITS OWN SIGNATURE SELECTION (B3-5★), made after the selection of the resolution around it, which it reads for every body outside the branch (`SelectedSignatures.Enclosing`). A branch body is closed, so no signature outside it depends on a body inside it, and the enclosing selection never analyzes it: the analysis and the rewrite of each selection walk the same bodies (THE REACH INVARIANT), and THE SELECTION INVARIANT holds of the provisional selection as of any other. A group the selector refuses inside a provisional body is the provisional body's alone and is NOT a program's error (the module may supply what the group lacks): nothing is reported, the body stays the detected one (A1's recovery route), and its forwardings are then not charged. The fact is RECORDED where it is decided, on the region the tree holds (`StaticStructureDeferredContext.UnresolvedProvisionalBodies`, beside the forwardable bindings: the names of the refused bodies, in first-occurrence order; `ImplicitArgumentResolver.LeftUnresolved`), with no side table; every later fork of the region copies it, and a placeholder met inside a materialization's run gets its own from that run. It decides nothing: it only words a later refusal (known limitation (b) below). NOTHING IS REPORTED from a provisional body: the rewrite runs with no diagnostics sink, as provisional detection does, and the region memo keys the reporting mode, so no body processed there is served to a reporting reach and no branch report is replayed from it; every diagnostic of the body is the materialization's, made against the real module members. Load directives stay the opaque source constructs they are in every unelaborated tree (MOD-07): no `UnboundLoadReference`, no lifting through them. PROVISIONAL FACTS ARE NOT COMPLETED FACTS (B3-2★): the resolved body lives in the TREE — it is the placeholder's body, carrying the pass's fork of the region, and it is what the exposure summary channel and the editor read. In the static-structure operation its resolved views exist only while the run that made them needs them (its reach check, alias targets and structure cells): the resolution runs inside `StaticStructureOperation.EnterProvisionalResolution`, and the run retracts them before the completed lookup facts are built (`RetractProvisionalResolutions`). WHAT IS RETRACTED is what could hand a provisional view to a later reader: the resolved publications first made inside a provisional resolution, each with the source-to-resolved-view entry that recorded it (such a context keeps its detected view), and the region aliases recorded inside one, through which a context with no publication of its own reads another's. WHAT STAYS in the operation, and why no reader after the run can observe a provisional view through it: (i) the view-to-source entries of the provisional bodies — that map is keyed BY the view, so it tells a caller that already holds a body which admitted definition it is a view of (the placeholder bodies in the tree are such views) and hands a view to no one, and `Freeze` exports the detected and resolved views of the contexts that still hold a resolved view, and no other entry; (ii) structure cells created or completed from a provisional view — a cell is keyed by its terminal context, cells are read only while a run rewrites (before the retraction) and afterwards by `Freeze` alone, for the contexts that still hold a resolved view, so a retracted context's cell stays reachable only from the aliases of the provisional body itself, in the tree; (iii) static-ledger charges, cumulative by policy. So `CompleteLookups` finds no resolved view for those contexts and builds a placeholder's facts from its DETECTED view through its provisional channel, the frozen continuation holds no provisional publication, view, alias or cell, and a materialization cannot observe one. Two facts carry the argument: a context inside a placeholder is unreachable from outside it, and the region memo keys the provisional mode, so no reach outside a provisional resolution is ever served from, or aliased to, a body processed inside one. A materialization admits its unit under a fresh region key (`StaticStructureQueries.MaterializedBranchIn`) and elaborates it from the loaded source, but a fresh key alone would not be enough: a block written as an open target is rooted at the prelude, so its context is the same in the placeholder and in the materialized body. WHAT A UNIT INHERITS: its completed facts start from the publication it materializes from, and an inherited entry stands only for a context the unit did not publish; where such a block is also the same source object (the loader keeps a load-free clause branch by reference) the inherited entry is the placeholder's, with no body, and the unit, which completes the facts of every context it published itself, builds its own entry over it, never over an inherited entry that has a body ([`ownership-and-lookup.md` § Static open-target validity]). THE WORK IS CHARGED to the operation's static ledger like any other resolution, in the parse: per materialized level the resolver now does what detection does, one region for the body plus the provisional chains of the placeholders it defers again, and a load-bearing branch whose own lifting exceeds the static-structure envelope is refused by the PARSE (`StaticStructureLimitExceeded`), selected or not, where it used to be refused when selected (a limit observes a run and never chooses how it runs, so it is a refusal, never a fallback to the unresolved body); only what the loaded module decides is still charged to the materialization alone (`LiftingChainAdmissionTests`).
- THE FAMILY'S PROVISIONAL CONTRIBUTION is recorded once per family, when exposure forks the regions of its load-bearing branches (`PropertyExposureResolver.ProvisionalFamilyContribution`, carried by `DeferredBranchContext.FamilyContribution`): the union over ALL the family's clauses that the summary channel computed (`PropertyDependencyGraphBuilder.CollectSummarySeed` of a conditional, read from the resolution's summary memo) — a load-bearing clause contributing its resolved provisional body, with every written parameter read and every lifted or forwarded call in it — settled at the level the family is written in into one canonical FE-4a requirement set. A requirement is owner-qualified — a name AND the scope that binds it, by reference — and that settled set is exactly what decides the enclosing declarations' exposure (`ClassifyDeclaredProperty` reads nothing else): the seed's required ancestor-owned names and owner-qualified parameters, the summaries of the names it reads, and the settlement of every reference that escapes the family. Every deferred branch of the family holds the same set; nothing is copied per region.
- THE COMPARISON runs at EVERY materialization, a region nested inside a materialized body included, whatever route supplied the binding (`PropertyExposureResolver.UnchargedByFamily`): the materialized body's contribution is computed as the family's own walk would compute it (`PropertyDependencyGraphBuilder.BranchBodyContribution`, the body under the branch pattern's binders, through the demand-time run's summary memo), settled at the same level, and compared by owner-qualified identity (`PropertyDependencyGraphBuilder.UnchargedRequirements`), never by spelling: a same-named parameter of another owner charges nothing. A requirement of an owner inside the family (the branch's binders, a parameter of a body the branch writes) never escapes, so `F(y) = { open 'u' ⏎ X }` and `K(y) = X + 0` inside the branch are accepted. The settlement is ordinary settlement work, charged to the operation's ledger.
- WHAT IS REFUSED: a materialization whose contribution is not a subset of its family's, and nothing else (`DeferredModuleRegion.CompileLoadedBody` refuses whenever `UnchargedByFamily` is non-empty). These are the programs in which a member of a module the branch loads on demand receives an enclosing binding that nothing else in the family reads, by whatever route the branch names the member: its own `open 'u'`; a holder it declares (`M = load('u')`, read as `M.X` or through `open M`); an alias of that holder (`open P` with `P = M`); an in-branch alias of the member or a formula over it (`P = X ⏎ P`, `P = X + 1 ⏎ P`); a block the branch writes (`B = { T = X + 1 ⏎ T }`); a member that shadows a name an outer open provides. The two shapes that used to give the right value by luck are refused as decided (a single activation, and a branch that forwards through an inner property). The report is POSITIONED, never decided, by `ImplicitArgumentResolver.FindEnclosingForwards`: the first reference of the materialized body, in source order, whose synthesized call passes the uncharged binding, read from the calls the resolver just synthesized (no lookup is re-encoded and no table is kept on the tree). A reference with no spelling as a name path (`{ … }.K`) keeps its position and is not named; a binding no call passes, and a reference inside imported content, are reported at the region's site.
- ACCEPTED PROGRAMS keep exact eager parity — value, number of evaluations, cache behaviour and accessibility. A self-contained branch stays `Exported` and evaluates once per run. `X(y)` written in the branch, a written read of `y` in the branch, and another clause of the family that reads `y` (the FAMILY's contribution holds it) are local-only exactly like their eager twins. So is every forwarding the modules do not decide: a root callable in a formula or as a lone row (`W + Z`, `W`), a callable or a clause family the branch declares (`K(y) = y * 2 ⏎ K + Z`), a member of a block an enclosing algorithm opens, an in-branch alias of any of them, and a callable an inner `open` of a declared block provides even where the module declares the same name. A member of the module may then receive the same parameter implicitly (`W + X` beside the module's `X = y * 10`): the family charged it already. Exposure itself is never revised: no `Property` record changes after the parse, and no classification depends on which branches a run selected.
- KNOWN LIMITATIONS, both conservative (a right value or a refusal, never a wrong value). (a) A name the module provides AND an ENCLOSING level's open provides is read, provisionally, as the enclosing provider's, so the family is charged what that provider needs, whether it reads the parameter itself (as before the 2026-11-16 decision) or would receive it by forwarding (since). With `open L` and `L = { public X = y * 7 }` in `G(y)` and the module `public X = 3`, `F(n) = { open 'u' ⏎ X }` / `R = trace(F(1))` / `G(1), G(2)` gives `(3, 3)` with `trace` called twice, the eager twin once, and `G.R` is `LocalOnlyProperty` where the eager twin's is accessible. (b) A group the selector refuses inside a provisional body (it can settle only with what the module supplies) leaves that body unrewritten, so a forwarding written beside the group is not charged, and the materialization is refused where the eager twin has a value. That refusal is for a forwarding to a callable the program may declare itself, so it is WORDED from the region's record and never blames a module: `'W' would receive the enclosing parameter 'q' implicitly. This branch's implicit arguments could not be resolved before its module was loaded (the parameters of 'A' and 'B' cannot be decided without it), so it passes enclosing parameters only explicitly: write W(q).` (without a reference to name: `This branch would depend on the enclosing parameter 'q' implicitly. … pass 'q' as a written argument where the branch needs it.`). It says why the forwarding was not decided earlier and names the refused bodies, in the bounded list form of the selection's own report; code, position and repair are those of every other refusal, and every region whose provisional body WAS resolved keeps the module wording (`DeferredModuleRegion.ReportUnchargedEnclosingParameters`). Both limitations are pinned by `DeferredBranchForwardingRefusalTests`, so removing either is a visible test change.
- THE EMPTY-MODULE SWEEP pins the assumption itself (`DeferredProvisionalParitySweepTests`). The provisional body is resolved as if its modules provided nothing; when the module really provides nothing the branch names, that is exactly true, so the deferred program must behave exactly like the same text without the load, and neither limitation can produce a difference. Every program `P` of the language-specification and semantic-explorer corpora whose standalone parse needs no downloader becomes the body of a load-bearing clause `ZzFam(ZzN) = { P ⏎ ZzHold = load('…/unused.kat') }` of a host `ZzHost(…)` that reads it through `ZzRead = ZzFam(1)`; the plain twin holds `ZzHold = 0` instead (the same tokens up to the load, so a program that ends incomplete has the same syntax error in both), and a second variant writes `open '…/unused.kat'` as the body's first line. The module declares one name nothing reads. Two host shapes: `P`'s own root parameters, and those plus the parameter names of its root-level callables (minus its root property names), so that formulas of the branch forward ENCLOSING bindings. For every program, variant and shape: neither twin throws from `Parser.ParseAsync` or `KatLangEngine.RunAsync` (the resolver's reach and selection invariants throw from the parse for a body shape the provisional path does not cover); every parse error of the deferred twin is the plain twin's; a value is the same display; an evaluation failure has the same codes; a front-end error of the branch (the plain twin's parse error) is the deferred twin's evaluation failure when the unit is selected, carrying the plain twin's first code (the engine reports a refused materialization as one error, its primary diagnostic); the deferred twin never reports `DeferredBranchForwardsEnclosingParameter`, and `UnknownName` only where the plain twin does; and the frame's family and reader have the same exposure in both parses. Minimum counts keep the sweep from going empty.
- REFUSAL SEMANTICS are those of every required deferred refusal: the materialization fails and publishes nothing, the failure is not cached, so every reselection is judged and refused again, and downloads stay charged, while the attempt's own splices are released and it leaves nothing in the lookup cache of the chain recorded at the branch (SPLICES CHARGE THE AGGREGATE below); an unselected branch does no module work and is never judged. The host sees the existing materialization-failure `EvalFailure` carrying `DeferredBranchForwardsEnclosingParameter`, one report per enclosing binding, positioned at the first reference in source order that received it — the reference's own span, or, for a family written inside module text (which has no location in the document), the current document's import site, otherwise the branch's first load directive; it is outside `IsResourceLimit` and never a clause non-match (Q-71). `DeferredBranchForwardingRefusalTests`.

Source-size and acquisition limits are host admission policy, applied when the language's source-processing contract requires a source unit, before its contents are used. A deferred unit is processed only when an operation requires its compilation, including required identity information; refusal stops that operation and its run, while an undemanded deferred computation performs no source work.

The selected whole deferred compilation unit includes its static imports even when its output never reads an imported member. CALLABLE member identity can require those declarations without zero-argument VALUE demand (`Apply(M.Inc, 3)`); an already-known family identity (`Ignore(F)`) leaves its deferred body untouched. Ordinary eager imports keep their schedule. Arbitrary spread needs VALUE to form supply; collector-slice opening transfers existing cells.

Initial/eager admission failure remains `RunResult.ParseFailure`; required deferred materialization failure is `RunResult.EvalFailure` carrying its diagnostic and import provenance. Reached refusal stops that operation/run, never a branch non-match or alternative successful value; `IsResourceLimit` continues to classify evaluator/preflight/rendering protection.

Materialization's structural gates are the FRONT END's (`FullyRecursive`, judged against the loader's unweighted traversal depth); they do not admit a body for evaluation. Every materialized body — first-time or cached, on every consumer (branch selection, the algorithm-output path of a placeholder view, sync and async families) — passes the evaluator's own preflight, by the run that consumes it, before the evaluator descends into it (`Evaluation.DeferredBodyAdmission`): the `EvaluatorIterativeJoinSpines` weights, the run's effective `MaxAstDepth`, and the weighted PREFIX of the placeholder's position — the deepest root-to-placeholder path of the run's admitted root, or, for a nested region, of the admitted body that contains it (`AstStructuralPreflight.AccumulateDeferredRegionPrefixes`). So the composed tree the evaluator consumes is judged exactly as ordinary admission would have judged the eager tree. A rejection is `AstDepthLimitExceeded` (the run's effective limit) or `AstCycleDetected`, positioned at the crossing node's nearest span or else the region's import site, and is terminal for the run; it is a run verdict, never a materialization failure, so the shared cached body is untouched and a later run judges it under its own limit (`DeferredBodyEvaluatorAdmissionTests`).

The materialized root's obsolete `Parent` is excluded from this additional check: branch selection wires the body under its family and output preparation takes the placeholder view's parent, while the attached placeholder parent was already checked at the same structural position. Descendants' parent links remain part of the check. Completed subtree heights and deferred-region reach are retained only within the consuming run, so distinct bodies sharing a module do not repeatedly scan its payload. Prefix propagation likewise retains each node's deepest arrival and skips known region-free subgraphs; an arrival can grow only up to the fixed structural ceiling. A rejection is replayed through the ordinary preflight to preserve its deterministic location even after shared-subtree memo hits.

A URL string the lexer rejected is recovery text, not an admitted source directive. Its literal carries that provenance through parsing and rewriting; the loader refuses that directive before invoking the downloader, relying on the original lexical diagnostic even when the diagnostic list omitted it. This applies to an unterminated single-quoted string and to every rejected double-quoted run (STR-03), for `open` sugar and explicit `load` alike. Other valid imports retain their ordinary schedule. `UnsupportedDoubleQuotedStringTests` record actual requests, including malformed downloaded source and diagnostic overflow.

Per-source/aggregate source ceilings, download count, import depth and transport byte/time limits protect host source processing. A transport may reject during acquisition; the loader's decoded UTF-16 string-length check follows delegate completion and cannot alone bound network buffering. Source and evaluation cancellation retain separate tokens; CPU parsing has no per-token inner cancellation point. `DeferredSourceAdmissionTests` pin these boundaries using deterministic in-memory downloads.


Every error produced by source processing blocks evaluator entry in `Run` and `RunAsync`, including a load failure or invalid downloaded source. Recovery trees remain available to parsing and editor consumers; they are never evaluated to obtain additional diagnostics. This also prevents host calls, random draws, evaluation cancellation checks, and evaluator budget charges after a front-end failure. Deferred modules still materialize when a previously valid program selects their branch; an error there stops that evaluation at the materialization boundary.

**Retained parse results and downloader failure details**

A fetch failure keeps `DiagnosticCode.LoadFetchFailed` and its load-site span through eager parsing and deferred materialization. `ModuleLoadTarget.EchoDownloaderFailure` uses the existing bounded, escaped message when readable. If the host's `Exception.Message` getter throws, the diagnostic identifies the original downloader exception by type. The loader checks for cancellation before and after reading the getter. Source cancellation carries the configured source token. A consumer leaving a deferred materialization through evaluation cancellation gets its evaluation token. An `OperationCanceledException` from the downloader or its getter with neither cancellation requested is a failed fetch (`DownloaderFailureDetailTests`).

Evaluate `ParseResult.Root` only when `HasErrors` is false. The source engine enforces this check. Direct evaluation bypasses it. A failed parse cannot publish the elaboration context needed to compile a deferred branch. If inference stopped before the branch's contexts were recorded, selecting it can throw `InvalidOperationException` before any download. If the contexts were recorded, a module load can fail first with its ordinary diagnostic, or succeed and be cached and charged before compilation throws for the missing eager publications. The branch itself remains unmaterialized (`RetainedParseLifecycleTests`).

A tree returned by `Parser.ParseAsync` retains its `SourceProcessingCancellationToken` for all deferred materialization. A later evaluation can reuse a materialized branch after source cancellation. On reaching a branch that still requires compilation, the materializer observes the source token before the evaluation token and throws `OperationCanceledException` carrying the source token, even when the module source is already cached. After cancellation, compiling further branches requires parsing the source again with a live token. `KatLangEngine.RunAsync` and `EvaluateToAtomsAsync` observe the same rule when source cancellation occurs during evaluation (`RetainedParseLifecycleTests`).

DEFERRED COMPILATION UNITS ARE VALIDATED WHOLE (Q-61, October 2026; constitution MOD-10 and RUN-04 — the scheduling is C#-only, the validity rules are the ordinary ones). A load-bearing clause branch is ONE deferred compilation unit: its written text plus the modules it loads. Its written text is parsed with the program, so a parse error there blocks entry like any other. Before selection nothing else of the unit is judged: no module is acquired, no elaboration diagnostic is reported, and no resolver or validator verdict is issued — not even one that no module could change, because whether a verdict depends on the unit's modules is not syntactic (`P(x) = Unknown + 0` in the branch is `UndeclaredIdentifier` with one module and valid with a module that provides `Unknown`). Once a call selects the branch, the whole unit is elaborated and validated exactly as eager source in the same recorded context — every declaration, whether or not anything demands it (never requested-member-only: an unread invalid sibling rejects the unit), in every position, open targets included — and any error stops that evaluation at the selection boundary before the body runs, as an `EvalFailure` carrying the code eager compilation reports at the corresponding written or import-site span, never at the call that selected the branch. Call-by-need governs supplied computations, never declaration validity (`call-by-need.md` § NEED-05). With the position held fixed, eager and selected-deferred compilation of the same module agree; a module written as an open target is validated like any body (`ownership-and-lookup.md` § Inline and module open targets; the former C# gap X-48 is resolved). Pinned by `BranchLazyModuleLoadingTests` (`DeclarationCollisionInDeferredBody_IsCheckedAfterCompletionBeforeBodyEvaluation`, `ElaborationDiagnosticsInsideADeferredBranch_AreReportedWhenTheBranchIsSelected`), `InferredParameterOutputValidationTests`, and `OpenTargetResolverValidationTests` (a selected unit's open target rejected at the selection boundary with the eager code before the body runs; an unselected unit unacquired and silent).

Default parse/run entry points reject unresolved `load`; only elaboration-enabled paths may consume it. SOURCE LOADING IS ASYNC-ONLY: the one downloader contract is `RunOptions.DownloadCode : Func<string, CancellationToken, ValueTask<string>>` — and it is the ONLY transport: the library ships no default/ambient HTTP downloader (a `ModuleLoader` cannot be constructed without an explicit delegate; `AllowedHosts` governs load-target policy while transport behavior, redirects included, belongs to the host delegate; blank or null `AllowedHosts` entries must fail closed with `ArgumentException` at sync and async options boundaries; trim valid entries and keep loader matching fail-closed even below those boundaries) — module acquisition awaits it inside `ModuleLoader.ElaborateAsync` (an incomplete download genuinely suspends source processing and resumes it at the same point; the downloader is never replayed), and the elaboration-enabled entry points are the asynchronous ones (`KatLangEngine.RunAsync` + async conveniences, `Parser.ParseAsync(source, options)`). The synchronous source-level entry points remain for downloader-less configurations only and reject a downloader-configured options object with `InvalidOperationException` before parsing (enforced in the synchronous `FrontEndPipeline.Process(source, RunOptions?)`) — never a blocking sync-over-async bridge. Parsing and all other elaboration passes stay synchronous CPU work. Loader traversal is routed: load-free subtrees take the calibrated synchronous walk unchanged; load-bearing spines (marked by a linear pre-scan) take `// MIRROR OF`-tagged async twins; clause families are handled by ownership (B2c, branch-lazy module loading): a family-owned open list is shared by every alternative and elaborates eagerly, while each alternative branch body that owns an unresolved load is a DEFERRED module-elaboration region that initial elaboration never descends into — its modules are fetched, elaborated, and budget-charged only when evaluation selects that branch (`DeferredModuleRegion.MaterializeAsync`, at the conditional-call selection boundary in the async evaluation family), nested alternatives inside it staying deferred, a never-selected branch performing zero module work, and a tree carrying deferred regions requiring the async evaluation entry points; the region is CARRIED by the placeholder body that stands for it (`Algorithm.DeferredRegion`, an equality-transparent record slot copied by `with`, never a side table keyed on body identity and never a root mark): the loader clones one placeholder per deferred branch OCCURRENCE (two branches sharing one raw body get two regions while their placeholders keep the body's one `Declaration` — region identity is not declaration identity), each rewriting front-end pass installs a fork of the region it found (the same occurrence plus its own context) on the output view it produces, the non-rewriting declaration validator records its bindings in place, every ordinary `with` view — parent wiring, parameter replacement, a host copy, the flat-binder equivalent — shares the region object and its one materialization, and routing is answered by walking the tree to the first placeholder (`DeferredModuleRegion.RequiresAsyncEvaluation`); a deferred materialization is OWNED by the evaluations that selected it — concurrent selections share one underlying run, a cancelled evaluation leaves at once with its own token's identity, and the run's download and elaboration are cancelled (linked with the source-processing token, which stays authoritative and keeps its identity) exactly when its last consumer leaves, caching nothing, while ordinary eager loads observe only the source-processing token; REENTRANT SELECTION IS A HOST CONTRACT (B16, owner decision 2026-11-10; C# host policy, no Lean counterpart): while a region's load holds the loader's turn (every downloader call plus the parse and elaboration of what it returns), an evaluation running in that downloader's execution context (the delegate, its continuations, and what it starts while context flows) that selects a not-yet-loaded branch of the same tree is refused at once with one `LoadCycle` diagnostic, without a token and after the cache and token checks, counting and charging nothing; the library cannot observe whether the downloader waits for that evaluation, so the refusal prevents a possible cycle without proving one, independent work must start after the load or under `ExecutionContext.SuppressFlow`, and a downloader must never wait for an evaluation of the program it is loading for, because a cycle built outside its context is not caught (`RunOptions.DownloadCode`, Reentrancy; `docs/design/async-evaluation-2026-08.md`); the scan and both walks carry a per-level stack-reserve backstop that turns residual exhaustion into the structured `ModuleElaborationStackExhausted` diagnostic. A placeholder's body is a PROVISIONAL elaboration — a name the region's module will supply reads as undeclared there — so it is judged only when it materializes: the materialization runs the front-end validators over the completed body (the declaration-collision, open-provider and inferred-parameter output rules; the parser already checked its written heads), and the evaluator's pre-evaluation gate never descends into a placeholder (X-07, October 1 2026: the gate used to reject, at the start of every run and whether the branch was selected or not, a program whose deferred module supplied the very name the provisional body took for a parameter — `F(n) = { open 'u' ⏎ G = { a, b = q, 1 } ⏎ K + n }` with `q` provided by the module). A selected body that is invalid fails its materialization with the positioned front-end diagnostic (`InferredParameterOutputValidationTests`). Downloader error semantics are unchanged (thrown or faulted-awaitable exceptions → `load: failed to fetch` unless the source-processing token is cancelled, which stays authoritative). See `docs/design/async-evaluation-2026-08.md` and `ModuleLoaderAsyncTests`.

MODULE ELABORATION TRANSFORMS ONLY DIRECTIVES (PV-02, September 2026; C# front end — Lean's load judgment already replaces nothing but the directive). A configured downloader is a capability, never a meaning: module elaboration replaces each `load` directive with its module — the one prelude-rooted declaration of its canonical URL ([`ownership-and-lookup.md` § Loaded modules]) — at the directive's site, and changes nothing else. A `load` URL is a single-quoted string literal; a rejected double-quoted run is recovery text and never a URL (STR-03). With host operations, seed and limits held fixed, load-free source has the same semantic structure, members, signatures, `open` provision, values and editor resolution with or without `RunOptions.DownloadCode`, on supported entry points and subject to the existing resource-policy refusals. In load-bearing source, including downloaded text, every non-directive boundary is preserved; actual imported declarations can still change surrounding lookup, inference and evaluation. The one shape change is MOD-07's definition-body rule, decided by what was WRITTEN (`ModuleLoader.MergeLoadDirectiveBody`, applied by both walks). A definition body with no parameter patterns, explicit parameter list, declarations or opens, whose sole row is a written directive — `P = load(u)`, and so `P = { load(u) }`, which the parser's one definition-body merge already made the same body — IS the spliced module, exactly as `P = { module text }` is the block. Every other body keeps its written boundaries: a written `P = { { … } }`, load-free or not; a directive nested in a written block (`P = { { load(u) } }` means `P = { { { module } } }`); a refused directive, kept as written; a deconstruction source; a parameterized definition (`F(y) = load(u)` keeps the module as one nested block output); and a branch body. The former shape-only unwrap (`AstHelpers.UnwrapSingleBlockPropertyBody`, deleted) merged every such wrapper. A written `A = { { public X = 1 ⏎ 5 } }` thus lost a boundary whenever a downloader was configured: `A.X` read the inner member instead of falling back, `A = { { y + 1 } }` gained the parameter `y`, and a load-free clause branch (never walked) disagreed with the same text in an ordinary body. A deferred region materializes through the same walk, so a selected branch means what eager elaboration of its text means. Pinned by `ModuleCapabilityTransparencyTests` and the CLI's `AllowLoading_DoesNotChangeTheMeaningOfLoadFreeSource`.

MISSING MODULES ARE AN ANALYSIS STATE (September 2026, from the KatLangWeb 0.8.222 integration review; C# front end and editor tooling, no Lean counterpart — Lean's `postElabInvariant` describes a SUCCESSFUL load elaboration, which a result with a missing module never is, and Lean models neither source loading nor the editor). A `load` directive whose module the front end does not elaborate is MISSING: UNAVAILABLE when the parse has no downloader (`LoadElaborationGuard.ReportUnavailable`: one `LoadElaborationUnavailable` error per written directive, arguments included), or REFUSED by the loader — every early return of `ModuleLoader.ProcessLoadAsync` / `FetchAndSpliceAsync`: a runtime position, a malformed argument list, a non-literal URL, a refused target, a cycle, a splice-budget, import-depth, module-count, or nesting limit, a failed or empty fetch, an oversized or invalid module source, each reported at its site. A missing module's directive stays in the published tree AS WRITTEN: the loader's `RefuseLoad` returns the original node and records it in `ModuleLoader.RefusedLoads` (reference identity; replaced at each walk's start — the root elaboration or one deferred materialization, nested module walks included). No stand-in value is ever substituted: the former `Num(0)` placeholder made the tree claim the module was the number 0, so every name the module would have supplied read as undeclared — cascading `UndeclaredIdentifier` errors in the parse result and `Unresolved` editor classifications for correct code. The post-elaboration invariant (`LoadElaborationGuard.TryFindFirstUnresolvedLoad(root, refusedLoads, …)`, checked by the pipeline and by a deferred materialization) accepts a refused directive — opaque, its arguments included, because the loader refused the whole written construct — and reports only a load the traversal never reached (`InternalError`). A tree that still carries an unresolved directive outside deferred regions is elaborated PROVISIONALLY (`FrontEndPipeline.FinalizeElaboration(provisional: true)`): the elaboration structural gate reports into the operation's list as always, every pass runs identically — so the provisional tree is exactly the tree the program elaborates to without the missing modules — and the passes report into a bag nothing commits. They are withheld WHOLESALE because a missing module's reach is not confined to its load site (an `open` covers every nested scope, and a dot path can navigate into a module property from anywhere), so any of them may depend on the missing members; a failed deferred materialization likewise runs no pass at all. Consequences: `ParseResult.Root` is ALWAYS an elaborated tree — never raw syntax (the no-downloader parse used to publish the raw syntax root, which also escaped the elaboration structural gate, so a deep program made `SemanticModelBuilder.Build` throw `ArgumentException` for a parser result); the result's diagnostics are why its modules are missing plus syntax and structural diagnostics; it is never evaluated (those are errors); and `SemanticModelBuilder.Build` models it without throwing (`editor-semantics.md` § Scope visibility and module provenance). The trade-off is deliberate: a module-independent elaboration error elsewhere in the document surfaces once the modules are available, while no diagnostic ever blames written code for a module that is merely missing. `MissingModuleAnalysisTests`, `FrontEndTraversalTests.ModuleLoader_AsyncWalkNeverSkipsALoadInsideARecursiveChild`, `FrontEndDagComplexityTests.Loader_SharedLoadNodeAtDifferentLiveDepths_DoesNotReuseDepthRejection`, `StaticOpenOwnershipBoundaryTests`, `EditorFuzzHarnessTests`.

LOAD-TARGET ADMISSION AND MODULE IDENTITY (#13, September 2026; C# host policy, no Lean counterpart). `ModuleLoadTarget` is the ONE owner of which source-written targets reach the downloader, decided before it is invoked on the target as `System.Uri` parses it — never on the written text: an absolute `https` URL; NO user information (refused, and never echoed — it is a credential); a host that is a DNS name with a valid IDNA form or an IPv4/IPv6 literal (an IPv6 zone index is refused); and an allowed host. The allow-list compares CANONICAL forms on both sides — DNS names in lower-case ASCII IDNA form (`Uri.IdnHost`, the form every transport resolves), admitted by equality or as a whole-label subdomain; IP literals in canonical text, admitted by equality only — so letter case and Unicode/punycode spellings of one name match, a trailing dot stays significant (fail closed), any port of an allowed host is admitted, and an entry that is not a bare host admits nothing. Matching the raw Unicode `Uri.Host` is forbidden: it admitted names such as `evil.test` + U+FF0F + `.katlang.org` whose TEXT ends with an allowed suffix while the name has no DNS form, and forwarded them to transports that may NFKC-normalize the authority into another host. An admitted target becomes its CANONICAL MODULE URL — `https://`, the canonical host, the port when not 443, the escaped `PathAndQuery`, no fragment (RFC 3986 §3.5: never sent), printable ASCII, verified to re-parse to itself — and that one string is the module's identity: the per-URL cache key, the cycle key, the exact text `DownloadCode` receives, and the name every diagnostic uses. Every downloader INVOCATION consumes one module slot, committed immediately before the call and kept whatever the call returns; aggregate source is charged for text accepted for parsing and for every further splice of a cached module (next paragraph); failed and rejected fetches are still retried by another load site but each retry is charged, so `MaxModuleCount` bounds downloader invocations per run (formerly failures were free and a 2 MiB source could drive ~70k requests); host cancellation releases only the active frame's aggregate-source reservation, never the charge for a downloader invocation that already occurred. This also bounds retries across evaluations of one parsed tree. Deferred-branch materializations use the same loader, policy, cache, and budget, so a parse result's budget is shared by every evaluation of that tree. Malformed targets containing an at sign are omitted from diagnostics because their credentials cannot be safely isolated; explicitly empty userinfo is refused too. Other invalid targets are quoted bounded to 200 code units with every code unit outside printable ASCII as `\uXXXX` (a lookalike never reads as the delimiter it imitates), and a downloader exception message bounded to 500 with its control, format, separator, and unpaired-surrogate code units escaped. What KatLang cannot see — redirects, DNS answers, resolved addresses, proxies — is the downloader's responsibility, so `AllowedHosts` is a source-host policy, not an SSRF defense on its own; downloaded code runs with the run's host operations. ANY HOST (November 2026): `RunOptions.AllowAnyHost` is the ONE way a host admits every host. It is typed and off by default, so a host that does not set it keeps the list (`katlang.org` when none is set); it replaces the list by `ModuleLoadTarget.AllowedHosts.Any` and lifts ONLY the allowed-host check — the scheme, user-information and host-validity checks and the canonical module URL are unchanged — and it holds at every depth like the list. No ENTRY means it: an entry is a bare host, and a wildcard such as `*` admits nothing. The option together with a non-null `AllowedHosts` is a contradiction, refused with `ArgumentException` where a parse or run begins (`FrontEndPipeline.NormalizeHostAdmission`, on the synchronous and the asynchronous front end; the pair is judged there, not in the init accessors, so initialization order never matters). With every host admitted the downloader is the only barrier between a program and the network it runs in, so the option belongs with a downloader that resolves the host itself, refuses the addresses it must not reach, connects only to an address it checked, and refuses redirects. `ModuleLoadingSecurityTests`, `SourceProcessingLimitsTests`.

THE SHIPPED CLI'S DOWNLOADS (November 2026; `src/KatLang.CLI`, host policy of that one host, no Lean counterpart and no change to the package's downloader contract). The CLI downloads two things through ONE downloader (`HttpSourceDownloader`): the program a URL operand of `run`, `check` or `source` names, with no option, because naming it is the request; and, with `--allow-loading`, every module, for which it sets `AllowAnyHost`. Without the flag `DownloadCode` stays null, so a plain run contacts no host a program names. Either download may name any host, so both pass the same guardrails: HTTPS only (`http://` refused, never upgraded); no user information; PUBLIC ADDRESSES ONLY (`PublicAddress`: loopback, RFC 1918, carrier-grade NAT, link-local, unique-local, unspecified, multicast and reserved ranges are refused, an address that carries an IPv4 address — IPv4-mapped, NAT64, 6to4 — by that address), judged on what the host RESOLVES to and enforced in the handler's connect callback, which resolves the name once and connects to an address it passed, so no later DNS answer can change it; no proxy (a proxy would resolve the name itself); no redirects; only `200 OK`; at most 1 MiB; one absolute 15-second deadline; no cookies, credentials or decompression; HTTP/1.1 with the fixed `User-Agent` `katlang-cli/<version>`. A PROGRAM's response must be `text/plain` (any charset) or `application/octet-stream`, decided from the headers before the body is read; a module's media type is not judged. Every refusal is one `DownloadException`, worded once for both kinds. WHICH OPERAND IS WHAT is decided in ONE place (`ProgramOperand.Classify`, where `CliApplication` obtains the source): for `run` and `check` an operand starting with `https://` or `http://` is a link and everything else a file path; `source` reads every operand as a link; a link with a `#` is a CODE LINK, decoded offline by `CodeLink.DecodeLink` and never downloaded (a fragment of any other shape is `invalid code link`, not a download); every other web link is a PROGRAM URL. A shared program's page `https://<katlang.org or a subdomain>/a/<GUID>` is read from its raw-code sibling `/api/a/<GUID>` (`ProgramUrl.DownloadAddress`; the one route the CLI knows). Once the text is in hand the command is exactly what it is for a file. `run` and `check` evaluate a code link's program as it is, unpaired surrogates included: an output row writes U+FFFD for one (`CliApplication.WellFormed`), a diagnostic shows it escaped, and only `source` refuses to print such a program. `CodeLinkOperandCliTests`, `ProgramUrlCliTests`, `DownloadGuardrailTests`, `HttpSourceDownloaderTests`.

LOADED MODULES ARE HYGIENIC UNITS, ONE DECLARATION PER CANONICAL URL (Q-31 H-P + Q-32 I-U, decided 2026-10-06; the language rule is [`ownership-and-lookup.md` § Loaded modules]). A loaded module is a source unit rooted at the prelude, so its elaboration is the same wherever it is spliced, and the front end elaborates it ONCE per program: each pass processes a module root through its module arm, once per operation, with its diagnostics reported once at the first import site; the units of an error-free operation are published to the document loader's `ModuleUnitRegistry`, so a deferred materialization that splices the same import view receives the very declaration the parse elaborated (one member binding: a re-elaboration would mint new member properties, and with them new cache entries), while an operation that reported an error publishes nothing. A materialization that receives such a declaration does not validate it again: its collision, open-provider and output rules do not walk into it (`ModuleUnitRegistry.SkipsValidationOf`; the operation that completed it judged it, in the empty context every holder gives a module, and reported nothing), and it judges its own open targets that name it from the facts the completing operation published ([`ownership-and-lookup.md` § Static open-target validity], A UNIT DOES NOT JUDGE AGAIN A MODULE AN EARLIER OPERATION COMPLETED). What a materialization still does over completed content is listed below (A MATERIALIZATION'S WORK OVER COMPLETED CONTENT). Source admission is unchanged (Q-71): one fetch per canonical URL per operation, and every splice is charged as before (next paragraph). `ModuleIdentityTests`, `ModuleHygieneTests`, `DeferredBranchSharedModuleTests`.

A MATERIALIZATION'S WORK OVER COMPLETED CONTENT (2026-11-23; C#-only work sharing and accounting, no value, diagnostic or classification changes, no Lean counterpart; finding `modules-deferred-materialization-rewalks-completed-content` of the 2026-11-21 investigation, baseline `8947d21e`). A deferred branch's materialization is a front-end operation of its own, and the content earlier operations completed (the parse, earlier materializations) is an input of it in three ways. (1) COMPLETED PROVIDER SUMMARIES. When the branch body reads ONE member of a completed body — a module an enclosing scope opens, a member path through a property that holds a module or an inline block — exposure charges that member's summary seed, one entry of the provider body's whole summary (every member's seed and their sibling fixed point). Each materialization has its own summary memo, so each one computed that summary again: K selected branches over one W-member provider cost O(K × W), in a shape no source-processing limit bounds (each branch splices only its own small module, one download). Measured before: about 2.5 KB of allocation per provider member per selection for members that read nothing, 15 to 21 KB for members that read a sibling, a builtin or an outer name; 400 selections over a 2,000-member module, 92 KB of source, allocated 2.36 GB in 5.8 s against 113 MB for the same program with nothing deferred. The loader now keeps those summaries per program (`CompletedProviderSummaries`, held by the document's `ModuleUnitRegistry`): a materialization that publishes its unit publishes the summaries of completed content it computed, and a later one adopts them. A summary is kept only when its context and every context above it are completed inherited content (an entry with a body, not provisional, that the computing unit did not build itself; such entries are final, since a completed body is never rebuilt and a unit overrides only inherited entries that have no body) and its computation entered only bodies whose own facts are sealed, read no summary the memo computed earlier under a context, and replaced no context by a representative of a shared body; a provider that holds a load-bearing branch of its own is therefore never kept (that branch's facts change when it materializes). A MODULE A BRANCH HOLDS (2026-11-25). In `L = load('lib')` followed by `L.F(n)`, the branch's property needs the resolved module's output seed. For a user module with no opens and no output rows this seed is empty. The sibling fixed point supplies only names read from those rows or opens, so no provider, reference origin, anchor or required owner can contribute. Outside a keep window a deferred materialization can return a fresh empty seed when its loader has already completed this exact resolved module. Inside a keep window it performs the full walk to preserve the checks on every dependency. The member path independently charges the classified body at its exact completed context. The first selection that inherits it can keep that summary under the existing rule. A module with opens or output rows still gets the ordinary output-summary computation. A seed names a facts object only through the origins of its anchored references, so an adopter receives each seed RE-ANCHORED at the context of the same key in its own facts (the computing unit's table becomes the adopter's; a sealed table stays itself, under the adopter's requirement view), seed by seed and only when the seed is read: reading one member of an adopted provider costs that member's seed. Anchoring at the reader, settlement and the settlement frame charge stay the adopter's own. An attempt stages what it computes and publishes it with its unit, under the materialization gate and the region's run lock, beside its module declarations; an attempt that publishes nothing (a load or front-end error, a structural or stack refusal, cancellation, abandonment) leaves nothing, a refused branch reports the same diagnostics whether or not others published before it, and the share holds seeds, names, owners and keys of completed tables only — no branch body, region, attempt, diagnostic list, token or evaluation state. It is bounded by the completed providers the program's deferred branches read, never by the number of selections, and belongs to one loader (one parse of one document); nothing is static. After: the first selection that reads a provider pays its width once (cold), and from the third selection on every traversal counter of a materialization is the same for a provider of 24 members and one of 240; the 92 KB program allocates 314 MB in 1.7 s. A test seam runs every materialization without the share (the reference) or verifies each adopted summary against the adopter's own computation, seed by seed (`CompletedProviderSummaries.ModeForTesting`). (2) A COMPLETED MODULE THE BRANCH SPLICES ITSELF. The unit receives the declaration (previous paragraph); its collision, open-provider and output rules skip it, as the open-provider walk already did, and since 2026-11-24 its other walks of the module take facts of the EXACT COMPLETED VIEW instead (`CompletedModuleViews`, held by the `ModuleUnitRegistry`; C#-only work sharing, no value, diagnostic, classification or limit change): the loader's structural gate over the composed body takes the view's published height under the fully recursive profile where it fits at the occurrence, one gate walk at the raw-syntax cap decides both ceilings from the composed root's measured height (only a body over the elaboration ceiling is walked again, the ordinary way, for the established diagnostic), the unresolved-load check does not enter the view (the operation that completed it checked it, and a completed view holds no load it could flag), the static admission measure counts the view as one published UNIT — its own nodes and edges, its open and output-spreading flags, and the views it splices in turn, each a unit counted once — so the totals and the limits are exactly the ordinary walk's (a view's own nodes are disjoint from every other part: each view is its own parse's import view, spliced by reference; a total that reaches an envelope bound replays the ordinary walk, so a refusal is the established one), signature analysis does not analyze a module an EARLIER operation resolved (`ModuleUnits.ReceivesEarlierResolution`: the rewrite receives that declaration and processes none of its bodies, so a reference into it reads the FINAL signature the completing operation selected, from its resolved view, in the selection and the rewrite alike), and the resolver's region key of a body that opens or holds the module re-interns the module's published free reference names instead of walking it. Every fact is a pure function of the immutable view or declaration it is keyed by (reference identity: one canonical URL is one view per loader). An attempt stages what it computes and publishes it with its unit, under the materialization gate and the region's run lock, only for views the registry then holds as completed units; an attempt that publishes nothing (a load or front-end error, a structural, admission or stack refusal, cancellation, abandonment) leaves nothing, and nothing is static. Every splice still charges the module's weight, keeps its span and reports what it reported. Measured (Release, one later selection of a branch that opens a completed module of W members `public F(x) = Helper(x) + i`): 6,266 KiB at 1,000 members and 23,486 KiB at 4,000 before. At those widths, signature analysis used 3,921 / 14,200 KiB, each loader gate 442 / 1,915 KiB, the unresolved-load check 394 / 1,680 KiB and the admission measure 333 / 1,433 KiB. Later selections used about 651 / 1,975 KiB after sharing. The stage counters are independent of member count on this input. Re-interning free reference names still depends on the number of distinct free names. The first splice of a view is cold for the gate, load check and measure. Since 2026-11-25 EXPOSURE'S SHARED-BODY CENSUS of the materialization's resolved body counts every module declaration it splices as one CENSUS UNIT (`SharedBodyCensus.OfLoadedBody`; `CompletedModuleViews.CensusUnit`, kept per completed resolved declaration under the same isolation): the bodies the census marks inside the declaration (reached twice by its own walk, which stops at the module roots it splices, or below such a node down to a user body) and the edges it holds to each module root it splices. A declaration's own nodes are reached only through its root, so the one identity shared across independently contributed parts is a module root; the body and the units count their edges to every root, a root reached twice (two loads of one module in a branch, a module and that module's own dependency) is shared exactly as the ordinary walk finds it, the census marks exactly the ordinary walk's bodies, and every sharing and deferred-region decision read from it is unchanged (the verifying mode walks it the ordinary way too and compares). A module with no opens or output rows also receives the output-seed shortcut above. A unit that reaches a module root elaborated to a callable alias uses the ordinary census. Measured (Release, same module, KiB at 1,000 / 4,000 members, before → after): the census 331 / 1,432 → 0.7 per later selection; a later selection of a branch that opens the module 651 / 1,976 → 320 / 545; of a branch that holds it in its own property 16,349 / 62,300 at every later selection → 19,739 / 75,332 once, at the second selection (it summarizes the module and keeps that summary), then 168 / 297 (when the parse completed the module, the first selection keeps it, 21,973 / 84,367 against 18,322 / 70,210, and every later one uses 167 / 299); of a branch that holds it twice 16,822 / 64,622 → 198 / 328 from the third. With an output row F0(3) added to the branch-held module, later selections still spend about 15,851 / 60,788 KiB. An output-only cache for that case would need a rule for the exact earlier resolved view and evidence that all dependencies come from its sealed context. What remains per selection is linear in the width with a small constant: the lookup levels and name indexes an attempt builds over the module or over the branch's own open level (about 45 bytes per member for a module the branch holds, 110 to 220 for one it opens; an attempt's lookup-cache entries, (3) below), and, where the branch reaches the module through an inline open block (`open { public L = load('lib') }`), the child-key projection of the branch's own level below the block's reach (`CompletedStructureContext.For`, about 470 bytes per member in exposure and 365 in detection; unchanged, 987 / 3,684 KiB a selection in all, against 17,170 / 65,687 before). This work stays BOUNDED BY THE AGGREGATE: every splice charges the module's weight (next paragraph), so the selections of a W-member module are limited to `MaxAggregateSourceLength` / weight (79 selections of a 4,000-member, 106 KB module at the defaults; the 78 after the first allocated about 1.8 GB before, about 0.15 GB now). A test seam runs every materialization without the store (the reference: every walk, signature analysis of the module included) or verifies every reuse against the ordinary walk it replaces (`CompletedModuleViews.ModeForTesting`; `CompletedModuleSpliceTests`). (3) LEVELS OVER WIDE COMPLETED SCOPES. Each operation builds its own lookup level and name index over an enclosing scope its lookups reach (about 90 bytes per declared property per selection across the detection and exposure chains), and a provider an enclosing scope opens has its member index rebuilt by every attempt that reads a member (about 38 bytes per member per selection), because an attempt's entries in the recorded chain's lookup cache are taken back when it ends (next paragraph). This residual is linear in the width with a small constant and is bounded by the source size only. WHAT COUNTS IT: none of this is evaluation work. `MaxSteps` counts evaluator steps only, and a materialization charges it nothing; its work is charged to its own static ledger (static queries, settlement frames, bounded from its loaded body), to the aggregate (splices and downloads) and to the module count, and it observes the evaluation's cancellation token and the source-processing token throughout. A host that must bound the residual of (2) and (3) uses those limits and cancellation; a cumulative per-run front-end work budget is not current policy. `CompletedProviderSummaryTests`.

SPLICES CHARGE THE AGGREGATE (September 2026; C# host policy, no Lean counterpart). Before Q-31/Q-32 (October 2026) a module's names resolved against the scope it was spliced into, so the front end elaborated a cached import view once per enclosing scope — each splice in a distinct scope a distinct semantic region of the Shared-DAG guarantee — and before this rule a cache hit charged nothing: a 10 KB module spliced into 373 distinct blocks cost 3.2 GB of allocation and 5 s, work growing with the PRODUCT of the root's and the module's sizes (terabytes at the per-source ceilings), and every copy re-reported the module's front-end diagnostics, which no per-source ceiling bounded. Every module-cache entry therefore records the module's ELABORATED WEIGHT (`ModuleLoader.CachedModule.Weight`: the aggregate its own fetch and elaboration charged — its source, plus its nested downloads and nested splices), and every cache-hit splice reserves that weight against `MaxAggregateSourceLength` before splicing; a splice that does not fit is refused at its site with `AggregateSourceLengthExceeded` (wording `load: splicing the already loaded '…' again …`) and the load is refused — kept as written, a missing module (MISSING MODULES ARE AN ANALYSIS STATE, above) — with no module slot and no download. Weights compose, so the aggregate bounds the ELABORATED program: a view whose own walk spliced its dependency K times and that is itself re-spliced J times charges J times its full weight. A repeated load in the SAME scope is charged as well (conservative and simple), and the rule outlived the per-scope elaboration it was introduced for: the front end now elaborates a module once per program, but source admission stays host policy over the program as written. A walk frame's splice charges are recorded on its `SpliceLedger` — part of the loader's walk context, one per frame: the root walk (`ElaborateAsync`), a fetched module's nested walk, and one deferred materialization attempt — and eager cancellation that abandons the frame releases them together with the frame's own source reservation. Completed nested frames and downloader invocations stay charged. A deferred materialization ATTEMPT THAT PUBLISHES NOTHING releases its own splices and those of its uncached nested module walks, whatever the reason it did not publish (November 2026, audit F4) — a load failure, a front-end error in the body, a static-structure or stack refusal, cancellation, or abandonment after the load, during the compile or at publication: the region decides (only it knows whether the attempt published), the loader owns the ledger and the rollback (`ModuleLoader.ReleaseUnpublishedSplices`), the release happens under the materialization gate and zeroes the ledger, so the load's rollback on cancellation and the region's release subtract once. Failed retries therefore do not drain the aggregate through splices. AN ATTEMPT ALSO LEAVES NOTHING IN THE LOOKUP CACHE OF THE CHAIN RECORDED AT ITS BRANCH (2026-11-17; C#-only resource rule, no value, diagnostic or classification changes, no Lean counterpart), whether it published or not: the unit's demand-time passes (open-provider validation, exposure and its provenance finalizer) run under that chain, whose `OpenMemberIndexCache` lives as long as the parsed tree, and every level they create registers in that one cache, wherever it hangs (below the branch, directly under the chain's root for an inline open target or a module, or as a prelude level of its own). The cache journals what the attempt adds to its four tables (completed levels, block-head levels, member indexes, body levels) and the region takes exactly those entries back under the same gate and on every exit path, refusal, cancellation and a fault included (`OpenMemberIndexCache.BeginMaterializationAttempt`). An attempt that reaches the freeze takes them back immediately before it (`AttemptWrites.TakeBackBeforeFreeze`, called by `DeferredModuleRegion.CompileLoadedBody`), so they are not held through the freeze, where a materialization's live memory peaks; from then until the attempt ends the cache refuses a write, an internal error raised before any table changes, so nothing that still runs can leave an entry behind. An attempt that exits earlier takes them back beside the splice release, in `DeferredModuleRegion.RunCoreAsync`'s `finally` (`AttemptWrites.TakeBack`), which also ends the claim of an attempt that took them back early. The work is proportional to what the attempt wrote. Inside one attempt the sharing is unchanged, and every table is a work cache (a miss rebuilds an equal answer), so no lookup depends on an entry an earlier attempt left. Before, a refused selection left its levels, its facts and its elaborated body there at every selection (63 KB per refused selection of a unit with ten nested bodies, 3.7 MB with a thousand, linear in the number of selections); after, the retained size does not grow with the number of selections. `DeferredAttemptCacheRetentionTests`. The attempt's DOWNLOADS stay charged — the module slots, and the aggregate for text accepted for parsing, including text parsed by cancelled nested walks — because the aggregate also bounds total parsing: refunding them would let attempts that each fetch a new module and fail parse up to `MaxModuleCount` × `MaxSourceLength`. RESIDUAL, by design: a module an unpublished attempt downloaded and cached keeps its full weight, including its own splices, and its next splice charges that weight again. Under an aggregate limit that exactly fits the program, that parsed tree then stays refused (`AggregateSourceLengthExceeded`, wording `load: splicing the already loaded '…' again …`) until it is parsed again. This is over-refusal only and stable (retries do not climb); a "prepaid splice" that would remove it is an owner decision, not current policy. A published attempt keeps every charge. `SourceProcessingLimitsTests.CachedRepeatedModule_IsDownloadedOnce_ButEverySpliceChargesTheAggregate`, `DiagnosticBudgetTests` (`DistinctScopeSplices_*`, `SpliceWeight_*`, `CancelledMaterialization_*`), `ModuleLoaderCancellationTests`, `DeferredRegionSourceAccountingTests`.

## Bounded diagnostic lists

BOUNDED DIAGNOSTIC LISTS (September 2026; C# host policy, no Lean counterpart, no language-semantics change). Malformed source produces up to one diagnostic per UTF-16 code unit — a bad character, a stray closer, a semicolon — so, measured before this rule, one 2 MiB source kept 2.1 million diagnostics (up to 464 MB retained, three times what the largest valid program retains), a `;` flood rendered 400 million characters, 699,048 refused `''` targets fit in one maximal root, and `KatLangException.Message` and the CLI `check` loop rendered every one; real non-KatLang files pasted as source report 30–130 per thousand code units. `DiagnosticBag` is the ONE accumulator of one diagnostic LIST: a parse — `FrontEndPipeline` creates it from the run's `SourceProcessingBudget`, and the lexer, the parser, the load guard, the module loader with every module and nested module, and every elaboration pass report into it — or one demand-time materialization of a deferred branch, which gets its own bag from the loader's budget (it becomes that evaluation's `ModuleRegionMaterializationFailed` error; nothing accumulates across attempts or in loader state). Its capacity is `SourceProcessingLimits.MaxDiagnosticCount` (public; default and hard maximum `MaxSupportedDiagnosticCount` = 1000; only lower requests take effect, above-maximum ones clamp, non-positive ones throw). THE PREFIX LAW: a list keeps exactly the first N diagnostics the unbounded operation would report, in reporting order, then — only when more were reported — ONE `DiagnosticCode.DiagnosticCountExceeded` marker: unpositioned (a fact about the list, never about a place in the source), not counted toward N, and carrying the most severe dropped severity (an error for every KatLang diagnostic), so a truncated list always blocks evaluation and truncation never changes whether a list does. A report past N is COUNTED and dropped: no `Diagnostic` is constructed, and the high-volume sites never format a dropped message (the lexer's formatted messages go through the lazy `Report<TState>`; the densest parser flood, stray root closers, uses constant messages. Lazy formatting also covers implicit-argument resolution's `UnforwardableParameter` reports, one per callee parameter bare forwarding cannot supply, its `OutputSpreadMarkerOnAlias` reports, whose transparent-alias wording suggests a wrapper over every parameter of the target, the parser's module-holder report of that code, and every branch report re-issued for a further family sharing the body).
