A template implements only `IReadOnlyList<ParameterPattern>` (no mutation route), caches its content facts (captures, names, name set, first capture per name, collecting count, binding kinds, `CallableParameter`s per source) with atomic publication, holds shape ONLY — never an owner, activation, value, cache entry, host result, random state, or cancellation state — and lives exactly as long as the tree that references it (a run-local interner; no static registry). Identity stays owner-relative: an owner is its `Algorithm.Declaration` (plus its activation at run time) and a parameter is `(owner, slot)`, so owners sharing a template remain distinct declarations that bind in their own activations, keep their own property-cache entries, random draws, host calls, and cancellation observations, and are positioned by their own call nodes. A synthesized bundle is shared ACROSS owners exactly when the inputs `BuildImplicitCallArguments` reads are identical (`SharedBundleKey`: the callee's pattern list, the caller's single forwarded collecting stream or none, and the binding kinds the bundle actually reads — with a forwarded stream the owner's whole signature, otherwise the canonical set of the callee's COLLECTING capture names the caller binds ordinarily, including ordinary enclosing bindings that the final signature leaves in scope. The resolver indexes the binding kinds and collecting destinations of each shared part once and combines their canonical sets to decide re-spreads; every other slot is a context-relative read by name, so owners binding different declarations, and owners whose names meet the callee's, share one bundle); a closed context (a written list, a branch pattern) keeps FE-2's region-local bundle. Every later pass pays a template once per run or ONE layer per context: the collision validator's `ParameterBindings` extends a context by a template LAYER instead of L entries (a template of at least `ParameterBindings.TemplateLayerMinimum` names; the detector's `ParameterOwnership` never meets a lifted signature — detection runs before automatic parameter forwarding, Q-04 — so its former layers are deleted; a lookup consults the layers on its lexical chain, which structural depth bounds, and name sets are canonical `NameSetInterner` unions with the template's set); the summary channel summarizes a transparent argument bundle once per resolution and drops an absorbed completed seed whose names an owner's template owns without folding it (one residual per seed and template); the exposure rewrite returns a rewrite-invariant subtree unchanged, so a shared subtree stays shared across regions; the structural preflight enumerates a template as ONE weightless grouping child, replaying a rejection at the slot the slot-by-slot walk reports; walkers that only seek nested algorithms (collision and open-provider validation, the provenance finalizer) skip a subtree that reaches none through a per-walk `NestedReachIndex`; `CallableSignature` derives its flat parameters, names, and display text lazily from template facts; and the semantic model builds a template's implicit-parameter symbols and property parameter metadata once per build (shared read-only lists; `PropertySignatureInfo.DisplayText` composed on first read with unchanged value equality) and skips semantically inert synthesized subtrees. The public elaborated AST may therefore share one `ParameterPatterns` list between owners, and the semantic model one parameter-metadata list between properties; both are immutable. Measured on the public parse path at K = L = 1,024 (Release, one process per measurement): sibling owners fall from 2,133 MB to 34 MB allocated and from 65 MB to 1.1 MB retained (2,203 MB to 41 MB with an own parameter per owner), owners in distinct parent contexts from 4,305 MB to 75 MB, and the physical parameter-pattern slots from K × L to O(K + L) (1,049,600 to 2,048 for sibling owners); a 157-program before/after differential (reference-expanded elaborated trees, run results with error contexts, seeded draws, host-operation logs, async runs, diagnostics, editor dumps, exposure metadata) is byte-identical. Unchanged and outside FE-3 (FE-4): the editor model's per-scope `ScopeVisibility.Symbols` snapshots (K scopes × (K + L) visible symbols — output size by public contract; the sibling shape's editor model still retains 130 MB) and physical `Property.RequiredAncestorParameters` lists (K × W). Per-owner by design: an owner's own head records and its collisions — K owners meeting one L-wide tail at K DISTINCT names keep K distinct L-wide parts, as their orders are genuinely distinct, and the evaluator's preflight walks that structure in cancellable blocks (THE EVALUATOR'S ADMISSION WALKS ARE CANCELLABLE, below) — and K DISTINCT second-order callees, whose synthesized bundles stay flat `OutputBundle`s. The exposure pass's summary channel follows the same split: a shared bundle seed that an owner's TEMPLATE name set covers only in part (a forwarding owner reusing an enclosing binding of one of its names) is replaced by its residual, the uncovered names, read once per (seed, template set) (`SummaryMemo.PartlyOwnedResidual`), instead of being folded, qualified and stripped at the bundle's full width once per owner. Initial repair measurements on the public parse path at K = L = 1,024 (Release, `G = [v0, …]` lists): own-name meeting owners fell from 945 ms and 873 MB allocated, 1,025 templates of 1,049,600 slots, 1,024 bundles and 2.1 million evaluator preflight edges to 264 ms, 137 MB, 2 templates, one bundle and 13,315 edges, like the non-meeting control (282 ms, 139 MB); owners meeting at K distinct names from 873 MB to 291 MB (one bundle; their distinct parts keep 1.06 million preflight edges); enclosing-binding owners from 568 MB to 272 MB, their control's 269 MB (`Fe3MeetingNameSharingTests`: every resolver, exposure and preflight counter is an owner term plus a width term, against an independent merge oracle). The O(K + L) promise is for K owners over ONE shared tail: a chain of N DISTINCT-name lifting links (`F0 = x0 + 0`, `F(i) = F(i-1) + x(i)`) is Θ(N²) physical, because each link extends its callee's composed signature and so copies the callee's local head (link i holds an i-pattern head and builds an i-argument head bundle), and it is bounded by the static envelope: lifting storage is charged to the operation's ledger where it is allocated — 4 slots per pattern a flat template or composed head copies, 2 per argument of a bundle the resolver builds, nothing per read — so such a chain is refused as `StaticStructureLimitExceeded` instead of exhausting the host (measured 2026-11-14, Release: the default envelope admits 2,150 links and refuses 3,200 in 0.7 s at a 336 MB peak working set, where the unmetered elaboration took 6.2 s and 1.48 GB; 64 MiB admits 509; `LiftingChainAdmissionTests`). Pinned by `ImplicitSignatureTemplateTests` (exact counters — `SignatureTemplatesBuilt`, `SignatureTemplateSlotsBuilt`, `OwnerSignatureInstances`, `OwnerSignatureHeadSlots`, `OwnerSignaturesMaterialized`, `ContextTemplateLayers` — at two sizes and asymmetric K and L, the reference census, owner identity and runtime independence, cross-owner bundle-sharing exactness, immutability, concurrent readers, module and deferred owners, and a design-independent allocation guard), with `ImplicitArgumentSharingTests` and `FrontEndDagComplexityTests` re-pinned to the shared representation.

The FE-3 hostile-review guards also cover wrappers around composed signatures: forwarding a signature with unique captures and no empty structural groups preserves its template (host-built empty groups still use the ordinary recursive lifting merge), and extending it copies only local heads over the flat shared tail. Synthesized `OutputBundle` membership can likewise use a small head over one immutable tail; preflight, walkers, reachability, editor inertness, exposure invariance, and dependency seeds consume that tail once. Nested parameter-group membership is snapshotted once per resolution before template facts are published; public host-owned patterns retain their existing live-list contract outside templates. Grouped and collecting owner heads compose editor metadata without copying the tail. `SummaryResidualNameProbes` counts actual name inspections, including residual work that qualification counters alone miss. `Fe3HostileReviewTests` pins these cases and independent owner caches, suspended hosts, cancellation, nearest-owner lookup, and reachability. FE-4a (below) removed the `RequiredAncestorParameters` amplification; FE-4b, the editor model's per-scope `ScopeVisibility.Symbols` snapshots, remains separate and still materializes its public per-scope snapshots.

SHARED REQUIRED-ANCESTOR SETS (FE-4a, September 2026; C# front-end representation/performance, NO semantic change and no Lean counterpart). K properties can LOGICALLY require the same W ancestor inputs — K × W memberships of the relation "property P requires input A of owner O" — and until FE-4a every property also PHYSICALLY held its own W-entry `Property.RequiredAncestorParameters` array and its own W-entry internal `CaptureRequirements` array, while both fixed points behind them rebuilt W-wide sets per property per round: the exposure resolver's per-level `HashSet<Requirement>` summaries and the summary channel's member seeds, each iterated round by round (every round read the PREVIOUS round's map), so a chain of K properties took K rounds of all K — K² × W. The canonical shape (one owner of W parameters, a `Base` reading all W, K properties reading `Base`) retained (K + 1) × W entries per list; a 14 KB chain of 512 properties allocated 10 GB in six seconds. Requirement summaries are now CANONICAL IMMUTABLE SETS built by one run-local `RequirementSetInterner`, the owner-qualified instance of the one canonical-set engine (`CanonicalSetInterner<TElement>`, of which FE-1's `NameSetInterner` is the name instance): an element is a `Requirement` — the input's name AND its owning scope by reference, so same-named parameters of different owners never collapse — and equal content is one hash-consed node, so a property that inherits a sibling's summary SHARES it and comparing summaries is one reference comparison. Each level's least fixed point is solved dependency-driven (`PropertyExposureResolver.LevelSolver`): every equation is evaluated once, in dependency-first order (`DependencyFirstOrder`), and again only when a summary it read changed; a property's own requirements are resolved once; open, path, and visible-name settlements are memoized per top-level key and served only while every level-local summary they read is still the same set (a nested, possibly cycle-cut settlement is never reused), and the map keeps the former last-write-wins meaning for same-named declarations. The summary channel's member fixed point (`SolveMemberSeeds`) is dependency-driven too: a member whose base seed only names local members IS their one non-empty seed or holds it by reference beneath its own qualified delta, a strongly connected component of such members is solved as one unit (its least fixed point gives every member one seed), any other expansion is reused by exact base-seed content while the member seeds it read stand, change detection compares structure before folding, and the redundant final re-qualification is gone. A set read from another run — a deferred region's demand-time elaboration reads the eager run's ancestor summaries — is ADOPTED by its elements (sets carry their interner's identity token, never the interner), so interner keys are never mixed. `RequiredAncestorOutputs` projects each distinct final set ONCE per run into an immutable list shared by every property whose summary it is — names sorted ordinally, captures sorted by name then owner depth, exactly as before — and flat lists are further shared across sets by EXACT content (element-by-element equality; the hash only selects a bucket), which is sound because a list's content is its whole meaning (names; owner-RELATIVE depths the evaluator resolves from each property's own scope): sharing never merges properties, declarations, or owner activations. A set derived by one union from a much larger subset (at least `MinimumDerivationBase` inputs, extended by at most a quarter of them) is projected into a PERSISTENT list (`ImmutableSortedSet` of names, `ImmutableList` of captures) extending its base's by the difference, so K properties each adding a few inputs to one W-wide set cost O(W + K × delta × log W). Every list is immutable (`ReadOnlyCollection` over a private array, or a persistent collection), references only its elements, and is never shared across runs (no static cache): the public collection type changed from a down-castable mutable `string[]` to these immutable types, each nonempty property list now has its own immutable `RequiredAncestorList<T>` wrapper over that shared backing, preserving generated `Property.Equals`, `==`, and hash behavior based on list reference identity. A record copy retains its wrapper; host-supplied lists retain their existing contract. Property debug text preserves its historical array label. `EvalError.LocalOnlyProperty.ToString()` intentionally renders bounded requirement contents such as `[x, y]`, independent of collection type; its structured fields, content equality, hash contract, and formatted diagnostic messages are unchanged. Measured on the public parse path (Release, one process per measurement): the canonical K = W = 1,024 shape falls from 491 MB to 24 MB allocated and from 24.8 MB to 0.74 MB retained, its physical entries from 1,049,600 to 1,024 per list; a K = W = 512 chain from 10,118 MB to 14 MB (6,018 ms to 44 ms); a partial overlap (`P(i) = Base, x(i)`) at 1,024 from 590 MB to 33 MB allocated, 25 MB to 2.2 MB retained, and 1,050,624 to 13,302 physical entries; a cycle whose members each add an input at 512 from 11,243 MB to 17 MB; a 215-program before/after differential (reference-expanded elaborated trees with every required-ancestor list, diagnostics, sync and async runs, host logs, loops, editor dumps) is identical up to that `ToString` type name. Separate and unchanged: the editor model's per-scope `ScopeVisibility.Symbols` snapshots (FE-4b). Pinned by `RequiredAncestorRepresentationTests` (logical memberships vs. distinct backings vs. physical entries at several sizes and asymmetric K and W, the `RequiredAncestor*`/`SummaryMember*` work counters, transitive/diamond/cyclic/partial-overlap shapes, one settlement per opened or navigated member, exact identity under same-name shadowing, exact element-by-element content sharing under colliding hashes, ordinal order, immutability and retention, concurrent readers, stale-settlement and stale-expansion fixed-point cases, modules and deferred regions, an independent slow oracle over generated dependency graphs, and a design-independent allocation guard over the public parse path), with the FE-1 wide-owner probe guard re-pinned to the shared member solve.

SHARED SCOPE VISIBILITY (FE-4b, September 2026; C# editor-model representation/performance, NO semantic change and no Lean counterpart). The editor axis of the same guarantee: S semantic-model scopes each seeing V names are S × V LOGICAL visible-symbol memberships backed by O(distinct names + changed paths) PHYSICAL storage, never S retained V-entry snapshots. Every frame's visible set is a canonical hash-consed treap derived from its parent's by the frame's own open, parameter, and property layers (shared layers applied once per parent tree; an FE-3 template table a container already applied is not re-applied), each `ScopeVisibility` holds its own read-only wrapper over the shared view, and one `VisibleSymbol` object serves every scope that sees that symbol. The full statement, the public-contract review, and the eager differential oracle are in [`editor-semantics.md` § Scope visibility and module provenance]. `ScopeVisibilityRepresentationTests`.

SHARED OPEN-TARGET MEMBER INDEXES (September 2026; C# front-end and editor lookup performance, NO semantic change and no Lean counterpart). The open-provider axis of the same guarantee: K scope levels opening one W-member target cost O(K + W) member-index work per operation, never O(K × W). An `open` provider answers a name from its target's public-member index — for each ordinal name the target's FIRST PUBLIC property (a private same-name entry is skipped, never an answer; exposure takes no part in selection), plus the first public null-named entry — which reads nothing but the target's own ordered property list, so it is a pure, immutable function of the target OBJECT (`OpenTargetMemberIndex`). Every provider of the SAME target within one operation therefore shares one index through the operation's `OpenMemberIndexCache`, keyed by exact reference identity — never record equality, content, or spelling, so value-equal target objects and equal spellings over different targets get separate indexes. The operation owns the cache with its scope chain: parameter detection (each run), exposure resolution (its provenance finalizer inherits it), and open-provider validation create one at their chain root and every derived level inherits it; each `SemanticModelBuilder.Build` creates one and passes it explicitly to every level it derives, because its chains hang under the process-shared prelude level, which never carries one (its providers keep their private, prewarmed indexes). A cache is never static and never reachable from a target, so it dies with its operation; a chain retained for a deferred module region's demand-time continuation keeps its cache, and those continuations belong to the same parse and are serialized by its loader's materialization gate; each materialization attempt is an operation of its own, so what it adds to that cache is taken back when it ends, published or not (§ Source loading and deferred modules, SPLICES CHARGE THE AGGREGATE). Only the target index is shared: which providers a level has, their first-occurrence dedup, ambiguity, precedence, and shadowing stay per level. On the canonical shape (K sibling blocks each opening one W-member `Lib`, K = W = 1,024), detection builds 1 index instead of 1,024, and allocation falls from 141 MB to 42 MB for `Parser.Parse` and from 111 MB to 11 MB for `SemanticModelBuilder.Build`, both now linear. `LookupOpenMemberIndexBuilds` pins one build per distinct target per operation; `OpenMemberIndexSharingTests`, `ElaboratedLookupAccelerationTests`.

STATIC REFERENCE CHASES (M4★, ALIAS-UNIFY-1, B3-2★; C# front-end performance, NO semantic change and no Lean counterpart). The reference-chain axis of the same guarantee: K reads or opens through an L-long chain of transparent references (`A1 = A2`, …, `AL = Lib`, read as `A1.x` or opened as `open A1`) cost O(K + L), never O(K × L). No pass runs a chase or a chase memo of its own: detection, implicit-argument resolution, exposure and the editor read the reference-chain, member/provider and inference facts of the ONE admitted static-structure operation (Q1–Q7, `StaticStructureOperation` over `StaticStructureQueries`; `docs/design/static-structure-resolver-2026-10.md`), whose demand-driven engine keeps every query's answer for the whole operation, so a chain is followed once whatever reads it. Every parameter answer a hop reads is the answering level's own (`ElaboratedPropertyScope.BindsParameter`), never the asking site's. The resolver's levels are one object per static scope (`StaticStructureOperation.ResolverLevel`), and a written `open` target is resolved once per OD-D stratum, identical ordered providers sharing one first-PUBLIC member index, so a chain declared inside a navigated body (`Lib = { public A1 = A2 … }` read as `B = Lib.A1` or opened as `open Lib.A1`) and K aliases `R = M` of a W-member opened provider stay linear too. `AliasUnifySecondReviewRegressionTests.StaticChases_AllocationGrowsLinearly` guards the root-level shapes by parse allocation per doubling; `AliasUnifyThirdReviewRegressionTests` the navigated and opened ones by the operation's query work (`StaticQueryEvaluationCount`) and provider observations (`StaticOpenTargetComputations`, `StaticOpenIndexBuilds`, `StaticOpenIndexMemberVisits`); and `AliasUnifyTenthReviewRegressionTests` to `AliasUnifyThirteenthReviewRegressionTests` the nested owners, owner paths and open targets over nested owners by `StaticQueryEvaluationCount` per doubling. The per-pass chases and chase memos this replaced (the ALIAS-UNIFY-1 second and third reviews) are deleted; `StaticStructureDefaultCutoverTests` pins the removal of the superseded chase authorities.

FRONT-END PASS-LOCAL LIFETIMES (SEC-S1, October 2026; C# front-end representation and object-lifetime change, NO semantic change and no Lean counterpart). The Shared-DAG guarantee bounds front-end WORK; this rule bounds what the passes keep ALIVE while they work, for the same admitted program. Heap dumps at the live peak of `Parser.Parse` on dense source showed transient state four to eight times the returned result, including state retained past its last consumer: (1) the implicit-argument resolver's persistent signature map kept every superseded dictionary version reachable through its version chain — O(P log P) nodes for a level of P properties — although only a map's CURRENT entries are ever probed, so a `SignatureVersion` now records its changes (name, replaced entry, new entry) and `SignatureFootprints.Capture` recovers an ancestor's entry from the current one through the walked changes, with the same projections and probe counts; (2) summary seeds allocated four empty collections each and every property-less algorithm an empty member map, retained by the completed-summary memo for the whole resolution — the sets are now created on first write (a missing set reads as empty everywhere, and every public accessor materializes the real mutable set), short absorbed-part lists deduplicate by scan, and an empty member map is the shared `AlgorithmSummary.NoMembers`; (3) the collision validator kept one set per visited node — it now stores a node's first context inline and allocates a set only for a node a second distinct context reaches; (4) the pipeline's own frames kept every finished pass's input alive, because unoptimized (tier-0) code reports locals, parameters, and spilled argument temporaries live for the whole method — `FrontEndPipeline` now hands the tree from pass to pass through one `ElaborationInput` holder and runs each pass and validator in its own helper frame, so the raw syntax tree, the detected tree, and a finished validator's memo are unreachable once their consumer returns (apart from what the output shares). A future pass added to the pipeline must follow the same discipline: take its input from the holder in a helper, put its output back, and never hold a tree in a pipeline frame. State a pass leaves on the tree it publishes is held to it too: a dot edge's provisional miss (M4★, `Expr.DotCall.ProvisionalMiss`) carries the closures of both passes' state — the checks parameter detection deferred, the decider implicit-argument resolution registered — until resolution has made those checks, and then lets go of both (`ProvisionalMiss.Release`; `AliasUnifyTwelfthReviewRegressionTests`); and every level a resolution run walked — whose input entries and written opens reach the detected tree — lets go of them once the run ends (`ResolutionRun.ReleaseLevels`), while an alias's recorded target names no level at all: since B3-5★ its identity is the target's static context key, a chain of declaration tokens (`AliasUnifyThirteenthReviewRegressionTests`) — unless a deferred branch's context keeps the chain for its own run, as it keeps that run's signature map and processed bodies (part of the output). Measured with the isolated `fe-mem` probe (`fuzz/README.md` § Front-end memory probe): 2 MiB `Parser.Parse` peak commit −23% to −39% across dense, wide-deconstruction, implicit-lifting, and ancestor-requirement shapes, live peak −37% to −58% at 256 KiB, allocation −8% to −22%, retained results unchanged. `FrontEndMemoryRepresentationTests` pins the representation repairs (mutation-checked) and their parity; pass lifetimes are verified separately by heap roots and isolated process measurements. `FrontEndMemoryProbeTests` checks the physical census, nested metadata and alias coverage, typed result oracle, and valid-source checks. The `fe-mem-diff` corpus differential produced identical fingerprints of diagnostics, trees, exposure, requirements, and typed run outcomes on 6,221 inputs. These are bounded observations, not a complete equivalence proof.

## Traversal exhaustiveness

`Expr` is a C# `closed` record hierarchy (`Ast.cs`): its direct descendants are exactly the sealed nested variant records, mirroring Lean-modeled forms plus the front-end/native-only forms, and the compiler knows the set is finite. A switch EXPRESSION over `Expr` that names every variant is therefore proven exhaustive by the compiler with no catch-all arm, and because `CS8509` (a non-exhaustive switch expression) and `CS8655` (an unhandled `null` input) are errors in `src/KatLang` and `tests/KatLang.Tests`, a missing variant fails the BUILD at every such dispatch, naming the variant it does not cover. Every full-hierarchy dispatch that is naturally expression-shaped is written that way, with no `_ =>` arm: the structural preflight's child enumeration (`AstStructuralPreflight.TryGetExprChild`), the evaluator dispatch trio (`Eval`, `EvalCounted`, and its `// MIRROR OF` twin `EvalCountedAsync`, whose enumerated sync-delegable leaf group — Num, StringLiteral, BoolLiteral, Grace — is the one place a variant may run through the synchronous leaf core; `NativeCall` is NOT one: its declared-argument reads are ordinary `Expr.Param` value reads, so it has its own async twin case), `ResolveAlg`, `ExprKind`, the static structural-member classification (`AstHelpers.ResolveStaticStructuralMemberProvider`), and every REWRITING or SEARCHING front-end walk (`ParameterDetector` — ClassifyOpenTargetHeads, ProcessOpenExpr, RewriteParams, ProcessExpr, SpanSearchChildren; `ImplicitArgumentResolver` — ProcessOpenExpr, RewriteImplicitCalls, ProcessExprNested; `PropertyExposureResolver.RewriteExpr`; `PropertyDependencyGraphBuilder.CollectSummarySeed`; `ModuleLoader.ProcessExpr`). Multi-statement arms are extracted into named helpers so the dispatch itself reads as a table; a helper that CONTAINS a recursive evaluation adds one frame per nesting level and is admitted only where the calibrated depth pins (`AstStructuralDepthProcessTests`) still hold. The compiler cannot check a switch STATEMENT, so the six side-effect walks whose arms are inherently statement-shaped — `AstWalker.VisitExpr`, `ParameterDetector.CollectFreeParams`, `ImplicitArgumentResolver.CollectReferenceNames`, `ImplicitArgumentResolver.WalkLiftingRoles` (the one formula-lifting role walk behind the B3-5★ signature analysis and the preselection potential-lift reachability), `ModuleLoader.ProcessExprAsync` (its awaiting loops must stay inside the one calibrated frame), and `SemanticModelBuilder.VisitExpr` — enumerate their intentional leaves explicitly and keep a runtime guard that THROWS `InvalidOperationException` on an unhandled variant; never add a silent `default: break` to one of them, and never force one into an artificial value-returning switch expression. Deliberately PARTIAL classifiers with a semantic "other" arm (`ResolveAlgForOpen`'s `BadOpenForm`, `ZeroArgumentValueDemandError`'s anonymous shape, the loop planner's "unsupported → generic path", the parser's open-form normalization, the expression-spine machines' non-spine guard, `ExprNameRenderer`'s fallback spelling) are not full-hierarchy dispatches and keep their defaults. Tests cover what the compiler does not: `AstWalkerTraversalTests` pins the walker's per-variant child visits, `FrontEndTraversalTests` pins the runtime-guard matrix of the statement-form walks plus intentional-leaf reference identity and per-pass recursive-child semantics, and `AsyncEvaluation/AsyncExpressionDispatchTests` pins per-variant sync/async outcome equality and per-recursive-variant async-seam routing with genuine suspension. The ONE test-side variant enumeration is `tests/KatLang.Tests/ExprVariantCatalog.cs`; the behavioral tables above (and the static-classification and Lean-encoder sample tables) are ratcheted against it so a new variant fails a test until it has a row, because a present-but-childless arm is compiler-exhaustive and still wrong. ADDING AN `Expr` VARIANT therefore means: satisfy the compiler at every exhaustive dispatch (each error names the variant), classify it in the seven guarded walks, and give the behavioral tables a row. (The parser's former branch-body Grace scan, an eighth such walk, was deleted by Q-16 G-O on 2026-10-07.)

The same contract covers every KATLANG-OWNED FINITE VARIANT HIERARCHY (September 2026): `RunResult` (`KatLangEngine.cs`), `EvalError`, `Result`, `Algorithm`, `Pattern`, `ParameterPattern`, `CallableBindingNode` (`Signatures/CallableBindingPlan.cs`; internal since the September 2026 public API audit, like the rest of the evaluator's signature and binding planners), and `ErrorContext` (`ErrorContext.cs`) are `closed` records exactly like `Expr`. Each is a finite set KatLang itself defines — `Algorithm`, `Pattern`, `ParameterPattern`, `Result`, and `EvalError` mirror Lean inductives, `RunResult` and `CallableBindingNode` are C# host/binding shapes, and `ErrorContext` is the typed C# refinement of the free-text `String` in Lean's `withContext` — and none is an extension point: a `private` or `private protected` ordinary constructor had never closed them, because a record's synthesized copy constructor is `protected`, so `sealed record Rogue(RunResult o) : RunResult(o)` compiled from another assembly and could reach a production "unknown variant" fallback (`ErrorContext`, which declares no constructor at all, was derivable through its synthesized protected ordinary constructor as well). With `closed`, a foreign derivation is `CS9382` and a switch expression naming every variant is compiler-exhaustive, so the former unknown-variant arms are gone: `RunResult.RenderDisplay`, `KatLangEngine.EvaluateToAtoms(Async)`, and `OutputFormatter.RenderDisplay` (a switch statement converted to an expression around a `RenderSuccess` helper); `EvalError.Code` (context wrappers are unwrapped iteratively before the exhaustive terminal dispatch) and `KatLangError.FormatEvalError`, whose `_ => error.ToString()!` had been silently rendering `DuplicateProperty` and `DuplicateBranchPattern` — evaluator errors reachable only from host-built trees — as record `ToString()` text, now given messages; `Result.ToItems`/`AsNum`, the evaluator's operand and item descriptions, and the structured layout's block-line test; `Algorithm.WithParent`/`WithParams`/`AcceptsZeroSuppliedArguments`, `CallableSignature.FromAlgorithm`, `PropertyExposureResolver`, `PropertyDependencyGraphBuilder.CollectAlgorithmSummary` (statement converted to expression), and the semantic model's property-info construction (whose `null or Algorithm.Builtin` arm keeps the nullable input honest); the pattern matcher `MatchPattern` (the counted twin `MatchCountedPattern` was deleted with Model C; statements converted to expressions around bind/sequence-value helpers — Lean's `matchPatternInto` is the same total match; SEC-02-FU narrowed it to `MatchLiteralPattern`, still naming every variant) and the branch-head formatter; `MissingCapturePattern`, `BuildPatternArgument`, `CallableBindingPlan.CreateNode`. A `_` arm survives only where it is a SEMANTIC default — a partial classifier (`IsResourceLimit`, `IsLiftableError`, `Result.ValueCount`/`SingleAtomicNumber`/`SpreadItems`, `Pattern.TopLevelArity`, which mirrors Lean's own wildcard), a nullable input (`SupportsLexicalDotCall`), or a host-constructible enum kind (the parameter binders' `BadArity` defaults) — and guarded statement-form walks (`ValueTextRenderer.AppendValue`, `FrontEndRegionKeys`, `ParameterPropertyCollisionValidator`, `TryCreateBinderParameterPattern`, `CallableSignature.AddParameters`, `SemanticModelBuilder.CollectParameterPatternSpans`, `FreeReferenceNames`, `CollectAlgorithmSiblingDependencyIndices`) keep their fail-loud defaults, since the compiler checks no switch statement. This is not a guarantee for every existing walk: `Pattern.BoundNames`, pattern hashing, and `SequenceValueParameterPattern.Captures` have no unknown-variant guard, and the result comparer/hasher retains its existing semantic and permissive defaults. Those sites require an independent coverage decision when adding a variant. `EvalError` keeps one INTERNAL variant (`ModuleRegionMaterializationFailed`), so a consumer's switch over it is never exhaustive — hosts classify through `EvalError.Code`/`KatLangError.Code` — while every other root is consumer-exhaustive. Pinned by `ClosedHierarchyContractTests` (`tests/KatLang.Formatting.PublicApi.Tests`), which compiles generated consumer probes with the SDK used to build the tests against the built `KatLang.dll`: every derivable constructor route (the copy constructor; for `ErrorContext` the ordinary constructor too) is refused with `CS9382` and nothing else — each probe overrides the root's abstract members, so a missing override can never be the reason it fails —, a built-in variant of every root is constructible, the generated no-catch-all switches compile for every all-public root, an omitted variant fails, and the `EvalError` switch fails; the closed set itself is ratcheted against the assembly's `IsClosedTypeAttribute` metadata and recorded in the public API baseline (`public closed record`). `KatLangErrorCodeTests` pins that every `EvalError` variant renders a message. Closing a hierarchy is a compiler contract, not a CLR seal (an already-compiled foreign subtype can still load and is outside the supported hierarchy); do not close a type that is a deliberate extension point (`OutputFormatter` is derivable by design: a protected constructor, documentation inviting derivation, stated guarantees, and `PublicFormatterExtensionTests`), and never restore a `_ => throw` unknown-variant arm to a switch expression over a closed hierarchy. `ErrorContext` is the one root closed for OWNERSHIP rather than exhaustiveness (September 2026): its fifteen variants are the structured evaluation frames KatLang attaches through `EvalError.WithContext` — produced only by the evaluator and the sequence optimizer — and a foreign subtype could inject arbitrary `FormatMessage()` behavior into the otherwise closed `EvalError` graph while gaining nothing over the supported free-text channel (`TextErrorContext`, i.e. `EvalError.WithContext(string, inner)`, the exact image of Lean's string context), which hosts keep, together with every built-in context's public constructor. Its production consumers are not full-hierarchy dispatches and were not converted: `KatLangError`'s recognizers are deliberately PARTIAL, keyed on one `(context, inner error)` shape each, over the generic `{context}: {message}` arm that renders any other pairing through the virtual projection; `KatLangEngine.IsTopLevelNoProgramOutput` likewise recognizes one shape. The only exhaustiveness gain is test-side: the diagnostic-parity describer (`LoopDiagnosticParityAssertions.DescribeContext`) names all fifteen with no catch-all, so a new context kind fails that build.

The INTERNAL finite hierarchies are `closed` too (September 2026): `LoopExprPlan` (`Optimizations/Loops/LoopExprPlan.cs`), `FilterCountSourcePlan` (`Optimizations/Sequences/SequencePipelinePlan.cs`), `PreEvaluationAstViolation` (`Ast.cs`), and `Evaluator.PreparedSequenceBuiltinSuffixArg` (`Evaluator.Results.cs`, a private nested record). Their expression-shaped dispatches name every kind with no catch-all arm — `LoopOptimizer.EvalLoopExprPlan` (a switch statement converted to an expression around per-kind helpers; the planned unary/binary spine keeps its per-frame stack probe inside the helpers) and its diagnostic renderer `DescribeLoopExprPlanNode`, `SequencePipelineOptimizer.ExecuteFilterCount` and `SourceKind`, `Evaluator.PreEvaluationValidationError` — so the fake-variant experiment fails the build at exactly those sites. `PreparedSequenceBuiltinSuffixArg` is consumed by kind PREDICATES that check the prepared kind against the builtin's registered metadata kind (a mismatch is an internal metadata error, not an unknown variant) — it is not converted. (The class `OpenCandidate` was a closed root too until the open-candidate channel was deleted in November 2026.) Pinned by `InternalClosedHierarchyTests` (`tests/KatLang.Tests`, a friend assembly), which ratchets the internal closed set against the assembly's `IsClosedTypeAttribute` metadata and each root's exact sealed variant list.

## Variant-owned Algorithm payload

`Algorithm` (`Ast.cs`) is the closed four-variant hierarchy of the Lean inductive (the fourth, `Alias`, since October 2026), and since September 2026 its payload is owned by the variant exactly as each Lean constructor carries its own fields. The base type has NO payload member and no `init` accessor: it carries the two equality-transparent runtime slots (`Declaration`, `DeferredRegion`), the Lean-total functional updates `WithParams`/`WithParameters`/`WithParameterPatterns` (the identity on `Builtin`, `Conditional` and `Alias`, which have no parameter list — Lean's `withParams`/`withParameterPatterns`, modeled behavior rather than a fallback), and the clause-elaboration factories `ElaborateClauseGroup`/`ElaborateClauseDefinition`. `Algorithm.User` (Lean `.mk`) owns `Parent`, `ParameterPatterns`, `Opens`, `Properties`, `Output`, and `HasExplicitParameterList`, plus `FindDuplicatePropName()`; `Algorithm.Conditional` (Lean `.conditional`) owns `Parent`, `Opens`, and `Branches`, plus `HasDuplicateBranchPatterns()`; `Algorithm.Builtin` owns `Id`; `Algorithm.Alias` (Lean `.alias`, a CALLABLE ALIAS — FWD-02, binding indirection) owns `Parent`, `Opens`, `Properties` and the written `Target`, plus the runtime-only equality-transparent `ResolvedTarget` slot the front end records (the normalized target's identity, display name, algorithm and lifting signature, read by lifting, roles, forwarding and the editor; never by the evaluator, which resolves the target in its own scope). Before this change the base exposed ten virtual `{ get; init; }` payload properties (`Parent`, `Parameters`, `ParameterPatterns`, `Params`, `Opens`, `Properties`, `Output`, `Branches`, `ExplicitParameters`, `ExplicitParameterPatterns`) that `User` overrode partially, `Conditional` barely, and `Builtin` not at all, so `builtin with { Properties = … }`, `conditional with { Output = … }`, and `user with { Branches = … }` compiled and produced states Lean's inductive cannot represent (a probe showed `F.X` on a conditional with smuggled properties returning the member). Every such state is now a COMPILE error for every caller: a consumer gets `CS0117` (no such member) for a wrong-variant or base-typed payload write, `CS0200` for the read-only projections, and `CS1061` for a base-typed payload read, witnessed against the compiled assembly by the build SDK's compiler in `AlgorithmPublicSurfaceTests` (non-friend), which also compiles the positive control — every legitimate construction, update, and exhaustive consumer switch. Consequently a WRITE always narrows to the owning variant: the front-end passes match `Algorithm.User`/`Algorithm.Conditional` and `with` the variant (`ParameterDetector.ProcessUserAlgorithm`, `ImplicitArgumentResolver.ProcessUserAlgorithmRegion`, `ModuleLoader.ProcessAlgorithm`'s `else if (alg is Algorithm.User user)` arm, `PropertyExposureResolver.ProcessUserAlgorithm`), the evaluator's `WithParent` switches per variant, `ParameterDetector.ProcessConditionalBranchBody` rebuilds a branch body per arm — a host-built family in body position still has its OWNED `Opens` elaborated and their heads classified against the branch binders, keeps its branches, and receives none of the user-only row processing; only the payload-free `Builtin` returns at once — and a helper that mutates payload through a base-typed parameter is the thing this rule forbids — it would recreate the writable fake-universal slot.

Inside the assembly the Lean-total READS keep their spelling. `AlgorithmAccessors` (`AlgorithmAccessors.cs`) is an `internal static class` with a C# 14 `extension(Algorithm algorithm)` block whose properties are Lean's total functions: `Parent` (`parent`), `ParameterPatterns`, `Parameters`, `Params` (`params`), `ParameterCount` (`(params a).length`), `Opens`, `Properties` (`props`), `Output`, `Branches`, `HasExplicitParameterList`, and the total methods `FindDuplicatePropName()`/`HasDuplicateBranchPatterns()` — each an exhaustive switch expression over the closed set with no catch-all arm (a new variant fails the build there instead of silently inheriting `[]`), returning the owner's field and the empty value (`null`, `[]`, `OutputBundle.Empty`, `0`, `false`) for the others. An extension property has no `init` accessor, so `algorithm.Opens` reads on a base-typed value but `algorithm with { Opens = … }` does not compile; when the receiver is statically a variant its own instance member binds instead. The accessors are `internal`, so friend assemblies (`KatLang.Tests`, `KatLang.Benchmarks`, `KatLang.ParserFuzz`) read base-typed values the same way; a NuGet consumer narrows or uses the typed roots. The preflight child enumeration (`AstStructuralPreflight.TryGetExprChild`), `AsScopeCtx`, `LookupProp`, and every other total read go through them unchanged in spelling.

`User.ParameterPatterns` is the ONE stored parameter channel — Lean `.mk`'s `parameterPatterns` field. `Parameters` (Lean `parameters a = (parameterPatterns a).flatMap captures`) and `Params` (Lean `params a = (parameters a).map name`) are LIVE projections computed on every read and never stored: an empty stored pattern list returns the shared empty list, and a nonempty flat capture list uses one array per projection. Nested sequence-value patterns use the iterative `ParameterPattern.FlattenCaptures` with traversal/list storage (plus a name array for `Params`); the flat allocation bound does not cover nested or zero-capture grouped patterns. Nothing derived is stored because the caller-owned pattern list (and a nested `SequenceValueParameterPattern.Items` list) is read through — a host that mutates it sees the current state through every projection and every `with` copy (`AlgorithmParameterNamesTests`, `LoopStrategyPreparationTests.HostOwnedCallableMetadata_*`), and the loop-step snapshot (`SnapshotGenericLoopStepBindingContract`) snapshots the ONE channel. Count-only code reads the projection-free internal `ParameterCount` (`ParameterPattern.CountCaptures`, allocation-free for a flat list, an explicit stack for nested groups): the rule for new code is that a count is `ParameterCount`, a declaration list is `Parameters`, a name list is `Params`, and none of them may be stored on the record. A `CaptureParameterPattern` HOLDS its `ParameterDeclaration` (positional `Parameter`; the `(Name, Span, Kind)` constructor mints one; `Name`/`Span`/`Kind` derive from it; `CollectMarkerSpan` and the provenance note are the declaration's), mirroring Lean's `capture : CallableParameter → ParameterPattern`, so flattening returns the stored declarations instead of allocating copies and `ParameterDeclaration.ToPattern()` wraps `this`. Memos that used to key on the flat parameter LIST instance (which is now a fresh projection per read) key on the pattern list instead (`ParameterPropertyCollisionValidator.Extend`). The Lean-total updates follow Lean exactly: `WithParams` merges by name and, for a REPEATED capture name, keeps the first declaration (`mergeParameters` / `parameterForName?` — a host-built list may repeat a name, and a total update never throws), while `WithParameters` and `WithParameterPatterns` decide the variant before converting anything, so the `Builtin`/`Conditional` identity cases allocate nothing (`AlgorithmPayloadReviewTests`).

`HasExplicitParameterList` is the one explicit-list fact. The former `ExplicitParameters`/`ExplicitParameterPatterns` lists were verified over the whole corpus to be either empty or equal to the stored channel (a written list is CLOSED: the front end lifts nothing into it and reports an unresolved name as `UndeclaredIdentifier`), so they carried one bit. `ElaborateClauseGroup` sets the bit when a single ordinary clause head becomes the body's parameter list (narrowing to `User`; a non-user body is returned as it is — Lean's `withParameterPatterns` identity), the parser sets it on every assignment-deconstruction target helper, and nothing else does. Consumers read it where the explicit subset used to be read: `CallableSignature.FromUserAlgorithm` (parameter source `Explicit` vs `Implicit`), `ParameterDetector` (closed-list reporting and Grace policy), `ImplicitArgumentResolver` (the closed-specification gate), `LoopTempPlan`, `Evaluator.NamedParameters` and the semantic model (only a written list can contain the spanless `_error_` recovery binder, which must never be named or declared as a symbol), and `AstWalker.VisitUserAlgorithm`, which walks the stored pattern leaves of a written list for `VisitExplicitParameterDeclaration`. Pinned by `AlgorithmOwnershipTests` (explicitness per construct, the corpus law that a written list's captures are source-backed except the recovery binder and the synthetic deconstruction helper, lifted captures joining an inferred signature and never a written one).

Program roots are `Algorithm.User`: `ParseResult.Root` and `RunResult.Success`/`NoProgramOutput`/`EvalFailure`.`Root`. The parser's root is a user algorithm (`Parser.ParseRootAlgorithm`; every recovery placeholder is `new Algorithm.User(null, [], [], [], [])`), and every elaboration pass returns the variant it is given — a builtin as itself, a family as a family, a user algorithm as a user algorithm (`AlgorithmOwnershipTests.EveryElaborationPass_PreservesTheRootVariant`, `ModuleLoader_PreservesTheRootVariant`). The stage entry points stay base-typed because they are total over the NESTED algorithms they rewrite, so the type system states the root invariant once, at `FrontEndPipeline.ElaboratedUserRoot`, with a fail-loud guard for the variant-changing pass that must never exist. `SemanticModel.Root` stays `Algorithm` (the model is built over an arbitrary algorithm). Equality and hashing cover exactly the variant's own stored payload plus `HasExplicitParameterList`; the derived projections are not fields and the runtime slots stay transparent (`AlgorithmPublicSurfaceTests.StructuralEquality_CoversExactlyTheVariantsOwnPayload`). Measured on the paired Stopwatch harness (min of alternated rounds, 8 rounds): instance sizes `User` 168 → 88 B, `Conditional` 128 → 56 B, `Builtin` 112 → 40 B; bytes per run −0.2…−9.5 % across the 16 evaluator scenarios and −4…−9 % across four front-end elaboration shapes; time at parity within the machine's noise (`artifacts/api-cleanup-task1-perf/`).

## Budgets and cancellation

Persistent sequence/list materialization reserves its own IMMEDIATE stored-item cardinality exactly once, after the complete supply succeeds and before constructing its result. A nested constructed value then occupies ONE parent item; nesting never transfers, suppresses or duplicates its completed reservation. Canonical sequence capture costs zero for zero/one supplied items (empty stores no slots, singleton reuses its child) and reserves the immediate count for two or more; exact lists preserve their boundary and reserve their immediate length, including one. Redundant expression grouping creates no boundary. Ordinary output capture and unfolded capture/plain-block list elements share `Evaluator.MakeCheckedSequenceCaptureAtRows`; list construction independently uses `ReserveCollection`. Both twins' unfolded arms reach the owner through ONE `NoInlining` leaf, `MakeCheckedUnfoldedSlot`, and the owner takes the run's `EvaluationBudget`, never the by-value `EvalCtx`: the recursive written-slot frames recur once per nesting level, and in Debug each by-value context argument or struct temporary there costs every level (the first form of this repair grew that frame from 1,376 to 1,936 bytes per level). The temporary row-slot buffer a row loop or loop step collects is not a value and reserves nothing (it is transient-bounded, below); a spread layer's captured supply keeps its own reservation (`EvalSequenceSpreadCounted`). Completed child reservations remain cumulative if a later slot fails or cancellation interrupts the unfinished parent; the parent reserves nothing until its own supply succeeds. This is C# host resource policy, with no Lean budget counterpart, and does not strengthen R3′/Q-66 into operational equivalence. Pins: `NestedMaterializationReservationTests` (hand-derived exact thresholds, host-built empty/singleton structures, spread, failure order/spans, genuine async suspension, cancellation and strategy/work parity).

TRANSIENT ITEM SUPPLIES ARE BOUNDED (SEC-01, October 2026; C#-only resource policy with no Lean counterpart — Lean models no limits and stays the unlimited reference, so admitted-program semantics are unchanged). Every item supply a written expression list or row loop forms — a call's effective argument supply (`FormNeedSupply`, also every retained invocation plan's and argument view's), a postfix selection's selector supply (`FormSelectionSupply`), a loop step's row supply (`EvalAlgOutputSlots`, `EvalWhileVerdictSlots`, the planned handover `LoopOptimizer.MaterializeGenericHandoverSlots`), and the slot accumulators of list literals, output captures (`EvalOutputRowsPreparedCore`), written groups (`EvalExplicitSequenceValueRowSlots`) and internal sequence joins, in both twins — carries one `Evaluator.TransientSupply` and admits each slot through `AdmitTransient` BEFORE its items are appended, copied or wrapped in Ready cells. (A list literal with no spread element can never expand its supply, so only a spread-bearing literal allocates the bounded accumulator; any other accumulates in a plain list with identical admission — the SEC-01-FU allocation follow-up that removed 16 B per literal.) Once a spread opens (a known collector slice, an argument view's cells, computed items, or a multi-item loop row), the WHOLE supply is bounded by the always-active `MaxCollectionItems` ceiling (`EvaluationBudget.AdmitsTransientExpansion`), including written prefix and suffix slots. Thus `F(x, A*)` with a full `A` is refused before copying or wrapping its items. Purely written supplies remain source-bounded: `F(1, 2, 3)` and a `while` state plus its flag with no expanded rows keep their ordinary behavior under a tiny collection limit. Internal sequence joins are the exception to source-bounded written slots: a shared host AST may unfold into many more leaf occurrences than nodes. `SequenceConstructLeaves` streams leaves left to right with only a depth-bounded pending stack, and the result accumulator admits EVERY contributed item, plain leaves included, before growth; no leaf-occurrence metadata buffer is formed. An expanded supply whose total size passes the ceiling OVERFLOWS: it retains nothing more but forms COUNT-ONLY to its end, so later explicit spreads still evaluate in written order with their effects, failures and child reservations, and an earlier-established failure of a later spread still wins. At the end it is refused with `CollectionSizeLimitExceeded` whose `Requested` is the supply's full size: a list, capture, group or join through its own reservation over that size — exactly the error the unbounded accumulator reached, at the same position — and a call, selection or loop-row supply through `EvaluationBudget.RefuseTransientSupply`, positioned at the slot or row that overflowed it and retained as terminal. Ordinary suspended arguments and selectors stay undemanded. The bound is not a reservation: it never moves `MaterializedItems`. THE ONE OUTCOME CHANGE: a call, selection or loop round whose expanded total supply exceeds the ceiling is refused at the end of formation instead of reaching binding (where it met an arity verdict, an ignored collector's success, or the collector's own later refusal). `EvaluationBudget.PeakTransientExpansion` (diagnostics/tests only) records the largest admitted total expanded-supply size. Normal planned loop iterations apply the same row admission as generic execution, even when every spread row emits exactly one item; overflow still evaluates the remaining required rows before refusal. Pins: `TransientSupplyBoundTests` (every supply kind, default-ceiling `F(A*, A*)`, ordering and effects, known-slice and view laziness, generic/planned/async parity, and the peak never exceeding the limit).

HOST-VALUE ADMISSION (SEC-01-FU, October 2026; C#-only resource policy with no Lean counterpart — Lean models neither host operations nor limits). A host-operation return is the one value that enters a run without passing an evaluator reservation, so every SUCCESSFUL return is ADMITTED at the host boundary before any consumer, the zero-argument property cache, or a later evaluation sees it: `Evaluator.NormalizeHostOperationValue` → `Result.AdmitHostOperationValue`, the SAME DAG-safe iterative walk that normalizes it (one traversal, a reference-identity memo, an explicit frame stack), shared by the synchronous dispatch, the async twin's await site and the test-seam META receivers. (1) Every DISTINCT sequence or list node's IMMEDIATE item count must fit the effective `MaxCollectionItems` — checked when its frame is created, before any child is visited or rebuilt storage allocated; a nested value occupies one parent slot, so `[[1, 2, 3], [1, 2, 3]]` passes a limit of 3 and a shared tower of 2-item lists passes a limit of 2 however many leaf paths it has. (2) Every string leaf must fit the effective `MaxStringLength`; contents are never truncated. (3) Nested structures charge the SEC-02 structural-work unit — one per slot inspected inside a nested structure and one per nested structure completed; the top-level value's own slots are bounded by its admitted count and charge nothing, so flat and scalar returns charge nothing — counted per scan segment (at most one admitted node's slots) and flushed through `EvaluationBudget.TryChargeStructuralWork`, which observes host cancellation, whenever 256 units are pending and at completion. The first violation in depth-first, left-to-right order ends the run with `CollectionSizeLimitExceeded` / `StringSizeLimitExceeded` (`EvaluationBudget.TryAdmitHostCollection` / `TryAdmitHostString`), retained as terminal: the wrapper body fails with it, so the value is never cached, consumed, or absorbed by a consumer, and nothing — no later row, argument or host operation — runs after it. Admission is a SIZE CHECK, NOT A RESERVATION: the cumulative `MaxMaterializedItems` / `MaxMaterializedStringChars` budgets count what evaluation CONSTRUCTS, and a host return was constructed by the host — it may be a returned argument (already reserved once), a value the host retains across calls, cached by the property cache, or shared across runs — so admission never moves them, and charging every reachable node per return would double-charge returned arguments and repeated reads; a collection the run builds FROM a host value (`[H()*]`) reserves as ordinary construction. Every route admits identically (generic, planned, async twin, public engine and evaluator entries), so the accounting is strategy-neutral. Host exceptions and faulted awaitables propagate unchanged, a null return and a returned call reference stay fail-loud contract violations (`InvalidOperationException`), and a refusal is a structured `EvalError`, never an exception. GUARANTEE BOUNDARY: KatLang cannot bound what a host delegate allocates before it returns; it bounds what it ADMITS, the traversal work admission spends (node-bounded, charged, cancellable), and what it builds afterwards. The per-call HOST-ARGUMENT call-reference scan (`Evaluator.HostArgumentBoundaryError`, § Call references at the host boundary) — formerly uncharged and uncancellable although it repeats for every host call, so one large nested value passed to many calls was re-walked for free — charges the same unit (nested structures only) and observes cancellation the same way; the once-per-run result export stays uncharged. The argument scan uses indexed continuation frames bounded by nesting depth, visits children depth first from left to right, and charges only inspected slots and completed nested verdicts (empty structures included), never an untouched suffix. Public construction already snapshots its items and exposes read-only views, `with` copies share that immutable storage, and the closed hierarchy admits no host subtype, so an admitted value cannot change afterwards. Pins: `Hosting/HostValueAdmissionTests` (exact boundaries, default ceilings, nesting, shared DAGs, immutability, genuine suspension, every public entry point, cache, callbacks and loop steps, cancellation at a charge, the exception contract, terminality, the accounting policy).

EVALUATOR-OWNED STRUCTURAL COMPARISONS ARE CHARGED (SEC-02-FU, October 2026; C#-only resource policy with no Lean counterpart — Lean's `applyComparison` and `bindNeedPatterns` decide the same `resultValueEq` verdicts with no cost model, so no Lean edit applies). Every structural equality or hash the EVALUATOR decides observes host cancellation and charges the run's structural-work budget, whether it runs inside `contains`/`distinct` (SEC-02), in `==`/`!=`, or in NEED-04's repeated-name VALUE agreement: `Evaluator.ApplyComparison` — the one comparison link shared by the generic spine, its async twin and the planned loop arm — and the one inspecting binder (`BindAcceptedNeedPatterns`: ordinary calls, callbacks, loop steps, deconstruction and clause families) decide through `Result.EvaluatorValueEquals` / `Evaluator.RepeatedValueEquals`. A pair with no real work — scalars, one shared reference, different kinds or counts, empty structures, and strings shorter than 64 code units, of different lengths, or sharing one text — is decided by the context-free `Result.ValueComparer` in O(1) with no allocation and no unit; every other pair runs the SEC-02 operation comparer: a fresh single-comparison walk per `==`/`!=` link (`ValueSemanticComparer.SingleChargedEquals`; SEC-02-FU2 — it records each expanded pair once and keeps a frame only for a parent with children still to visit, folding tail-descended levels into a completion count charged unit by unit through `OperationMemo.ChargeEach`, so its verdict, units, charge boundaries and expanded pairs are exactly the operation walk's: both walks make the same class decisions in the same order), ONE per binder activation (`ValueSemanticComparer.TryEquals`) for its repeated-name agreements (they reuse each other's retained verdicts; it dies with the activation), with the walk's pending units flushed at the end of each comparison, so no unit outlives it. STRING PAYLOAD is work too, in every operation walk (SEC-02's builtins included): two distinct equal-length texts compared, and every string hashed, charge `length / 64` units (`ValueSemanticComparer.StringCharsPerUnit`) BEFORE the one native ordinal comparison or hash runs, and a verdict or hash worth at least 16 units (1,024 code units) is retained by reference for the operation, so N references to one long string are hashed or compared once. String verdicts keep their own retained dictionary (`OperationMemo.StringVerdicts`), apart from the structural memo, so no structural record can drop them (SEC-02-FU2 mixed-value memo repair). The native call itself has no internal checkpoint: the longest uninterruptible payload operation is one string of at most `EvaluationLimits.MaxSupportedStringLength` code units, charged — and cancellation observed — just before it. A REFUSED charge is the run's retained terminal error, returned as that error: never `false`, never the agreement's `BadArity`, never a clause non-match, so no later link, clause, demand, host operation or random draw follows (the run's retained terminal failure would stop them at their first checkpoint anyway; the binder never relies on that); cancellation stays the budget's `OperationCanceledException`. Literal string patterns use the same charged equality through the result-bearing `Evaluator.MatchLiteralPattern`; a literal is source-bounded but may be inspected repeatedly at run time, so its payload is charged on each comparison and a refusal aborts dispatch. Numeric and Boolean literals stay O(1); the C# matcher's former bind and structural arms, unreachable from every binder, were removed rather than left as an uncharged comparison route (Lean's total `matchPattern` keeps them for its guards). The host-facing `Result.ValueComparer` stays context-free and uncharged. Units depend on the operands' REFERENCE graphs: equal values with different internal sharing may charge different units and reach a step limit at different points, never decide differently. EQUAL KNOWLEDGE IS REFERENCE CLASSES (audit §1.9, November 2026; C#-only, verdicts unchanged): both evaluator walks skip a pair whose two references are already one equivalence class (`ReferenceClasses`, a union–find over references with path compression on its committed layer; it links by index — a mix of the runtime identity hash, so it stores no class sizes and records one entry per union, only the node linked below another one, November 2026), not only an exact pair met before. Pairs are recorded at the same points as before: seeded at first descent, entered when at least 16 wide, the top-level pair never during its own walk. In the operation walk a recorded pair is a TENTATIVE union with an undo log. When the comparison returns `true` the tentative unions are committed. When it returns `false` they are undone and only the unions of levels that COMPLETED are re-linked, because only the open levels were assumed equal and every completed level of a finite acyclic value is truly equal. Unequal knowledge stays exact ordered pairs (`OperationMemo.UnequalPairs`), retained exactly as before. The equal structural memo is therefore O(distinct nodes reached); retained unequal and string verdicts still grow with the distinct inspected pairs. Each entry follows charged work, so the step budget bounds the memo. The item limits (`MaxMaterializedItems`, `MaxCollectionItems`) never did. Two equal values whose sharing does not correspond node to node (a shift register against a path-mixing DAG of width m) now use O(|A| + |B|) expanded pairs and memory instead of about m² reference pairs per level. Find costs depend on link depth: hash-based linking has logarithmic expected depth under an independent uniform-order model, which runtime identity hashes are not guaranteed to satisfy; it has no deterministic depth bound for collision-heavy or hash-ordered inputs. One `==` of the m = 512, depth-40 witness fell from 11.1 million expanded pairs and about 309 MB to 58,381 pairs and 5 MB. Where the sharing corresponds node to node within one comparison, each class is one recorded pair, so the counts are the exact-pair walk's. Classes are symmetric, so one operation's reversed or transitive comparison of already-equal references is answered without a walk. `SharedValueGraphComplexityTests.ReferenceClasses.cs`. The host-facing `Result.ValueComparer` decides the same way (November 2026; C#-only, verdicts and hashes unchanged): its per-call walk skips a nested pair whose two references already share a class of that call's own `ReferenceClasses`, recorded where its exact-pair memo recorded — every nested non-empty pair when the walk descends into it, the top-level pair never. It uses the committed layer only and no undo log: the walk returns `false` at its first mismatch and drops the classes with the call, so the one shared static comparer keeps no state between calls or threads and no union of an open level is ever read again. Its hash was already one memoized hash per distinct node and is unchanged. One comparison of the m = 512, depth-40 witness fell from 8,388,097 expanded pairs and about 630 MB to 39,908 pairs and 5 MB; node-to-node corresponding sharing keeps its exact expansion counts. Since 2026-11-17 the two walks whose memo dies with one comparison (this one and the evaluator's single comparison) use `CommittedReferenceClasses`, one 24-byte parent entry per union, so the isomorphic control allocates exactly what the exact-pair memo did (1,963,096 bytes) instead of the two 32-byte class entries per first union that union by size stored. `SharedValueGraphComplexityTests.HostComparer.cs`. Number, Boolean, one-reference and O(1) string pairs are decided in place at the top of `EvaluatorValueEquals` with the comparer's own semantics (SEC-02-FU2; `ValueSemanticComparer.RequiresChargedStringWalk` is the one string rule). CLAUSE ATTEMPTS ARE OBSERVABLE to tests (SEC-02-FU2; C# test instrumentation, not language semantics): `EvaluationObservations.ClauseAttempts` (`ClauseAttemptObservations`, internal, opt-in, run-scoped, `null` in every production run) records, from both family drivers through shared observation helpers, each clause a dispatch begins binding — immediately before `BindAcceptedNeedPatterns`, never for a cardinality-skipped clause or a family-level decision — and its outcome (`Matched`, `NonMatch`, `Error`, or `Pending` when an exception abandoned it), so a dispatch that continued past a terminal refusal is visible even when Q-02 terminal retention stops the later clause before its first demand. Dispatch and attempt bookkeeping belongs to the opt-in observer; the hooks take contexts/results by read-only reference to preserve recursive driver stack space. The existing 40-link family chain on a 1 MiB stack pins the disabled-observation path. `SharedValueGraphComplexityTests` (SEC-02-FU files `SharedValueGraphComplexityTests.EvaluatorComparisons.cs`; SEC-02-FU2 `.SingleComparison.cs`, `.ClauseAttempts.cs`).

Runtime evaluator budgets are OPERATION-LOCAL. Dynamic depth is the only balanced protocol (`EvaluationBudget.TryEnterInvocation` / `TryEnterArgumentEvaluation`, released by `ExitInvocation`); steps, materialized items, and materialized string units are CUMULATIVE and are never refunded by a failure. The following balanced state protocols are encoded as `readonly struct` scopes disposed from a `using` — `Evaluator.BudgetLevel` for the depth chokepoints, `Parser.NestingLevel`/`Parser.DelimitedProduction` for the parser budget and owed closers, `ModuleLoader.WalkContextScope` for the loader's sink/traversal-base/token swap during a deferred materialization — so an exception, an early return, cancellation, or recovery can never skip the restore; a refused enter changes no owned state. Evaluator try-enter helpers yield a no-op default scope on refusal; parser entry throws its structured nesting-limit exception. Default instances of every scope restore nothing. The parser and loader scopes validate owner-held `StateScopeStack` tickets before restoration, rejecting duplicate, stale, or out-of-order disposal without changing state. Source-module budget entry/exit remains a separate `try`/`finally` protocol. A LOOP STEP invocation is deliberately outside the depth protocol (final audit, September 2026, recorded as a design question): the one generic round kernel `Evaluator.RunGenericLoopRounds` (every generic round: the generic route's and a planned handover's continuation) charges one step per iteration and never depth, because an iteration repeats work without nesting — so recursion routed through loop steps (`Step = Step(x).repeat(1)`) nests host frames `MaxDepth` never sees. On the synchronous route the host-stack backstop bounds that nesting (`EvaluationStackExhausted`, never an overflow); on the async twin route the stack hop can continue it on fresh stacks, as genuine suspension already could. THE LOOP ENTRY IS THAT BACKSTOP'S SITE (November 2026, finding 1 of the 2026-11-21 adversarial performance audit; C#-only host policy under Q-09a, no Lean counterpart): a loop that will run a round first passes `EvaluationBudget.TryAdmitLoopEntry` at the head of `Evaluator.EvalNeedLoop`'s setup, before the step is projected, planned or invoked, on the generic and planned strategies and both routes alike. The promise above had no probe behind it for most shapes: the round kernel charges its step through `TryChargeStep`, which probes nothing, and the loop-step row readers (`EvalWhileVerdictSlots`, `EvalAlgOutputSlots`) bypass the row funnels' probe, so the only probe such a recursion could meet was the one a FRESH need cell's VALUE computation runs. `F(n) = repeat(F(n), 1)` has one (its literal count) and was the one tested shape; `W = while(W())`, `F(n) = while(F(n))`, a `.while` written inside its own step, and `F(c) = repeat(F(c), c)` (a forwarded count) had no such probe. The investigation observed process stack overflows when no other limit fired first. The admission is NOT a level and charges NOTHING: no step, no depth, `peakDepth` untouched, nothing to release; with host-stack headroom it reads nothing at all, so an ordinary loop keeps its value, effects, counters and cancellation chokepoints (Q-09b), and a loop of zero rounds is not probed. Only its refusal reads the run, in the chokepoints' order: host cancellation throws first, a failure the run already retained keeps its place, otherwise the stack refusal is retained as the run's terminal failure. It reports no step verdict even when the step budget is exhausted: the round's own charge decides that, and a low stack must not move the point at which a semantic limit fires. The twin's entry first tests `StackHop.HostStackIsLow()` and hops (the site is one body with an `asynchronous` flag, so the hop is guarded by the flag and pinned as a `StackHop` branch point in `TwinNormalizationTests.FlagBranchPoints`), so on that route loop-step recursion continues on fresh stacks from EVERY shape, not only from those with another hop site in their cycle. Loop-step accounting remains unchanged: a finite `MaxSteps` or host cancellation can stop this recursion, but `MaxDepth` does not bound it; charging depth per step invocation would change every loop program's `peakDepth` and the planned/generic counter parity, so it is not done here. THE ASYNC TWIN HAS NO SEPARATE LIMIT ON LOOP NESTING (owner-decision task `4fa0755b82e8414f948f1915e434998e`, 2026-11-21 performance audit, finding 1, recommendation 3): each level holds its state machines on the heap, so an unbounded loop-step recursion without `MaxSteps` grows until the host cancels it or memory runs out, where the synchronous route stops at the backstop; a total loop-nesting limit separate from `MaxDepth` (so that `peakDepth` and the planned/generic parity stay as they are) would close it and is not decided here. The Q-36 context-chain readers a failure passes once per level it leaves (`Evaluator.NestMissingOutput`, `IsOwnMissingOutputError`, `IsMissingOutputError`, `ConvertOwnSpreadMissingOutput`, `IsLiftableError`) are loops over the chain (`EvalError.UnwrapContext`, `WithContextTerminal`) with the recursive definitions' outcomes — the same verdicts, the same copied frames with their spans and slots in the same order, the same terminal, an unchanged failure returned as the same object: one native frame per context frame overflowed the process when a failing twin recursion of 700 to 1,000 levels (about 1,200 context frames) unwound on a completing continuation's stack. Those that decide on the terminal — and every classification asked once per level a failure leaves (`IsTerminalForRun` before a context frame is attached, `IsResourceLimit` and the host-termination test at a failed need-cell completion) — read the terminal each frame recorded when it was built (`EvalError.UnwrapContext`; A CONTEXT FRAME RECORDS ITS CHAIN'S TERMINAL (2026-11-24, C# implementation). A failure leaving nested async loop steps acquires two context frames per level. Each level checks terminality and missing-output provenance, and a failed need cell checks for a resource limit and the host-termination marker. On `76df3dc4` these checks walked the growing chain. An instrumented Release run at 4,000 levels recorded 96 million frame visits during evaluation, 8,002 frames constructed and about 56,000 visits during formatting.

`EvalError.WithContext` records the inner error's terminal in an equality-transparent slot when it initializes `Inner`. Constructors and `WithInner` set both together, and a copy that changes a span or provenance keeps both. A host-built frame with a null inner error records null. Each classifier keeps its own handling of that case. `UnwrapContext` reads the recorded terminal in constant work and stack. `NestMissingOutput` copies the immutable chain once when it changes an own missing output to nested. Classifying an ordinary failure or an already nested missing output reads no inner frames. The frame's additional reference costs eight bytes on x64. Successful runs that construct no context frames pay no frame allocation.

`DeepFailureUnwindScalingTests` counts reads of `WithContext.Inner` in the evaluation flow at four depths on the async twin. It measures formatting separately, includes genuinely suspending steps, and compares the structured errors, provenance, messages and budget counters against synchronous runs at safe depths. `ContextFrameTerminalRecordTests` checks host-built chains and record copies, and `EvaluationLimitsProcessTests` exercises the bound in the Release stack suites.

SOURCE-PROCESSING CANCELLATION INSIDE THE ELABORATION PASSES (2.06, 2026-11-08; C#-only host policy with no Lean counterpart — Lean models no cancellation, so no Lean edit applies). `RunOptions.SourceProcessingCancellationToken` is observed at the front-end phase boundaries, around every module fetch, and at the start of every bounded block of elaboration work, through ONE checkpoint, `FrontEndTraversalObservations.ObserveCancellation`: an optional passive observer records the checkpoint (`CancellationCheckpoints`, per kind) and runs a test-only hook BEFORE the token is read, so the checkpoint that sees a cancellation throws and the block it guards never starts. The units: parameter detection — one per algorithm (`ProcessUserAlgorithm`, `ProcessBranchBody`) and one per property and output row; implicit-argument resolution — one per algorithm and one per property and output row of its rewrite (`ProcessUserAlgorithm`); the completed-signature validators (collision, open-provider and inferred-output rules) and exposure's dot-member provenance finalizer — one per algorithm the base walker expands (`AstWalker.CancellationToken`, the default token for every other walk); exposure — one per algorithm level (`ProcessScopeBody`), per level-equation evaluation (`LevelSolver`), per frame a settlement pushes (`PendingResolution.Run`), and in the summary channel one per algorithm summarized (`CollectAlgorithmSummary`), per property of a level's summary graph (`BuildSummaries`) and per level expansion and per name it carries outward (`ExpandAtLevel`; until November 18 2026 one per frame of its frame driver, the same count). Detection and resolution already observed the token at every static-query read and `Queries.EnsureUsable`; their checkpoints make that independent of where the reads fall. The token travels on the run objects that exist (`DetectionRun`, `ResolutionRun`, `ExposureRun`, `SummaryMemo`, the level solver and settlement), never ambiently: detection and resolution read the structural operation's token, and a deferred compile's operation, validators and exposure all carry the materialization's linked token, so a cancelled materialization escapes with the token today's mapping produces (the consumer's evaluation token, or the host's source token, which stays authoritative) and stays retryable. A cancelled pass publishes no usable completion: exposure rejects its unfinished publication, including cancellation during post-seal provenance finalization, while a previously completed foreign publication remains usable; explicit-stack drivers still dispose suspended frames. An uncancelled run is unchanged — trees, diagnostics, static-query statistics, ledger charges and every other counter are those of a token-less run; the checkpoints allocate nothing (each run object carries one token field). Measured in Release on Windows (fe-mem `parse-cancel-N`, three repetitions per cell, latency from the token's cancellation to the escaping exception; `fuzz/README.md`): cancelling the opened chain (`open_chain`) during exposure took 3.0–3.1 s at 2,000 links and 47.6–49.3 s at 5,000 before, and takes 1.3–1.6 ms and 2.0–2.3 ms now; the 2 MiB `dense_props` and `dense_funcs` exposure took 0.38–0.70 s and 0.25–0.51 s, and `dense_funcs`'s validator window up to 0.24 s, and all take 1.0–1.5 ms now; Outside exposure and validators, measured latencies were mostly 1–4 ms; the 5,000-link chain's baseline resolution samples took 14.8–31.8 ms, and final samples took 1.1–1.4 ms. The `wide_decon`, `implicit_lift` and `ancestor_blocks` shapes are refused by the static-query ledger during detection at 2 MiB, so they do not exercise later passes at that size. The largest measured cancellation latency in that matrix was 43 ms, in a pass transition covered by static-query checks. A roughly 0.55 s interval between exposure checkpoints also included a gen-2 collection; checkpoint spacing alone therefore does not isolate pass work or establish a cancellation bound. The settlement's N(N + 3) frames are unchanged (their complexity is a separate change). Pins: `FrontEndCancellationCheckpointTests`. The semantic model builder's cancellable entry (`SemanticModelBuilder.Build(Algorithm, SourceProcessingLimits, CancellationToken)`, November 2026) observes its token through the same checkpoint inside its walk as well — for each analyzed algorithm, output row (including hoisted deconstruction sources), and emitted scope, and while examining scope-close metadata, written opens for deferred ownership, and lookup levels for a deferred reference — besides before its static analysis and before it returns, so a cancelled build stops at the next block with the token and returns no model; an uncancelled build is unchanged (pinned by `SemanticModelBuildWorkTests`).

THE RUNTIME DUPLICATE-HEAD GUARD IS CANCELLABLE AND UNCHARGED (SEC-03 follow-up, 2026-11-09; C#-only host policy with no Lean counterpart). The family preparation's `PatternMatchEquivalenceIndex` (§ Zero-argument property cache, the clause-family preparation paragraph) observes the run's token (`EvaluationBudget.CancellationToken`) at its bounded checkpoints, so cancellation surfaces as `OperationCanceledException` carrying that token — never an `EvalError`, a verdict or a non-match. It charges no step and no structural unit: it compares AST PATTERNS, not language values, once per run per family list, with work bounded by the family's AST like the structural preflight, so the "every evaluator-owned structural comparison is charged" rule (over `Result` values) does not apply to it. The public `Algorithm.Conditional.HasDuplicateBranchPatterns()` keeps its token-free signature.
