A host-built head with shared subpatterns is keyed once per DISTINCT node (2026-11-10, closing consolidated item 2.7): the index serializes the minimal DAG of the head's tree — one definition per distinct canonical subtree, in first-completion post-order, with first-occurrence binder indexes that are the same on every path — through a call-local reference-identity memo, so the key, the verdict and repeated-name equivalence are exactly the expanded tree's while key construction is linear in the distinct nodes, edges, literal units and binder-name units. Per-head definitions and binders use exact compressed ordinal tries: prefix scans only advance through the new key, and child maps order single UTF-16 units from a fixed alphabet. CLR definition indexes have at most ten decimal digits. There is no per-head logarithmic factor or content hash; the family index retains its ordered-key bound. Text-buffer growth copies only live text in cancellable blocks, and passive observations count those copies and all definition writes, including discarded equal definitions. The former pre-order tree key remains only as a test oracle (`PatternMatchEquivalenceIndex.TreeCanonicalKey`, `ClauseFamilyDagKeyTests`).

THE EVALUATOR'S ADMISSION WALKS ARE CANCELLABLE (2026-11-23, finding lifting-signatures-fe3-sharing-breaks-on-meeting-names of the 2026-11-21 adversarial performance audit; C#-only host policy with no Lean counterpart). Before this repair the structural preflight and the pre-evaluation validation walk over a large or expanded program — owners whose meeting names keep their signatures distinct, or a host-built tree with a list per owner — ran to the end whatever the host requested. After route selection, both observe the run's token (`PrepareAdmittedRun` hands it in on the synchronous route and on both async routes): the preflight's admission walk (`AstStructuralPreflight.CheckAdmittedProgram`) before its first edge and after every `AstStructuralPreflight.CancellationCheckpointEdges` (1,024) edges, including blocks of slots replayed to position a shared-bundle rejection. Unwinding a deep zero-weight spine and finding its nearest enclosing span also poll in blocks of 1,024 frames. The descent and replay report each block through the checkpoint `FrontEndTraversalObservations.ObserveCancellation` with kind `EvaluatorPreflight`. The validation walker polls at each algorithm expansion (`AstWalker.CancellationToken`). For META placement it checks each composed signature's local head and reuses the verdict for its immutable flat tail. The parts have disjoint names and preserve pattern levels, so a nested or repeated META is invalid within a single part. The walker applies the ordinary placement check to host pattern lists. A cancelled run escapes as `OperationCanceledException` carrying that token, preempting a verdict exactly as an already cancelled token does at the entry. Nothing else changes: the walk stays O(nodes + edges) of the admitted graph and charges nothing; a walk that is not cancelled reaches the same verdict, span, program facts (J7 plan retention, runtime-alias reach, alias freedom, tree and node counts) and table hand-over; host-built trees and the front end's own structural checks (which pass no token and record no checkpoint) are untouched (`EvaluatorPreflightCancellationTests`). The route walk, the blocks inside one wide body and deferred-body admission followed (next paragraph).

THE ROUTE WALK, ONE WIDE BODY AND DEFERRED-BODY ADMISSION ARE CANCELLABLE TOO (2026-11-25, follow-up of the repair above; C#-only host policy with no Lean counterpart). Measured on `7de84650` before this change: the route-selection walk over a host-built root of 65,536 members read the member list 131,066 more times after its token was cancelled; the validation walk finished a 65,536-link comparison body under one algorithm checkpoint; and a deferred-body admission walked a whole materialized body (160,005 edges for an 80,000-member module) without a token. After the change the same controls read 2,036 and 1,011 more times, and a cancelled admission stops within one block of 1,024 edges. ROUTE SELECTION: `DeferredModuleRegion.RequiresAsyncEvaluationCounting` observes the run's token before its first edge and after every 1,024 edges, per edge, so one wide node is not one block (checkpoint kind `EvaluatorRoute`). Two callers pass the token: the synchronous entry's deferred-region guard (`Evaluator.ThrowIfDeferredModuleRegionsOnSynchronousEntry`) and every entry that builds its cache from the route fact (`Evaluator.DecideTreeRequiresAsyncEvaluation`): the public `Evaluator.RunAsync`/`RunFlatAsync` family, `KatLangEngine.RunAsync` and the observed harness funnel. A cancelled walk decides no fact and builds no cache. Precedence is unchanged. The synchronous family still observes the token first and rejects asynchronous host operations before the walk. A cancellation requested inside the walk, before it reaches a deferred placeholder, escapes as that cancellation, which the token-first contract already ranks above the deferred-region rejection. A placeholder reached before the next block still rejects the tree. An async entry that pairs its cache from the fact preempts nothing: the pairing guard cannot fail, and both routes observe the same token before any check that could. THE ONE REMAINING WINDOW is the route rule's own walk (`Evaluator.RequiresAsyncEvaluationPath`). Only an internal caller reaches it, by supplying a synchronous-only cache, no asynchronous host operation and no decided fact. It takes no token because its answer decides the twin family's cache-pairing guard, which fails loud even under a cancelled token. Observing the token there could only replace that rejection by a cancellation. The walk reads every node and edge at most once, up to its first placeholder, reading the host-supplied AST collections; no public entry reaches it. ONE WIDE BODY: the validation walker also observes the token once per 1,024 units below its algorithm expansions (`EvaluatorValidationBlock`). The validation countdown advances as it visits expressions and algorithms, unwinds join spines, scans clause heads and parameter slots, and traverses alias dependencies and name sets. Nested pattern scans and shared-node revisits count toward the same block. The route and prefix walks also check the token every 1,024 pending stack pops, including leaves and memo hits that enqueue no edges. DEFERRED-BODY ADMISSION: `DeferredBodyAdmission` carries the run's token (`EvaluationBudget.RecordDeferredBodyAdmission`). Four walks observe it before their first edge and after every 1,024 edges (`EvaluatorDeferredAdmission`): the prefix walk over the admitted root, the body check, the replay that positions its rejection, and the prefix walk over an admitted body. A cancelled admission publishes nothing reusable. The root's prefixes are published only when their walk completes. A body is recorded as admitted only after its check and its own prefix walk. A cancelled admission is ABANDONED: every later call rethrows the run's cancellation without reading its partial prefixes or arrivals, because an arrival recorded before its children were propagated would hide them from every later walk. The subtree memo keeps only completed subtrees' facts, as under a rejection. A repeated selection of an admitted body observes nothing. The cancellation is the run's ordinary cancellation: thrown with the run's token on a synchronous path, and inside an async twin run's host-termination boundary retained once while the run's marker returns (`EvaluationBudget.CarriesCancellation`). A later run builds its own admission and judges the region's cached materialization in full; the materialization cache itself is never touched. Without a cancellable token each walk adds one branch per edge and allocates nothing. A run that is not cancelled reaches the same route fact, verdicts, program facts, hand-overs, admissions, counts and results (`EvaluatorRouteAndAdmissionCancellationTests`).

HOST TERMINATION IS CARRIED BY VALUE ON THE ASYNC TWIN ROUTE (2026-11-22, adversarial performance audit finding 2; C#-only host-execution policy with no Lean counterpart — the async surface has none — and no language, API or observable-outcome change). THE CONTRACT IS UNCHANGED: a run its host ends — an exception thrown by a host operation, a faulted host awaitable, the null-return contract violation, a cancellation of the run's token observed at a chokepoint or thrown by host code, a required source-processing cancellation — ends with that EXCEPTION at the public entry point, the SAME OBJECT with the stack trace it had where it arose (`ExceptionDispatchInfo`), and a cancellation carries the cancelled token. THE TRANSPORT CHANGED: thrown through the twins, the exception crossed about twenty `async ValueTask` frames per KatLang call level, and every one of them freezes the exception's growing stack trace and restores it into the frame that awaits it, so ONE host failure or ONE cancellation cost time and memory quadratic in the evaluation depth (measured on `b061a98d`, Release: `F(n) = if(n == 0, fail(1), F(n - 1) + 1)` raised the exception 322 / 1,177 / 2,317 times and allocated 14 / 188 / 727 MB at depth 15 / 60 / 120, against 1.3 MB for the same recursion without the failure; through loop steps, which charge no depth, 41.9 GB and 20,037 raises at 1,000 levels; the audit's cancelled runaway loop recursion did not return in 200 s). The synchronous route unwinds natively and was never affected.

THE MECHANISM. The twin path's four run entries (`Evaluator.RunAsync`, `RunCountedAsync`, `RunCountedWithTopLevelPropertiesAsync`, `RunCountedObservedAsync`) open a HOST-TERMINATION BOUNDARY on the run's budget around their evaluation (`EvaluationBudget.OpenHostTerminationBoundary` / `CloseHostTerminationBoundary`, closed in a `finally`) and rethrow afterwards (`RethrowHostTermination`). Inside the boundary (`CarriesHostTermination`) the exception is RETAINED ONCE, where it arises, and the run's MARKER (`EvalError.HostTermination`, an internal variant like `ObservationPending`: no payload, no code, no message — classifying or rendering it fails loud) travels upward as an ordinary failed result: (1) every budget chokepoint (`CheckContinuation`, so every enter, charge and reservation) and every need-cell observation entry (`NeedCell.ObserveRun`, through `EvaluationBudget.ObserveToken`) retains the cancellation instead of throwing it — the exception object is still created by the framework's own `ThrowIfCancellationRequested`, thrown and caught in one frame; (2) both host-operation sites catch what the host throws and retain it (`Evaluator.InvokeSynchronousHostOperationCarryingTermination`, shared by the two twins so their bodies stay mirrored, and the awaiting `EvalAsynchronousHostOperationCountedAsync`, which also carries the post-await observation); (3) a deferred region's materialization retains what the loader lets through (`MaterializeRequiredRegionAsync`); (4) the retained collector's observation entry reads the token through the same call. THREE RULES keep the outcome the one a thrown exception produced. FIRST OUTCOME WINS: the first retained exception is the run's outcome; a later one never replaces it, and a retained resource limit never outranks it, exactly as an error value never stopped a thrown exception (a cancellation observed after a limit was reached is still the run's outcome, as before). TERMINAL: the marker is terminal for the run like a resource limit — `EvalError.IsTerminalForRun` (a resource limit or the marker) is the ONE internal test of "may this failure be treated as an ordinary one", read by the context helpers (`WithCtx`, `WithCallCtx`, `WithFilterItemCtx`, `WithDotCallCtx`), `IsDeferrableEvaluationFailure` and the builtin slot's demand report, while `IsResourceLimit` stays the public classification — and every chokepoint answers with it from then on, so no expression, call, loop round, host operation, random draw or `tick()` runs after it; clause dispatch aborts on it (a failed demand is never a non-match), the absence classifiers (`IsLiftableError`, `IsAbsentIdentity`) never read it as "no identity", and a consumer that inspects a failure and continues is stopped at its next chokepoint. The entry point rethrows whatever result the evaluation returned. SAME STORED STATE: a need cell, a runtime projection or an invocation plan whose computation ends with the marker records the retained `ExceptionDispatchInfo` exactly as it records a thrown exception — stored, never published on either channel, the flight completed cancelled or faulted, the plan's waiters woken, never restarted — and neither an identity-only projection nor a plan route publishes the marker; an observer inside the boundary is answered by value (the terminal check at its entry, or `NeedCell.ThrowOrCarry` over the stored exception), an observer outside it rethrows the stored exception as before. The test-only clause-attempt observer (`ClauseAttemptObservations`) likewise records nothing for the marker: a host-ended attempt stays `Pending`, as it does when the exception is thrown past the dispatch (`ObserveClauseOutcome`, `LiteralRunWalk.Failure`).

WHERE IT DOES NOT APPLY. The boundary exists only where an entry point will rethrow: the synchronous route, a budget built below the entry points (the internal tests that mark a hand-built budget asynchronous), and every observation after the boundary closed keep THROWING, so no marker can reach a caller nothing would convert back. The internal META-receiver seam (`TryInvokeMetaReceiver`, test runs only) suspends the boundary around the receiver, which demands and invokes the run's own cells and reads their outcome as an exception. Two callers that observe the token directly and have no failure to return still throw from where they stand: a clause family's preparation (`PrepareNeedFamily`, the duplicate-head guard and the literal clause index) and the `atoms` value walk (`EvaluationBudget.ObserveCancellation`, which inside the boundary throws the run's FIRST exception). The immediate evaluator callers (`PrepareNeedFamilyHeads` and `MakeLanguageAtomsResult`) retain that cancellation and propagate it by value even on a call chain containing no need cell. An exception that is not the run's retained one — an evaluator invariant, the async cache seam's own exception — is never converted and propagates as it always did. WHAT A HOST CAN STILL SEE: the same exception object and token; its stack trace keeps the frames it was thrown through up to the place it was retained and then the entry point's rethrow frames, instead of every evaluator frame in between. Re-entrant runs (a host operation that starts a nested run) have their own budget: the nested run's exception leaves it as an exception, and the outer run retains it only if the host operation lets it through.

Pinned by `AsyncEvaluation/AsyncHostTerminationTests`: the same exception object and host frames on every entry shape (synchronous operation, throw before the awaitable, faulted awaitable, throw after a genuine suspension) under four recursion shapes, cancellation at a chokepoint, after an await, while suspended and thrown by the host (own and foreign token), with the synchronous route as oracle for outcome and effect counts; a DETERMINISTIC unwind-work gate (first-chance raises of the run's own exception: constant, at most 10, at depth 15 / 60 / 120 and at 1,000 loop-step levels) and a whole-run allocation gate against the successful control of the same program on one thread (within 2× plus 256 KiB at depth 30 / 60 / 120, and at most doubling when the depth doubles); cancellation of a runaway loop-step recursion at level 2,000 through a synchronous and a suspending operation and while an operation is suspended; eighteen programs in which a consumer could absorb the failure (clause dispatch, repeated names, dot calls, callbacks, loops, selection, the property cache, an exported member, a captured call, a spread formed with the supply) for a host failure and for a cancellation; nested runs whose termination the outer host operation lets through or handles; depth conservation and the closed boundary after the run; and the stored state of cells, joiners and runtime projections inside and after the boundary. The existing identity, token, conservation and cell-state suites (`EvaluationCancellationTests`, `AsyncCancellationTests`, `Hosting/AsyncHostOperationTests`, `DeferredMaterializationCancellationTests`, `NeedCell*Tests`, `InvocationPlanTests`, `CallPlanRetentionReviewTests`, `RuntimeAliasCancellationRaceTests`) are unchanged.

## Resource limits are terminal for the run

RESOURCE LIMITS ARE TERMINAL FOR THE RUN (Q-02 / PV-06, September 2026; C#-only host policy with no Lean counterpart — Lean models no resource limits; it supplies the unlimited reference on its modeled fragment, while host effects, seeded randomness and full Decimal128 semantics use the unlimited C# meaning). A resource-limit failure — an `EvalError.IsResourceLimit` kind: dynamic depth, steps, the host-stack backstop, per-collection size, cumulative materialized items, per-string size, or cumulative string units (structural AST depth and display length are classified too, but the first is the pre-evaluation preflight and the second rendering state after a successful run, so neither arises inside an evaluation) — is a property of the RUN, not a latent value of one argument. Once any evaluation that actually occurs reaches a resource limit, the run terminates with that failure: nothing evaluates after it — no later argument, callee body, loop iteration, row, random draw, or host operation — and no forwarding, builtin arity verdict, earlier ordinary failure, or consumer choice can absorb, replace, or defer it. Since Model C (2026-10-02, `call-by-need.md` NEED-01..09) no site defers a demanded failure at all: supply formation evaluates only explicit spreads, cardinality precedes every ordinary demand, a supplied computation runs on its first VALUE demand (`NeedCell`), and a demanded failure — ordinary or limit — propagates at once. A demanded cell that reaches a limit COMPLETES TERMINALLY: `NeedCell` keeps that completion and the run budget retains the terminal error (`EvaluationBudget.RetainTerminal`, observed by `CheckContinuation` before any further semantic work, memo observation included), so it never restarts and is never a recoverable argument value or a clause non-match; an ordinary completed failure is its cell's final outcome (at most once, sequences-lists-and-calls.md § At-most-once argument value evaluation), while a failed PROPERTY evaluation is still never stored in the property cache (a separate memo domain, NEED-10). `Evaluator.IsDeferrableEvaluationFailure` survives only in the builtin value slot's report precedence (`SequenceBuiltinValueDemandError`): a limit outranks the zero-argument classification of the slot's callable channel. (Until Model C it was THE ONE decision of every site that could defer an ORDINARY failure it encountered — user-call argument assembly, `BuildCallArgumentInputs`, which retained an ordinary failure beside the slot's algorithm channel, and the builtin argument adapter, `BuildCallableCallItems`, which retained a value or surplus item's failure behind the arity verdict — and such ordinary failures stayed retained until demanded.) Laziness is unchanged: an argument the language never evaluates — an unselected `if` branch, a builtin callback or loop-step slot that is never invoked, an undemanded property or deconstruction — reaches no limit, and nothing is evaluated eagerly merely to discover one.

THE LIMIT LAW: a resource limit may stop a run; it never redefines the value of a run that succeeds. For fixed semantic inputs (program, version, seed, host-operation behaviour, supplied module source and entry point), a run that COMPLETES under a limit — with a value or with an ordinary failure — is exactly the run without that limit, so raising a limit may turn a resource-limit failure into that run, never one success into another and never an ordinary failure into a success. WHERE the host-stack backstop fires is not part of the law: it is machine-, build-, runtime/JIT-, route-, and suspension-dependent (async twin frames are larger, and a suspended twin resumes on another stack), so different routes and hosts can disagree on WHETHER a run stops (Q-09a) — but configurations cannot (they select no strategy, Q-09b; next section), and nothing can disagree on the value of a run that completes. Every semantic limit (depth, steps, items, strings) fires at a deterministic point that is the same on every evaluation strategy.

BEFORE (PV-06): an algorithm-channel argument slot whose evaluation reached a limit retained the failure beside its binding and surfaced it only on demand, so a callee that ignored the parameter let the run SUCCEED after partial evaluation, with random draws and host effects shifted by where the limit struck. `Deep(n) = if(n == 0, 0, 1 + Deep(n - 1))` / `G(x, y) = y` / `G({Deep(100) + randomInt(0, 1000)}, randomInt(0, 1000))` with seed 3 (whose stream begins 209, 711) returned 209, the first draw, although its unlimited meaning under that eager model was 711; with `Deep(20)`, `MaxDepth` 10..40 returned 209 and 50..128 returned 711; the self-referential `A = G({A}, tick())` returned a different value on the async twin than on the synchronous routes and per thread stack size; repeated names (`Q(x, x) = x` / `Q(Deep, 7)`), collecting-only callables, parameters captured by callbacks, and loop steps calling an ignoring function absorbed the limit the same way; and the builtin adapter kept a limit on its item behind the arity verdict (`count({Deep}, 2)` was `ArityMismatch`) while later slots ran. AFTER: a route that encounters a limit fails there, after exactly the effects performed before it; a route that completes returns the unlimited value. `Deep(20)` with sufficient depth returned 711 until Model C; since Model C the ignored argument is never evaluated, so the canonical program returns its unlimited Model-C meaning — 209, the first draw, made by the demanded `y` — at every depth limit. Historical stack thresholds do not predict which current routes have sufficient headroom.

Pinned by `ResourceLimitTerminalityTests` (the canonical reproducer and the explicit depth sweep, nothing-after-the-limit effect logs, every binding shape and consumer, builtin and user-defined agreement, fused-pipeline parity, every practically reachable limit kind under an explicit budget, the host-stack backstop on controlled thread stacks, ordinary-failure and laziness controls, the Q-01 interaction, and the limit law as a metamorphic relation over single-limit sweeps on the synchronous engine; separate route-matrix cases cover the synchronous and asynchronous engines, the genuinely suspending async twin, and the generic, optimized, and forced-twin evaluators; only for the recursive depth/step probes on the two twin routes, the every-limit-kind matrix accepts the host-stack backstop in place of the depth/step verdict when it performed exactly the oracle's effects; shallow collection/string probes and all non-twin routes retain exact failure equality, because the twin's larger frames can exhaust a resumed thread-pool stack before a deep recursion reaches the budget, and where a limit fires is not promised), `BudgetConservationTests.ResourceLimitFailures_AreNeverDeferrable_OrdinaryFailuresAre`, and the re-pinned `EvaluationLimitsTests`, `BudgetConservationTests`, `EvaluationCancellationTests`, `ArgumentValueOutcomeTests`, and `AsyncSuspensionTests` cases that had characterized the absorbed success. Source admission has the separate required-unit law in § Source loading and deferred modules: a reached refusal stops the operation/run, while unused deferred computations perform no source work. Admission diagnostics remain outside `IsResourceLimit`; that classification does not imply a demanded error can be swallowed.

## Limits observe a run; host-stack headroom is not semantic

LIMITS OBSERVE A RUN; THEY NEVER CHOOSE HOW IT RUNS (Q-09b) and HOST-STACK HEADROOM IS NOT SEMANTIC (Q-09a) — the owner's decision on Q-09, October 4 2026; C#-only host policy with no Lean counterpart (Lean models no limits, strategies, host stack, async, or JIT; it remains the unbounded reference meaning). It fixed PV-07 and resolved X-34.

THE RULES. (Q-09b) Configuring, raising, or removing an evaluation limit that a run does not reach changes nothing about that run — not its value, effects, random draws, budget accounting, the evaluation strategy that runs, or whether it completes — and an explicitly written default (`MaxSteps = long.MaxValue`, `MaxStringLength = EvaluationLimits.MaxSupportedStringLength`, an empty `EvaluationLimits`) is the same as no configuration. A limit that IS reached gives the same verdict, failure point, error tree and effects before it on every strategy. (Q-09a) Whether the host-stack backstop (`EvalError.EvaluationStackExhausted`) stops a run may depend on the route (synchronous evaluator or async twin), genuine suspension, the thread and its stack size, the build, the runtime and its JIT state (the same run on the same thread can stop early in a fresh process and complete once its code is recompiled), and the platform — never merely on which limits are configured. Only the error kind is promised, never a firing depth; there is no common route cutoff, and `MaxDepth` stays a deterministic SEMANTIC bound that does not predict host-stack capacity. "Same run" for Q-09b holds the program, version, entry point, seed, host-operation behaviour, module inputs, thread and stack size, build, runtime and (as far as practical) JIT state fixed, and varies ONLY the limits; it does not promise equal headroom across routes, builds, JIT states or platforms.

THE ASYNC TWIN NO LONGER SHALLOWS ON INVOCATION RECURSION (stack hop, 2026-11-14; C#-only host-execution policy, no Lean change). Synchronously completing `ValueTask` state machines keep every twin frame on the host stack, so a twin level used about 2.5 times the synchronous stack: at `a4b545e8`, on a 1 MiB harness thread, `F(n) = if(n == 0, 0, F(n - 1) + 1)` stopped with `EvaluationStackExhausted` from `F(42)` on the twin route — taken whenever ANY asynchronous host operation is configured, even an unused one — where the synchronous routes completed up to `F(101)`. Now every twin site that shares a synchronous stack probe first tests `StackHop.HostStackIsLow()` — the probe's `TryEnsureSufficientExecutionStack` condition, measured `StackHop.ChokepointMargin` (1 KiB) below the caller so that it covers the two small helper frames in which an invocation chokepoint runs its probe (an exact test left a window of a few hundred bytes where the check passed and the chokepoint's probe failed: a full-suite run reported `EvaluationStackExhausted` at `wait(F(n - 1))` on a 1 MiB thread); no side effect, no charge, no limit or configuration read — and, when it holds, awaits a `StackHop`, which queues the continuation to the thread pool — never to a captured `SynchronizationContext` or `TaskScheduler` — and resumes on a fresh stack. The sites: before the twin enters `EvaluationBudget.TryEnterInvocation` / `TryEnterArgumentEvaluation` (user, conditional, callback, planned, origin-family and origin-user calls; zero-argument property reads; resolved value demands and builtin argument levels; the `.string` lexical, alias and structural receivers), at the heads of the two twin row-loop funnels, before a `NeedCell`'s asynchronous VALUE or runtime CALLABLE computation, and (since November 2026) before a loop that will run a round passes its entry probe (`EvaluationBudget.TryAdmitLoopEntry` in `Evaluator.EvalNeedLoop`, one body with an `asynchronous` flag that guards the hop; § Budgets and cancellation, THE LOOP ENTRY IS THAT BACKSTOP'S SITE); each is marked TWIN-ONLY beside its `// MIRROR OF` counterpart, and the synchronous family is untouched. The probe stays after the hop, which happens at most once per site visit, so a fresh stack that is still low ends structurally instead of hopping forever. The hop is invisible to the language: value, effects, evaluation order, random draws, every budget counter and the cancellation chokepoints are those of the run without the hop; a run that hops would otherwise have stopped at that probe (or, at an invocation chokepoint, with the limit verdict the chokepoint still reports first) unless it came within the margin of the reserve without reaching it, in which case it now only completes asynchronously; twin invocation recursion is then bounded by the deterministic `MaxDepth` (`F(200)` → `EvaluationDepthExceeded` at 128, `F(120)` → 120 on the same thread), as for a twin that genuinely suspends before descending; recursion routed through loop steps charges no depth, so no deterministic limit bounds its nesting on this route (the open owner decision recorded in § Budgets and cancellation). Synchronous helpers the twins call — alias chases, receiver and `open` navigation, sync-delegated leaves — keep their own backstops, so a twin run can still stop with `EvaluationStackExhausted`, including shallower than the synchronous evaluator; Q-09a is unchanged (both directions remain permitted, no depth is promised). A run that hops completes asynchronously even when nothing else suspends — the one exception to the async entry points' synchronous-completion note: blocking on it from a single-threaded `SynchronizationContext` is safe where the runtime has a thread pool (evaluator awaits capture only a fold's private scheduling context), and blocking on browser WebAssembly stays unsupported, as for genuinely asynchronous operations. No hop site is reached while thread-affine state is held: a `lock` cannot span an await; the one `[ThreadStatic]` scope, the non-initiating `NeedCell` observation, is synchronous and refuses asynchronous starts (`Evaluator.ResolveObservedAsync` exits it before awaiting); plan-preparation markers (`Interlocked` on `InvocationPlan.Supply`, waiters through a `TaskCompletionSource`), need-cell flights (`TaskCompletionSource`) and the loader's materialization gate (`SemaphoreSlim`, held only around a region's download, parse and compile, never around the twins that evaluate it) are not thread-affine; and the flow-local state — the need-cell wait-for graph (`NeedCell.Current`), `PreparingInvocations`, the deferred region's held gate turn — is `AsyncLocal` and resumes with the awaiting method's `ExecutionContext`. SUPPRESSED FLOW (2026-11-23, finding `async-route-suppressed-flow-cycle-self-join-hang` of the 2026-11-21 adversarial performance audit; C#-only host-execution policy): where the host has suppressed `ExecutionContext` flow there is no context to resume with, so a hop hands the wait-for node and the preparation frames to its continuation itself — never the held gate turn, which `RunOptions.DownloadCode` defines by the execution context, nor any of the host's ambient state — and suppressed flow is a SECOND condition under which a twin hops, at three sites only: a need cell's asynchronous VALUE or runtime CALLABLE computation and an invocation-initiated plan preparation, whose flow-local state must be installed where the context flows ([`call-by-need.md` § Production implementation map], THE DEMANDING NODE UNDER SUPPRESSED EXECUTION-CONTEXT FLOW). The computation or preparation that takes the hop runs on the pool in a context that flows. A host operation reached there can observe a different thread, culture or tracing context even when its own awaitable completes synchronously. The hop itself consumes no evaluation budget (`SuppressedFlowRunTests`, `NeedCellSuppressedFlowTests`). Pinned by `AsyncStackDepthTests` (1 MiB thread, an asynchronous operation configured but unused, awaited: `F(120)`, `F(200)`, the waiting shapes, the heaviest shapes' depth verdict, and a start-offset sweep that lands every chokepoint in the former check/probe window), `StackHopTests` (the awaiter's pool resumption and context flow, both need-cell sites on a genuinely low stack, a self-demand across a hop is still `DemandCycle`, awaiting and blocking on a deep run from a single-threaded context), `EvaluationCancellationTests.CancellationDuringAHopHeavyTwinRecursion_EscapesWithTheSuppliedToken`, the twin mirror pin in `StructuralNestingStackBackstopTests`, and `AsyncTwinDifferentialTests` (outcomes and counters unchanged).

A RUN HANDS OUT ONLY CONCRETE VALUES (lazy collections, issue #204, November 2026). A list evaluated on demand exists only inside its run: the run's assembled output is materialized — every position of every list read left to right, depth first — WHILE THE RUN IS ACTIVE (the asynchronous twin does it inside its host-termination boundary, awaiting), before the run-result boundary checks it; a named top-level property is materialized as it is read, after the output; a host-operation argument is materialized where the operation is called, in argument order. A failure, a resource limit or a cancellation reached while a position is read there is the run's outcome. A passive property observation reads nothing: it shows a concrete form of what the run has read of a list (its whole form, a one-level form, or — when the run settled its cardinality and every position's VALUE completed successfully — a new list of those memoized values, however the positions were read; the lists inside resolve the same way) and is `Withheld` when the run read some list too little, or when those forms and positions lead back to a list the snapshot is already inside (`RootPropertyObservationSink.Complete`, `Evaluator.TrySnapshotValue`). C#-only host policy: a list of known cardinality reserves its positions when it is built, a scan when it completes, a discovered stage when its first concrete form is built; whole-value reads keep explicit frames (no native recursion per nesting level) and observe cancellation; a run that has created no lazy list skips every whole-value scan (`EvaluationBudget.HasLazyLists`); a result that keeps building lists while it is read whole ends at the recursion limit (`EvaluationBudget.TryEnterNestedRead`: `F(x) = [x].map(F)` is `EvaluationDepthExceeded` when read, as it was as an eager recursion), and stages stacked at run time are read without one native call chain per `map` stage ([`sequences-lists-and-calls.md` § Lazy collections and pipelines]).

A `reduce` FOLD'S SUSPENDED STEPS ARE FOLD CONTINUATIONS (lazy `reduce` accumulators, issue #203, November 2026; C#-only host-execution policy, no Lean counterpart — Lean's fold is a chain of deferred applications and models no depth, stack or strategy). A `reduce` step that demands its accumulator is suspended until the preceding step has run, and that step may demand its own accumulator in turn: a fold whose reducer uses every accumulator holds one suspended step per element ([`sequences-lists-and-calls.md` § Lazy `reduce` accumulators]). DEPTH. The suspended steps are accounted apart from call nesting: the accumulator's computation enters `EvaluationBudget.EnterFoldContinuation(foldDepth)`, which sets the dynamic depth back to the depth the fold began at and counts the continuation (`ActiveFoldContinuations`, `PeakFoldContinuations`: observations, no limit reads them), and `ExitFoldContinuation` restores the suspended depth from a `finally`. So every step of a fold runs one invocation level below the fold, a fold over any admitted collection stays within `MaxDepth` exactly when one step does (`PeakDepth` does not grow with the collection), and genuine recursion inside a reducer — a reducer that calls itself, a fold started inside a reducer — still counts from that level and ends with the same `EvaluationDepthExceeded`. The number of continuations suspended at once is bounded by the collection (`MaxCollectionItems`) and by nothing else: there is no limit on fold continuations, as there is none on loop-step nesting (§ Budgets and cancellation); adding one is an owner decision. CHARGES. An executed step charges one invocation (`TryEnterInvocation`) and its own work at the existing chokepoints, a skipped step charges nothing, and cancellation is observed at those chokepoints: a cancelled run starts no further step. STACK. A fold keeps its suspended steps on the heap and runs on the thread that demands it (single-thread fold progress, November 2026; `Evaluation/FoldTrampoline.cs`). The computation of one accumulator is a SEGMENT of its fold's TRAMPOLINE (`FoldTrampoline`, `FoldSegment`). On the asynchronous twins every accumulator demand queues its segment there and returns a pending task, so the demanding step suspends at its demand site as heap state machines. The trampoline's DRAIN LOOP starts each queued segment from its own frame, and a completed segment's outcome reaches the step that demanded it by resuming that step from the drain loop, never inside the completing segment: the native stack holds the drain loop and one step's frames, at descent and at completion alike, however long the fold is, and since exactly one chain can run at any time the order is the nested order. Whoever queues work while no drain loop of the trampoline runs becomes that loop, on its own thread, and drains until nothing is queued: the fold's first accumulator demand, or a step that resumes after a genuine suspension (an asynchronous host operation, an awaited module download) wherever its awaitable completes. The synchronous evaluator keeps a short chain on the host stack — a plain synchronous step and the accumulator demand's context boundary per suspended step, at most `FoldTrampoline.HostStackAllowance` (16) fold continuations per thread and no more than `HostStackByteAllowance` (128 KiB) of stack below the outermost of them, measured by stack addresses where a platform's addresses decrease with depth (the count bounds the chain where they do not) — so a short fold runs exactly as before. Beyond that an accumulator demand CONTINUES ITS FOLD ON THE HEAP: it runs the accumulator's segment on the asynchronous twins through the same trampoline and PUMPS on the calling thread until that segment has completed (`FoldTrampoline.TryRunSynchronously`). While a pump runs: the run carries a host exception or a cancellation by value (the twin run's boundary, `EvaluationBudget.CarriesHostTermination`, opened and closed by the pump, which rethrows the retained exception on the calling thread as the same object with its dispatch information); no twin hops to the thread pool — `StackHop.HostStackIsLow` answers false on a pumping thread, so the probe after a hop site decides as on the synchronous family (genuine recursion inside a reducer still ends with the structured `EvaluationStackExhausted` or the depth limit), and a hop that suppressed flow asks for is resumed by the pump itself, with the same hand-over (`FoldTrampoline.TryPostHop`); execution-context flow is not suppressed (a host that suppressed it gets it back suppressed), so every heap continuation carries the need-cell node, the invocation preparation frames and the host's ambient state in its execution context, each segment starting under the demander's and each step resuming under its own; and evaluator awaits capture the private synchronization context of the pump. Deferred continuations return to its queue, including under a custom host task scheduler or at low stack. The twins read the run's property cache through an asynchronous member: a synchronous run's cache offers them its TWIN VIEW over the same store, publication law and statistics (`IZeroArgPropertyResultCache.TwinView`, `Evaluator.EvalCtx.PropertyAccessCache`), and a run whose cache offers none (a host-built cache) keeps every fold on the host stack. They run the generic loop and sequence strategies they mirror, which charge every budget as the optimized ones do (Q-09b; `ThrowIfAsyncStrategyPinningViolated` asserts the pinning for asynchronous runs only). A segment is exactly the computation the demanding frame would have run in place, started at the demand and finished before the demanding step resumes: value, evaluation order, effects, random draws, every budget counter, limit verdicts, the cancellation chokepoints and `DemandCycle` are those of the nested evaluation, nothing reads a limit, a value or configuration (Q-09b), and how much of a chain stays on the host stack is headroom, not semantics (Q-09a). A synchronous pump drains ready work to completion. An empty queue with an incomplete target is an invariant violation (`FoldTrampoline.StalledPumps`). Until 2026-11-24 the synchronous evaluator instead moved a long chain to new 64 MiB threads while the demanding thread waited, and where no thread could start (a browser worker) it evaluated in place until `EvaluationStackExhausted`: the issue's programs failed in a browser from 500 elements with 0.8.277, and from 200 after a warm-up or a large streamed fold. HOSTS. On both routes the host operations a long fold reaches run one call at a time and in the order of the nested evaluation; on the synchronous route they run on the calling thread (`FoldTrampolineTests.IssuePrograms_CompleteOnTheCallingOneMebibyteThread`, `LazyReduceAccumulatorTests.LongFoldCallbacks_RunInOrder_OnTheCallingThread`), and on the asynchronous route a step resumes where its host's awaitable completes. Synchronous callbacks inherit the host context at the demand, including its synchronization context and flow suppression. After an accumulator read, callbacks resume with their own ambient values. A shared method whose synchronous execution already has a method-builder context boundary forks that host state on entry (`FoldTrampoline.ForkHostScope`), including a nested fold, list read or inspecting binder. Plain user calls retain the enclosing state. The thread is this implementation's, not the language's. COST. A general-route fold that uses every accumulator costs time and memory proportional to its length where the former loop held one step. Until 2026-11-24 the synchronous route cost more: GC pauses grew with the depth of the suspended native stacks. Profiling implicated stack scanning but did not measure it separately. Measured on one development machine (Windows x64, Release build, the concurrent collector on, `Id(x) = x` / `Add(item, acc) = Id(item) + acc` over `range(1, n)`): when the fold landed, 10,000 steps took about 0.9 s and 175 MiB synchronously and 0.6 s and 95 MiB asynchronously, and 100,000 steps, the collection ceiling, 41.7 s and about 1,217 MiB over 16 helper threads synchronously (about 7 s where the host process disables the concurrent collector) and 2.6 s and about 518 MiB asynchronously. At `136c9efe` 100,000 steps took 20.1 s and 1,166 MiB over 15 helper threads synchronously, 19.2 s of GC pauses across 20 gen-0 collections; separate forced-collection probes at depth measured roughly 13 µs per suspended step per collection, and a suspended synchronous step kept about 9.7 KiB of host stack in 17 frames of optimized code. The November 24 frame repair (plain synchronous methods for the step and callback dispatch, one `AccumulatorComputation` per cell, the accumulator demand's context boundary in `ReduceFold.Evaluate`) cut a suspended step to about 7.1 KiB in 13 optimized frames and the 100,000-step fold to 16.34 s, still with 15.48 s of GC pauses. With the private scheduling context and heap continuations (`76df3dc4` plus the reviewed change, Windows x64, .NET 11 Release, `KatLangEngine.Run`, three warm-up runs of 1,000 steps, median of three runs), the four general reducers took 0.25–0.43 s at 10,000 steps and 1.83–2.79 s at 100,000. At 100,000, the conditional sum took 2.79 s, `Math.Abs(item) + acc` took 1.94 s, the structural accumulator took 1.83 s and the conditional maximum took 2.62 s. On the same machine the unmodified checkout took 17.3–29.1 s, and the published eager 0.8.276 package took 0.15–0.23 s. The reviewed runs allocated 6.8–9.9 KiB per element for the complete workload and peaked at 0.73–1.49 GiB of working set. Suspended state machines and their continuations remain live until each step resumes. The three streamed controls took 0.16–0.31 s at 100,000. A fold that skips its earlier steps (`Pick(item, acc) = item`) runs one step whatever its length. THE STREAMED ROUTE removes that cost for the usual reducer shape (C#-only implementation optimization, no Lean counterpart). A reducer that LEADS WITH ITS ACCUMULATOR (`acc + x`, `acc * 10 + item`, `acc < item`; `Evaluator.ReduceFold.LeadsWithItsAccumulator`, decided from the reducer's own structure — never from values, limits, configuration or the route) demands every accumulator of its fold before anything else its step does, so the nested evaluation is fully determined: every step entered, last step first, with no effect but its invocation charge; the start evaluated; every step completed, first step first. `ReduceFold.Stream` performs that sequence with no step suspended — the same value, effects, ordinary failures and total charges; only the moment at which a step's expression work before its accumulator read is charged differs (when its body runs, not on the way down), which a step limit that runs out inside the fold can observe ([`sequences-lists-and-calls.md` § Lazy `reduce` accumulators], THE STREAMED ROUTE) — so such a fold has no fold continuation and no heap segment and costs what the former loop cost (100,000 steps: about 0.7 s and 58 MiB synchronously, 0.8 s asynchronously). The internal `ReduceFoldTestSeam` withholds the streamed route so that tests can compare the two; production never sets it. Pinned by `LazyReduceAccumulatorTests` (`AccumulatingFold_HoldsOneContinuationPerElement_AtTheDepthOfOneStep`, `SkippingFold_ChargesOnlyTheStepsItRuns`, `GenuineRecursionInsideAReducer_StillReachesTheDepthLimit`, `StepLimit_CountsExecutedStepsOnly`, the two cancellation tests, `LongAccumulatingFold_OnAOneMebibyteThread_CompletesOnTheCallingThread`, `HostExceptionOnTheHeap_ReachesTheCallerAsTheSameObject`, `SuspendedSteps_KeepOnlyTheAccumulatorContextBoundary_OnTheSynchronousHostStack`, `SynchronousCallbacks_ResumeWithTheirOwnHostContext`, `LongFoldCallbacks_RunInOrder_OnTheCallingThread`, `LongFoldWithGenuinelySuspendingCallbacks_KeepsTheirOrder`, `LongSkippingFold_RunsOnlyTheStepsItDemands_OnTheCallingThread`, `AccumulatingFoldWork_IsTheSameForEveryStep_WhereverItsChainContinues`, `GeneralFoldSteps_AllocateLittleMoreThanStreamedSteps`) `StreamedReduceFoldEquivalenceTests` (the streamed route against the general one on six routes — values, error positions, host logs, steps and raw checkpoints, every step limit — the route classification, and `LongAccumulatorLedFold_StaysOnTheCallingThread_AtTheDepthOfOneStep`), `FoldTrampolineTests` (the issue's programs and an effectful fold at 10,000 elements on one 1 MiB thread through `KatLangEngine.Run` and the Playground's `RunAsync` call shape, every host call on the calling thread in the nested order, at least one pump and no wait; a genuinely suspending entry; a host synchronization context; a native stack that does not grow with the fold, with `PeakHostStackFoldContinuations` within `HostStackAllowance`; genuine recursion inside a heap step; long folds against the explicitly nested calls on six routes; step limits inside a long fold), `ReduceFoldStackProcessTests` (in the Release stack-suite job: folds of 100,000 steps on one 1 MiB thread in a child process), and `SuppressedFlowRunTests` / `NeedCellSuppressedFlowTests` (a cycle and a long fold in pumped segments under both flows).

THE MECHANISM. The strategy is chosen from the program, the requested optimizations, and the route only: `Evaluator.CreateRootCtx` passes the loop-planning and sequence-fusion flags through unchanged, and `EvaluationBudget` holds only EFFECTIVE thresholds — there is no "was this limit configured" fact (the former `HasStepLimit` / `HasConfiguredStringLimit` / `HasConfiguredMaterializationLimit` are deleted; never reintroduce one). The route is chosen by capability — an async-capable cache, an asynchronous host operation (configured even if unused), a deferred module region — and the twin always runs the generic strategies. Every strategy charges every budget at the SAME LOGICAL points, so no budget needs a strategy of its own:

- Steps: a planned loop charges ONE step per iteration where the generic loop charges it (`LoopOptimizer.TryEvaluateWhile` / `TryEvaluateRepeat`; this also bounds a runaway planned loop under a finite `MaxSteps`). The bulk expression-work unit — one step per `EvaluationBudget.ExpressionCheckpointsPerStep` (4096) checkpoints, a checkpoint being each `Eval`/`EvalCounted` dispatch head and each expression-spine machine transition of the GENERIC evaluation — is strategy-neutral: a planned expression records exactly the checkpoints the generic evaluation of its source expression records, in the same order relative to every other charge and effect (`LoopOptimizer.EvalLoopExprPlan` with its `LoopPlanDispatch`: HEAD for a step row, a temp body row, or an operand the spine machine delegates; SPINE for an operand the machine pushes as a frame, decided by the same `Evaluator.IsExpressionSpineNode`; ARGUMENT for a written `if` argument, whose supply cell enters one head unless it transports a parameter's already bound cell; plus one transition before each operand request and before each apply, as the machine does). Each checkpoint is recorded outside the planned node's diagnostic boundary, where the generic head records it, so a step limit there is the generic error at the generic point. A forwarding temp call is planned only when the generic call would transport every argument's existing binding (`LoopOptimizer.TransportsForwardedArguments`). The fused filter→count pipeline records the dispatch heads of the stage expressions it elides — the `filter` stage, and a direct `range` source inside the stage's boundary — at the commit point.
- Materialized items: the fused pipeline RESERVES, without allocating, the item slots the generic composition materializes, at its points and with its limit-error spans — the range source's slots after its bounds (`EvaluateRangeCallArgumentsForSequenceOptimizer`) and the filtered list's slots after the last predicate call (the empty list included). `EvaluationBudget.TryReserveCollection` is the one reservation primitive (the former reserve-free `CheckCollectionSize` is deleted).
- Strings: fused and generic paths create the same strings at the same points; no accounting change was needed, only the removal of the strategy switch.
- Structural value work (SEC-02, October 2026): every `contains`/`distinct` evaluation — on every route, since all of them reach the one builtin implementation — runs its comparisons and hashes through ONE operation comparer (`Result.CreateOperationComparer`) whose reference-identity memo spans the operation and keeps the completed equality knowledge (equivalence classes of equal references and exact unequal pairs, § Budgets and cancellation) and hashes of every pair or node that descends into a nested structure or is at least 16 wide (`ValueSemanticComparer.RetainedWidth`; `Result.ValueComparer`'s walks stay per call and uncharged), and charges one unit per child slot inspected and per expanded structure completed (equal, unequal, or hashed), so a deep singleton chain stays cancellable while its frames unwind to `EvaluationBudget.TryChargeStructuralWork`: flushed every 256 units (each flush observes host cancellation first) and at completion, ONE step per 4096 units from its own batch, never the expression-work batch. Top-level leaves and memo answers charge nothing beyond the inspection that found them, so flat collections are unaffected; a refused charge is the ordinary terminal step-limit error, and a walk interrupted by cancellation or refusal retires its comparer. Units depend only on the operands' reference graphs, so equal-sharing operands charge equally on every strategy. Pinned by the SEC-02 section of `SharedValueGraphComplexityTests`. SEC-02-FU extends the same comparer and the same units to `==`/`!=` and repeated-name agreement, and charges string payload (64 code units per unit) in every operation walk, top-level string items included (§ Budgets and cancellation, EVALUATOR-OWNED STRUCTURAL COMPARISONS ARE CHARGED); a short string still charges nothing beyond its inspection.
- Depth: already equalized (the shared `Evaluator.BudgetScopes.cs` helpers; Model-C supply cells add no level).

Before this decision a configured `MaxSteps` (any value) disabled loop planning and sequence fusion, and a configured `MaxMaterializedItems`, `MaxStringLength` (even at its own default) or `MaxMaterializedStringChars` disabled fusion, so on one public entry point, thread and program a non-binding limit could turn success into `EvaluationStackExhausted` (`R(n) = if(n > 0, range(n, n).filter({ R(x - 1) >= 0 }).count, 0)` / `R(35)`, Release, 1.5 MiB) — or, for recursion routed through a loop step, failure into success — and loop-heavy runs under `MaxSteps` were 3–20× slower with up to ~400× more allocation. Removing the switch alone would have made verdicts strategy-dependent and let a runaway planned loop run forever under `MaxSteps`; the parallel accounting above is what makes the removal sound.

Pinned by `StrategyNeutralBudgetParityTests` (generic-vs-optimized equality of steps, RAW checkpoints, depth, items, strings, error trees and host traces over a corpus covering every planned-node kind in every dispatch context; every-step-budget, materialization and string sweeps; long loops walking the first three 4096-checkpoint block boundaries; runaway planned loops), `LimitConfigurationTransparencyTests` (every non-binding configuration on the same route and on the engine, strategy markers included; explicit defaults; limit independence; a representative embedding configuration; Model-C non-demand; cancellation; tight budgets across the synchronous strategies and the forced twin; a genuinely suspending run), `CorpusExecutionEquivalenceTests.Optimized_ChargesExactlyTheGenericBudgets` and `NonBindingLimits_ChangeNothing` over both corpora, the exact-accounting `OptimizerEquivalenceSweepTests`, and `EvaluationLimitsProcessTests.NonBindingLimits_KeepTheHostStackOutcome_InSubprocess` (with tiered compilation off, the PV-07 witnesses at a probe-FOUND minimal stack headroom on ONE probe thread: every non-binding configuration completes where the unconfigured run completes and stops where it stops — no hard-coded size or depth; threads that merely request the same stack size are not the same host, since glibc can reuse a larger cached stack). The shipped XML documentation states both rules and none of the retired claims (a limit choosing the strategy, a one-directional twin headroom claim, a depth ceiling tuned to the host stack): `LimitDocumentationContractTests`, with the matching stale shapes of `MaintainedDocumentationConsistencyTests` over the current-rule documents and source comments. Route variability stays permitted by the async-route tests (`ResourceLimitTerminalityTests`' twin backstop acceptance, `AsyncStackDepthTests`).

## Call references at the host boundary (CallReference S1, 2026-10-17)

A call reference (`Result.CallReference`, Lean `Result.callRef`) is run-scoped: every public evaluator and engine result (`Run*`, `RunFlat*`, the top-level property of an engine run, sync and async), every host-operation argument (sync and async) and every display or formatting path refuses a value that contains one at any depth (`Evaluator.ExportRunValue` / `ContainsCallReference`, an iterative DAG-safe scan that never follows a plan; for a host-operation argument it repeats per call and is charged as structural work, `HostArgumentBoundaryError`, SEC-01-FU). The RUN-RESULT EXPORT (`ExportRunValue` → `RunExportBoundaryError`, SEC-01-FU2; output and named top-level property alike) is SKIPPED in a run that does not retain invocation plans (`EvaluationBudget.RetainsInvocationPlans` false): a reference is built only from a plan, plans exist only in retaining runs, and no reference enters a run from outside (every boundary here refuses one, a returned one is a host contract violation, the property cache is run-keyed), so such a run holds none — an implementation fast path, not a semantic change (Lean `exportRunValue` is unchanged). Every retaining run — META-capable programs, deferred module regions, test seams — scans the full value. The export scan runs once, after evaluation, over a value the run already paid for (memo-bounded by distinct nodes; since SEC-01-FU3 a nested structure of at most `Evaluator.ShallowProbeMaxItems` = 8 slots is probed in place instead of memoized — a leaf-only one is re-inspected at each reference, at most 8 slots per edge, and the probe stops at the first structural item and the containing structure is memoized as before; that stopping slot counts both in the probe and on frame resumption — an allocation refinement with no Lean change), so it charges NO step or structural work — a successful run never fails because its result was inspected — and observes cancellation once at least 256 slots were inspected, at segment boundaries and after every shallow probe, including a fallback to an already memoized structure (a segment adds at most MaxCollectionItems slots plus one probe to fewer than 256 previously unchecked slots) (throwing the run token's `OperationCanceledException`, the completion-boundary outcome, only sooner). `CallReferenceRunExportTests`. The refusal is `TypeMismatch` "A call reference cannot leave its run". A RUN-RESULT refusal carries the span of the written construct that exports the reference, decided after the refusal from the exported tree, the refused value and the offending root item retained by the one DAG-safe export scan (`Evaluator.RunExportSite`, 2026-11-10; no evaluation or value scan is repeated): a single output row's span; with several rows, none a spread, whose value has one item per row (Q-26), the first row whose item holds a reference; otherwise (a spread row among several, or an item count that differs from the row count) the hull of the output rows when every row is located; the root's `Property.FirstDeclarationSpan` for the named top-level property (checked after the output, so an escaping output wins); a non-program root's own span. A missing row or declaration location falls back to the exported root expression's existing span — in particular, a directly evaluated locationless import view retains its local import site on that expression. When neither location is carried, the refusal stays unpositioned (host-built trees or detached import views; never an invented location). The positioned form is the ordinary `EvalError.Span` of the unchanged error, sync and async alike; Lean errors carry no span, so Lean parity (by category) is untouched (`CallReferenceRunExportSpanTests`). Host-argument and formatting refusals are outside this rule and unchanged. A host operation is never invoked with one, a host can neither construct nor receive one, and a host that returned one would violate its contract (fail-loud, like a null return). The public `Result.CallReference` variant exists only to keep the closed hierarchy exhaustive (a host switch must name it; it never arrives). Internal language operations transport references freely. Run identity and terminality: `call-by-need.md` § Call references (CR-6); the J7 retention decision is run state on `EvaluationBudget` (CR-7). Since S2 the same variant also carries a reference's ARGUMENT VIEW (`R.Arguments`, an internal facet `IsArgumentView`; Lean `Result.argView`), which every boundary above refuses identically — no new public type, no host-visible handle (`call-by-need.md` § Argument views, CR-12).

## Async evaluation and host operations

The ASYNC evaluation surface (`Evaluator.RunAsync`/`RunFlatAsync`, `KatLangEngine.RunAsync`) is a host-execution surface with NO Lean counterpart, built as a "sync-delegating async twin spine" (`Evaluator.Async.cs`): the synchronous evaluator remains the semantic oracle; engine `RunAsync` first awaits the async front end (`FrontEndPipeline.ProcessAsync` — module downloads may suspend source processing, see the source-loading bullet above), then an async entry point routes ONCE per run, by capability (`RequiresAsyncEvaluationPath`) — a run whose zero-arg property cache is not async-capable, whose host-operation set has no asynchronous operation, and whose tree carries no deferred module region executes the synchronous pipeline inline and completes synchronously (never `Task.Run`, never artificial yielding), while an async-capable cache, an asynchronous host operation (configured even if unused), or a deferred module region (materializing a selected branch can await the module downloader — § Source loading and deferred modules; `DeferredModuleRegion.RequiresAsyncEvaluation`) routes through the internal `async ValueTask` TWIN family. The route decision walks the tree at most ONCE per run (SEC-F5 follow-up, 2026-11-09; C#-only): the public entries, engine `RunAsync` and the observed harness twin decide the deferred-region fact once (`Evaluator.DecideTreeRequiresAsyncEvaluation`; no walk at all when an asynchronous operation is configured) and hand it to the cache-pairing factory, the route rule and the fast path's synchronous guard, and an internal funnel given no fact lets the route rule decide it once and reuses it — the fact lives only in the call's synchronous prefix and is never stored, so routing is still answered by the tree. Engine `RunAsync` (and, mirrored, `Run`) observes `RunOptions.EvaluationCancellationToken` right after a successful front end, before that walk and the run-cache construction — a front-end failure still wins over a cancelled evaluation token, and the cancellation reaches an async caller through the returned task (`AsyncRouteDecisionTests`). Every twin is marked `// MIRROR OF <sync name>` and MUST be updated in the same change as its synchronous counterpart — the twins re-implement only awaitable child-evaluation sequencing and share every non-evaluating helper. `TwinNormalizationTests` enforces the `MIRROR OF` rule mechanically, and the allowlist only shrinks: every resolved pair is either pinned as identical (`IdenticalPairs`) or matches an allowlist entry exactly (pair, classes, and the hash of the positioned normalized difference), every pinned pair must keep resolving, qualified marker references honor their containing type, blocking adapters are normalized only on the synchronous counterpart, and a marker naming no single method fails. The baseline of 72 entries, 43 of them with a class beyond the audit's (`CountedOnly`, `AsyncLanguage`, `FlagBody`, `DeferredRegion`, `EntryRoute`, `AwaitedJoin`, `AsyncHostOperation`, `InlinedHelper`, `SyncDelegated`), was accepted by owner direction on 2026-11-14 after an entry-by-entry review; that acceptance grants no new exception. The guard compares source, not semantics: a `bool asynchronous` body has no twin to compare (only its branch points are pinned), and a synchronous specialization whose asynchronous side carries no marker is not compared at all; the outcome corpora cover both. (The Model-C call drivers have no flag body: `EvalNeedUserBodyCounted` / `EvalNeedUserSupplyCounted` / `EvalNeedFamilyBodyCounted` / `EvalNeedFamilySupplyCounted` / `BindNeedFamilySupplyCounted` are the one synchronous implementation that ordinary calls, invocation plans, loop steps and callbacks reach, each paired with its marked twin.) Twin rules: counted-family mirrors only (plain wrappers are counted projections — the Lean-modelled plain/counted value equivalence); the twin root context pins the GENERIC loop/sequence strategies (fail-loud guard `ThrowIfAsyncStrategyPinningViolated`); the two row-loop funnels carry a `TryEnsureSufficientExecutionStack` backstop (first twin-only, because async frames are larger than the calibrated sync frames and structural nesting passes no invocation chokepoint; since K2-R1 on both families), and since 2026-11-14 each twin precedes it — like its invocation, argument-level and need-cell probes — with the TWIN-ONLY stack hop (§ Limits observe a run); cancellation is the Phase 1 contract via the shared budget chokepoints (OCE through the returned task, never an `EvalError`). Divergence is pinned by `AsyncEvaluation/AsyncTwinDifferentialTests`: the language-spec + explorer corpora run through the twin path with outcome AND operational-counter equality against the sync generic strategies, plus a genuine-suspension pass; keep that suite green when touching either family. `AsyncRouteCorpusDifferentialTests` also runs every program of the LanguageSpec, SemanticExplorer (internal-node trees included), SemanticGenerated (all members) and HostParentDifferential corpora through `KatLangEngine.RunAsync` or `Evaluator.RunAsync` with an asynchronous host operation, compared with the synchronous route; Q-09a stack-headroom exceptions require exact program ids and reasons. PUBLIC host operations generalize the Math-module mechanism, not a new subsystem: `HostOperation`/`HostOperations` (`RunOptions.HostOperations`, `Evaluator.Run`/`RunAsync` host-operation overloads) synthesize one ambient prelude property per operation — a parentless wrapper `Algorithm.User` whose body is `Expr.NativeCall("host:" + name, params)` (`':'` keeps host dispatch names disjoint from Math natives) — so resolution, shadowing, arity, and the `A` vs `A()` cache rule are the existing Lean-modelled rules unchanged, and the front end resolves the names through the configuration's extended signature-only semantic prelude (`ParameterDetector.DetectPrevalidated`; a referenced operation never becomes an implicit parameter). The configuration rides the run-scoped `EvaluationBudget`; sync operations dispatch in the shared `EvalNativeCall`, async operations await at ONE guarded `EvalCountedAsync` NativeCall case (invoked exactly once — suspension never replays), and every SUCCESSFUL host return value is normalized into the canonical `Result` representation — and, in the same walk, admitted against the per-collection and per-string limits (§ Budgets and cancellation, HOST-VALUE ADMISSION) — at the host boundary (`NormalizeHostOperationValue` — the existing `Result.Normalize` walk, shared by both dispatch paths, applied BEFORE the zero-arg property cache stores the wrapper outcome; failures/cancellation untouched, identity on already-canonical values). Routing is enforced: any async-operation configuration, and any tree carrying a deferred module region, selects the twin path with an async-capable cache (public entries construct one, internal entries fail loud), sync entry points reject both with `InvalidOperationException` before evaluating, and host exceptions/faulted awaitables propagate to the host unchanged (never `EvalError`). Native-call wrapper bodies (Math members AND host operations) read their declared argument names through `LookupNativeArgument`, which IS the `Expr.Param` value read (`EvalParamCounted` projected to its value: the parameter's supplied cell, then `valEnv`) — not a separate lookup rule. The supplied cell makes them work DIRECTLY as callback and loop-step references (`map(abs)`/`map(Enrich)`) and gives an argument its ordinary value-demand outcome (a parameterized callable's zero-argument arity error, a clause family's conditional value-access error, a zero-parameter algorithm's value or failure) instead of `Unknown name` for the wrapper's own parameter (the separate algorithm-channel and counted tiers, which no production binding site ever wrote, were retired on 2026-10-13). Because the cell's demand can enter an algorithm body, `Expr.NativeCall` is a RECURSIVE variant with its own `EvalNativeCallAsync` twin (the shared pure `ApplyMathNative` keeps the member set from drifting, and host-built native signatures must match authoritative registry metadata exactly, after argument binding, with malformed counts returning structured errors), never a sync-delegable leaf. A same-named ambient value can never be captured or forwarded to host code in place of the bound argument: every binding path shadows the callee's parameter names out of every inherited tier (`ShadowInheritedParameterEnvironments` / Lean `EvalCtx.bindParameters` for the need-cell tier, `ShadowValEnv` / Lean `ValEnv.shadow` for the value tier). See `docs/design/async-evaluation-2026-08.md` and `Hosting/*Tests`.

A HOST-ENDED TWIN RUN CARRIES ITS EXCEPTION BY VALUE (2026-11-22; C#-only). "Host exceptions and faulted awaitables propagate to the host unchanged" and "cancellation surfaces as `OperationCanceledException` carrying the supplied token" are unchanged contracts of the entry points. Between the place such an exception arises and the entry point, a twin run does not throw it through the `async` frames: the run's budget retains it once and an internal marker travels up as a failed result, and the entry point rethrows the retained exception. The twins do not implement this: both host-operation twins call one shared helper, and everything else is the shared budget chokepoints, need cells and plan supplies, so `TwinNormalizationTests` needed no new pinned difference. Full statement, rules, exclusions and pins: § Budgets and cancellation, HOST TERMINATION IS CARRIED BY VALUE ON THE ASYNC TWIN ROUTE.

## Read-only views never hand out their storage

Safe managed code using the public API, casts and interface members cannot recover writable storage that KatLang owns or shares through a published collection. This C# hosting contract covers both the backing of a view and the destination of a snapshot taken from host input. A cached property, a need cell and several positions of a shared result can all hold the same value. Every reader must see its original elements in their original order.

| Covered collections | Implementation |
| --- | --- |
| List and sequence values, including values passed to host operations | Both constructors in `Result.cs` wrap the owned array. Public construction first snapshots the supplied items. `Evaluator.Expressions.cs` and its async mirror wrap the argument list itself. |
| Root output rows, atoms, observations, run and parse errors, retained deferred diagnostics | `Result.cs`, `KatLangEngine.cs`, `PropertyObservations.cs`, `KatLangError.cs`, `FrontEndPipeline.cs` and `DeferredModuleRegion.cs`. Reformatting a successful run shares its atoms. |
| Name lists on evaluation errors and binding contexts | `Evaluator.cs`, `Evaluator.Loops.cs`, `Evaluator.Needs.cs` and `Evaluator.Builtins.cs` publish read-only payloads. The required parameter names on a member-access error use that property's own view. |
| Host operations and their parameter names, allowed hosts, lexer tokens, diagnostics and keyword names, formatters and prelude catalogs | `HostOperations.cs`, `RunOptions.cs`, `Lexer.cs`, `Formatting/OutputFormatters.cs`, `BuiltinRegistry.cs` and `Semantics/SemanticModelBuilder.cs`. A formatter callback receives the published output rows. |
| Shared elaboration data, editor records, model indexes and query results | `ImplicitSignatureTemplate.cs`, `RequiredAncestorSets.cs` and the `SemanticModel`, `SemanticModelBuilder`, `PropertyInfo` and `ScopeVisibility` files in `Semantics/`. FE-3 shares parameter metadata. FE-4a keeps its per-property required-ancestor views over interned backing. FE-4b keeps distinct scope and member wrappers over shared symbol data. |

`SafeReadOnlyCollection.Over(array)` takes ownership of an array. `OverList(list)` reads a library-owned list in place, including the list filled during the atoms projection. The internal `ReadOnlyCollection<T>` subclass reimplements `ICollection.SyncRoot` to return the view on every read. Its other collection operations retain BCL indexing, copying, enumeration and mutation rejection. `SharedPropertyParameterList` derives from the same helper. Collections such as `RequiredAncestorList<T>` that implement only the read-only enumeration and indexing interfaces already keep their storage private.

Use `SafeReadOnlyCollection.Snapshot` for host-supplied collections. `CopyHostInput` implements the same operation for constructors that validate the copied array before wrapping it. Bulk copying is reserved for trusted concrete collections: an array, an exact `List<T>` and KatLang's own view. Caller-defined lists and enumerables supply elements without receiving the destination buffer. Each is enumerated exactly once and supplies its elements through that enumeration alone, in its order. A caller-defined `IReadOnlyList<T>` is never read through its count and indexer instead, so a failure its enumeration raises reaches the caller, as `RunOptions.AllowedHosts` documents. A count the source reports without being enumerated only sizes the private copy.

A nonempty value still allocates its record and one wrapper over the owned array. The subclass adds no instance field. Empty values share an immutable empty view. A record copy shares its original view, preserving record equality and hashing. A loop-state binding context owns a distinct view of its step parameter names for each failure, also when there are none, so two independently produced context records stay unequal as they were when each held its own list. Nonempty FE-4b scope views retain their record debug text. Hosts render values through KatLang's formatters. Concrete collection types and debug text for payloads converted from raw arrays or lists are implementation details.

Reflection, unsafe code, `MemoryMarshal`, `CollectionsMarshal`, `ImmutableCollectionsMarshal` and `Unsafe` are outside this guarantee. A host retains ownership of the storage it supplies to a host-built AST. Parsed AST lists also have a separate owner contract, including properties, opens, parameter patterns, branches, expression links and declaration spans. The shared `Property.RequiredAncestorParameters` is covered. An empty array is a valid empty view.

`ReadOnlyViewStorageContractTests` exercises host callbacks, public construction, snapshots, catalogs and error payloads from a non-friend assembly, including the single enumeration of a caller-defined read-only list and the escape of its failure. `LoopStepUnboundStateMessageTests` pins the distinct empty name list of each loop-state failure. `PublicCollectionStorageSweepTests` walks nonempty published collections, checks declared output types and records exact exemptions. `ReadOnlyViewSourcePolicyTests` guards wrapper construction. `SafeReadOnlyCollectionTests` checks interface behavior and identity. `ReadOnlyViewAllocationTests` checks ownership transfer and public array construction in the GC-isolated child. `RequiredAncestorRepresentationTests`, `ScopeVisibilityRepresentationTests` and `ScopeVisibilityPublicContractTests` pin sharing and the editor contract.

## Plain/counted ownership

Plain/counted evaluator ownership: where a plain/counted pair is semantically equivalent, the COUNTED implementation is canonical and the plain implementation is its value projection — first in Lean (`eval` is the one-line total projection of `evalCounted`, which matches every `Expr` variant explicitly with no default arm; the call/dot-call/builtin/lookup plain families are `.fst`/`.alg` projections), then mirrored in C# (`ProjectCountedValue` over the counted twins; the former independent plain call/dot-call/builtin implementations and the resolve-only lookup chain are deleted). Never reintroduce an independently implemented plain evaluator body, and never make a counted function delegate its semantics to a plain one — the deliberate exceptions (C# shared uncharged leaves Num/StringLiteral/BoolLiteral/Grace for the sync and async dispatches, the slot-view family, the opens-free direct lookup used by open resolution) are enumerated in `SEMANTIC-ALIGNMENT.md` § Plain/counted evaluator ownership and its deliberate exceptions.

## Zero-argument property cache

HOST-LEAN-O6 also preserves evaluator-owned component identity. C# compares each nonempty
opens/property list by reference and empty lists as equivalent. `LeanAstEncoder` preserves those
components through context-qualified property ids and annotates distinct owned views of one
declaration with `PropertyIdentity.sharedView`/`moduleView`. The declaring-scope relation retains
the view; callable identity and an alias's own declaration key use `callableDeclaration` to erase
only their OWN view. Parent scopes still participate. These provenance dimensions are separate
from host tokens and activations; equal printed lists do not establish reference correspondence.
Component tokens are GLOBAL across declarations; when owned variants or shared alias literals need
them, the encoder annotates every owned node of that graph. The actual cache key has a typed
`componentOwners` field, and the alias key has a second independently delimited prefix of component
marks. Both retain every ancestor, including parents with no property to carry binding provenance.

Deconstruction-target explicit calls (Q-38, owner decision 2026-10-13): `a()` freshly invokes the target’s projection wrapper, which reads the separately cached shared right-hand side. It never requests a fresh RHS evaluation; a first call of a target can cause the RHS’s first VALUE demand. A successful RHS read uses the owner and activation identity of its source entry and follows the Q-37 publication law. Like every qualifying zero-supply call (CACHE-PUB-1, below), an `a()` that finds `a`'s own entry empty establishes it with the value it projected. C# may reuse its shared binding; Lean can bind the same cached source again. The reuse never changes what a target computes (DECON-CACHE-2, October 2026): C# shares a bind only between helpers that bind the same ACTUAL pattern list to the same source entry — found by the ordinary lexical lookup from the caller, keyed by its complete property-cache key — whichever consumer reads it, and stores it only when that source read obtains the PUBLISHED value (never a Q-37 re-entrant intermediate, never a value a cache does not retain) and no bind of the same execution — group, actual pattern list and source entry — is already running (that bind is re-entrant; until 2026-11-22 a running bind of any execution of the GROUP suppressed storage, so a recursive owner whose right-hand side calls it again bound every inner activation once per target read, 1 + D·N binds for capture width N and re-entry depth D instead of D + 1, `DeconstructionRecursiveReentryTests`); only a helper retaining its parser-origin signature, recorded output mapping and empty body scope qualifies, with O(1) verification through `AssignmentDeconstructionGroup`. Other helper shapes run as ordinary calls, preserving laziness and body validation (`DeconstructionMemoEligibilityTests`, `DeconstructionMemoFallbackTests`). Since OD-1 (October 2026) the memo key names no reader: every consumer of one published source entry — callbacks, helpers, explicit `a()` calls, structural and opened reads within one declaring activation — shares one bind. Local-only sources follow the declaring binding identity. An exported source has one entry per owning invocation and a separate run-wide entry where no call owns its declaring chain. Distinct source owners and host scopes keep separate binds.
