This is C# resource accounting, not semantics: the shared bind performs once its one charged source read (one step and its checkpoints) and, on the first VALUE demand by any consumer, a collector's one materialization and reservation; every consumer still pays its own target read, helper invocation and demand. Programs whose targets are first read from several consumers, or by the inner activations of such a recursion, therefore consume fewer steps, checkpoints and materialized items than under the former per-reader key and group-wide guard, and a run near a limit may complete or reach the limit at a later operation. Eliminating the source-read invocation can also lower peak depth for a deeper later consumer and turn a depth refusal into success (`DeeperConsumer_SkipsTheRepeatedSourceReadDepth`: 6 to 5, completes at `MaxDepth = 5`, still refuses at 4); values, effects and their order, and non-resource errors are unchanged (`Od1AccountingChange_IsExactlyTheEliminatedBinds`). `DeconstructionExplicitCallDecisionTests` pins scalar/list sources, effects, owner activations and seeded draws across all six routes.

Direct evaluator property selection uses a run-local `PropertyBindingIndexCache` on `EvaluationBudget`. Lists with at least 32 properties build an ordinal name index on their first lookup; smaller lists keep the ordered scan. Each entry is the FIRST matching `Property` instance, regardless of visibility or exposure, preserving Lean's `lookupPropDefAny?` and the reference identity used by value-cache keys. A host-built list can hold a property whose name is null (no front end builds one); the ordered scan compares that name like any other, so a named lookup never selects such a property and a null lookup selects the first one, and the index keeps it apart from its name dictionary, which refuses a null key (2026-11-22: from 32 properties on, building the index of such a list threw `ArgumentNullException`, while the scan served the same list one property below). Local and parent-scope lookups share indexes by PROPERTY-LIST reference, never algorithm equality, declaration token, or name. Parent wiring and unrelated `with` copies can reuse the same list; a copy replacing `Properties` selects from its replacement. Host-owned executable metadata must stay unchanged DURING evaluation (the existing `Evaluator.Run*` contract), and every independent run creates a fresh cache, so list replacement, reordering, additions, removals and same-count edits BETWEEN runs are observed. No cache is attached to an AST, scope, or shared prelude, and `EvalCtx` does not grow. Public-only open-member selection and the subsequent accessibility check retain their existing rules. Index construction is O(P), subsequent selection O(1), with O(P) storage per distinct indexed list consulted in the run. This is a C# implementation optimization, with no Lean semantic change; `PropertyBindingIndexTests` pins linear-oracle reference equivalence (nameless properties included, on both sides of the threshold), list identity, host mutations, bounded list reads, and ownership/cache/alias behavior on six routes.

NAMELESS HOST PROPERTIES. A host-built property list may contain a property whose name is null. An ordinary lookup selects the first property with the requested name. A lookup through an open selects the first public match. Null and the empty string are distinct in both cases. Alias preflight uses the same ordered declaration lookup. Its shared-body region records where a free null head resolves, and a local nameless declaration shadows that head in the body. As for other names, the body's opens are read outside its declarations. At body entry, a second nameless property is a duplicate. The first repeated declaration determines the `EvalError.DuplicateProperty` and its `Name`, including null. The loop planner consults the same verdict. Receivers and prewired scopes inspected only for lookup retain their ordinary first-match behavior. The public `Algorithm.User.FindDuplicatePropName()` returns the repeated name, so a null result means either a nameless first duplicate or no duplicate. The error record distinguishes null from the empty string even though their messages render identically. Lean names are strings. `PropertyBindingIndexTests` and `NamelessHostPropertyPreflightTests` cover the C# host case.

Clause-family preparation uses a run-local `Evaluator.NeedFamilyPreparationCache` on `EvaluationBudget` (SEC-03 follow-up, 2026-11-09; C# implementation optimization, no Lean change). Lean decides `callee.hasDuplicateBranchPatterns` on EVERY family call as a pure function of the callee; C# decides that verdict and translates every clause head ONCE per run per `Conditional.Branches` LIST REFERENCE (`ReferenceEqualityComparer`), and every call still checks the memoized verdict first and then decides its own arity from the memoized heads, so `DuplicateBranchPattern` still precedes the `ArityMismatch` decision and any clause binder. An entry holds the verdict, the translated heads (absent for a duplicate family) and the branch references read at preparation, and the driver selects the matched body from that same view. Every lookup returns a fresh wired copy (`family with { Parent = … }`) sharing its list, so all copies of one family share one entry; a `with { Branches = … }` copy is prepared separately; the declaration token is never a key. The memo follows the property-index lifetime above: never static, never attached to an AST, scope or prelude, a fresh memo per independent run, so caller-owned branch-list edits BETWEEN runs are observed, and `EvalCtx` does not grow. An entry is published only after its computation completes, so a cancelled preparation leaves nothing behind. The lookup runs in a `NoInlining` helper outside the recursive drivers' frames. Tests read `EvaluationObservations.ClauseFamilyPreparationCount` and may pass `EvaluationObservations.FamilyDuplicateIndex` (a `PatternComparisonObservations`) into the runtime index; both are opt-in and null in production. `ClauseFamilyPreparationTests` pins one preparation for `Fib(15)`'s 1,973 calls with unchanged counters on three routes, wired copies and aliases, replaced lists, between-run edits, cancellation inside the index, and verdict/clause equivalence.

Literal clause runs (2026-11-22; C# implementation optimization, no Lean change; finding `values-builtins-numerics-literal-clause-table-quadratic` of the 2026-11-21 adversarial investigation, baseline `8947d21e`). Lean's family dispatcher is the ordered clause search (`NeedBinder.chooseBranch`), and C# attempted every clause before the match, so a table of N literal clauses cost O(N) attempts per call and a workload that reads every key O(N²): 8,390,656 attempts for 4,096 keys, 16,781,312 when every key misses. The preparation above now also indexes each maximal RUN of at least `Evaluator.MinimumLiteralClauseRunLength` (2) consecutive LITERAL-ONLY clauses of one arity (`Evaluator.LiteralClauseRun`: one to 16 patterns, each a numeric, Boolean, or non-null string literal shorter than `StringCharsPerUnit` (64) code units — exactly the literals `MatchLiteralPattern` compares uncharged, infallibly and in O(1) for every value) as a trie over the head's positions, keyed by `MatchLiteralPattern`'s own equality (`Decimal128.Equals` with a hash consistent with it, ordinal text, the Boolean value). A node holds the first clause, in written order, whose literals before its position equal the values that reach it, so equal literals share a path whose clause is the earlier one. A dispatch with the run's arity selects through the index only if the first cell observes the cancellation tokens and run budgets that every later cell checks on demand. A Ready spread item observes neither, so a Ready first item followed by a run-bound suspension keeps ordered dispatch. Eligible supplies select through the index (`SelectLiteralRunClause` and its async twin, one demand site, the trie stepped out of line by `LiteralRunWalk`). It replays, each with the demands its ordered attempt begins with and in written order, only the ordered attempts that demand a supplied value for the first time in the dispatch (each node's clause: its cell 0, then the cell at the node's position), the attempt right after each replayed mismatch (its first demand, the ordered route's next observation of host cancellation and of the run's retained terminal failure) and the selected clause. Every attempt it skips would demand only completed cells, compare purely and not match, so the selected clause, the first error and its nesting (Q-36), every evaluating demand and its order, charges, effects, diagnostics and cancellation are the ordered route's. A run replays at most 2·arity + 1 attempts, and the clause-attempt observation (`ClauseAttemptObservations`) records that subsequence of the ordered attempts with their outcomes. Every other head — names, repeated names, structural patterns, collectors, long or null strings, mixed literal-and-name heads, an arity change — ends a run and keeps the ordered route, and the clauses around a run are attempted in written order before and after it (first passing clause). The index has the preparation's lifetime: one per run per branch-list reference, built after the duplicate verdict from the very translated heads the ordered route reads, uncharged and AST-bounded (linear storage and expected linear build time), observing the run's token every 1,024 clauses, and published only once complete. A lookup hashes no text of 64 or more code units, and a selection allocates nothing. The synchronous driver's frame grows by 16 B (FullOpts), 32 B (Tier-0) and 96 B (Debug), and the run path (driver and selector) by 16, 32 and 48 B over the flat-literal binder it replaces. Tests read the opt-in `EvaluationObservations.LiteralClauseRunCount` and `LiteralClauseRunSelectionCount`, and the internal `OrderedClauseDispatchTestSeam` withholds the index from the runs of its flow, so a test can compare the index with the ordered route. `LiteralClauseRunTests` pins the index against `MatchLiteralPattern` (quanta, signed zeros, NaN, infinities, the 63/64-unit boundary, value kinds), the selector against the ordered binder (selection, demand order, errors, recorded attempts, both twins), whole programs against the ordered route on the plain, counted generic, optimized, invocation-plan, forced-async and suspending routes (values, errors and spans, effects, ticks, every budget counter, every step limit and depth limit, a cancellation requested inside an argument), at most three attempts per call for one position and 2·arity + 1 for more, no allocation per selection, the preparation's lifetime, and the seven Lean-compared LanguageSpec and SemanticExplorer programs that select through a run, whose pinned Lean outcomes therefore check the index; `CoreTests/LiteralClauseTables.lean` guards the shared-tier table programs with the values the C# routes compute.

Wide parameter frames and wide-body duplicate verdicts (2026-11-22; C# implementation optimization, no Lean change; findings `evaluator-runtime-quadratic-parameter-width` and `evaluator-runtime-duplicate-check-hashset-per-body-evaluation` of the 2026-11-21 adversarial investigation, baseline `8947d21e`). The parameter tiers stay Lean's ordered association lists — the same bindings in the same order, first match wins, a callee's own bindings before the inherited ones — with indexed membership for activated frames of at least `Evaluator.MinimumIndexedParameterCount` (32) names. The activation's capture (`ParameterActivation.Capture`) filters both tiers through ONE ordinal index of the owned names (`OwnedParameterNames`, in place of the filter's former name set) and keeps it with its need tier (`OwnedNeedBindings`): whether the head binds a name (`Evaluator.ScopeBindsParameter`, also read by the nearest-owner walk and the accessibility check) takes one hash lookup, captured need tiers with at least 32 bindings also use the index for cell lookup, and the index answers only for the very list it was built from — a scope whose activation was captured for another list is scanned. The tier a call mints for a callee binding at least 32 cells of its own (`Evaluator.Concat`, a `WideBoundNeedEnv`) borrows that index for the names the activation owns — every read of the head's own parameters and every parameter it forwards — and scans for any other name, as before. A callee of at least 32 names shadows an inherited tier of more than 8 bindings through one set (`UnshadowedBindings`). Each parameter index uses the name list projected for that activation, so a host edit of a stored head between two calls of one run is still observed by the second call; narrower frames keep the scans. Separately, the runtime duplicate-property check that every body evaluation asks (`Evaluator.FirstDuplicateProperty`; host-API defense, see the alignment manifest's defensive-branch note) retains one verdict per run for each property-list reference with more than 16 entries (`PropertyBindingIndexCache.FirstDuplicate`, with the property-index lifetime above: wired views share the verdict, a `with` copy replacing the list gets its own, edits between runs are observed); shorter lists keep the allocation-free pairwise scan. Overlapping first checks can both scan the same list outside the lock. They publish one verdict, which later checks reuse. `WideParameterFrameTests` and `WideBodyDuplicatePropertyCheckTests` pin the ordered-scan oracles, the absence of per-read rescans and allocations, the once-per-run verdict, and whole programs on the plain, counted, optimized and async routes.

Wide stored heads and scopeless head names (2026-11-23; C# implementation optimization, no Lean change; follow-up of the 2026-11-22 wide parameter frame audit, measured on `3fb45cf5`). A head of more than `Evaluator.FlatUniqueNameBindingWidth` (16) patterns missed every direct binding path: each ordinary call translated its stored head again (`NeedParameterPatterns`, about 64 B per pattern) and every binding of a wide level — an ordinary call, a callback, a generic loop round, a clause attempt — ran the general binder, whose multiplicity count, visit table, work stacks and binding dictionary grow with the level (Release, per call at 1,024 parameters: translation 65,592 B, the binder 380,640 B; a whole ordinary call 532 KB and 0.26 ms, a clause call 729 KB, a generic loop round 679 KB, a recursive level about 600 KB). A FLAT translated level of more than 16 patterns is now a `WideNeedLevel` (the same records, a read-only collection like the array) that decides its binding shape once with one ordinal set of its names (`WideLevelShape`): a level of pairwise distinct, non-null names with at most one collector and no META capture or literal binds directly — pattern `i` to its supplied cell, the collector to its exact slice (`WideDistinctNameBindings`) — which is what the general binder's add-only dictionary enumerates in written order; a clause head of literals and distinct plain names binds through the literal head's existing direct path (`BindFlatLiteralHead`); a repeated name (NEED-04), a null name, META, a second collector, a literal beside a collector and every structural level keep the general binder. The loop step's binding and a family's clause heads were already translated once per loop and once per run, so they now bind directly every round and attempt. An ordinary call's stored head of more than 16 capture patterns is translated once per run per pattern-list reference (`Evaluator.NeedHeadPreparationCache`, `EvaluationBudget.HeadPreparations`, the family-preparation lifetime: never static, never on an AST, fresh per run), but unlike a branch list a stored head is read through on every call, so an entry is served only after it is VALIDATED against the caller-owned list — the same count and, position by position, the same pattern references, which, capture patterns and their declarations being immutable records, are the same names and kinds. A host edit between two calls of one run is therefore still observed: an edited list is prepared again and replaces its entry (one entry per list), and a head holding a structural pattern, whose nested lists a reference check cannot cover, is translated per call as before. The run's weak identity table retains the translation only while its list is reachable, so a host-built single-branch family can release the temporary user head it creates per invocation. Each translated capture carries its original pattern reference. Validation reads each pattern once and allocates nothing, so a wide call reads its stored head 2N times instead of 3N. Separately, a head pushed WITHOUT a head scope — the step the loop planner pushes to classify its body's references, a fully planned round — answered whether it declares a name by projecting its whole `Params` list on every captured-parameter read (`CapturedParameterActivation`), 8N + 24 B per read and one projection per captured reference the planner classifies; it now reads the stored patterns (`HeadDeclaresParameter`, `ParameterPattern.HasCaptureNamed`, the projection's membership, expanding a shared group once). A clause family's and a builtin's `Params` are the shared empty list, so those heads never paid. Measured after the change: an ordinary call at 1,024 parameters 102 KB and 0.11 ms, a clause call 102 KB, a generic loop round 315 KB, a recursive level 125 KB, and planning a 1,024-wide step whose rows read a captured parameter 1.4 MB instead of 9.8 MB (still O(N²) name comparisons: the planner's own state-name scan and this membership are linear per reference); ordinary plain heads of widths 8 and 16 have the same per-call allocations as the baseline. What remains per call is per-invocation state and its name projection — the environment, the minted tier, the activation's captured cells, the head scope's `Params` (each call's own list, which keeps head scopes of different calls distinct records) and its owned-name index — while structural and deconstruction heads keep their per-call translation and the general binder. A flat repeated-name head reuses its translation and still uses the general binder. `WideHeadPreparationTests` pins the shape, the direct paths against the general binder on random levels and supplies, the membership against the projection on random host DAGs, the cache's validation and entry count, the reads per call, exact allocations, retention, and whole programs on the plain, counted, optimized, plan-retaining and async routes.

Narrow boundaries over wide inherited tiers (2026-11-24; C# implementation optimization, no Lean change; follow-up of the 2026-11-23 wide-binding report, measured on `d59493f9`). Every binding boundary mints its callee's need tier as Lean's `needBindingContext`: the callee's own cells, then the inherited tier with the callee's names shadowed out. A NARROW boundary — fewer than `Evaluator.MinimumIndexedParameterCount` (32) cells and names: an ordinary call, a selected clause, a callback, a loop round, a zero-argument demand that binds a pattern — under a WIDE caller used to copy the caller's tier twice per call (the shadow filter, then the prepend), and its activation's capture scanned the copy: under a frame of 1,024 cells about 33.9 KB and 3,072 reads of the caller's tier per call, so the report's `loopreads` shape, a 1,024-wide loop step whose rows each call a one-parameter helper, allocated 34.6 MB per round. Such a boundary over a flat tier the evaluator made of at least 32 bindings — a tier a boundary minted (`BoundNeedEnv`), or the loop planner's shadowed step tier (`ShadowedNeedEnv`, built once per planned loop) — now mints a `LayeredBoundNeedEnv`: a narrow prefix (its own cells, then the cells of the layer it was composed over that its names do not shadow), the shared base, and the names hidden from the base. A boundary over a layer composes with it, keeping the base and taking the union of the names (`filter` distributes over `++` and composes as a conjunction), so a layer never reads through or retains another layer, however deep a recursion, a call chain or a run of loop rounds; a planned loop's handover continues its generic rounds over the loop's own context, and a composition whose prefix or names would reach 32 is minted as the flat copy, which the next boundaries layer over. Lookups, the activation's capture (the boundary's own names have no binding left in the filtered base) and the shadow filter read the parts; any other reader enumerates the flat list, built once and published compare-and-set. Only evaluator-made tiers, which nothing changes after they are minted, are shared: a host-built list is still copied at the boundary, so a later host edit cannot reach the callee, and wider boundaries and narrower tiers copy exactly as before. The cells are the inherited cells themselves, so VALUE and CALLABLE transport, memoized outcomes and flights are shared as before, and each boundary still mints a fresh tier object, its callee's binding-context identity for local-only properties, inner bindings and runtime alias cells. Measured (Release, TC=0, at 1,024): a narrow call 33.9 KB → 1.1 KB, a narrow recursion level 35.6 KB → 2.8 KB, a generic or planned narrow loop round under the frame 33.8–67.4 KB → 1.0–1.8 KB, `loopreads` 34.6 MB → 0.98 MB and 57.8 → 0.92 ms per round; narrow frames and wide callees allocate byte-identically. `LayeredNeedTierTests` pins the flat-list oracle on random boundary chains, the copying controls and host-list freshness, the caller-tier reads per call, the absence of retention chains, programs on the plain, counted generic, optimized, plan-retaining and async routes, per-invocation inner properties and alias cells, and exact allocations.

Key facts per scope level (2026-11-22; C# implementation optimization, no Lean change; finding `caching-retention-key-depth-per-read` of the 2026-11-21 adversarial investigation, baseline `8947d21e`). Every request of the zero-argument property cache builds its key — a property-style read and a qualifying zero-supply invocation (`ZeroArgPropertyCacheKey.FromExecution`), a deconstruction's source lookup, a runtime alias's binding cell (`ZeroArgPropertyCacheKey.ForAliasBindingCell`) — and so do the alias memos (`Evaluator.AliasTargetKey`, the chase visit). Each of them copied its owner's whole parent chain into a fresh array (`StructuralOwnerIdentity`), hashed it, compared it level by level with the stored key, and walked the declaring chain again for the owning invocation or binding identity (`Evaluator.DeclaringInvocationIdentity`, `Evaluator.DeclaringBindingIdentity`), on every request, hits included. Measured on the 2026-11-22 HEAD (Release, FullOpts; a `reduce` callback reading a property declared at lexical depth D, per read, one-time work cancelled by comparing two repeat counts): the key and one published-store probe cost 117 ns and 120 B at depth 1 and 1,611 ns and 1,544 B at depth 90 (about 17 ns and 16 B per level), a whole read 1.3 µs and 1.7 KB at depth 1 and 3.3 µs and 3.1 KB at depth 90, and an alias read 1.8 µs and 2.2 KB at depth 1 and 8.7 µs and 14.6 KB at depth 90 (about 140 B and 78 ns per level in all: its memo key and memo-owner walk beside the property key's 16 B and 22 ns). A level's KEY FACTS — the structural identity of its chain (`ScopeChainIdentity`: the level's opens and properties lists, the level object itself when it is an ownerless host level, and the identity of the level above) together with the nearest activation of its chain that owns an invocation and the nearest activation of it at all (`LevelKeyFacts`) — are a pure function of the level's immutable chain, so they are computed once per evaluator-owned level, from its parent's (`ScopeKeyFacts`), and every key built over that level shares them: an owner identity is the owner's own lists over its parent's memoized chain identity, the last one built over a level is reused while the declaration and the owner's lists are the same objects, the declaring level's own activation is read directly and the search above it reads the parent's facts, two identities over one level compare in constant time (a shared tail ends the comparison, and the key comparer reads the binding, environment and run components before the owner), and the alias memos find their owner — the nearest activation of the alias's declaring chain — and their detachment test — whether the chain's oldest activation lies below the watermark — through the same facts (`AliasMemoOwner`, `AliasMemoDetached`). The same key now costs 44–60 ns and 0 B at every depth, a whole read 1.2–1.4 µs and 1.6 KB at depth 1 and at depth 90 alike, and an alias read 1.4 µs and 1.6 KB; at depths 1 to 4 a read is 6–10% faster than before (alternating runs) and allocates 120–340 B less. Equality is still the former flat-chain comparison — the same number of levels, and at every level the same host level (or none) and opens/properties that are the same list or two empty ones — and the hash is computed once per level from exactly what equality compares. WHERE THE FACTS LIVE, AND FOR HOW LONG: on the level's own runtime state, never in a table — a static evaluator-owned level's runtime slot (`ScopeCtx.StaticKeyFacts`, beside its resolved open providers in one holder when it has both), an activated level's activation (`ParameterActivation.KeyFacts`, held directly until the activation needs its memo holder; an activation belongs to the one level created with it) — so they live exactly as long as the level, and a `with` copy, which carries the slot, computes its own (each memo names the level it was computed for). The chain identities handed to keys are structure only — no evaluator-owned scope, activation, value or run — so a published key, a run-scoped one included, keeps alive exactly the lists and host levels the former arrays kept. An ownerless HOST level (a host's blueprint, shared across runs and threads) and an AST node never carry facts: a structural key describes that part of the chain afresh. Activation-only lookups skip host levels without allocating structural keys. Readers use one snapshot of the matching memo. The two cold publishers on a static level synchronize so its resolved providers and key facts are both preserved. Nothing else changes: which keys are equal, the publication law, failed publication and cancellation (a computation evaluates and charges nothing, and publishes only complete facts), the alias, alias-cell and deconstruction policies, and detachment (`DetachedLocalOnlyRead` reads the same nearest activation through the facts). `PropertyKeyDepthTests` pins the identity and the declaring identities against the former flat comparison and walks on random chains (host levels, invoked and read activations, empty and shared lists, structural twins, `with` copies), the reuse rule, level computations independent of the repeat count and linear in the depth on both synchronous routes, allocation per read independent of the depth for exported, under-call, local-only, alias, deconstruction and explicit-invocation reads, the cache law at depth 24 on the six engine routes (once per owning call, per activation, distinct owners, aliases, runtime alias cells, one deconstruction bind, a failing owner, cancellation, one `Result` object, detached evaluation and its refusal, and an alias chased outside an exported evaluation chased again inside it), and retention: keys over an activated level keep no activation alive, a run-scoped key over a finished read's activation does not keep it, finished calls whose levels held facts are not retained (with a control the probe sees), the live program keeps neither finished run, and the shared host scope and its declaration list can be collected while the prelude remains live.

The zero-argument property cache is observable semantics, modeled in Lean. THE RULE: a zero-parameter property is evaluated once for each resolved property binding IN ITS OWNING INVOCATION. STORAGE FOLLOWS THE RESOLVED DECLARING BINDING AND ITS OWNING INVOCATION (INNER-PROPERTY LIFETIME, owner decision of the EXPORTED-DETACHED follow-up, November 2026; Lean-first; until then an exported binding had ONE entry per run and only a local-only one was per activation): every ACTUAL CALL of an owner — a user call, a selected clause of a family, a loop round, an explicit zero-supply `B()` — has fresh bindings for the properties and runtime aliases declared under it, whether their definitions use the call's arguments or are self-contained (`F(x) = { T = tick() ⏎ T + x }` / `F(1), F(2)` is `(2, 4)`); reads inside one owning invocation share that invocation's result or cell; the lexical `A` and the structural `F.A` are ONE binding when they resolve to the same active one, and no callback, helper, open or referring caller gives a binding another lifetime; a declaring chain no actual call entered — a root or module property, a member read from OUTSIDE its owner's call — has the separate RUN-SCOPED binding, which no call supplies or replaces (`F(y) = { public A = tick() ⏎ A, F.A }` / `B = F.A + 0` / `C = F.A + 0` / `B, F(10), F(20), C, tick()` is `(1, (2, 2), (3, 3), 1, 4)`; `F(y) = { T = tick() ⏎ P = T + 1 ⏎ P + y }` / `F(1), F(2), F.P` is `(3, 5, 4)`); outside every call `F.A()` is fresh, may establish the EMPTY outside entry, and never reads or replaces an established one (`F.A(), F.A, F.A(), F.A` is `(1, 1, 2, 1)`; `F.A, F.A(), F.A` is `(1, 2, 1)`); an undemanded property does no work. EXPOSURE STILL DECIDES ACCESSIBILITY AND DETACHMENT (below) and no longer how long an inner binding lives; declaration-chain identity, CACHE-OWNER-D1, root, module, host-scope and run identity, and dot precedence are unchanged. Lean: the invocation mark `EvalCtx.enteringInvocation`, set by exactly the three call sites (`evalNeedUserSupplyOutput`, the user arm of `runNeedStepWith`, `wireSelectedBranchBody`), consumed by `EvalCtx.push` and cleared by a property-style read's entry (`enterPropertyOwnerBody`); `ParameterActivation.ownsInvocation`; `propertyOwnerInvocation` (the binding context of the nearest invoked level of the declaring chain, `none` where there is none); `propertyKeyBindingContext`; laws `KatLangArityLaws/InnerPropertyLifetime.lean` (ledger E-23). C#: `EnterAlgorithmBody(invoked: true)` at the same call sites and at a planned round's `LoopRunFrame.BeginIteration`, `ParameterActivation.OwnsInvocation`, `Evaluator.DeclaringInvocationIdentity`, `ZeroArgPropertyCacheKey.EnvironmentIdentity`. Pinned by `InnerPropertyLifetimeTests` (optimized, generic and async routes) and the `inner-property-lifetime-*` spec cases (compared with Lean). AN ALIAS OWNS NO ENTRY (M4★ OD-E, ALIAS-UNIFY-1, October 2026, superseding the alias-owned entry of FWD-02): a transparent reference or a forwarding carrier read bare is its TARGET's bare read under the target's own binding, owner and activation (`Only(*xs) = tick()` / `A = Only` / `A, Only, A` ticks ONCE; a runtime reference reads its binding cell's VALUE), and `A()` is the target's fresh call under the TARGET's publication origin, which may establish the target's empty entry (below). A runtime-target alias's BINDING CELL is no entry, but it has its property's KEY (ALIAS-CELL-LIFETIME, November 2026): the same structural scope and the same environment component as the entry of that row written as a property — the owning invocation when the alias's own property is exported (the run-wide sentinel where no call owns the declaring chain), the declaring binding identity when it is local-only (`ZeroArgPropertyCacheKey.ForAliasBindingCell` beside `FromExecution`, Lean `aliasBindingCellKey` over `propertyKeyBindingContext`) — so the cell lives exactly as long as that entry would (`ownership-and-lookup.md` § Runtime-target callable aliases, *The binding cell's lifetime*). The key still names the binding it is read under — now always a non-alias binding (Lean: `zeroArgPropertyCacheKey` reads the binding, never the resolved algorithm, law `zero_arg_cache_key_carries_the_binding_name`; that the VALUE terminal is never an alias: `production_value_terminal_is_no_alias`, `KatLangArityLaws/WholeAlgorithmTransport.lean`). Repeated VALUE access to that same binding reuses the result (the entry its OUTERMOST successful qualifying evaluation published (a read or explicit zero-supply invocation) — see the publication law below); a shadowing property is a different binding and has its own entry; an explicit call `A()` requests a fresh evaluation and never reads or replaces A's entry; since CACHE-PUB-1 (below) an `A()` that finds the entry EMPTY establishes it (formerly it never populated it); a grouped member callee is that same explicit call, so `(Box.V)()` is `Box.V()` (Q-18 C-B3: `Box.V, Box.V(), (Box.V)(), Box.V(), Box.V` evaluates V for the first read, for each of the three calls, and never for the last read; `(Box.V)(), Box.V` evaluates V once) — while the property reads nested inside that fresh evaluation keep their own rules. CACHE IDENTITY FOLLOWS SEMANTIC BINDING IDENTITY, not the spelling of the name: the key is the resolved binding — its declaring scope, identified structurally, and its declaration — so two properties that merely share a name never share an entry, while the lexical, structural, and opened spellings of ONE binding share one. Exported bindings use one entry per declaring scope and owning invocation across every spelling, user-call argument, callback, and open; the environment component of their key is the owning invocation's identity alone — never the access context's. A loaded module is ONE declaration wired at the prelude wherever it is held (Q-31 H-P + Q-32 I-U), so every reach of its member is ONE binding: `A = load('u')` / `B = load('u')` / `A.R, B.R` evaluates `R` once, as does `open 'u'` beside them, while the holders `A` and `B` stay bindings of their own for the module's OUTPUT (`ModuleIdentityTests`, `CoreTests/ModuleUnits.lean`). HOW A PROPERTY VALUE IS CONSUMED DOES NOT AFFECT CACHING: the decision to reuse or evaluate belongs to the property ACCESS, never to the consumer, and every consumer receives the value that access produced — an operator or comparison operand, a list element, a selection (`A[0]`), a user-call argument in either spelling (`F(A)`, `A.F`) and the parameter it binds (forwarded any number of times, and reusing the outcome its argument slot established — a failure included, which is never re-evaluated through the parameter: sequences-lists-and-calls.md § At-most-once argument value evaluation), a builtin VALUE slot in either spelling and in any grouping (`sum(A)`, `A.sum`, `sum((A))`, `count(A)`, `take(A, 2)`, `first(A)`, the selected `if` branch or condition, a loop's initial state, the `repeat` count, the `range`/`atoms` arguments, `reduce`'s initial accumulator), the `.string` intrinsic's receiver (`A.string`, `Obj.A.string`, and a parameter's `v.string`, which converts the bound value), and a callback body that reads the property. No consumer can re-run the property's body, and purity is irrelevant: the rule is the same for a random- or host-backed property as for a constant one. It covers every callable a zero-argument demand accepts — a collecting-only `Roll(*xs)` included — in EVERY position: since Q-03 (September 28 2026) the front end never lifts a bare reference to such a callable into a fresh forwarding call, so no lifting position (an operator or comparison operand, a list element, a selection receiver, a spread operand, a strict Math argument, a block body) can re-run its body either, and `Roll == Roll` is `true` (ownership-and-lookup.md § Zero-argument value demand follows actual call arity). The mechanism sits at argument resolution, never in individual builtins: a builtin argument that NAMES a property resolves on its VALUE channel to the ordinary property read — exactly as a parameter with a value binding resolves to a read of that bound value — and the named algorithm rides along only as the argument's callable identity, for the slots that INVOKE their argument (callbacks and loop steps; an invocation is an explicit call, so it never reads the cache) and for the zero-argument value-demand law and signature classification, which judge the named callable (the argument is the call's need cell. Lean: `formNeedSupply` forms it; its VALUE demand `demandNeed` evaluates the written argument, whose own read applies the zero-argument law; an invoking slot takes its CALLABLE projection `projectNeedCallable`; a collection argument or fixed control whose demand fails records that projection unless its written slot is a parameter, so its rejection report judges the callable the argument names (`demandSequenceBuiltinCallItemValue`, with each argument's written source from `formNeedSupplySourced`). C#: `ResolveArgAlgsWithSequenceSpread` → `ResolveNeedSupply` attaches the cell as `ResolvedArgumentAlgorithm.Cell` with its written `Source`, read by `NeedCell.Demand` and projected by `NeedCell.ProjectCallable` (`FinishNeedCallItemDemand` for a failed demand); only a hand-built argument without a cell is its algorithm, `ResolvedArgumentAlgorithm.Algorithm`); the `.string` receiver reads a named property through that binding's property access and a parameter through its value binding (Lean `evalDotStringReceiverValue`, C# `EvalDotStringReceiverAlgOutput`); and the planned loop reads a temp passed to a planned `if` through the planned temp access, inside the same argument level (`EvalLoopIfArgument`). A property read in a builtin value slot or the `.string` receiver therefore charges the slot's own depth-only argument level plus exactly what the value-position read charges (the zero-argument access's dynamic invocation: one step and one depth level, the property's declaration span on a rejected enter), and a cache hit charges only that access; two depth levels per self-referential step keep `A = count(A)` and `A = A.string` bounded by the deterministic depth limit. This supersedes the former builtin-slot bypass (before September 27 2026 a builtin VALUE slot and the `.string` receiver demanded the named property's algorithm directly and neither read nor stored its entry, so `sum(A)`, `A.max`, `if(c, A, 0)`, and `A.string` re-ran A's body — observable as extra draws for random- or host-backed properties and as repeated materialization — and a forwarded parameter's `v.string` re-ran the argument's body through its algorithm channel); the open design question it recorded is closed. All keys retain the owner declaration token and identify its resolving scope structurally, so a record copy preserves an entry but two independently constructed owners sharing a property list remain distinct. HOST-DECLARED BINDINGS FOLLOW THE SAME RULE (CACHE-OWNER-D3, owner decisions O-2/O-3/O-4, October 23 2026): a property declared in a host-built `ScopeCtx` that the evaluator keeps (the parent of an opened inline block, `WireOpenBlockToGlobalScope`) has no evaluator owner, and its declaring owner is THAT HOST SCOPE OBJECT, by reference (C# `PropertyDeclaringOwner`; the key comparer compares it with `ReferenceEquals`, never `ScopeCtx`'s record equality). Its ordinary reads, Q-37 outermost publication and qualifying CACHE-PUB-1 invocations (`P()`, an empty spread) use the ordinary key: repeated reads through one scope object share one entry per run, a grandparent host level is the owner of what it declares, and an opened provider inside a host level keeps its provider owner. Two independently constructed host scopes are two owners even when they are record-equal, share a `Property` or a property list, or one is a `with` copy of the other — unlike evaluator owners above, a host level establishes identity only by being the same object (ownership-and-lookup.md). The same rule covers a host level ANYWHERE on an evaluator owner's resolving chain (HOST-NESTED-1, October 24 2026, owner decision OD-1(a) of the HOST-LEAN-O6 research): every ownerless host ancestor — including an empty or opens-only level — contributes its original object by reference, while equivalent rebuilt evaluator-owned levels keep their structural identity and a host level never equals an evaluator-owned one. Host `{ S = tick(); P = { public Q = S } }` read as `(S, P.Q)` through `H` and a distinct `H with { }` (or a second object over the same property list) is `((1, 1), (2, 2))`; before the fix the nested `Q` compared its host ancestor structurally and the second object's `P.Q` returned the first object's `S`, `((1, 1), (2, 1))`. Only deliberately constructed host ASTs reach this shape; no ordinary source program does. The runtime-alias memos use the same identity (ownership-and-lookup.md § Runtime-target callable aliases); binding, environment/activation and run remain separate key components. Exposure stays the host's responsibility: an `Exported` host property is trusted as self-contained and is run-cached, so one that reads a caller's binding returns its first value (G(1) + G(2) over `P = x` is 1 + 1, exactly as for an owned property); a local-only host property has no declaring activation and keys on the access binding context. Until October 23 2026 C# bypassed the cache for these reads (every read re-evaluated, Q-37 and CACHE-PUB-1 did not apply), a divergence from Lean, which always cached them (`lookupInParentsStructuralProperty`, owner `none`). HOST-LEAN-O6 (October 31 2026) aligns Lean with this identity. Every ORIGINAL host level carries its object's host-scope token in its identity slot (`PropertyIdentity.host`, a kind of its own, never a declaration, module or runtime identity), and every identity built from a scope chain reads the tokens at EVERY depth: the cache key's typed `hostOwners` component (the host marks of the owner's whole chain), runtime-alias binding cells, target lookups and chase visits (`aliasCellKey`, `aliasChaseKey`), and the declaring-scope relation of NEED-04 repeated-name callables, Q-19 open providers and the accessibility walk (`sameDeclaringScope`). One host object reached through several edges is one owner, one cell and one declaring scope; distinct objects — record `with` copies, levels sharing a property list or a declaration, empty and opens-only levels, at the declaring level or as any ancestor — are distinct ones; declaration provenance (`shared`) stays a separate dimension, and a chain with no host level keeps exactly its former identities. Lean cannot recover object identity from equal values, so the token is an INPUT CONTRACT: one object, one token, distinct objects distinct tokens, and the declarations under a level reached twice carry their `shared` identities. `LeanAstEncoder` supplies it from a reference map (the host-parent differential corpus exercises it, HP-14b included); Lean refuses a token outside a host level's slot, on a level with an owner body, or on two different level descriptions or ancestries (`hostProvenanceViolation?`, `illegalInEval`), but cannot detect one token given to two equal C# objects. An unannotated Lean host level (no token, `CoreTests/HostScopePropertyCache.lean` 5b) is an abstract value with no host-object correspondence. Laws: `KatLangArityLaws/HostScopeIdentity.lean`; guards: `CoreTests/HostScopeIdentity.lean`; C#: `HostScopePropertyCacheTests`. Local-only keys use the binding-context identity captured by the existing `ParameterActivation` at the DECLARING owner's entry, found on the selected algorithm's parent scope: the need-cell list its call boundary minted — EVEN AN EMPTY ONE, since every call binding boundary is a new activation however many cells it binds (CACHE-OWNER-D2, October 2026; Lean `EvalCtx.bindParameters`; `Evaluator.BindingContextIdentity`) — or, below every boundary (the root, a host entry), the value list. DECLARING-CONTEXT INHERITANCE (CACHE-OWNER-D1, owner decision October 23 2026; Lean and C# together): a parameterless owner entered through a property-style READ (a bare zero-argument demand of a zero-parameter user body) inherits the binding-context identity of its DECLARING activation — the nearest activated level of its in-context declaring chain, the identity its own cache key already carries (Lean `EvalBody.evalPropertyDemandCounted` / `enterPropertyOwnerBody` over `propertyOwnerBindingContext`; C# `EvalPropertyOwnerBodyCounted` / `DeclaringBindingIdentity`) — never the context of the consumer that happens to read it first, while an EXPLICIT call of that owner (`B()`, a META `R.Algorithm()`, a CACHE-PUB-1 invocation) binds a fresh context whose locals are its own. So a callback, helper call, loop step, branch or `R.Invoke()` that reads `B` and the structural `B.V` or opened `V` of the same owning activation select ONE entry, in either order: `F(k) = { B = { V = k + tick() ⏎ V + 0 } ⏎ [1].map{ n * 0 + B }[0] == B.V }` is `true` with one tick (formerly `false` with two), while `B() + B.V` evaluates `V` twice and two activations of `F` (equal arguments included) never share one. (With a row of just `V`, `B` is a carrier with no context of its own: `B()` establishes `V`'s one entry and `B.V` reads it.) Only the recorded identity changes: the entered body keeps the reader's need tier, values and call stack. With no activated declaring level (the root, an unactivated host scope) the reader's context is used, so host-built scopes without a retained activation keep the existing environment fallback (`ParameterlessOwnerEntryContextTests`, `CoreTests/PropertyCacheConsumers.lean` § CacheOwnerD1, `KatLangArityLaws/PropertyOwnerContext.lean`). The same inheritance applies to a property-style READ of a user owner whose signature accepts zero supplied arguments through a collecting-only pattern list, `Only(*xs)` (COLLECT-CACHE-1, October 23 2026; Lean and C# together): the read is still a binding — the ordinary binder binds the EMPTY supply, so `xs` is exactly `[]` inside the body and shadows any outer `xs` — but the activation it records carries the declaring activation's identity, not the fresh one of that binding, so `F(k) = { Only(*xs) = { V = k + tick() ⏎ V } ⏎ Only == Only.V }` is `true` with one tick (formerly `false` with two) and a callback's `Only` and the structural `Only.V` share `V`. A WRITTEN call `Only()` or `Only(1, 2)` remains an ordinary fresh call whose locals are its own (CACHE-PUB-1 still lets a qualifying `Only()` establish `Only`'s empty entry, and later calls never replace it). Eligibility is the binder's actual cardinality: `Fix(x, *xs)` and a grouped `Grp((*xs))` refuse the empty supply exactly as before; a structural `Only.V` whose `V` reads `xs` stays an accessibility error; `open Only` stays refused (`CollectingPropertyOwnerContextTests`, `CoreTests/PropertyCacheConsumers.lean` § CollectCache1, `PropertyOwnerContext.lean` § 4). Ordinary environments use list reference identity; the planned loop environment supplies an explicit per-iteration identity through `ValueEnvironmentCacheIdentity`: `Evaluator.Concat`, the shared helper that prepends a binding's own entries to an inherited tier, always mints a fresh list — never returning an operand even when the other is empty — because two activations that bind nothing new (`Inner()` twice, a zero-parameter callback, a stateless iteration) must still be two binding contexts, while the shadow helper (`ShadowValEnv`) may keep its instance because a shadowed inherited tier is a view of the caller's context, not a new one (`EnvironmentIdentityInvariantTests`). Every planned step temp, exported or local-only, uses per-iteration memoization: a round is an actual call of the step and owns its temps (until the inner-property lifetime rule a planned exported temp used the run cache). An explicit temp call never reads its OWN entry while sibling reads still use the declaring step's memo (`LoopTempMemoSuspensionTests`); a zero-supply call of a parameterless temp is that temp's qualifying invocation (CACHE-PUB-1): it establishes the iteration's memo when it is still empty (planned temps read only earlier temps, so no temp call is re-entrant). WHAT A PLANNED ROUND PUBLISHES (KL-06 F01, owner decision Option A, November 21 2026): a publication is counted when a zero-argument property first establishes a reusable value in its owning invocation, and a loop round is an actual call of the step. The first store of a temp's memo entry in a round is therefore that round's publication of the property, exactly as the generic strategy's cache store is — for an exported and a local-only temp alike, made by a bare read or by the establishing zero-supply call. A later read of the entry, a fresh call of an established entry, an undemanded temp and a calculation that fails before its store publish nothing, and a later failure of the run keeps what was published. The planned loop keeps its frame storage (the run's cache holds no entry for a planned temp): `LoopRunFrame.EstablishTempSlot`, the one store site, reports the store under the declared binding — an alias temp's target — to the run's `IPlannedLoopPublicationObserver`. Only a harness cache implements that interface, so an ordinary run pays one null check per established entry; the report evaluates and charges nothing. The generic, optimized and async routes and Lean then publish the same entries under the same names (`PlannedLoopPublicationTests`, `lean/CoreTests/PlannedLoopPublications.lean`; the generated family of planned, fallback, exported and undemanded temps `PlannedLoopTempFamilyTests`, `lean/CoreTests/PlannedLoopTempFamily.lean`; `docs/design/differential-observations.md` § Cache publications, § Planned-loop temp family). Partially planned loops with local properties already route those reads through the generic shared cache. The evaluator trusts exposure metadata exactly as open/dot lookup do: host-built captures must declare local-only exposure. The front end charges every MAY-selected fallback using the detector's scope-aware verdict (`Expr.DotCall.ElaboratedMissSelection`, projected through `LexicalFallbackMayBeSelected`, so a `.string` edge charges no fallback); definite structural winners charge no fallback, and navigated members charge their own requirements (a structural path or an opened name is a `PendingReference` the exposure resolver settles at the owning level with the evaluator's lookup order). The detector's verdict is final: exposure never changes what a name resolves to, so there is no recheck round. Lean assigns declaration identities before execution (`identifyPropertyExpr`, with explicit `shared` identities for host DAGs) and fresh activation identities at `EvalCtx.bindParameters`; ancestor keys identify the declaring scope, not a lookup wrapper. Failed properties are never stored; a failed parent does not roll back the entries that the owners of OTHER keys published (another binding, or the same local-only binding under another activation). THE OUTERMOST EVALUATION OWNS THE ENTRY (Q-37, owner decision October 11 2026, superseding the first-successful-completion rule; extended to qualifying explicit invocations by CACHE-PUB-1, October 22 2026): a property-style read that misses — or a qualifying explicit zero-supply invocation that finds the entry empty — while no evaluation of the same cache key is running becomes that key's OWNER; a read of the same key while its owner is still running — directly (`A → A`) or through other properties and calls (`A → B → A`), an explicit call's frame included — is RE-ENTRANT: it evaluates the body and returns its own, possibly intermediate, result but publishes nothing; only the owner's successful result is published, every later ordinary read reuses it, and publication never replaces an entry. A failed owner publishes nothing for its key (a re-entrant result computed during the failed attempt is never a candidate), and releases the key on every exit — success, ordinary failure, resource limit, host exception, cancellation. So `A = if(tick() < 3, A + 1, 0)` / `A, A` is `2, 2` after three ticks (the innermost read returns 0 and the middle 1; before Q-37 the innermost 0 was stored first and the second row read `0`), sibling re-entrant reads each evaluate (`A = if(tick() < 3, A + A, 1)` / `A, A` is `3, 3` after five ticks), and an explicit `A()` that finds the entry empty is the owner itself (CACHE-PUB-1; until October 22 2026 it owned nothing and the first property-style read inside it was the owner, so `A = if(tick() < 3, A + 1, 0)` / `A(), A` was `2, 1` and is now `2, 2`). Different keys are independent — another binding, or the same local-only binding under another owner activation, has its own owner. Within one run evaluation is serial (the async twin awaits every access), so the active owners are exactly those on the current evaluation chain and a re-entrant read never waits on its owner. C# `ZeroArgPropertyPublications` (shared by both run-scoped caches and both members of the async one; a hit pays nothing extra, a miss one set insertion and removal; the C# deconstruction memo admits only reads that obtain the published source value, and only the outermost successful bind of each exact execution may store its result), Lean `evalZeroArgPropertyAccessCounted` over `EvalState.activeZeroArgProperties`; `OutermostPropertyCachePublicationTests`, `CoreTests/PropertyCachePublication.lean`. Keep `ZeroArgPropertyCacheScopeTests`, `OutermostPropertyCachePublicationTests`, cache-key/loop/exposure tests, `LeanAstEncoderTests`, and CoreTests `zeroArg*` guards aligned. Other implementation-only caches need no Lean mirror.

EXPLICIT ZERO-SUPPLY CALLS ESTABLISH THE PROPERTY CACHE (CACHE-PUB-1, owner decision October 22 2026, superseding "an explicit call neither reads, replaces, nor populates the entry"). **Bare property access reuses the established cached value. Explicit invocation remains fresh, but an owning successful zero-supply invocation may establish an empty property-cache entry. Established entries are never overwritten.** `A` and `A()` stay different operations: `A` obtains the property's established value, calculating it if necessary; `A()` performs a fresh invocation and, if no value has been established yet and it owns publication, establishes it. For one binding, declaring environment and run: `A` on an empty entry evaluates under publication ownership and publishes success, on an established entry returns it; `A()` on an empty entry invokes freshly under publication ownership and publishes an owning success, on an established entry invokes freshly and leaves the entry unchanged. So `A = random(0, 100)` / `A(), A, A(), A` is r1, r1, r2, r1 (r1 and r2 are distinct DRAWS) and `A = tick()` / `A(), A` ticks once.
* ONE ownership domain. Reads and qualifying invocations are requests of the same law — the same key, the same active-owner set, the same success-only, never-replacing publication (C# `ZeroArgPropertyPublications.EvaluateMiss` / `Invoke`, Lean the `read` / `invoke` requests of `evalZeroArgPropertyAccessCounted`). The first qualifying evaluation that begins while the key is inactive owns it; re-entrant evaluations of the key — reads or invocations — execute and return their own results but never publish; only the owner's success publishes; failure, cancellation, a resource refusal or a host exception clears ownership without publishing, and other keys' publications stay. An explicit call owning an inner read, a read owning an inner explicit call, and an explicit call owning an inner explicit call all publish the OWNER's result (`A = if(tick() < 3, A() + 1, 0)` / `A(), A` is `2, 2` after three ticks; under the old rule `2, 0` after four).
* An invocation never returns a cached result instead of executing, and its failure propagates even when a cached value exists (`A = if(tick() == 1, 5, 1 / 0)` / `A, A()` fails with the division by zero).
* WHICH invocations qualify. An ordinary call whose callee came from an identifiable property BINDING (its origin: a lexical name, an argumentless structural path or a grouped one, a parameter's supplied cell — its written argument in its own caller context, so forwarding transports it — a selected member, or a captured plan's canonical callee cell for `R.Invoke()`, `R.Algorithm()` and `(R.Algorithm)()`), whose EFFECTIVE supply is empty after the ordinary supply formation (an explicit spread contributing no slot qualifies; one supplied `()` or `[]` is one slot and does not), and whose callee accepts zero supplied arguments (a collecting-only signature and a zero-arity clause family included). The binding and key are exactly those a property-style read of that binding uses (C# `CalleeOrigin` / `ZeroSupplyPublication`; Lean `CalleeOrigin` / `originBinding?`), decided only after the supply is formed and read without evaluating anything. A builtin, a host operation or built-in Math member (a native prelude wrapper), an anonymous block, a computed callee, a callback invocation and a loop step name no binding; a call with supplied arguments never seeds the entry. Since M4★ OD-E no alias owns an entry: an alias's `A()` is its TARGET's call under the target's publication origin — a static chain's terminal binding, a runtime-target alias's binding cell's own written origin (C# `AliasOriginBinding`, Lean `aliasOriginBinding?`) — so it may establish the TARGET's empty entry exactly as the target's own `P()` would (`P = tick()` / `A = P` / `A(), P` is `(1, 1)`; `Capture(meta c) = c` / `Only(*xs) = tick()` / `R = Capture(Only())` / `A = R.Algorithm` / `A(), Only` is `(1, 1)`), while a value-only runtime reference's zero-supply call returns its binding cell's VALUE and publishes nothing (`call-by-need.md` § Runtime aliases).
* What is published is the invocation's own execution BEFORE the call's value boundary — the property-level counted result a read would publish (`P = 1, 2` / `P(), P` stores two emitted rows, exactly as `P, P()` does), while the call's caller receives that same execution at the ordinary call boundary. Publication bookkeeping adds no step, depth, callback or demand; a later read that now reuses an invocation's value no longer evaluates the body (fewer charges and host effects — an intended consequence, never compensated). Laziness is unchanged: an undemanded supplied `A()` stays undemanded, and a completed argument cell is never re-executed.
* Observations (below) record an invocation's publication like a read's: one observation when the entry is first established — it is the ESTABLISHED value, not the latest call's. Absence still proves nothing.
* Compatibility (deliberate): programs that call a zero-argument property before reading it now evaluate its body fewer times, draw fewer random numbers and perform fewer host effects; an explicit `MaxDecimalPlaces()` (or `MinDecimalPlaces()`) in the output now supplies the engine's read of that source setting (when this was decided the one setting was named `DisplayDecimals`). Programs that read first are unchanged.
Pinned by `ExplicitCallPublicationTests` (six routes: the call/read matrix, ownership, eligibility, identity, callable and META routes, counts, observations, the planned loop and step-limit sweeps), `OutermostPropertyCachePublicationTests`, `CoreTests/PropertyCachePublication.lean` and the laws of `KatLangArityLaws/PropertyInvocationLaws.lean`.

RESULT OBJECT IDENTITY SEEN BY A HOST (KL-01, November 19 2026; a C#-only host contract with no Lean counterpart, because Lean has no CLR reference identity). Rereading or forwarding an existing `Result` preserves its CLR object identity. Equal values computed independently may share an object or use distinct objects. A host operation that compares its arguments with `ReferenceEquals` therefore sees ONE object, on every strategy, for two reads of one established entry (`T, T`), for a qualifying zero-supply call that establishes an empty entry and a later read of it (`T(), T`), and for one bound argument forwarded twice (`a, a`), also when a conditional output row selects the entry (`if(a < 1, T, 0), T`) or the forwarded argument is a parameter of an enclosing algorithm (`k, k`). The planned loop keeps this for a local-only temp. When an iteration's memo entry is established, it boxes the temp's unboxed numeric value once (`LoopRunFrame.EstablishTempSlot`). Every read, and the establishing call, then receives that one result, while the arithmetic fast paths keep reading the unboxed number. This rule also boxes arithmetic-only temps. Selective boxing saved 32 bytes per such temp per iteration. Its timing relative to an inline control varied with JIT tiering. [KL-01 measurements](../../../benchmarks/README.md#planned-loop-numeric-temporaries-kl-01). The rule covers every planned temp (an exported one handed on the run cache's stored result until the inner-property lifetime rule moved it into the per-iteration memo). No identity is promised for independent evaluations: a fresh call of an established entry (`T(), T()`, `T, T()`), two arithmetic results computed from one value (`T + 0, T + 0`), or separate executions of one literal site. The planner reuses its boxed numeric and Boolean literals across iterations, while the generic evaluator boxes each execution, and either verdict is valid. The boxing charges nothing and changes no value, effect or emitted count. KatLang `==` and structural hashing give the same verdict whether or not two operands are one object, and the public API is unchanged. `LoopPlannedResultIdentityParityTests` covers this on five routes (generic, planned, `KatLangEngine.Run`, `RunAsync` with an asynchronous host operation, and the forced async twin), with equal-value controls that accept either verdict.

STORAGE LIFETIME OF RUN-CACHE ENTRIES (identity-partitioned run caches, 2026-11-14; a C#-only storage policy, unobservable, no Lean change). A LOCAL-ONLY entry, and a deconstruction shared bind over a local-only source, is stored as long as the binding-context identity it is keyed by is reachable — the need-cell list, value list or planned-loop iteration identity above. An EXPORTED entry, and a bind over an exported source, is stored as long as its OWNING INVOCATION's identity is reachable — it is keyed by that identity since the inner-property lifetime rule, so it is partitioned exactly like a local-only one — and for the run only where no call owns its declaring chain (the run-wide sentinel). The rule covers every run-scoped add-only store of such keys: the published entries (`ZeroArgPropertyPublications`, shared by the sync, async and generic routes), the statistics key sets of `RunScopedZeroArgPropertyResultCache` and `UncachedZeroArgPropertyResultCache.CreateForRun`, and `RunScopedDeconstructionBindingCache`. The Q-37 active-owner set and the deconstruction group guard are transient and unchanged. Nothing observable depends on it: every key comparer compares that identity by reference and every key holds it, so once the identity is unreachable no key can reach, re-add or remove those entries. Partitions follow the identity OBJECT, never a `ParameterActivation` — CACHE-OWNER-D1 and COLLECT-CACHE-1 give several activations one identity, and they keep sharing one partition. Nothing is evicted when an activation exits: a returned activation re-entered through a call reference still holds its identity and still hits its entries. No hit, miss, publication, charge, diagnostic or value changes. The counts — published entries, distinct keys, maximum cache size, stored binds — are monotonic and equal the numbers an unpartitioned store reports, because nothing is removed while it is still reachable. Memory therefore grows with the activations still reachable, not with the total number of calls in the run; before this, each finished call left about 0.7–2.7 KB until the run ended. C#: `PartitionedMap`, `PartitionedSet` and `BindingContextPartitionTable` in `Evaluation/Caching/BindingContextPartitionedStore.cs`: a weak table keyed by the identity, one weak entry per identity shared by one cache's stores, owned by that run-scoped cache and attached to no AST, `Algorithm`, `ScopeCtx`, prelude or `EvalCtx`. The garbage collector clears a dead identity and its dependent partition. The table reclaims the entry on its next insertion into a full table. The zero-argument property cache's tables — the published entries of `RunScopedZeroArgPropertyResultCache` and `RunScopedAsyncZeroArgPropertyResultCache` and the statistics cache's shared table — use the PROMPT-RECLAIM form (property-cache prompt reclaim, 2026-11-17): the same dependent-handle association, with a dead identity's entry freed in place the next time the table fills, so a loop that mints one binding context per step keeps a flat peak (a local-only property per loop step: 61.5 / 109 / 214 MiB peak at 10⁵ / 10⁶ / 3·10⁶ steps before, 56.5 / 57.0 / 57.2 MiB after, Release; the live heap after a forced collection was under 4 MiB either way). That form is sequential and takes no lock: a run is one evaluation flow, no table operation awaits or calls back into evaluation, and an async continuation resumes the flow on another thread only after the previous operation returned (a DEBUG build fails loudly on an overlapping operation). The deconstruction shared-bind memo and the uncached cache's statistics key set use it too (deconstruction-memo prompt reclaim, 2026-11-18: a local-only deconstruction per loop step 63.6 / 101.3 / 156.3 MiB peak at 10⁵ / 10⁶ / 3·10⁶ steps before, 58.7 / 59.7 / 59.2 MiB after, Release, the live heap under 3 MiB either way; the same serialization, with no lock: one memo per run, reached by that run's one flow, and the uncached statistics cache only on the synchronous route), so no library store kept the `ConditionalWeakTable` form, which frees dead entries only when its replaced, finalizable containers are finalized — after full collections. Since 2026-11-20 the prompt-reclaim table is `BindingContextPartitionTable`'s ONLY form: the weak-table form, which only the store-level tests still reached as the constructors' default, was removed (implementation-only; no store changed), so those tests exercise the table every store uses. A reclamation is FAILURE-ATOMIC: it allocates the doubled arrays before it changes anything, so a failed allocation (`OutOfMemoryException`) leaves every entry, handle and bucket chain as it was, and every identity the table held is still found. The two statistics key sets exist only in a cache constructed WITH statistics (the default, kept for the tests and harnesses that read `GetSnapshot`): nothing a run computes reads them, so every `RunScopedZeroArgPropertyResultCache` the library constructs — the engine, the public and internal evaluator entries and the cache-pairing factory — passes `collectStatistics: false` and keeps only the published store, in a one-slot table (statistics opt-out, 2026-11-15; implementation-only, it changes no hit, miss, publication, charge, diagnostic or value; pinned by `ZeroArgPropertyCacheStatisticsOptOutTests`). Pinned by `LocalOnlyCacheRetentionTests` (live partitions per route after a forced full collection, a control holding activations, the D1-sharing and call-reference pins on six routes), `LocalOnlyCachePeakMemoryProcessTests` (four `reduce`s of one shared range over a local-only callback body stay within 6 MiB of one: −4.2–4.0 MiB measured, 9.0–22.0 MiB with the property cache's former weak table, which the earlier 40 MB bound did not catch — 129 pairs each on ten GitHub-hosted runners and one Windows machine, 2026-11-22; the former program, which built a range per `reduce`, reached 6.6 MiB with the prompt-reclaim table on some runners), `PropertyCachePromptReclaimTests` (every run-scoped property cache uses the prompt form; several async flows at once read, publish, fail, cancel, invoke and drop partitions with exact evaluation and publication counts, periodic nested lookups on a dedicated thread, kept entries still hitting and bounded table capacity; concurrent evaluator runs probe the same table on both sides of a dedicated-thread handoff and a full collection; the DEBUG overlap tripwire), `DeconstructionMemoPromptReclaimTests` (the memo and the uncached statistics use the prompt form; several async flows at once bind, hit, nest, fail, cancel and drop partitions while hopping threads, with exact bind and stored counts, kept binds still hit and the table bounded; concurrent async runs of a loop whose deconstruction suspends every step, collected at a fixed step interval so the table's bound does not depend on the host's GC configuration), `PromptReclaimTableFailureAtomicityTests` (an injected failure of either doubled array changes nothing, and the next addition succeeds; through a map and a set sharing one table, a failed growth stores and counts nothing, every live key still reads its value, and the next addition reclaims the dead partitions) and `LocalOnlyLoopStepPeakMemoryProcessTests` (a local-only property, one holding a call reference, a local-only runtime alias, a local-only deconstruction, that deconstruction under the uncached statistics, and a recursive deconstruction owner, per loop step, stay within 16 MiB from 10⁵ to 10⁶ rounds). The run-scoped stores outside the caches follow the same rule (next paragraph).

STORAGE LIFETIME OF THE OTHER RUN-SCOPED STORES (identity-store retention audit, 2026-11-16; C#-only, unobservable, no Lean change). Every run-scoped store outside `Evaluation/Caching/` whose key holds, directly or inside it, an object that dies with an activation, a call or a loop round keeps an entry only while that object is reachable; every other store is keyed by program structure and lives for the run. DIE WITH THEIR IDENTITY: (1) the J7 per-site callee memos `EvaluationBudget.CalleeTransportedNames` and `CalleeBuiltinSlotSummaries` (keyed by a callee's body lists, `Evaluator.CalleeBodyKey`) are partitioned by the key's PARAMETER-PATTERN LIST (`CalleeBodyKey.PartitionOf`), in one prompt-reclaim table: a written callee's list belongs to the program, so every per-call wiring of its body keeps sharing ONE entry for the run, while the user equivalent a host-built single-clause flat-binder family dispatches through (`TryGetFlatBinderUserEquivalent`) mints a fresh list per call, and its entry dies with it — it used to keep about 0.7 KB per call, the view and through its parent chain the call's activation (756 MB live after 10⁶ calls); the per-site NODE memo (`TransportedNames`) no longer keys a callee wired for one call (no later walk reaches that record as a child) or a `CaptureParameterPattern` (it has no child and adds no name). (2) The per-plan supply-formation witness of an OBSERVED run (`EvaluationObservations`, tests and harnesses only) is partitioned by its plan: only a holder of the plan can read it, and the strong dictionary kept every plan of the run — its caller context, values and supply — alive, about 1.4 KB per round of a META loop. (3) Already so: the runtime-alias binding cells (`AliasBindingCells`, ALIAS-CELL-LIFETIME above) and the static-end and `.Algorithm` route memos of an alias declared under an activation, which live on that `ParameterActivation`. LIVE FOR THE RUN, keyed by program structure only: the per-site node memos (AST nodes), the run-wide alias static ends and route decisions (an alias declared outside every activation: its declaration and the structural identity of its declaring scope chain — AST lists and host levels, which the evaluator never mints), `PropertyBindingIndexCache` (property lists), `NeedFamilyPreparationCache` (branch lists), `DeferredBodyAdmission` (regions and the nodes of the bodies it admitted), the loader's module and unit caches (canonical URLs and module declarations, loader-scoped), the optimizer diagnostics (string keys) and `RootPropertyObservationSink` (exported root keys). TRANSIENT: the wait-for edges, an asynchronous resolution's `ResolutionRecord`, the Q-37 active-owner set and a loop plan's `LoopTempCallArgumentMemo`. INTENDED, left as they are: NEED-10 retention through a live call reference, the call-reference loop retention (`call-by-need.md`), the exported entries, and the opt-in `ClauseAttemptObservations` log, which records every attempt with its family by design. Measured in Release on loops of 10⁵ and 10⁶ rounds (peak working set, and the live heap after a forced collection at the end of the run): every META shape — a reference never stored, created and dropped through a local-only property or an argument, a local-only or exported runtime alias, references created in `reduce` callbacks, several plan sites per round — and every per-site shape stays at 54–60 MB with a live heap of at most 1.3 MB (a materialized 10⁵-item range included), except that a local-only property whose value is a call reference showed a PEAK creep of about 42 MB per 10⁶ rounds (62 → 104 MB; live heap 3.1 and 0.3 MB): the property cache's former `ConditionalWeakTable` freed dead entries only when its replaced containers were finalized — GC sizing, not retention. The property cache's prompt-reclaim table removed it (2026-11-17, previous paragraph: 57.6 / 58.1 / 58.1 MiB at 10⁵ / 10⁶ / 3·10⁶ rounds, Release). No hit, miss, publication, charge, plan, diagnostic or value changes, and the counts (`AddedCount`, `PlanSupplyFormationCount`) equal the unpartitioned ones. Pinned by `IdentityKeyedRunStoreRetentionTests` (live partitions after a forced full collection on the planned and generic routes, the counts, a written callee's one shared entry, unchanged classification on three routes, store laws, and a call reference re-entering returned activations that keeps its witness) and `IdentityKeyedRunStorePeakMemoryProcessTests` (flat-binder per-site loops over arithmetic and builtin argument slots, and an observed META loop, stay within 40 MiB from 10⁵ to 10⁶ rounds).

### Detached evaluation of exported properties

EXPORTED MEANS DETACHED (EXPORTED-DETACHED, November 2026; Lean-first; origin: the independent adversarial audit of 2026-11-14, its final recommendation). The exposure classification is an INPUT of the evaluator: the front end computes it, a host supplies it, and Lean takes it as `PropExposure`. Until November 2026 the evaluator TRUSTED it — an `Exported` entry was then run-wide and an `Exported` member is accessible everywhere — so a classification that under-approximated a property's dependence on an enclosing call produced a value shared across calls, or a read of whatever a dynamically live caller had bound under the same name. Three front-end routes of that one class were found and fixed (inline-block heads, deferred-branch forwarding, transparent-reference target paths). The evaluator now ENFORCES the classification ON THE PATHS IT CHECKS, so on those paths the same class of defect — an undiscovered front-end route, or a host's wrong flag — is a deterministic refusal instead of a wrong value. A retained runtime-alias cell, which the alias chase reaches instead, is judged at every reuse, and its computation's input reads at the demand ("Retained runtime-alias cells" below; ledger R-1, qualified, November 2026).

**The rule.** Every evaluation that can create or own an `Exported` zero-argument key runs DETACHED from the activations that exist when it starts: the property-style read's miss and its re-entrant calculation, the qualifying zero-supply invocation (CACHE-PUB-1) whether it owns the key or runs fresh beside an established entry, their async twins, and the row of a runtime-target alias whose own property is exported (its binding cell is the alias's counterpart of the entry, with the entry's lifetime). The detached context has NO inherited need cell and NO inherited value, and carries an activation WATERMARK: every activation recorded before the evaluation started is detached in it. A detached activation stays an IDENTITY and is never an INPUT.
* Identity, unchanged: cache keys (the key of the exported entry is the one the reader's context computes, and the binding-context identity the detached evaluation computes below its boundary is the reader's), runtime-alias memos and binding cells, the anchoring of static levels, and the declaring-context inheritance of CACHE-OWNER-D1 and COLLECT-CACHE-1.
* Input, refused: every EXECUTED OPERATION on a captured parameter whose lexical owner's activation is detached — its VALUE read, its CALLABLE projection (so a call through it, `y(5)`: the refusal names the parameter, never a missing callable capability), and its direct TRANSPORT as a supplied argument, callee, link receiver, spread operand or alias row, which fails while the supply is formed even when the receiver never demands it (`Ignore(y)`) — is the structured `UnknownName(name)` at the written occurrence, under the context "parameter 'y' is an input of the enclosing call, not of an exported (self-contained) property" ([`ownership-and-lookup.md` § Parameter channels and shadowing] states the rule for every unavailable owner, static ones included); a local-only member one of whose required owners is detached is inaccessible — the existing `LocalOnlyProperty` refusal; and a property-style read of a local-only property keyed on a detached activation is `LocalOnlyProperty` BEFORE the cache is consulted, so an established entry is refused exactly like a miss (a hit would hand the exported evaluation an enclosing call's value, and no evaluation would expose it). Selector supply formation transports direct parameter selectors before validating any index. Loop setup transports the captured callee before demanding the repeat count.

The watermark rides on the context, so a head pushed, a callee bound and a cell suspended inside the evaluation all carry it: a closure or an invocation plan created detached stays detached wherever it is demanded or invoked later (`P = Cap(Inc(y))` publishes an inert captured call; the later `P.Invoke()` is the refusal). A nested exported evaluation may raise the watermark and never lowers it. Activations recorded INSIDE the evaluation are ordinary: a callee it calls reads its own parameters, and a local-only property keyed on such an activation is read as always. An explicit invocation of a LOCAL-ONLY property is never refused up front: it is its own fresh calculation, which the read rules confine.

**Neutrality.** A correctly `Exported` body reads none of the refused inputs — that is what the classification means — so its value, its key, its charges (steps, depth, materialization), its effects and its random draws are exactly the ones it had. Exposure is observable through ACCESSIBILITY (an exported member can be read from outside its owner's call, where it is the run-scoped binding) and no longer through sharing across calls: since the inner-property lifetime rule every call has its own inner bindings (`F(x) = { T = tick() ⏎ T + x }` / `F(1), F(2)` is `(2, 4)`; at the detachment-only boundary of the follow-up it was `(2, 3)`, one `T` per run). Detachment does not repair a classification: it guarantees that whatever an exported evaluation computes on the checked paths is independent of the activations that existed when it started, and only the front end can guarantee that the classification is right. When a tutorial example, a corpus case or a fuzz case starts failing with one of these refusals, the classification is wrong for that program: report the route and fix the analysis; never weaken the refusal.

**What changed for hosts.** A host-built tree may declare any exposure. A falsely `Exported` property that directly reads an unavailable parameter now fails at that occurrence (an exported `P` whose body reads a name it does not bind, read from `G(x)`: `G(1), G(2)` was `(1, 1)` and is `UnknownName`).
