Together with lexical-only reads ([`ownership-and-lookup.md` § Parameter channels and shadowing]) a host level that lists a name among its `ScopeCtx.Parameters` is that name's lexical owner, and no call activates a host level: a property reading the name through it finds no binding either (formerly it read its consumer's same-named binding). `ScopeCtx.Parameters` keeps its role in the accessibility check. A host that used an exported property as a memo of a caller-supplied input must pass that input as an argument.

**Planned loops.** A planned temp is memoized per iteration in the loop frame's slots, with no evaluation context of its own (a round is a call of the step; until the inner-property lifetime rule a planned exported temp was served from the run cache). An EXPORTED temp whose plan reads the loop state, a captured slot or an enclosing parameter's cell — directly, or through a temp that is not itself a self-contained exported temp — is therefore left unplanned (fallback reason `exported temp reads loop or enclosing input`): the row that reads it becomes a generic fallback INSIDE the planned loop, which evaluates it detached and refuses the read, so both strategies agree. A correctly exported temp reads none of these and is planned as before. THE DEPENDENCY WALK EXPANDS EACH DISTINCT PLAN NODE ONCE, by reference identity (`LoopOptimizer.ExportedTempReadsLoopOrEnclosingInput`): the planner shares one node between every occurrence of one expression object, so a shared host tree gives a plan DAG whose paths can outnumber its nodes exponentially, and an unbounded walk would do that work while PREPARING a temp nothing reads; the verdict is the one an unshared walk gives (`LoopOptimizationDiagnostics.ExportedTempDependencyVisits`).

THE PLANNED ITERATION HEAD NEEDS NO MARKER. The audit's design marked the head scope of a planned iteration (`ScopeCtx.ContextBindingSerial`) as the one exception to lexical-only reads, because a planned round then pushed the step without an activation and its nested captured reads depended on the dynamic fallback. The marker is not implemented (owner decision of the 2026-11-21 follow-up: keep the simpler loop only with evidence for both modes). A FULLY planned round — every row and temp a plan — still pushes the step without an activation, but runs NO generic evaluation under that head: state is read from the frame's slots and an enclosing parameter through the cell the planner found when it built the plan, so no captured parameter is looked up through the head, and a detached read it executes (a pending initial-state computation of a detached caller) is refused where the generic strategy refuses it. A PARTIALLY planned round — at least one row a generic fallback — enters the step's body with a REAL activation every round (`LoopRunFrame.BeginIteration`), so a nested captured read is an ordinary lexical read of the current round (`Step(a, n) = { { a * 2 }, n + 1 }` / `Step(1, 0).repeat(3)` is `(8, 3)`). `DetachedPlannedLoopModeTests` proves each mode from the planner's own observations and compares values, effects and raw checkpoints with the generic strategy and the outcome with the async twin; if a future change makes a fully planned round evaluate generically under its head, that suite fails and the marker becomes necessary.

**Implementation (C#).** Each activation carries a run-local serial (`ParameterActivation.Serial`, `EvaluationBudget.NextActivationSerial`). The watermark is carried by the call-stack node (`Evaluator.DetachedReadOnlyList<T>`), so `EvalCtx` keeps its size and a context outside every exported evaluation allocates exactly what it did. `Evaluator.DetachedEvaluation` is the one constructor of a detached context and `Evaluator.IsDetached` the one predicate. Memos that record the outcome of access checks are watermark-aware: a level's resolved open providers answer a reader at or below the watermark they were resolved under, and one above it when no activation of the level's chain lies between the two (`ResolvedOpenLevel.AnswersWatermark`); an alias's static-end and route memos are not used when an activation of the alias's declaring chain is detached in the reading context (`AliasMemoDetached`).

Lean: `EvalCtx.detachedBefore`, `EvalCtx.detach`, `capturedParameter`, `EvalCtx.usableActivation` in `memberAccessible?`, `localOnlyReadRefused`, `EvalBody.evalZeroArgPropertyAccessCounted` over `zeroArgPropertyPublication`, `aliasCellContext`; laws `KatLangArityLaws/DetachedEvaluation.lean` (ledger E-22). C#: `GetOrEvaluateZeroArgPropertyResultCore` and its async twin, `InvocationBodyContext`, `DetachedLocalOnlyRead`, `AliasCellContext`, `LoopOptimizer.ExportedTempReadsLoopOrEnclosingInput`. Pinned by `DetachedExportedEvaluationTests` (mis-exposed trees on the optimized, generic and async routes — the value read, the invoked parameter, the transported parameter and the other parameter operations, each with its correctly classified control, its position and its publication count; the mutation guard `DetachedEvaluationTestSeam` proves the refusals come from detachment; the neutrality pins hold correctly classified programs to their exact values and charges; section 5c pins the retained-cell reuse of ledger R-1), `DetachedPlannedLoopModeTests` (fully and partially planned rounds; the dependency walk's bound), the host-built HP-18, HP-19 and HP-20 cases of `HostParentDifferentialCorpus` (compared with Lean), the exposure-shape family `ExposureShapeSpecFamily` — its four Lean-comparable routes in the spec corpus and its deferred-branch route in `ExposureShapeDeferredRouteTests` (async, with a counting downloader: a selected materialization, its eager twin and a never-selected zero-download control per cell) —, `FrontEndElaborationBoundaryTests`, and the Lean guards `CoreTests/PropertyCachePublication.lean` and `CoreTests/HostScopePropertyCache.lean`.

**Retained runtime-alias cells (ledger R-1, QUALIFIED; November 2026, Lean-first; repaired 2026-11-24 after the independent review `050804fe` and the owner's decision of that day; one open qualification, item (4) of "What the judgment does not say").** The watermark blocks DIRECT access to a pre-existing input on the paths above. A RETAINED runtime-alias binding cell is reached by the alias chase instead, and its computation may read such an input — before the exported evaluation started, or later, in a part of it that runs on demand. So every retained cell — any non-parameter row: local-only, exported or property-less — keeps an INPUT RECORD: the activations that existed when it was established and the LEAST of them its computation has read an input through. An input read is a captured parameter operation, an admitted local-only property read, or an ADMITTED ACCESS OF A LOCAL-ONLY MEMBER on a structural route (a dot access or a static alias path step: the access uses the owner's activation whether or not the member's body reads the owner's parameter). A read through an activation the cell's own computation recorded is the cell's own work: it is recorded nowhere and is an input of no cell that reused it. A cell's recorded read becomes the read of every cell whose computation reused it, as far as it is an input of that cell too. A local-only alias's row is supplied at the watermark of its DECLARING LEVEL, whichever reader reaches it first, so the cell is the level's own binding and is never tied to the reader that happened to establish it.

THE REUSE IS JUDGED BEFORE THE CHANNEL RUNS. A reuse of the cell's VALUE or CALLABLE — the bare read, the value view's zero-supply call, the runtime step of the alias chase — by a reader that detaches an input the cell is KNOWN to have read is refused with the alias's own `LocalOnlyProperty` (the cell is a binding of an enclosing call), and nothing of the cell runs: no demand, no effect, no work. Any other reader shares the cell — the same value and identity — and is ADMITTED, and the channel runs SERVING the reader's watermark.

THE DEMAND DECIDES (owner decision, 2026-11-24). An input read is refused, with its own refusal at its own position (`UnknownName` with the detached-parameter context for a captured parameter, `LocalOnlyProperty` for a member or a local-only property), exactly when a detached evaluation that detaches the activation is being SERVED — an exported evaluation in progress, or a reader whose reuse is running the cell's channel — and the read is an input of a cell that admitted a reader detaching it. Otherwise it is recorded. So a detached reader's own demand never makes a cell read an input the reader may not use — in the row, in a lazy position, in a captured call's argument — while a read an ATTACHED reader demands afterwards uses its own inputs: a detached reader may count a lazy list without restricting a later attached reader.

The independent review's witness (`review-8013526d`, finding 6), with `Cap(meta r) = r`, `Inc(n) = n + 1`, `Dbl(n) = n * 2` and `Sel(k) = if(k == 1, Cap(Inc(0)), Cap(Dbl(0)))`:

    F(y) = {
      A = Sel(y).Algorithm
      P = A(5) + 0
      A(5), P
    }
    F(1), F(2)

is `((6, 6), (10, 10))` as classified by the front end. With `P` alone forced `Exported`, the row reads `A` attached first and establishes the call's cell from `Sel(y)`, recording the read of `y`; `P`'s detached evaluation then reuses a cell known to depend on `y` and is refused, `LocalOnlyProperty` for `A`. In the other row order (`P, A(5)`) the sibling's own demand runs the row, and the read of the written `y` is refused where it happens, `UnknownName(y)`. Until November 2026 the attached-first order was not refused (it gave the declared values under the inner-property lifetime rule, and `((6, 6), (10, 6))` at the detachment-only boundary of the 2026-11-21 follow-up).

* **Deferred reads.** With `F(y) = { Mk(v) = { Z(*xs) = [1, 2].map{ n + y } ⏎ Cap(Z()) } ⏎ A = Mk(5).Algorithm ⏎ P = A[0] + 0 ⏎ A.count, P }`, the correctly classified tree gives `((2, 2), (2, 3))` and the mis-exposed one `UnknownName(y)` at the callback's `y` in both row orders: the sibling itself demands the position. With `P = A.count + 0` instead, both `A.count, P, A[1]` and `P, A.count, A[1]` give the declared `((2, 2, 3), (2, 2, 4))`: neither count needs `y`, and the position read belongs to `F`. A sibling that demands a position the row has already read (`A.count, A[1], P` with `P = A[1] + 0`) is refused at the reuse.
* **Member access.** With `Box = { public G(x) = if(true, x + 1, y + 0) }`, `A = Cap(Box.G(0)).Algorithm` and `P = A(5) + 0`, the correctly classified tree gives `(6, 6)`; the mis-exposed one is `LocalOnlyProperty` in both row orders (for `A` when the row established the cell, for `G` when the sibling's own demand makes the access), although `y` is never read.
* **Before the channel runs.** With `Effect(*xs) = tick()`, `Sel(k) = if(k == 1, Cap(Effect()), Cap(Effect()))`, `Same(f, f) = 0`, `A = Sel(y).Algorithm`, `P = A + 0` and the rows `Same(A, A), P`, the first row warms the CALLABLE — which reads `y` — and runs no tick; the mis-exposed `P`'s VALUE request is refused with no tick. A bounded range in place of the tick is never built, so the refusal is not replaced by the collection limit, which the correctly classified program — whose channel does run — still reports.
* **Freshly owned inputs.** In the correctly classified `Build(z) = { Z(*xs) = [1, 2].map{ n + z } ⏎ Cap(Z()) } ⏎ B = Build(5).Algorithm ⏎ Build2(v) = { W(*xs) = B ⏎ Cap(W()) } ⏎ A = Build2(0).Algorithm ⏎ Observe = A.count + 0`, the activation supplying `z` is recorded inside `B`'s own computation, so `B.count, A.count, Observe, A[0]` is `(2, 2, 2, 6)` in every order of its reads.

A cell whose computation read no such input — an independent row, or one the exported evaluation owns itself — is shared with the same value, effects and cell count, whichever row establishes it. Only a mis-exposed tree is affected: a correctly classified program keeps its values, keys, charges and effects.

WHAT THE JUDGMENT DOES NOT SAY. (1) It is CONSERVATIVE, never a wrong value: the record belongs to the cell, so a reuse is judged by everything the cell's computation read so far through any channel or deferred part — a sibling that would only count the list is refused once the row has read a position that needs `y`, and is not in the other order. (2) The served watermark is DYNAMIC: an exported evaluation is served while it runs. A lazy value it returns unread and an attached reader forces is that reader's read (the row's `P[0]` of `P = A.take(2)` gives the declared value), while a detached reader that forces it is refused; a deferred part the exported evaluation created itself is detached for its own reads wherever it runs, but a read it then causes in a retained cell's computation is judged against whatever is served at that time. (3) A read refused for a detached demander fails its position, and failures are sticky. (4) AN `open`-PROVIDED MEMBER IS NOT RECORDED: an `open`-provided name and a dotted `open` path are resolved by the lexical lookup, which the Lean model keeps a state-free judgment, so the admitted access of a local-only member on that route is not noted on the cell. A falsely exported sibling that reuses a cell whose computation used such a member is not refused, in either row order, although its own direct use of the member is (`open Box` with `A = Cap(G(0)).Algorithm` and `P = A(5) + 0` gives `(6, 6)` both ways); it is no wrong value — a read of `y` would be refused — and it is pinned as an open qualification. (5) What stays open is the other half of E-22: that the RESULT of an exported miss does not depend on the reader's binding context, call stack or plan is not proved (`exported_request_ignores_reader_tiers` covers readers that differ in their need tier and value environment).

Lean: `CellInputs`, `CellRecords` (`serving`), `noteCellInput`, `noteMemberAccess`, `CellRecords.lateRefused`, `servingDetached`, `CellInputs.admit`, `judgeRetainedCellReuse`, `reuseRetainedCell`, `EvalCtx.reattach`, `aliasCellContext`; laws `KatLangArityLaws/RetainedCellReuse.lean`. C#: `Evaluator.AliasCellInputs` under the run's one gate (`EvaluationBudget.RetainedCellGate`; live readers keep their sources’ dependency records, producers hold readers weakly, and each source keeps the admitted watermark limited by the establishment watermarks along its forwarding path even after the reader is collected), `Evaluator.RetainedCellAdmission`, `EvaluationBudget.ServingDetached` (`EnterServing` / `ExitServing`), `EvalCtx.Reattach`, the notes in `CapturedParameterActivation`, `AnnotatedLocalOnlyRead` and `IsAccessibleFrom` (`AdmitMemberAccess`). A pending async projection carries the watermark at the observation site through its awaited execution (HP-21a). Pinned by `DetachedExportedEvaluationTests` § 5c and the HP-19, HP-20 and HP-21 cases of `HostParentDifferentialCorpus` (compared with Lean); the mutation guard `DetachedEvaluationTestSeam.DisableRetainedCellJudgment` restores the old reuse path and its unrefused values.

## Passive root-property observations (IV-1/IV-2, 2026-10-21)

`RunOptions.ObservePropertyValues` (default `false`) makes `KatLangEngine.Run`/`RunAsync` return `RunResult.PropertyObservations`: the entries the run's zero-argument property cache PUBLISHED for source-declared root properties, in publication order (owner decisions D0(A), D1(A), D2(A)). It is a host-facing view of the existing Q-37 state, not a language rule: no Lean semantics, cache law, demand order, or evaluation strategy changes, and Lean's `EvalState.zeroArgPropertyCache` restricted to exported root-owner keys is the modelled counterpart.

- THE HOOK is the success-publication statement of `ZeroArgPropertyPublications.EvaluateMiss` and its `EvaluateMissAsync` mirror — the one place where "owner vs re-entrant" and "published" are already decided — so sync, async-twin, generic, planned, and fused routes share one recording point and no second active-key law exists. The optional run-owned `RootPropertyObservationSink` is told after `TryAdd` succeeds; hits, re-entrant completions, failures, and explicit calls of an already established entry never reach it. Since CACHE-PUB-1 an owning explicit zero-supply call publishes through the same statement (`Invoke` delegates to `EvaluateMiss` on an empty entry), so it is observed exactly like a read's publication — the established value, not the latest call's. Without the option no sink exists and the cost is one null check per publication.
- ROOT MEMBERSHIP is reference identity, never name, span, or record equality: the binding must be one of the FINAL elaborated root's own `Property` instances, `Exported`, with a real anchor (`FirstDeclarationSpan`, line ≥ 1), and the key's owner must be the root — its declaration token and its own property list (`StructuralOwnerIdentity.IsPropertyOwner`), since wiring gives the root a run-specific parent. The spanless `$deconstruct$N` source, prelude/host-op wrappers, module members (bindings of the module root), nested, local-only, and host-built source-less bindings are therefore never observed; a `Property` reused under another owner publishes under that owner's key and is not mistaken for the root's.
- AT MOST ONE observation per root binding is the contract (an exported root binding has one root-owner key per run). The sink counts a second kept publication of one binding (`DuplicateBindingPublications`) instead of merging or dropping it; the corpus differential asserts zero.
- SNAPSHOT: after the unchanged `ProjectEvaluationOutcome` and its cancellation checks, the one shared finalizer (`AttachPropertyObservations`) builds the immutable `PropertyObservations`. A value holding a list evaluated on demand is first replaced by its passive snapshot (`Evaluator.TrySnapshotValue`; [`sequences-lists-and-calls.md` § Lazy collections and pipelines]): the whole or one-level form the run built, or — for a list whose cardinality the run settled and whose every position's VALUE completed successfully — a new list of those memoized values (`LazyList.SettledCount`, `LazyList.PeekSlot`, `NeedCell.TryGetCompletedValue`: memo reads that demand, create, discover, reserve, charge and observe nothing); a list read too little, or a cycle through those forms and positions, withholds it. One reference-identity snapshot memo per snapshot keeps every completed structure, shared by every root that reaches it, and every structure proved to have none, so the walk stays within the graph the run resolved. Each value is then classified by the context-free `Evaluator.ContainsCallReference` scan — iterative and DAG-memoized, uncharged, with no token observation (never the run-cancellable `RunExportBoundaryError`) — with one reference-identity memo of completed presence verdicts per snapshot. A fully inspected structure is clean; finding a reference proves its active ancestors forbidden, never clean. Both verdicts and completed clean prefixes are reused across roots, including withheld graphs; unvisited suffixes acquire no verdict. This prevents repeated uncancellable scans of shared structure. A value containing a call reference anywhere is `Withheld` with no value; the run is not failed for a reference held by a property it never outputs. Reference-free values are shared, not copied. Raw records are released; the public types hold only names, spans, statuses, counts, and export-safe values.
- NULL/EMPTY: `null` without the option and for every `ParseFailure`; otherwise non-null, possibly empty, on `Success`, `NoProgramOutput`, and `EvalFailure` (whose snapshot holds what was published before the failure — partial runs are labelled by the variant). Cancellation still propagates as `OperationCanceledException`, so no snapshot is returned.
- RESULT IDENTITY: the snapshot lives in a `RuntimeStateSlot` on `RunResult`, so equality, hashing, and `with` copies are unchanged; `RunResult.PrintMembers` is written out so record printing keeps its member layout. `RunResult.RenderPropertyValue` renders an available value through the canonical bounded renderer with the property's own row boundary (`EmittedCount`) and the result's display configuration.
- FORWARDING BINDINGS (M4★ OD-E, ALIAS-UNIFY-1): an alias of either kind owns no entry, so a root property that is a transparent reference or a forwarding carrier is never observed under its own name — its reads and calls publish under the TARGET's binding, which is observed only when it is itself a root property (`P = tick()` / `A = P` / `A` observes `P` only). A forwarding root property whose target is not a root binding therefore leaves no observation under any name: the helper-block idiom `Area = { W = 3 ⏎ H = 4 ⏎ Result = W * H ⏎ Result }` is a carrier of its own nested `Result`, and `K = Box.V`, a module member or an opened member are references to non-root bindings, so a host's property inspector (KatLangWeb) no longer lists them; a host that wants such a value observed writes a calculation row (`Result + 0`), which is a body of its own. This is a deliberate host-visible change; nothing fakes an entry for the alias (`WholeAlgorithmTransportTests.R4_NoAliasOwnsARootPropertyObservation`).
- ABSENCE PROVES NOTHING: unused properties, properties only called with arguments or through a callable that names no binding, and re-entrant-under-failed-owner calculations all have no entry (an explicit zero-supply call that finds the entry empty now establishes one, CACHE-PUB-1); hosts must not label absence "never calculated". Document-version correlation is the host's (spans are coordinates of the source that run evaluated).

Pinned by the `RootPropertyObservations` suites: `PropertyObservationNeutralityTests` (every program of both corpora sink-off vs sink-on on the optimized, generic, and forced-twin routes: outcome, all budget counters, loop/sequence diagnostics, cache statistics, traversal counters, random-stream position; engine outcomes on both entry points; host-effect logs and seeded draws on sync/async/suspending engine routes; cross-route snapshot agreement; zero duplicate publications; mutation-checked), `PropertyObservationBehaviorTests`, `PropertyObservationSourceMappingTests` (semantic-model round trip; host-built defensive filters), `PropertyObservationExportSafetyTests`, `PropertyObservationPositionReadTests` (lists read one position at a time on three engine routes, observation off and on; the snapshot's passivity at evaluator level), `PropertyObservationApiContractTests`, and the consumer probes of `PropertyObservationPublicContractTests`.

## Root output-row counts (2026-11-20)

`RunResult.Success.OutputRowCounts[i]` is the number of entries in `OutputRows` produced by `Root.Output[i]`. A host can walk the counts in order and label each successive slice of values with its source row. The counts are nonnegative, one per elaborated root output row, and sum to `OutputRows.Count`. A plain source row supplies one value, including an empty sequence, list or string. An elaborated `Expr.SequenceSpread` supplies zero or more items. Both a written spread and a call to a declaration marked `*` can produce that node.

`EvalRootProgramCountedWithTopLevelProperties` and its async twin offer a run-owned recorder for the wired root's output. On entering the shared row loop, the matching invocation takes the offer off the budget before evaluating its first row and carries it across awaits as its row source. Nested bodies, including one that shares the root's rows, and later display-property reads use the ordinary row source. That value type contains only the existing row-list reference. Its specialization of `EvalOutputRowsPreparedCore` and its async twin skips the recording branch.

After each root row appends its slots, the recorder saves the increase in the accumulator's length. Only a successful output passes the resulting array to the engine. Neither recording nor projection evaluates values, charges the budget or observes cancellation. `KatLangEngine.ProjectOutputRowCounts` uses the same zero/one/many presentation rule as `RunResult.RowsOf`. Multiple physical slots form a sequence and each keeps its row owner. `CombineOutputSlots` passes a sole slot through as the value itself. If that value presents several entries, the row that supplied it owns them all, and surrounding zero-width rows keep their positions.

Every engine success exposes the array through `SafeReadOnlyCollection.Over`. Direct construction requires a valid partition. The other result variants carry no counts. A `with` copy, including `WithHostDecimalPlaces`, shares the view. The ancillary `RuntimeStateSlot`, explicit `GetHashCode` and `PrintMembers` preserve the earlier equality, hash and printable members. Deconstruction still yields the root, value and atoms.

`OutputRowCountsTests` covers explicit and inserted spreads, empty rows, source slices, loaded modules, suspension, concurrent runs and copies. `OutputRowCountsCorpusSweepTests` checks the partition across both source corpora on the synchronous, asynchronous and twin engine routes. A program that succeeds on one route must succeed on the others, except for the route-dependent `EvaluationStackExhausted` verdict, which it recognizes by error code. `OutputRowCountsNeutralityTests` covers the claim, operational counters, random draws and effects. It also bounds what an attached recorder allocates over nested bodies against a detached recorder on the same build. That is not a comparison with an earlier release. `OutputRowCountsRecordContractTests` covers record identity, storage and the projection of a sole slot. The async parity and non-friend host API tests also cover the new member.

## Callable aliases in the evaluator (FWD-02, October 2026)

> **M4★ (ALIAS-UNIFY-1, October 2026).** The evaluator now separates FOUR chases over one iterative engine (`Evaluator.ChaseAlias`, Lean `resolveAliasChase`): the INVOCATION chase (through references and carriers to the callable a call invokes — `ResolveAliasTarget`), the STRUCTURE chase (through transparent references only, stopping at a carrier — members, `.Algorithm`, NEED-04 identities), the VALUE chase (to the terminal binding a bare read is keyed by — `ResolveAliasValueTerminal`, which also records whether the read crosses a carrier, Q-36) and the STATIC-STRUCTURE chase (through references, a carrier the end, nothing projected — a member path's receiver, the Q-36 carrier crossing and a reference's runtime reach); the stratified structure of an `open` provider is a separate fuel-bounded walk (`OpenStructure`, Lean `openStructure`, never through an open). A chase records what it passed by BINDING — declaration, declaring scope and activation (`AliasChaseVisit`, Lean `aliasChaseKey`) — and a structure it computes for a step's route decision reads with the structure chase's own context only (an invocation or VALUE chase lends it none: `aliasStructureContext`), so a reference behind a carrier whose row reads it back (`A = B` / `B = { public F(x) = x * x ⏎ A.F }` / `A(3)` is 9) and two same-shape references in one chase both resolve. Calls through an alias publish under the target's origin (`TakesOriginDispatch`), a loop normalizes an alias step to its invocation target once before planning, and no alias owns a cache entry (§ Zero-argument property cache). The text below describes the FWD-02 binding-indirection mechanics these chases refine.

A callable alias (`Algorithm.Alias`, Lean `Algorithm.alias`; the language rule is `ownership-and-lookup.md` § Callable aliases and bare forwarding) is BINDING INDIRECTION, and the evaluator keeps its two identities apart with ONE normalization, applied where a BINDING becomes a CALLABLE: `LookupLexical` (over the raw `LookupLexicalRaw`, which keeps the alias as the alias for written receivers and for the chase itself), a structurally navigated member (`EvalAliasMemberCounted`), an inline block (`ResolveInlineAliasTarget`, in `ResolveAlg`, value position and spread operands), and the top-level property read (`NormalizeTopLevelBinding`); call, callback and zero-argument-demand dispatch carry a safety arm for host-built trees. The chase (`Evaluator.ResolveAliasTarget`, Lean `resolveAliasTarget`) is iterative — no host recursion proportional to a chain — and resolves each link's written target in that link's OWN scope (the alias pushed as the head): a name by the ownership-first lookup, a member path by structural navigation of DECLARED members with the accessibility check. The structure computations a step nests — a member path's ALIAS receiver, an `.Algorithm` link's route decision over its receiver — are NESTED CHASES on the same explicit stack (`AliasChaseMachine`, implementation-only), never host frames: their structure context is ONE visited set whose entries a nested chase removes when it finishes (an undo log, not a copy per nesting), and a receiver's static end is remembered under the binding that selected it, so a chain of member links through alias receivers is chased once in work linear in its links, like a chain of names. An `.Algorithm` link's binding cell re-decides its own route when it projects, so a chain of such links would re-navigate every link below each one; a route decision navigated CLEANLY — no alias cycle or static navigation failure, no `ResolveAlg` fallback, no non-exported member, every runtime projection it read the cell's PUBLISHED (sticky) outcome, success or failure — is therefore SETTLED per alias declaration and declaring scope beside the binding cell (`ParameterActivation.AliasRouteDecisions`, run-wide `EvaluationBudget.AliasRouteDecisions`) and reused by every later chase, observing the run's continuation first as its first projection did. A decision that met a cycle, a flow-local `DemandCycle`, a pending observation or a resolution's recorded-only outcome is never settled. Inside an asynchronous resolution (`ResolveAsync`), a chase whose runtime projection is reported pending PARKS on the resolution's flow-local record (`NeedCell.ResolutionRecord.ParkedChase`), and the retry — the same resolver over more determined facts — resumes that chase at the projection instead of walking the chain above it again. Both are implementation-only reuse: the outcomes, errors, spans, charges and demand order are those of a fresh navigation (`AlgorithmLinkChains_ChaseInLinearWork`, `AlgorithmRouteDecisions_*`). It is memoized per alias binding and resolving scope (`AliasTargetKey`: the binding by reference and `StructuralOwnerIdentity.FromScope` of its scope chain), every link of a chain at once, in the memo of the nearest activation of the declaring scope (`ParameterActivation.AliasTargets`, forgotten with that call) or run-wide outside every activation (`EvaluationBudget.AliasTargets`), holding resolved algorithms only — never a value, a cache entry or a failure. So a 10,000-link chain is chased once and a thousand calls through its last link cost one memo read each; an alias inside a recursive body resolves per activation. An alias is never an invocation: no step, no depth, no binding context, no frame. A revisit of an alias declaration in the same resolving scope is a cycle, `IllegalInEval` (`AliasCycleMessage`); the pre-evaluation gate already rejects every static cycle and every target that is no static path (`PreEvaluationAstViolation.AliasCycle` / `AliasTargetNotStaticPath`, `StaticAliasTargets`), so only a cycle through an `open` reaches the runtime check. Frame-size discipline: every alias branch is a `NoInlining` helper in `Evaluator.Aliases.cs` (with its `// MIRROR OF` async twin), so the calibrated recursive frames only test the variant and delegate. Pinned by `CallableAliasBindingIndirectionTests` (name, member-path and `.Algorithm`-link chains on a 1 MiB stack, host-built chains and cycles, resource accounting, six routes) and `CoreTests/AliasForwarding.lean`.

Static alias-cycle validation is scope-sensitive on host DAGs: a shared alias reached in a safe scope never exempts it in another scope, and revisiting its declaration in a different resolving scope is not itself a cycle. `StaticAliasTargets` keys both settled and active chases by alias reference and the relevant property context; the validation walk revisits alias-bearing subtrees only per that context, retaining node-only visits elsewhere. Its context omits scopes declaring none of the static target heads, so unrelated declarations cannot multiply traversal paths. `CallableAliasBindingIndirectionTests.SharedHostAlias_*` pins rejection order, valid revisits, spans, forced async entry, shared containers, and bounded traversal growth; `CoreTests/AliasForwarding.lean` pins the equivalent tree meaning. A dotted alias step also retains its selected member binding, so the runtime memo remembers every link just as for a lexical name (`DottedAliasChain_FirstNormalization_RemembersEveryBinding`). These are C# preflight and memoization repairs, not changes to source-language alias semantics.

Parking requires an untainted chase prefix. In particular, a recorded-only outcome can be superseded by a cell's published outcome before the next retry; a chase that read it must restart, so that memo-before-record ordering remains observable (`AlgorithmRouteDecisions_RetryRechecksAnUnpublishedEarlierOutcome`). The tested root-scope chain families have fixed scope depth and a constant-size target per link: each key construction, context comparison, visited-set insertion/removal and memo operation costs constant work. Each clean route is settled once, and each parked retry repeats only its pending projection and constant resolver prefix; the initial observation does one full walk. Thus both generic and forced-async evaluations do amortized linear total chase work over those families. Tainted navigations retain fresh retries and have no such bound.

## Numerics

Lean core numeric semantics use `Int`; the C# runtime uses IEEE 754 `System.Numerics.Decimal128` (34 significant decimal digits) end-to-end — literals parse directly into it, arithmetic and every Math function compute in it natively, and there is no `decimal` or `double` stage anywhere in core numerics. Do not silently widen or reinterpret numeric behavior without checking Lean first. Division/modulo by a zero-valued divisor (the EVALUATED value — `1 / 0`, `1 / (1 - 1)`, `1 / -0`, and an underflowed-to-zero divisor all alike) stays the Lean-modeled `divByZero` error and zero-base powers obey the domain-error policy in `src/KatLang/SEMANTIC-ALIGNMENT.md` (the "Core numeric semantics" row and § The two-tier numeric model); `div` uses `Decimal128Numerics.IntegerDivide`: exact mathematical truncation when representable, otherwise leading 34 digits toward zero for finite IEEE quotients (the consecutive-integer domain is |q| <= 10^34, with sparse larger integers also exact). IEEE division overflow and special values are unchanged; the mathematical div/mod identity requires a representable truncated quotient, and its runtime recomposition also requires exact intermediate arithmetic. Other arithmetic follows Decimal128's IEEE behavior (overflow produces a signed infinity, domain violations produce NaN, NaN/±Infinity/−0 are ordinary values). Structural value equality (`==`, `distinct`, `contains`, ValueComparer) uses `Decimal128.Equals` semantics — NaN is one value, quantum is ignored — while the ordering operators are IEEE (comparisons with NaN are false). Transcendental Math results are quantum-canonicalized (IEEE `reduce`, value-preserving) so exact results like `Math.Lg(1000)` stay `3`; ordinary arithmetic is not quantum-canonicalized, so the Decimal128 operation's result quantum remains visible (`0.5 + 0.5` displays `1.0`). `range` alone canonicalizes accepted whole-number bounds before enumeration: exponent 0 except at ±1e34 (exponent 1), and unsigned zero. Keep this at the shared range-validation boundary; do not normalize other whole-integer arguments. Numeric-change ROUTING is two-tier — the "Core numeric semantics" row in `src/KatLang/SEMANTIC-ALIGNMENT.md` is authoritative: Lean-modeled laws on the COMMON EXACT integer subdomain (exact integer arithmetic/comparison, the representative truncating `div`/`mod` cases, the zero-divisor rule where applicable, `0 ^ negative` at its integer instance — the runtime rejects EVERY exponent below zero, the fractional side is Decimal128-only — structural equality, Boolean logic over Boolean operands) change Lean and C# together with Lean-comparable LanguageSpec coverage, while Decimal128-specific behavior (finite precision/range even for integral inputs, fractional values and quantum, NaN, ±Infinity, signed zero, overflow to infinity, gradual underflow, IEEE ordering of specials, non-exact `/`/`avg` results) is an explicit reviewed model divergence pinned by C# tests plus a canonical C#-only LanguageSpec case with a `LeanExclusionReason`, never a fabricated Lean encoding. Do not classify from source spelling alone: "integer input" is not sufficient once a Decimal128 operation rounds or overflows. ACCURACY AND ARRANGEMENT (final audit, September 2026, Decimal128 tier): `Math.Ln`/`Lg`/`Log`/`Acos`/`Asin` are the platform `Decimal128` functions under the shared Math-result canonicalization (DEC-RC2-1, October 2026: the .NET 11 RC2 accuracy correction superseded the former log1p window [0.5, 1.5] and inverse-trig endpoint reformulations; their accuracy is a sampled, not a correctly-rounded, contract; the DEC-RC2-1 measurements are in the git history of `src/KatLang/SEMANTIC-ALIGNMENT.md`), and a delegated power — a fractional exponent, one beyond `long`, or a negative integral exponent whose positive power overflows — of a base whose MAGNITUDE is in [0.99, 1.01] is `exp(y · ln |x|)` (a negative base takes the integral exponent's parity sign, so `(-b) ^ n` is exactly `(-1) ^ n · b ^ n` on both sides of every routing boundary: the band once admitted only positive bases, and `(-0.9999999999999999999999999999999999) ^ 1e34` kept four correct digits — numeric audit #6, September 2026, `Evaluator.DelegatedPower`) with the exponent product, the fractional exponential, and `e^n` computed as 80-place fixed-point approximations, with final Decimal128 rounding once (`NearOnePower` over `RoundRational`), because the preview-era runtime's `Decimal128.Log` lost about log10(1/|x − 1|) digits to cancellation near 1 and `Pow` inherited that loss (the RC2 `Pow` still measured up to 1.9 ulp on the D4 near-1 sample where this path stayed within 0.5, so it is retained) — and because a power loses log10 |y · ln x| digits whenever that product IS rounded to 34 digits (the hostile pass measured 123–2,089 ulp for a rounded-product composition; sample powers are pinned at one ulp against a binomial-series oracle and separately derived arbitrary-precision constants; logarithm truncation is amplified by the exponent, conservatively below 10^-39 in the evaluated product band, and the result is not interval-certified); the tutorial's "correct to roughly the last digit or two" claim is pinned against an INDEPENDENT BigInteger series oracle in `Decimal128NearOneLogarithmTests` (one ulp), never against the runtime functions themselves. `order` is a STABLE sort under `Decimal128.CompareTo` and `orderDesc` its reverse (`Evaluator.SortStable`; Lean `sortIntsAsc`/`sortIntsDesc`): ascending equal-comparing values keep their written order and descending reverses those groups, observable through the quantum and zero sign display preserves, so the arrangement never depends on the runtime sort algorithm. SIGNED-ZERO EXTREMA (September 2026, Decimal128 tier): KatLang spells the two zeros `0` and `-0` — numeric literals are unsigned, `-0` is prefix minus over the ordinary zero, and there is no unary `+` (`+0` is a parse error); IEEE arithmetic also signs computed zeros (`0 * -1`, `-7 div 8`) — and they are ONE value to structural equality and to every ordering operator (`-0 == 0` is `true`; `-0 < 0` and `-0 > 0` are `false`). `min`/`max` alone decide a tie between them by sign, exactly as IEEE 754 `minimum`/`maximum` do (`Decimal128.Min`/`Max`, folded by `Evaluator.EvalMinCounted`/`EvalMaxCounted` — the one implementation the synchronous dispatch, the async twin, and the planned loop's generic fallback reach): `min` returns a negative zero whenever one takes part in a zero minimum and `max` an ordinary zero whenever one takes part in a zero maximum, in every element order; the result is always one of the elements, so no sign is manufactured (`max((-0, -0))` is `-0`, `min((0, 0))` is `0`), and the sign matters only where zero IS the extremum (`min((-1, -0, 0))` is `-1`). Which of several SAME-SIGNED equal values with different quanta is returned (`1` vs `1.0`, `-0` vs `-0.0`) is unspecified. Display spells the results `0` and `-0`, never `+0`, and that text reads back as the same zero. Lean's `Int` has one zero, so the rule is a Decimal128 refinement that agrees with Lean's `evalMinCounted`/`evalMaxCounted` on every `Int` value (`SignedZeroExtremaTests`). `MaxDecimalPlaces` rounds midpoints AWAY FROM ZERO — the rule `Math.Round` applies — in `ValueTextRenderer.FormatAtom`, which only edits the digits of an already-rounded value (§ Decimal places); the runtime fixed-point formatter's own tie rule (changed in .NET 11 RC1) is never the owner. `avg` is the EXACT mean (numeric audit #6, September 2026): the exact total divided by the count, rounded once. `sum` stays the left-to-right Decimal128 sum, which can round on the way, so `avg` keeps `total / count` bit-for-bit — IEEE quantum and zero sign included — only while every addition was exact (decided cheaply from the result quanta by the ONE `SumNumbers` loop, and exactly when that is inconclusive), and otherwise divides the exact BigInteger total once (`Evaluator.AverageFiniteNumbersExactly` over `Decimal128Numerics.TryExactDecimal128`, Horner-assembled so thousands of distinct quanta stay cheap); the mean formerly divided the rounded running sum unless it overflowed, so `avg((1, 1e34, -1e34))` was `0`. A NaN or infinite element keeps the ordinary IEEE result. C#-only spec cases `logarithm-near-one-is-accurate`, `order-is-stable`, `max-decimal-places-rounds-ties-away-from-zero`, `avg-is-the-correctly-rounded-exact-mean`, `negative-near-one-base-power-is-sign-symmetric`, `min-max-signed-zero`.

VALUE, REPRESENTATION, PRESENTATION (Q-28, decided N-F on 2026-10-07; Decimal128 tier, C#-only observation — Lean unchanged, FORMAL-04). Three layers stay distinct. (1) VALUE: `==` is the language's ONE numeric value relation — `Decimal128.Equals` at numeric leaves (`1 == 1.0 == 1.00`, `0 == -0`, NaN equal to NaN), recursively structural over sequences and lists, kinds kept — and it is the "same value" of every consumer: `==`/`!=`, `contains`, `distinct`'s equivalence classes, numeric literal patterns (`F(0)` matches `-0` and `0.0`, `F(1.5)` matches `1.50` and `3 / 2`), duplicate clause-head detection (`F(1.5)` beside `F(1.50)` is `DuplicateBranchPattern`), and repeated-name VALUE compatibility (`Result.ValueComparer`; there is no second, representation-sensitive numeric equality). (2) REPRESENTATION (observable through `.string` — `1.50.string` is `1.50`): a number also carries its exact Decimal128 datum — quantum, zero sign, NaN sign — which is DATA the value carries, produced by the operation from its operands' representations, never provenance, source spelling or route (`(0.5 + 0.5)` and the literal `1.0` are bit-identical). Value-carrying operations — binding, storage, selection, passing, collecting, sorting — preserve the chosen representative; only an operation whose own contract says so normalizes it (transcendental Math results, `range` bounds). An operation that collapses several `==`-equal inputs states which representative survives: a REPEATED NAME binds ONE original contribution — the first callable-bearing one, otherwise the first value-only one (the group-aware NEED-04, `call-by-need.md` § NEED-04), the first in written pattern traversal order (left to right, depth first) among equally rich ones supplying the complete retained value — never a synthesized, canonicalized or leaf-wise merged representative (`P(x, x) = x.string`: `P(1.5, 1.50)` is `'1.5'`, `P(1.50, 1.5)` is `'1.50'`, `P(0, -0)` is `'0'`, `P(-0, 0)` is `'-0'`; with `A = 1.50`, `P(1.5, A)` and `P(A, 1.5)` are both `'1.50'`; C# `Evaluator.BindNeedPatterns`, Lean `bindNeedName` over one `RepeatedGroup`, which keeps the first callable-bearing contribution, else the first value-only one); `distinct` keeps the first occurrence of each class; `contains` is membership by `==`; `order` is stable and keeps every input representative, `orderDesc` its exact reverse (COLL-06's current rule, whose equal-item question Q-28 does not decide); `min`/`max` follow OP-13, signed-zero law included, with same-signed quantum ties unspecified. Permuting all contributions of one repeated name preserves satisfiability (the narrowed Q-28, on its stable domain). On success every group preserves callable identity and channel availability, and a MIXED group — a plain value takes part — the bound VALUE up to `==`; a REFERENCE-ONLY group, whose arguments all name one definition, keeps the first representative but not necessarily its counted VALUE, since two cells of one identity may hold different completed values (`Pick(x, x) = x` / `Probe(p, q) = p + 0, q + 0, Pick(p, q), Pick(q, p)` / `A = if(tick() == 1, Probe(A, A), tick())` / `A` reads `(3, 5, 3, 5)`); among equally rich compatible contributions, the first in written traversal order supplies the retained representation. Failure reporting follows ordinary binding order (NEED-04, PAT-10, Q-27): when different incompatibilities coexist, permutation may change the error category, blamed contribution, payload, message or span, although every permutation still fails. (3) PRESENTATION is host rendering (§ Decimal places: minimum and maximum, source and host): it may round, pad and limit visible places, and it never changes a value, a representation, `.string`, comparison, pattern matching, `distinct`, `contains`, arithmetic, ordering or any effect already produced. `.string` (`1.50.string == 1.5.string` is `false`) is the in-language observer of the QUANTUM and among the several in-language observers of the zero sign — IEEE-sign-sensitive Math results such as `atan2` also see the zero sign (DOT-08, OP-14): `1.50 == 1.5` is `true` while `1.50.string == 1.5.string` is `false`, `0 == -0` is `true` while `0.string == (-0).string` is `false`; it cannot show a NaN sign or a positive quantum exponent (`1e3` and `1000` both read `'1000'`), which only the host API's Decimal128 values expose. N-F is the behavior C# and Lean already had: no production code changed. Pinned by `NumericRepresentativeSelectionTests` (22 representation pairs × four shapes × five origins, families, callbacks, collections and literal patterns, compared representation-exactly on six routes) and the C#-only spec cases `repeated-name-keeps-first-equally-rich-representative` and `decimal-literal-patterns-match-by-value`.

> Owner follow-up (2026-10-07): repeated-name compatibility is symmetric; failure reporting is ordered. With `A = 1.0`, `B = 1.00`, `P(x,x,x) = x`, `P(A,B,2)` reports `TypeMismatch`, while `P(A,2,B)` reports `ArityMismatch`. Both are unsatisfiable. This resolves the independent review's earlier blocker by narrowing its over-strong error-category claim; NEED-04 and PAT-10 evaluation order are unchanged. See the Constitution's owner follow-up and `NumericDisplayIndependentReviewTests.MixedThreeContributionConflicts_ReportTheFirstExecutedContract`.

## Boolean values

BOOLEANS ARE A VALUE KIND, NOT A NUMBER (September 2026). `true` and `false` are first-class scalar values — Lean `Result.bool`, C# `Result.Bool` — written as the reserved literals `true` / `false` (lexer keywords `KeywordTrue`/`KeywordFalse`, parsed to `Expr.BoolLiteral` / `.boolLiteral`; in a clause head they are the literal patterns `Pattern.LitBool` / `.litBool`, so `F(true) = …` and `F(1) = …` are different clauses). Being keywords they are never identifiers: they can be neither declared nor shadowed and never become implicit parameters, and a head such as `true = 1`, `false(x) = x`, or `public true = 1` is the targeted `UnexpectedToken` diagnostic at the literal ("'true' is the reserved Boolean literal, not a name: it cannot be declared as a property, clause, or parameter, and it cannot be shadowed."), reported by `Parser.ReportReservedLiteralDeclaration` after `IsReservedLiteralDeclarationHeadAt` (the reserved-literal arm of `Parser.ClassifyDeclarationStartAt`) recognizes the head under the ordinary declaration-head line rule; the head is skipped so the body parses as an ordinary row. PRODUCERS: every comparison chain (`Expr.Comparison`, `syntax.md` § Operator precedence, comparison chains, and prefix `not`) — its links are the ordering operators `<`, `>`, `<=`, `>=` (numeric scalar operands only; `Evaluator.TryCompareNumeric` is the ONE ordering comparison, shared by the generic link application `Evaluator.ApplyComparison` / Lean `applyComparison` and the planned-loop arm) and the total structural equality `==`/`!=` over every value kind (`true == 1`, `false == 0`, and `true == 'true'` are `false`, never errors; `Result.ValueComparer` tags Booleans with `BoolTag`, so `distinct` and `contains` treat them as ordinary items), and a chain is `true` iff every adjacent link holds (`1 < 2 == true` compares `2` with `true`, which is `false`; a `false` link never stops the chain, an error does) — the logical operators `and`/`or`/`xor`/`not`, and `contains`. CONSUMERS: `if`, `filter`, the `while` continuation flag, and the logical operators read the ONE truth view `Result.asBool?` / `AsBool` — a Boolean value, or a redundant singleton sequence boundary around one (`(true)` is `true`, exactly the normalization `asInt?`/`AsNum` applies to numbers) — and reject everything else. THERE IS NO NUMERIC TRUTHINESS: no `0`/nonzero convention, no first-atom flattening (`Result.atoms`/`ToAtoms`, `truthValue?`/`TruthValue`, and `singleAtomicTruthValue?`/`SingleAtomicTruthValue` no longer exist), so `if(1, a, b)`, `not 1`, `1 and 2`, `filter{x}` over numbers, and a numeric `while` flag are all value-kind errors. A Boolean is never a number and a number is never a Boolean: there is no implicit conversion in either direction, `expectInt`/`ExpectInt` reject a Boolean with `typeMismatch "Expected a number, got a Boolean value"` (so arithmetic through the numeric-scalar operand message, `range`, `take`, `repeat` counts, and the Math natives all reject it, and a Boolean element of a numeric aggregate is the same value-kind `TypeMismatch` — § The evaluation-error taxonomy), the atom views `languageAtoms`/`hostAtoms` omit it (`atoms(true)` is `[]`; `runFlat`/`EvaluateToAtoms` never show a Boolean — hosts read the structured value), and `.string` keeps its numeric-receiver contract. Booleans are NOT ORDERED: `true < false` and `true > 0` are the numeric-scalar operand rejection, and no `false < true` order exists. The logical operators require a Boolean on every side they EVALUATE (`requireBooleanOperand`/`RequireBooleanOperand`: "operator `and` expects Boolean operands, but the left operand was numeric value 1", wrapped in the same binary-operand context as the numeric rejection, at the binary expression's span). `and` AND `or` SHORT-CIRCUIT (November 2026, issue #202; Lean and C# together, no compatibility mode): the left operand is evaluated once and must be a Boolean — its failure, or its value-kind rejection, is the expression's outcome before any right operand work — and when it alone decides the operator (`BinaryOp.leftOperandDecides` / `Evaluator.LeftOperandDecides`: `false and _` is `false`, `true or _` is `true`) the result is that Boolean and the right operand is NOT evaluated: it has no effect (`tick()`, a random draw, a host call), no failure, no suspension and no charge, its value kind is never checked (`false and 1` is `false`), and a parameter or property that only it reads is never demanded. Otherwise the right operand is evaluated once, must be a Boolean (the ordinary right-operand rejection), and is the result. So `x != 0 and 1 / x > 0` is `false` for a zero `x`, never a division by zero. Only EVALUATION skips: both operands are parsed, resolved and validated by the front end exactly as before (parameter inference, forwarding, lifting and every front-end error are unchanged), and precedence and associativity are unchanged. `xor` needs both operands and stays EAGER — both are evaluated left to right, then the left and then the right one is checked — and a comparison chain stays eager too (a `false` link never stops it). The C# routes that choose which operand to evaluate — the expression-spine machine, its `// MIRROR OF` async twin and the planned loop evaluator (`LoopOptimizer.EvalLoopBinaryPlan`) — ask the ONE post-left decision `Evaluator.DecideLogicalAfterLeftOperand` in the transition that receives the left operand, and `Evaluator.ApplyBinaryOperator` judges the left operand by the same rule, so scheduling and application cannot disagree and a skipped operand records no expression-work checkpoint on any strategy (Q-09b accounting stays strategy-neutral); `not` requires a Boolean operand ("operator `not` expects a Boolean operand, but the operand was …"), while `-` rejects a Boolean as a numeric-scalar operand error. Every Boolean-REQUIRED position reports the ONE `typeMismatch` message `booleanRequiredMessage`/`BooleanRequiredMessage` — "`<role>` must be a Boolean value (true or false), but was `<operandDescription>`", roles "if condition", "filter predicate result", "while continuation flag (the step's last output)" — so a number in a predicate position is reported as the value-kind mismatch it is, never as an arity, implicit-parameter, or resolution error; `operandDescription`/`DescribeOperand` names the value kind first ("a Boolean value: true", "numeric value 1", "a sequence value with 2 sequence elements: (true, false)"). The `while` flag is the step's LAST output; a single-output step's one slot is both the next state and the flag, so it must be Boolean too (`Step = x - 1, x > 1` is the shape; `Step = x - 1` alone is rejected). Booleans participate in every structural rule like any other scalar: `true, false` is a two-row output, `(true)` normalizes, `[true, false]` is an exact list, deconstruction and collecting bindings bind them, callbacks and the zero-argument property cache carry them, and DISPLAY is lowercase `true` / `false` everywhere (`ValueTextRenderer.FormatBool`, `Result.boolText`, the neutral spec encoding). The planned-loop evaluator mirrors all of this: its unboxed numeric fast path serves arithmetic and ordering only, the short circuit of `and`/`or` is the shared `DecideLogicalAfterLeftOperand` (a skipped operand's plan is never entered: no checkpoint, temp read, parameter demand or fallback), `not`/`and`/`or`/`xor` and every rejection delegate to the shared `ApplyUnaryOperator`/`ApplyBinaryOperator`/`ApplyComparison` (a planned comparison chain applies each link through the same `ApplyComparison` as the generic spine machine, `LoopOptimizer.EvalLoopComparisonPlan`), a planned `if` and the optimized `while` flag read `PlannedLoopValue.AsBool` and report the same messages as the generic paths, and the sequence-pipeline fusion consumes `EvalFilterPredicateTruth`, which applies the Boolean rule. Pinned by `BooleanValueTests`, `ShortCircuitLogicalOperatorTests` (the short circuit on every route: outcomes, effects, suspension, diagnostics and spans, planned-loop routing and accounting), `EvaluatorOperatorTests`, `EvaluatorIfBuiltinTests`, `EvaluatorLoopTests`, `EvaluatorSequenceCallbackTests`, `LexerTests`, `ParserTests`, the Lean `CoreTests` (`Numerics`, `Conditionals`, `ListValues`, `SequenceCallbackBuiltins`, `ParityGuards`), `KatLangArityLaws` (`asBool_*`, `asInt_bool_none`), and the `LanguageSpecCases`/`SemanticExplorerCases` differential artifacts.

## String values

STRINGS ARE ORDINARY DATA (STR-03, decided 2026-11-11, superseding STR-02; Lean and C# together). A string value (`Result.Str`, Lean `Result.str`) comes from exactly three producers: a string literal (`Expr.StringLiteral`, raw single-quoted text — `syntax.md` § Strings), the `.string` intrinsic on a number, and a host (a host-built AST literal or a host operation's result). It is first-class: bound, passed and returned like any value, compared by `==` / `!=` (exact, ordinal, charged by length — SEC-02-FU), matched by a literal clause pattern, stored in lists and sequences as one item of its own kind, counted as one value (`count('ab')` is 1; a string is never decomposed into characters), displayed without quotes, and passed to and returned from host operations. Arithmetic and ordering are not defined for it: `+` on strings is a `TypeMismatch`.

NOTHING BUILDS TEXT FROM VALUES. String interpolation and double-quoted strings were removed completely (the evaluator's `InterpolatedString` spine case and frame state, `InterpolatedTextBuilder`, `EvaluationBudget.StringConstructionCapacity`, `ValueTextRenderer.AppendCanonicalValue`; Lean `interpolateWith`, `Result.canonicalText`, the `interpolate_*` and `canonical_text_*` laws), and no replacement exists: no concatenation, no formatting builtin, no implicit conversion of a value to text, and no special evaluation rule for a string inside a sequence. A sequence or list that holds strings and numbers holds them as distinct values — it is never a dynamically built string — so a number beside a string is still a number and `MinDecimalPlaces` / `MaxDecimalPlaces` and host numeric formatting still apply to it. Labelling a result is presentation and belongs to the host, which can show each result beside the expression that produced it.

`.string` IS UNCHANGED. It stays the dot-only intrinsic of the dot route (Q-17 S-C; `ownership-and-lookup.md`): a number's canonical culture-invariant text (`ValueTextRenderer.FormatNumberInvariant`, Decimal128 quantum included — `1.50.string` is `1.50`; never `MinDecimalPlaces` / `MaxDecimalPlaces`, a formatter or the current culture), a `TypeMismatch` on every other receiver, a call reference and an argument view included. Lean: `resultToString` (`string_intrinsic_text_of_a_number`; the integer tier's `toString`).

LIMITS (C#-only host policy). Every string a run creates is reserved once through the one reservation (`EvaluationBudget.TryReserveString`, from `Evaluator.MakeStringResult`: string literals, `.string`, the planned loop's string constant) against the per-string ceiling `EvaluationLimits.MaxStringLength` and the cumulative `MaxMaterializedStringChars`; a host operation's returned strings are admitted against the per-string limit before use. Since no source form grows a string, the ceilings are defence in depth for host-returned text and hand-built ASTs; a crossing is the ordinary terminal `StringSizeLimitExceeded` / `StringMaterializationLimitExceeded` (Q-02).

CALL REFERENCES HAVE NO TEXT (CallReference S1). There is no reference display syntax and no text conversion that could reach one: a run result containing a reference is refused at the run boundary, `.string` rejects it as a non-number, and the value renderer reaches a reference only from a diagnostic, which names its kind in words.

Pinned by `EvaluatorStringTests`, `StringAndDisplayLimitsTests`, `UnsupportedDoubleQuotedStringTests`, `CanonicalNumberTextTests`, `CallReferenceS1Tests` / `CallReferenceS2Tests`, CoreTests `Strings`, and the `string-*` spec cases.

## The evaluation-error taxonomy

C# RECORD OPERATIONS ON CONTEXT FRAMES ARE STACK-SAFE (November 2026; C#-only host safety and implementation-only, NO semantic change, no public API change and no Lean counterpart: Lean's `withContext` is an inductive value with derived equality and no host stack). `EvalError.WithContext` is the one error record that holds an error (`Inner`; pinned by reflection over the error and context records), so its synthesized `Equals`, `GetHashCode` and text followed `Inner` once per frame on the caller's stack. A host can construct an arbitrarily deep chain (synthesized equality and hashing overflowed the stack there), and failures from loop steps on the async route can also have more frames than the call-depth limit. The three members are now loops with the synthesized contract. EQUALITY compares each frame's base part (`EqualityContract`, `Span`, the transparent runtime slot) and its structured `ErrorContext` — as a record, never as text — and then the inner errors, in the generated order, stopping at a frame both chains share. The HASH is the compiler's field fold: `Inner` is the LAST field, folded in with coefficient one, so a chain's hash is the sum of its frames' own parts plus the terminal error's hash, with no stack and the value the synthesized members give (a frame's own part also folds the recorded terminal's transparent slot, declared before `Inner`, below, so frame hash VALUES moved once in November 2026; equality did not). The TEXT is the synthesized member list (`Span`, `IsResourceLimit`, `Code`, `ErrorContext`, `Inner`, `Context`) written into one builder. A frame has ONE inner error, so a chain is a list: there are no shared paths to memoize, unlike the AST records. `IsResourceLimit` (formerly recursive) and `Code` classify the terminal error the outermost frame recorded (formerly through one iterative unwrap), and the text reads both once per chain instead of once per frame (it was quadratic). The record printer retains the compiler's entry stack check when a caller-owned context collection re-enters `ToString`. Host-built frames keep their answers: a null inner error or a null context compares and hashes as a null field, and `IsResourceLimit` and the text of a frame around a null inner error still fail with `NullReferenceException`. A frame forged by reflection into its own inner error has no answer: formerly a stack overflow, now a loop that does not end. THE MESSAGE of a chain — `KatLangError.FromEvalError`, the public message projection, and the fragment-by-fragment rendering behind `ToTerminalSafeString` — is the same walk: a tailored message when the error has one of the recognized shapes, otherwise `{context}: {message of the inner error}` for a frame and the variant's own message for any other error. It was written as that recursion (`KatLangError.FormatEvalError`: one native call per frame with no stack guard, the text joined again at every level, so 200,000 host-built frames had about five bytes of a 1 MiB stack each — established from the code, not by running it), and three of its recognizers search every frame of the remaining chain (an assignment-deconstruction, a structural-pattern or a reduce-accumulator frame may sit below ordinary call and property frames), so it was quadratic in the chain as well. It is now ONE loop over one builder: the recognizers in their one fixed order (`TryFormatRecognizedShape`), the per-variant messages in a compiler-exhaustive switch (`FormatUnframedEvalError`). The chain searches are asked ONCE, for the outermost error: each matches a chain exactly when some frame of it has the shape, so one that found nothing in the whole chain finds nothing in any inner part of it; the one-frame recognizers keep their order at every level. Nothing a host can observe moved: every message is the same text, a renderer receives the same fragments in the same order, and a host-built null inner error or null context fails with the exception, and at the frame, it always did. `EvalErrorRecordEqualityTests` (the synthesized contract restated over reflection, on every native route over a corpus that includes the host-built null frames; the inventory; 200,000 frames, alone and above a shared half), `NativeRecordTextTests`, `KatLangErrorChainFormattingTests` (the former recursion kept as the oracle — every recognizer for every frame, then the join — against the loop, with and without a renderer that marks fragment boundaries, over every error kind below every context kind, every pair and triple of frames and seeded chains of up to 16; the null frames; 200,000 frames over an ordinary error, a tailored shape and each chain-searched shape), `AstStructuralDepthProcessTests.DeepHostAsts_NativeRecordEqualityAndHashing_AreStackSafe_InSubprocess` and `DeepHostRecords_NativeTextAndErrorMessages_AreStackSafe_InSubprocess` (the deep chains on a 1 MiB thread in a child process).

A CONTEXT FRAME RECORDS ITS CHAIN'S TERMINAL (2026-11-24; C# implementation only — no semantic, diagnostic, API or equality change and no Lean counterpart: Lean's classifiers are definitions on an inductive value with no cost model; the deep-failure unwinding cost left after the host-termination transport of 2026-11-22, adversarial performance audit of 2026-11-21, measured on `76df3dc4`). A failure leaving a recursion is asked at every level whether it is terminal for the run (`IsTerminalForRun`, before each call, dot-call, property or item frame is attached), whether its missing output is its own (`Evaluator.NestMissingOutput` at every subordinate boundary), and, where a need cell completes with it, whether it is a resource limit or the run's host-termination marker; each answer walked the WHOLE chain grown so far. On the async twin route, where recursion through loop steps charges no depth and continues on fresh stacks, a failure at the bottom of N levels gathers two frames per level, and those walks read about 1.5·F² frames for F frames (an instrumented copy: 96 million at 4,000 levels, against 8,002 frames constructed and about 56,000 frame reads to format the message); 10,000 levels took 3.4 to 5.7 s in Release across runs and shapes where the successful control took about 0.5 s. THE RULE: a frame owns its inner chain and both are immutable, so `WithContext` records the chain's terminal when it is BUILT — `Inner`'s init accessor writes it from the inner frame's own record (or the inner error itself), one read of that record per frame — and `EvalError.UnwrapContext`, the one door of `IsResourceLimit`, `IsHostTermination`, `IsTerminalForRun`, `Code`, `Evaluator.IsOwnMissingOutputError`, `NestMissingOutput`'s test and `NeedCell.IsObservationPending`, returns it. The record cannot go stale: the constructors and `WithInner` (the Q-36 rebuilds) set `Inner` through the accessor, every other copy (`with { Span = … }`, a runtime slot) keeps `Inner` and the record together, and a host-built frame around a null inner error records null, so every classification of it fails as before. It is an equality-transparent runtime slot declared before `Inner` (`EvalErrorRecordEqualityTests` pins the inventory and the synthesized contract), so equality is unchanged and a frame's hash value folds one zero more. Cost: eight bytes per context frame on the failure path; the success path constructs no frame (measured: the successful control's allocation within 0.1 % and its time within run-to-run noise at 1,000 and 10,000 levels). Unchanged: every verdict; the frames, their contexts, spans and order; the terminal and its missing-output provenance (own, nested, kept blame); the retained host exception and cancellation identity; the resource-limit classification; steps, checkpoints and depth counts; and every message — a Release probe's digest of every frame's context and span, the terminal and the message is the baseline's at 1,000 to 10,000 levels for every shape measured. After the change, 10,000 levels take 0.4 to 0.6 s for an ordinary or a missing-output failure, sync-completing or suspending at the bottom (1.2 s when every level genuinely suspends); the evaluation reads no frame of the chain (a missing output's one nesting rebuild reads one) and formatting reads each frame about ten times. Still linear by design: the Q-36 rebuild (`WithContextTerminal`) copies every frame once when it changes a terminal (an own missing output, a spread's own), `IsLiftableError` passes down to the first projection mark (asked of one resolution's outcome, never once per level), and the message is one walk. TEST SEAM `ContextChainReadTestSeam` counts the `WithContext.Inner` reads of one flow and of the flows it starts (awaits, stack hops, host-operation continuations), apart from the frames it constructs; with no observation open a read pays one static load. Pins: `AsyncEvaluation/DeepFailureUnwindScalingTests` (the forced twin at 250, 500, 1,000 and 2,000 levels for ordinary, missing-output, resource-terminal, host-terminal — thrown at once and after a genuine suspension — and successful controls, with sync-completing and genuinely suspending steps: at most one chain read per level, in fact the same constant at every depth; frames, steps and checkpoints on the linear prediction of two shallow synchronous runs; peak and current depth; formatting at most 16 reads per frame; frame-for-frame parity — contexts, spans, terminal, provenance, counters and message — with the generic and planned synchronous routes at safe depths; the public asynchronous engine against the synchronous one), `ContextFrameTerminalRecordTests` (every host construction, copy and Q-36 rebuild records the walked terminal and leaves its source unchanged; classifying 200,000 frames reads none and nesting reads each once; the observation's flow scope), and the same gate in Release in `EvaluationLimitsProcessTests` (the 3,000-level twin failure, the 100,000-frame synthetic chains).

EVALUATION ERRORS NAME THE VIOLATED CONTRACT (Q-06 + Q-27, owner decisions of October 5 2026; Lean and C# changed together; constitution FORMAL-06). An evaluation error names the FIRST semantic contract the executed operation cannot satisfy, in that operation's ordinary evaluation order. The category belongs to the contract, never to the helper that happens to notice the failure, so one contract is reported alike on every route (generic, planned, fused, async twin) and in every spelling (written call, dot call, alias, forwarded parameter):

| The executed operation cannot satisfy | Category (Lean; C#; public `KatLangErrorCode`) | Examples |
| --- | --- | --- |
| supply or output CARDINALITY | `arityMismatch` / `badArity`; `EvalError.ArityMismatch` / `BadArity`; `ArityMismatch` | a call or builtin given the wrong number of arguments; a right-kind structural pattern of the wrong length; a `while` step that emits no slot ("a while step must output at least its continuation flag"); NEED-04's unequal repeated VALUE ("repeated parameter 'x' requires equal arguments"); a zero-parameter callable given an item; a zero-argument VALUE demand of a callable that requires arguments, in every spelling — `Inc`, a named alias `A = Inc`, an inline alias `{ Inc }` (FA-OQ-1, 2026-10-10: formerly the written block's `UnresolvedImplicitParams`). A `map` transform or `reduce` step returning `()` or several rows is NOT a cardinality failure: its result is the ordinary call result (Q-25, 2026-10-10: formerly "map transform must return a single element" / "reduce step must return a single accumulator value") |
| a present VALUE of the wrong KIND | `typeMismatch`; `EvalError.TypeMismatch`; `TypeMismatch` | every non-number operand of unary `-` (`-()`, `-(1, 2)`, `-[1]`, `-'a'`, `-true` share one message, "operator `-` expects a numeric scalar operand, but the operand was …"); a Math argument; a selector (`A['x']`, `A[true]`, `A[[0]]`, `A[(0, 1)]`); a `range` bound; a `repeat`, `take` or `skip` count that is not a number; a non-numeric element of `sum`, `avg`, `min`, `max`, `order` or `orderDesc`; every Boolean position (§ Boolean values); a structural pattern given the other kind; NEED-04's incompatible callable identities |
| CALLABILITY | `notAnAlgorithm`; `EvalError.NotAnAlgorithm`; `NotAnAlgorithm` | `map([1, 2], 7)`; `repeat((1, 2), 1)` (a value for `meta step`: the META refusal); `K(0)` for `K(z) = Apply((Inc, Dec))` (a closed list; at the root a capture's elements are VALUE roles, so `Inc` lifts and the root reports `UnresolvedImplicitParams` instead); `App(7, 1)` for `App(f, x) = f(x)`; `Apply(ReturnF(Inc), 3)` |
| a SELECTION naming no position | `badIndex`; `EvalError.BadIndex`; `BadIndex` | `()[0]`; `[1, 2][5]`; `A[-1]`; `first(())`; `first([])`; `last(())`; `last([])`; `().first` |
| a zero DIVISOR | `divByZero`; `EvalError.DivByZero`; `DivisionByZero` | `1 / 0`; `5 mod (1 - 1)` |
| any other DOMAIN failure of a valid-kind value | `illegalInEval`; `EvalError.IllegalInEval`; `IllegalInEval` | a non-whole count or bound (`take([1, 2, 3], 2.5)`, `range(1, 2.5)`); a negative `repeat` count; an invalid random interval; `0 ^ -1`; an empty `min`, `max` or `avg` |

MISSING CALLABILITY (Q-06, option (a)). A position that INVOKES its argument — a user higher-order parameter called as `f(x)`, a `map` transform, a `filter` predicate, a `reduce` reducer, a `while` or `repeat` step, and an invoking slot reached through a builtin used as a step — reports a supplied argument with no CALLABLE identity as `NotAnAlgorithm`, whatever the consumer. The builtin slots and the loop steps reach it through the ONE invoking-slot projection, Lean `projectInvokingSlot` (with `invokingSlotRole`) / C# `Evaluator.ProjectInvokingSlot` (with `InvokingSlotRoles`): from the callback projection (`expectPreparedSequenceBuiltinAlgorithmSuffixArg` / `ResolvePreparedCallback`, used by `evalFilterCounted`/`evalMapCounted`/`evalReduceCounted` and their C# twins; the fused `filter`→`count` path through `PrepareFilterPredicateArgument`) and from the loop (Lean's `while`/`repeat` arms; C# `ProjectLoopStep` in `EvalNeedLoop`). Its structured description is the slot's ROLE — "map transform", "filter predicate", "reduce reducer", "repeat step", "while step" — which `KatLangError` renders as "The map transform is not callable: the argument supplied for it is a value, not an algorithm. …", positioned at the supplied argument; a user higher-order parameter keeps its `param(name)` description ("Parameter 'f' is not callable here: …"). The projection is CALLABLE projection only (NEED-06): it is made only when an invocation is needed, it never demands the argument's VALUE to find out (a value whose evaluation would fail, draw a random number or never terminate is not evaluated by it: `map([1, 2], 1 / 0)` is `NotAnAlgorithm`, never `DivisionByZero`), and an unused invoking slot — an empty `map`/`filter`/`reduce`, a zero-iteration `repeat` — is never projected or validated (CALL-03, LOOP-05). A slot that HAS callable identity is that callable and keeps every ordinary binder and dispatch verdict when invoked: a zero-parameter callable — a property, a block, a structural member, `Math.Pi` — given an item is `ArityMismatch`, exactly like any call that supplies more arguments than its callee accepts. There is no value-to-thunk fallback: the former hand-written `ArityMismatch(0, k)` of the builtin slots and the loop-state `ArityMismatch` for a value step (X-30) are gone, while the loop-state `ArityMismatch` still describes a real zero-parameter step.

VALUE KIND, CARDINALITY AND DOMAIN (Q-27, policy P2). The numeric conversion `expectInt` / `ExpectInt` reports every present non-number — a string, a Boolean, a sequence (`()` included), a list — as `TypeMismatch` ("Expected a number, got …"), so selectors, `range` bounds, `repeat` counts and Math arguments of the wrong kind are kind errors. Unary `-` reports every non-numeric operand, strings included, with the one numeric-operand message through `Evaluator.ApplyUnaryOperator` / Lean `evalUnaryCounted` (its span is the unary expression's, `UnaryOperandSpanTests`), as `not` already did for its Boolean operand and the binary operators for theirs (SYN-01). The collection-element check of the numeric aggregates (`collectSingleAtomicNumbers` / `CollectSingleAtomicNumbers`) reports a non-numeric element as `TypeMismatch` naming the builtin, the item index and the element's kind, and the whole-number control of `take`/`skip` (`prepareSequenceBuiltinSuffixArgItem` / `PrepareSequenceBuiltinSuffixArg`) reports a count that is not a number as `TypeMismatch` and a number that is not whole as `IllegalInEval` (the integer core has none), exactly as the `range`, `repeat` and `randomInt` bounds report theirs. The empty policy is builtin METADATA, decided by what the builtin IS (`SequenceBuiltinEmptyPolicy`, applied by `applySequenceBuiltinEmptyPolicy` / `ApplySequenceBuiltinEmptyPolicy`): `first` and `last` SELECT, so an empty collection names no position — `RequireSelectablePosition`, `BadIndex` under the context "first selects from an empty collection, which has no position to select", the outcome of `()[0]` — which makes `first(A) ≡ A[0]` and `last(A) ≡ A[count(A) - 1]` TOTAL (laws `first_is_select_zero_total`, `last_is_select_last_total`, `first_empty_is_badIndex`, `last_empty_is_badIndex`); `min`, `max` and `avg` AGGREGATE, so an empty collection is outside their domain — `RequireAnyItem`, `IllegalInEval` "min requires a non-empty collection" (law `empty_policy_follows_what_the_builtin_is`). Genuine cardinality stays `ArityMismatch` everywhere, with two contexts that name the contract: `whileStepWithoutFlagContext` / `WhileStepWithoutFlagContext` and NEED-04's `repeatedParameterContext` / `RepeatedParameterContext` (the categories are unchanged; NEED-04's value-versus-identity split is a frozen binder rule, not an exception to the taxonomy). PAYLOAD HYGIENE: a builtin arity error carries the builtin's real minimum supplied count — `if` 3, `while` 1, `repeat` 2, `range` 2, `atoms` 1, a collection builtin 1 plus its controls — beside its descriptive context (Lean `builtinMinimumArity` in `builtinArityError`; C# `WrongBuiltinArity` with `ArityFacts.MinTopLevelArgumentCount`), never the former placeholder `0`.

NO PUBLIC SURFACE CHANGE. No public error code was added, removed, renamed or renumbered: the decision moves failing programs between existing `KatLangErrorCode` members and `EvalError` variants, whose XML documentation states the contract each one names, and the public API baseline is unchanged. No program that evaluated before changes its value, count, demand or effects (the production check: 539 probe cells on six routes, 205 category changes, all from `ArityMismatch`; the Q-06 + Q-27 record is in the git history of `src/KatLang/SEMANTIC-ALIGNMENT.md`).

Pins: `ErrorTaxonomyPolicyTests` (six routes: the Q-06 invoking positions through alias, qualified, opened, dot and forwarding spellings — one to three levels, collectors, a builtin used as a step — with role, span and message; the zero-parameter contrasts; never-projected unused slots; precedence; the kind and domain matrices; the total selection law over 18 targets; genuine cardinality including the clause-family witness; the builtin arity payload; NEED-04; the public projection), `UnaryOperandSpanTests`, `SelectionValueBoundaryTests.SelectionFromAnEmptyCollection_FailsInEveryForm`, `EvaluatorIndexingTests.Eval_Index_NonNumericStructuredSelector_FailsWithTypeMismatch`, the CLI's `Eval_ValueStep_IsNotCallable` and `Eval_ErrorTaxonomy_NamesTheViolatedContract`, Lean `CoreTests/ErrorTaxonomy.lean` and the guard `builtinEmptyPolicyFailuresAreNotArityErrors` in `CoreTests/ParityGuards.lean`, the laws above with `invoking_slot_without_callable_is_notAnAlgorithm` / `invoking_slot_with_callable_is_that_callable`, and the spec cases `invoking-slot-without-callable-is-not-an-algorithm`, `zero-parameter-callable-in-invoking-slot-is-arity`, `kind-errors-are-type-mismatch`, `first-last-empty-is-bad-index` and `aggregate-empty-is-domain-error`.

## Seeded randomness
