KatLang's random operations — `Math.Random` / `random` and `Math.RandomInt` / `randomInt` — draw from ONE run-scoped random stream, and a host may seed it: `RunOptions.RandomSeed` (engine entry points and every convenience over them), the `Evaluator.Run` / `RunAsync` / `RunFlat` / `RunFlatAsync` overloads that take a `long? randomSeed`, and the CLI's `--random-seed <integer>` for `run` and `eval` (`check` refuses it because it does not evaluate). `null` keeps evaluation UNSEEDED and nondeterministic; every `long` is a valid seed and every distinct value names a distinct stream (the seed's bit pattern is the initial state, never folded, hashed, or truncated). This is host/runtime evaluation configuration with NO Lean counterpart: Lean models neither the Math runtime surface nor native random execution, and seeding changes where the raw words come from, never which calls execute — that is decided by the existing Lean-modeled evaluation rules (supply formation evaluates a call's explicit spreads first, then its supplied arguments run in the order they are first demanded and an undemanded argument never draws — Model C, `call-by-need.md` NEED-03/07; at-most-once written arguments for every callee shape, patterned callees included — a parameter read never draws again, even after its demanded slot failed (sequences-lists-and-calls.md § At-most-once argument value evaluation) — lazy `if` branches, the zero-argument property cache with explicit `A()` re-evaluation, callbacks in sequence order, output rows before the engine's reads of the root's `MinDecimalPlaces` and then `MaxDecimalPlaces` source settings, which happen after successful output whatever the host settings are). A seed reproduces a stream, it never memoizes: removing an earlier random call generally changes every later value (bounded rejection consumes a variable number of raw words).

The stream is run-scoped MUTABLE state on the run's `EvaluationBudget` (`EvaluationBudget.RandomSource`), constructed EAGERLY at budget creation by `RandomSourceFactory.Create(seed, entropyProvider)` — a supplied seed never consults entropy; an unseeded run reads 8 bytes from `Random.Shared.NextBytes` exactly once (the full 64-bit state domain; `NextInt64()` is deliberately not used) and never touches `Random.Shared` again. Because every copied `EvalCtx` carries the budget by reference, nested calls, callbacks, loop frames, property evaluations, the engine's `MinDecimalPlaces` / `MaxDecimalPlaces` source-setting reads, and async suspension/resumption all share the one stream and none can reset or duplicate it; `ApplyMathNative(name, args, randomSource)` receives it EXPLICITLY from `ctx.Budget` in both `ApplyMathNativeCall` (the read-then-apply step of `EvalNativeCall`) and its `MIRROR OF` async twin, with no optional or static fallback. The stream must never live on `RunOptions`, `EvaluationLimits`, or `HostOperations` (immutable configuration), in a static, thread-local, or `AsyncLocal` slot, or in a cache keyed by seed: reusing one options object for sequential runs replays the stream from its beginning, concurrent runs sharing it get independent stream instances from the same seed, and no lock exists on the source because evaluator access within one run is sequential even when an async continuation resumes on another thread. `EvalCtx.Empty` and every direct `EvaluationBudget.Create` caller that supplies no seed remain unseeded.

The production generator is a KatLang-owned SplitMix64 (`SplitMix64RandomSource`, sealed) with the exact published constants, advance-before-mix order, `hi`-then-`lo` 128-bit composition, and top-incomplete-cycle rejection followed by modulo for bounded draws (a bound of 1 still consumes one word; an invalid bound throws before consuming any); the samplers `SampleRandomUnitFraction` (high 17-digit component, then low, on the exact 10^34 lattice) and `SampleUniformInteger` (exact `Int128` span, `2^128 mod span` rejection) are unchanged by seeding and validate before every draw, so an invalid call consumes nothing. Sync/async routing, optimizer strategies (planned loops with generic random rows, fused `filter`/`count` with a random predicate), and unrelated `EvaluationLimits` settings that leave the program's control flow unchanged must all preserve draw count and order; optimizers are never disabled because a seed is present. The exact stream is reproducible for a given KatLang version across platforms, is NOT promised across versions (a deliberate generator, sampler, or evaluation-semantics change is release-noted), and is not cryptographically secure. The full internal contract, with the seed → state map, the constants, the bounded-rejection algorithm, unseeded initialization, and the version-stability policy, is `docs/design/seeded-randomness-2026-09.md`. `Parser.Parse` / `ParseAsync` ignore the seed; the canonical `LanguageSpec` and `SemanticExplorer` corpora carry no random case. Pinned by `RandomSourceTests` (published SplitMix64 vectors plus an independent BigInteger derivation and the test-side `ReferenceRandomOracle`), `RandomFractionSamplingTests`, `RandomIntSamplingTests`, `SeededRandomnessTests`, `SeededEvaluationOrderTests`, `SeededOptimizerParityTests`, `SeededAsyncEvaluationTests`, `SeededConcurrencyTests`, the `SeededStream` scenario of `ConcurrencyDifferentialTests`, and `CliApplicationTests`.

## The `tick()` diagnostic counter (October 31 2026; Lean and C# together)

`tick()` is a native, zero-argument builtin for OBSERVING evaluation: whether a computation executes, is skipped, or reuses a value already computed. It is an ordinary prelude binding (`BuiltinId.@tick`, Lean `Builtin.tickBuiltin`; signature `tick()`, fixed arity 0, no dot parameters) and adds no syntax, keyword, AST node, result kind or effect framework.

- **Counter.** Each run owns ONE counter that starts at zero: C# `EvaluationBudget`'s tick counter (the run-scoped object every copied `EvalCtx` shares, like the random stream), Lean `EvalState.tickCounter` (zero in `EvalState.empty`). Every actual execution of the primitive advances it by exactly one and returns the NEW value — `1`, `2`, `3`, … — as one ordinary number (Decimal128 in C#, exact integer conversion; emitted count 1). Nested calls, callbacks, aliases, META replay and every evaluation context of the run share it; a separately started run — another engine or evaluator call, concurrent or started reentrantly by host code — has its own. It is reset only by starting a new run: there is no reset, read, naming, timestamp or configuration API. It is independent of steps, depth, item and string budgets, property-cache statistics, time and the random stream.
- **Execution point.** The increment happens only when dispatch reaches the primitive — the `.tickBuiltin` arm of `applyBuiltinCounted` (`runTick`; C# `ExecuteTick` in both `ApplyBuiltinCountedResolved` twins) — after the route's ordinary admission, arity, step, depth, cancellation and terminal-state checks. Nothing is rolled back when a surrounding computation later fails. Parsing, elaboration, static structure resolution, callable identity inspection, editor analysis and optimization never execute it, and the optimizers neither fold, merge, hoist, remove nor duplicate a demanded tick: planned loops and the fused filter→count pipeline reach it through the same dispatch and charge identically. C# concurrent demands of one run, if any, still return distinct values (compare-and-set), but no new ordering guarantee is introduced for them.
- **Exhaustion.** The counter is bounded and checked: the largest value is 2^63 − 1 = 9223372036854775807 (`EvaluationBudget.MaxTickCount`, Lean `tickCounterLimit`), returned exactly. At the limit, `tick()` is the ordinary domain failure `IllegalInEval` (`TickExhaustedMessage` / `tickExhaustedMessage`) and the counter does not move — it never wraps, rounds or saturates into a duplicate value. It is not a resource limit (`IsResourceLimit` is false; nothing configures it). Tests reach the boundary through the internal `TickCounterTestSeam`, never a public API.
- **Invocation.** `tick()` is the only executing form and accepts exactly zero effective supplied arguments: `tick(1 / 0)` is the ordinary `ArityMismatch(0, 1)` before any argument is demanded, an explicit spread is still formed first (`tick(trace(5)*)` traces, then rejects), and an extension call supplies its receiver (`5.tick` is `tick(5)`). Bare `tick` is the builtin's CALLABLE identity wherever builtins can be aliased, passed or projected (`T = tick`, `Apply(tick)`); obtaining or transporting it executes nothing. A bare VALUE demand of a zero-arity builtin is refused by `EvalBuiltinValueCounted` / `evalBuiltinValueCounted` as INVOCATION REQUIRED — `IllegalInEval` "`tick` is a builtin operation that runs only when it is invoked: write `tick()`." (`BuiltinInvocationRequiredMessage` / `builtinInvocationRequiredMessage`) — never the misleading "expected 0, received 0" arity report; every other builtin keeps its own empty-call arity error. A bare `tick` is never lifted (its signature requires nothing), so the refusal is a runtime demand in every value position, including through an alias.
- **Composition.** The primitive composes with the existing CALL, VALUE and NeedCell machinery and adds no cache or bypass of its own: `A = tick()` is an ordinary property (declaring it ticks nothing; `A + A` is 2; `A() + A()` is 3; `A(), A, A(), A` is `(1, 1, 2, 1)` under CACHE-PUB-1; `A, A(), A` is `(1, 2, 1)`), one supplied computation demanded twice runs once (`Twice(tick())`), an unused argument and an unselected `if` branch never run, aliases and carriers keep their targets' invocation and publication behavior (OD-E), and a META `op.Invoke()` is a fresh execution of the captured call. A user binding named `tick` shadows the prelude completely.
- **Hosts.** `tick` is a reserved prelude name: `HostOperation.Create("tick", …)` is refused like every builtin name. This is a compatibility change for embedding hosts that registered an operation of that name; rename it (the test suite's injected counter is `tock`).

Lean: `KatLangArityLaws/Tick.lean` (`runTick_advances`, `runTick_exhausted`, `tick_value_demand_is_refused_without_effect`, `apply_tick_without_arguments_runs_the_primitive`, `apply_tick_with_arguments_is_the_arity_rejection`), `KatLangArityLaws/TickGuards.lean` (whole programs, final counter included), the `tick_*` / `builtin_minimum_is_positive_except_tick` laws in `KatLangArityLaws.lean`, `CoreTests/ParityGuards.lean`. These are serial Lean facts; C# routes, async suspension, cancellation, single-flight and concurrent runs are C# evidence. C#: `TickBuiltinTests` (six routes), the `tick-*` `LanguageSpecCorpus` cases.

## Decimal places: minimum and maximum, source and host

A RUN HAS TWO INDEPENDENT PRESENTATION SETTINGS, AND EACH COMPOSES ACROSS ITS TWO ORIGINS BY THE MINIMUM (Q-10, decided D-F on 2026-10-07 for the one former setting `DisplayDecimals`; extended to two settings by the owner decision of 2026-11-12, which replaces `DisplayDecimals` outright — issue #118's feature; C#-only host presentation policy, no Lean counterpart — Lean models no rendering). The settings are `MinDecimalPlaces`, the fewest digits shown after the decimal point, and `MaxDecimalPlaces`, the most. Each is one integer from `0` through `99`, and each can come from two origins:

- the SOURCE setting — the program root's OWN declared property of that name (`Evaluator.LookupPropBinding` over the root's properties; a nested, opened, inline-opened, member, module, module-root or parameter `MinDecimalPlaces` / `MaxDecimalPlaces` is an ordinary binding and never a setting);
- the HOST setting — `RunOptions.MinDecimalPlaces` / `RunOptions.MaxDecimalPlaces`, the CLI's `--min-decimal-places <integer>` / `--max-decimal-places <integer>` (valid for `run` and `eval`, refused by `check`, which does not evaluate), or a host application's display setting mapped onto them.

Both are presentation only. They never change a value, its Decimal128 representation, a comparison, `.string`, or any intermediate result.

**Composition.** Each setting composes independently with its counterpart of the same name: an absent one imposes nothing; one present is the effective value; both present give their MINIMUM; neither leaves that setting absent (`null`).

| Source | Host | Effective |
|---|---|---|
| absent | absent | absent |
| `s` | absent | `s` |
| absent | `h` | `h` |
| `s` | `h` | `min(s, h)` |

The same table applies to `MinDecimalPlaces` and to `MaxDecimalPlaces`, through the ONE composition helper `KatLangEngine.CombineDecimalPlaces(source?, host?)`. Neither origin overrides the other — there is no source-over-host or host-over-source precedence: source max `7` with host max `5` shows at most 5 places, source `5` with host `7` shows at most 5, and a shared `MaxDecimalPlaces = 7` program shows up to 7 places to a recipient without a host setting and to one whose maximum is `10`. Source min 2 and max 4 with host min 1 and max 3 is effective min 1, max 3; source min 2 alone with a host max of 4 is effective min 2, max 4. NEITHER SETTING SUPPLIES A VALUE FOR THE OTHER (owner decision 2026-11-12): a setting no origin sets stays absent, in the engine, in `RunOptions` and in the CLI — with only a minimum there is no maximum, so nothing is rounded (`MinDecimalPlaces = 2` shows `1.234567` as `1.234567`), and with only a maximum nothing is padded (`MaxDecimalPlaces = 4` shows `10` as `10`). A fixed number of places is written by setting both to the same value. The result's internal `DisplayOptions` stores the FOUR inputs (source and host, minimum and maximum) and derives the effective pair on every read (`MinDecimalPlaces`, `MaxDecimalPlaces`), never storing it, so every rendering surface reads the same pair and replacing the host settings (`RunResult.WithHostDecimalPlaces`, below) leaves no stale composition. D-F superseded the former D-H precedence (a declared property decided, the host value was only its fallback) and the investigation's recommended D-O (no source hook); the decision lineage is recorded in the Constitution (Q-10).

**Consistency.** An effective minimum above the effective maximum is an error. It is never repaired by clamping, swapping, or dropping a value. When each origin supplies a complete, self-consistent pair the composed pair is always consistent (a minimum of two lower bounds never exceeds the minimum of the upper bounds above them), so a conflict comes only from one origin's own pair being inconsistent or from PARTIAL settings across origins. Where each case is reported:

| Case | Reported as |
|---|---|
| CLI flags inconsistent with each other (`--min-decimal-places 5 --max-decimal-places 3`) | CLI usage error at command-line parsing, before the source is read (for `check`, the refusal of the option comes first) |
| Host pair inconsistent in `RunOptions` | `ArgumentException` (parameter `options`) from `KatLangEngine.Run` / `RunAsync` before parsing or evaluation — through the returned task on the async entry points — never a source diagnostic |
| Source pair inconsistent with itself | `EvalFailure`, `IllegalInEval`, whatever the host settings are (a host setting never excuses an invalid source configuration, even one that would make the effective pair consistent) |
| Effective pair inconsistent across origins | `EvalFailure`, `IllegalInEval` |

The two `EvalFailure` rows are ONE check, `KatLangEngine.DecimalPlacesConflict`, applied first to the program's own pair and then to the effective pair. Its message names both values and where each came from — `MinDecimalPlaces must not exceed MaxDecimalPlaces: the minimum is 4 (from the program) and the maximum is 2 (from the host).` — and it is positioned at the declaration of the source property that supplies a conflicting value: `MinDecimalPlaces` when the minimum is the program's, otherwise `MaxDecimalPlaces`. A value both origins supply equally is attributed to the program (no run can observe that tie: with each origin's own pair consistent, a tied value is never one side of a conflict). `RunOptions` is immutable with `init` accessors, and an init accessor sees one property at a time, so the two cannot validate each other at initialization without an initialization order — or a replacement of both values, one accessor after the other — passing through an inconsistent intermediate pair. Each value's own RANGE check therefore stays in its init accessor (`ArgumentOutOfRangeException`, never a clamp, so an out-of-range host setting never reaches an entry point and never becomes a source diagnostic), and the PAIR is checked where a run begins (`KatLangEngine.RequireConsistentHostDecimalPlaces`, the first thing `Run` and `RunAsync` do after the null check; `RunOptions.ThrowIfHostDecimalPlacesConflict` is shared with `WithHostDecimalPlaces`). `Parser.Parse` / `ParseAsync` ignore both settings and so never judge the pair.

**The engine's read of the source settings.** They are an ENGINE-LEVEL host presentation convention, not core evaluator semantics, and ordinary properties in every other respect: resolution, inference, caching and evaluation are unchanged, and a program may read them like any other name. `KatLangEngine` evaluates them — raw `Evaluator.Run*` reads neither, so a malformed, failing or inconsistent root setting can fail an engine run that the raw evaluator completes; that boundary is intentional (X-35). The engine's order is fixed: (1) evaluate the program output; (2) if it failed, stop with that failure — neither setting is read and neither masks it; (3) read `MinDecimalPlaces` and then `MaxDecimalPlaces`, each — when the root declares it — with the ordinary cached zero-argument VALUE read (`EvalTopLevelZeroArgPropertyCounted` and its `MIRROR OF` async twin, through `RunCountedWithTopLevelProperties` / `RunCountedWithTopLevelPropertiesAsync`, which take the ordered name list and return one value per name) in the SAME run — the same `EvalCtx`, step budget, zero-argument property cache, random stream, host operations and cancellation token; if the first read fails, the second is not attempted and the run fails with that error; (4) validate each source value (one numeric value, integral, non-negative, at most `RunOptions.DecimalPlacesLimit` — negative, fractional, above-99, non-numeric and multi-value results are each rejected, never clamped, with `IllegalInEval` "MinDecimalPlaces must be …" / "MaxDecimalPlaces must be …" at that property's declaration span), the minimum first; (5) check the source pair; (6) compose each with the host setting; (7) check the effective pair; (8) construct the run's `DisplayOptions`; (9) render if and when the host asks. The reads happen WHATEVER the host settings are: a host setting never skips one, never excuses an invalid or failing value (the run fails with the property's own diagnostic — the validation error, the property's evaluation error, its arity rejection or `NoMatchingBranch`), and never changes its effects, draws, step charges, cancellation or cache reuse. Because they are evaluation, the reads can trace, draw from the run's stream after the output's draws (the minimum's draw before the maximum's, whatever order the program declares them in), spend steps, be cancelled, or fail; only the final rendering step is semantically inert. Each is the ordinary cached read: when the output already read the property, the engine reuses that entry (one evaluation); an explicit `MaxDecimalPlaces()` in the output is a fresh call that never reads the entry, but since CACHE-PUB-1 (2026-10-22) one that finds the entry empty establishes it, so the engine's read reuses that call's value as the source setting; a call after a cached read stays fresh and changes nothing. The three source states of EACH property stay structurally distinct and no falsy or default-value shortcut may collapse them: the probe returns `null` exactly when the root declares no such property, without touching the property cache; a declared property's evaluation failure is an ordinary evaluation error; and a declared `0` — like a host setting of `0` — is a real setting, never absence.

**The host settings** are display-only HOST configuration with no language presence. `null` means NO host setting — never zero places, never canonicalization, never a reason to ignore the source setting, and never a value derived from the other setting. They are never injected as source text or as AST properties, so name resolution, ownership, shadowing, collisions, spans, diagnostics, the semantic model, and completion see the program exactly as written (a program that reads `MinDecimalPlaces` without declaring it still has an implicit parameter). They are consumed only AFTER evaluation, so they cost no evaluation step, depth, or materialization, draw no random word, invoke no host operation, add no cancellation checkpoint, and create no cache entry; configuring them changes no `Result` value, Decimal128 representation, emitted count, output row, host atom, or evaluation error — only rendered numeric text, or the one consistency failure above. The accepted range `0` through `99` has ONE owner, the public `RunOptions.DecimalPlacesLimit` — a VALIDITY bound that counts decimal PLACES (not significant digits), deliberately not a `MaxSupported*` clamping ceiling — read by the source validation, by both init accessors and `WithHostDecimalPlaces` (`RunOptions.ThrowIfDecimalPlacesOutOfRange`), and by the CLI, which sees only KatLang's public surface and reports an out-of-range or malformed value as a usage error through the value rule it shares with `--random-seed` (`TryTakeValue`, `IntegerValueStyle`; each option at most once, in any position, alone or together, mapped onto the `RunOptions` property of the same meaning and never defaulted from the other). A host may also re-apply the host settings to a completed result without re-running: `RunResult.WithHostDecimalPlaces(int? min, int? max)` REPLACES both host settings (validated like the `RunOptions` pair; `null` removes one) and returns what a run made with them would have returned — for a `Success`, a `Success` that shares the run's root, value, atoms and output rows and keeps its source settings and display-length bound, or the consistency `EvalFailure` above (carrying the run's root and property observations) when the new effective pair conflicts; every other result variant — the failure of an invalid root setting included — is returned unchanged, and nothing is evaluated. Because a conflict yields a failure that later calls return unchanged, hosts re-apply from the original `Success`.

**THE DISPLAY RULE HAS ONE IMPLEMENTATION.** Every rendering surface reads the result's one `DisplayOptions` — `RunResult.ToDisplayString` / `RenderDisplay`, the `exact` / `readable` / `concise` formatters, custom `OutputFormatter`s through `BoundedOutputWriter.AppendAtom`, and the structured-layout measurer — through the ONE `ValueTextRenderer.FormatAtom` (a canonical number reaches a sink through `ValueTextRenderer.AppendAtom` / `AtomLength`, which write and measure the same fixed-point format from a stack buffer: identical text, no intermediate string). With `min` and `max` the effective settings:

1. NEITHER IS PRESENT: the canonical display, unchanged and reached without entering the decimal-places code (`DisplayOptions.HasDecimalPlaces`) — the value's stored digits (`2.5000` prints `2.5000`). There is no default rounding.
2. THE VALUE IS NOT FINITE: its canonical spelling (`NaN`, `Infinity`, `-Infinity`), always.
3. AT LEAST ONE IS PRESENT (`FormatDecimalPlaces`): (a) if `max` is present, round to `max` places with midpoints away from zero (`Decimal128.Round(value, max, MidpointRounding.AwayFromZero)`, applied exactly when the value stores more than `max` fractional digits; no new rounding policy); (b) write the result positionally with the fewest fractional digits that represent it exactly, so trailing fractional zeros are removed even when they were stored; (c) pad with zeros up to `min` places (none when `min` is absent); (d) write no decimal point when there are no fractional digits; (e) keep the sign, including for a zero and for a value that rounds to zero.

So once any setting is present the text depends on the numeric VALUE only, not on its stored quantum. Intended consequences: an explicit `MinDecimalPlaces = 0` or `--min-decimal-places 0` is not the same as no setting (it removes stored trailing zeros); `MaxDecimalPlaces = 99` is not the same as no maximum, for the same reason; and integers are padded — the former exemption of whole numbers carrying an integral quantum is gone.

| Value | min 2 | max 4 | min 2, max 4 | min 2, max 2 | min 0 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `10` | `10.00` | `10` | `10.00` | `10.00` | `10` |
| `10.00` | `10.00` | `10` | `10.00` | `10.00` | `10` |
| `2.5` | `2.50` | `2.5` | `2.50` | `2.50` | `2.5` |
| `2.5000` | `2.50` | `2.5` | `2.50` | `2.50` | `2.5` |
| `1.234` | `1.234` | `1.234` | `1.234` | `1.23` | `1.234` |
| `1.234567` | `1.234567` | `1.2346` | `1.2346` | `1.23` | `1.234567` |
| `0` | `0.00` | `0` | `0.00` | `0.00` | `0` |
| `0.125` | `0.125` | `0.125` | `0.125` | `0.13` | `0.125` |
| `9.9996` | `9.9996` | `9.9996` | `9.9996` | `10.00` | `9.9996` |
| `-0.004` | `-0.004` | `-0.004` | `-0.004` | `-0.00` | `-0.004` |
| `-0` | `-0.00` | `-0` | `-0.00` | `-0.00` | `-0` |

RENDERING IS INERT. It never changes a value, its representation, `.string` (which observes the representation and never any display setting), a comparison, a pattern, `distinct`, `contains`, arithmetic, ordering, `round` or any other numeric operation, the returned `Result`/`Atoms`/output rows, or any effect already produced; Booleans and strings are untouched, and numbers nested in printed sequences and lists are formatted like top-level ones. Output is culture-invariant, exact (one Decimal128 rounding and digit-level text edits of the canonical positional spelling; no binary floating point), and subject to the existing display-length and output-size limits. No setting lives in static, thread-local, `AsyncLocal`, or other process-wide state; `RunOptions` is immutable, a declared property never writes back into it, and runs cannot leak configuration into one another.

`DisplayDecimals` AND `--display-decimals` ARE GONE, with no compatibility path, deprecation period or migration diagnostic (KatLang had no external users). At the root, `DisplayDecimals` is an ordinary property name like any other — no display meaning, no diagnostic, no reserved-name mechanism — and `--display-decimals` is rejected by the CLI's unknown-option path with no special hint. `RunOptions.DefaultDisplayDecimals`, `RunOptions.MaxDisplayDecimals` and `RunResult.WithHostDisplayDecimals` were removed without `[Obsolete]` shims.

Pinned by `Hosting/DecimalPlacesCompositionTests` (for BOTH settings: the composition table and the whole-domain law of `CombineDecimalPlaces`; independence of the two settings; "off" is no setting and an explicit `0` is a real one, per setting and origin; source validation with and without host settings; effects, cache reuse, the explicit fresh call, seeded draws, steps, cancellation; the read order and failure precedence of the two reads on three engine routes; root-own scope including loaded modules; the raw-evaluator boundary; `.string`, value and representation isolation; later calculations and comparisons; repeated and concurrent runs), `Hosting/DecimalPlacesFormattingTests` (the table above through the renderer and the engine from each origin; canonical display byte-identical without a setting; rounding ties, carries, values that round to zero, both zero signs, extreme magnitudes, 99 places, non-finite values; strings, Booleans and `.string`; the structured-layout measurer), `Hosting/DecimalPlacesConsistencyTests` (each `EvalFailure` and host row with kind, message and position; pair validation that never throws for a valid final state; two complete consistent pairs never conflict; tie attribution; the removed names), `Hosting/HostDecimalPlacesTests` (host-setting configuration and neutrality, rendering equivalence, sync/async parity, every formatter, the LanguageSpec corpus differential), `Hosting/DecimalPlacesHostileReviewTests`, `Hosting/HostDecimalPlacesReapplicationTests` (`RunResult.WithHostDecimalPlaces`), `ZeroArgPropertyResultCacheTests`, `Randomness/SeededEvaluationOrderTests`, the `DisplayDefault` scenario of `ConcurrencyDifferentialTests`, `RootPropertyObservations/PropertyObservationBehaviorTests`, `HostApiContractTests` / `HostilePublicApiTests` / `PublicFormatterExtensionTests`, the C#-only `max-decimal-places-rounds-ties-away-from-zero`, `min-decimal-places-pads-and-never-rounds`, `equal-min-and-max-decimal-places-fix-the-places` and `decimal-places-show-the-value-not-the-stored-digits` spec cases, and the CLI's `CliApplicationTests` / `DecimalPlacesCliReviewTests`.

## Diagnostic transparency of synthetic frames

A user-facing diagnostic describes the KatLang program the programmer WROTE. The generic evaluation-context enrichment — the frames that read `while evaluating call to F`, `while evaluating property P` — names user-semantic constructs only; a frame the FRONT END synthesized to lower a surface form is DIAGNOSTICALLY TRANSPARENT and contributes no frame at all (September 2026, F3). The transparency test is the parser's STRUCTURAL provenance mark, never the spelling of a name: `Algorithm.IsSyntheticDeconstructionFrame` is the ONE predicate, reading `Algorithm.User.IsSyntheticDeconstructionPlumbing` (the hoisted `$deconstruct$N` right-hand-side source, `IsAssignmentDeconstructionSource`, and each target's inline projection helper, `AssignmentDeconstructionTarget`).

Assignment deconstruction is the one surface form that is lowered this way: `x, *y, z = RHS` becomes a hoisted zero-parameter source property plus one anonymous inline helper per target, each helper applied to that source (`Parser.AddDeconstructionProperties`). Both are invisible to the programmer, so neither may be named. Before F3 both were, and the leak was not rare — the helper's frame was attached to EVERY failing deconstruction: `x, y = 1/0, 2` reported `while evaluating call to {...}: Division by zero`, `x, y = { }` reported `while evaluating call to {...}: Property '$deconstruct$0' has no defined output.`, and a deconstruction whose right-hand side deconstructed again stacked two `{...}` frames.

The rule is suppression of the synthetic frame, never of context in general. Genuine user-written frames keep their identity, their payloads, and their outermost-to-innermost ORDER: `Bad(n) = n / 0` with `x, y = Bad(1)` still reports `while evaluating call to Bad: Division by zero`, and `Outer` calling `Inner` through a deconstruction still reports both, in that order, with only the helper's frame gone from between them. A deconstruction's OWN binding failure keeps its assignment-focused wording (`DeconstructionBindingContext`, "Assignment pattern `x, y` expects 2 values …"), which is the same principle applied to the shape error rather than to a frame.

Implementation map — two attachment points, both already the single place their frame is built, so sync, async, and planned execution share one decision:
- Call frames: `Evaluator.CallDiagnosticName.FromExpression` marks a callee `IsDiagnosticallyTransparent` when it is an `Expr.AlgorithmExpr` over a synthetic frame, and `WithCallCtx` / `CalleeResolutionFailure` then attach nothing. `EvalCallCountedExpr`, its async twin `EvalCallCountedExprAsync`, and the optimizer's shared `WithPlannedCallBoundary` all derive their name through it.
- Property frames: `Evaluator.WithPropertyContextOnMissingOutput` takes the RESOLVED algorithm and attaches no property frame for a synthetic source, so an output-less right-hand side stays the written expression's own failure at the written expression's own span. A builtin argument slot passes `null` (a written argument is never deconstruction plumbing).

Diagnostics only. Error kinds, structured payloads, evaluation values, counts, and source spans are unchanged — a reported span is exactly the span reported before F3 — and the suppression removes only a `WithContext` layer, so classification that resolves through wrappers (`EvalError.IsResourceLimit`, `Code`, the terminal-error walk) is untouched. Resource limits were already exempt from context enrichment and stay exempt. C#-ONLY: Lean's `CtxMsg.call` applies to every call in Lean's model, which has no surface parser, no deconstruction lowering, and therefore no synthetic frame to mark. The two differential corpora compare the innermost error category (`errCategory` discards `withContext` wrappers). Standalone Lean guards can inspect those frames. For example, `test150` and `test151` in `lean/CoreTests/CollectionBuiltins.lean` use `hasContext` to check the diagnostics for `first` and `last` on empty collections. Pinned by `DeconstructionDiagnosticTransparencyTests` — including a generated shape × failure-origin matrix, a corpus-wide net over `LanguageSpecCorpus` and `SemanticExplorerCorpus`, the genuinely-suspending async twin, and optimized/generic parity — with `DeconstructionBindingTests` keeping the wording rules.

Two consequences worth stating because a name-based filter would get them wrong. A WRITTEN identifier whose text resembles the internal convention (`deconstruct`, `synthetic`) is an ordinary property and keeps its frame. And a host-built `Expr.Call` over an `Expr.AlgorithmExpr` is a USER-AUTHORED anonymous callable — the parser emits that shape for deconstruction helpers only — so it still reports `while evaluating call to {...}`.

## Blame for an output-less written argument

An omitted slot is an arity error. A demanded written expression with no output is its own MissingOutput failure. An ordinary empty value (`()`, `[]`, `''`) is one legitimate value. An explicit spread of an empty value supplies zero cells; a spread operand whose OWN output is missing is SpreadMissingOutput, while a missing output nested in the spread operand — `F({})*`, `Coll({})*` — keeps exactly this blame under one added `SpreadOperandContext` frame (X-29 / Q-36, decided 2026-10-24; `sequences-lists-and-calls.md` § Spread failure origin). These distinctions are decided from original written provenance, never inferred from supply count.

`NeedCell.Source` and `Span` retain the original expression across transport, while `ResolvedArgumentAlgorithm.Source` retains the current written slot for builtin parameter context — on every route that hands a builtin its supply: a call, a planned dispatch, and the FIRST round of a `while`/`repeat` loop over a captured builtin call, whose state is that call's own sourced supply (`sequences-lists-and-calls.md` § Loops; a later round's Ready cells have no written slot). That occurrence positions a fresh refusal of required argument capability: META capture at its binding call (`call-by-need.md` CR-2), or missing CALLABLE identity at an invoking slot (META-DIAG-2). The transported computation's own VALUE and projection failures keep their original provenance. First demand uses `BlameWrittenArgumentSlot` / `BlameWrittenArgumentForMissingOutput`; named sources keep their own property blame, and anonymous blocks read through a fixed parameter use `ParameterSlotFailure`. Inner-element spans precede the containing argument fallback. No Force, heap-address or synthetic-wrapper frame is exposed. A callee with no output keeps its own call blame after cardinality and inspecting patterns.

An unused cell produces no failure or blame. Completed failures keep their original payload, span and context on repeated demand and forwarding — including the origin of a missing output (own or nested, Q-36), which is decided where the failure is produced and never by a later consumer; a consumer that nests it works on its own copy. A callback projection never constructs VALUE blame. `OutputLessArgumentBlameTests`, `AbsentSourceLocationTests`, `ModelCProductionTests` and the canonical output-less-argument case pin the boundaries. The public ArgumentEvaluationContext remains supported; Model-C adds the ordinary public DemandCycle error and code.

A NESTED MISSING OUTPUT IS NOT ITS CONSUMER'S (2026-11-08). A report may NAME a binding or callee as output-less — "Property 'P' …", "Parameter 'x' …", "Cannot call 'F' …", "The value `R.n` …" — only when the missing output is that subject's OWN, as the Q-36 origin already records it; the ONE test is `Evaluator.IsOwnForBlame` (beside `IsOwnMissingOutputError`). `H2 = {} + 1` / `H2` HAS an output; its missing output is the `{}` operand's, nested once the operator consumes it, so the report is the innermost generic one at the operand's span ("[1:6] Algorithm has no defined output."), exactly what root `{} + 1` says, and enclosing frames render as ordinary context (`F(x) = {} + x` / `F(1)` is "while evaluating call to F: Algorithm has no defined output."; `H2*` keeps its `SpreadOperandContext`). Where it is decided: the evaluator attaches a property or parameter frame only to an own missing output (`WithPropertyContextOnMissingOutput`; `WithParameterContextOnMissingOutput` and the builtin slot's `Param` arm for a BARE failure), and the renderer's call arm and the member/extension half of its dot-call arm claim only an own one (`KatLangError.TryFormatMissingOutput`). The `.string` intrinsic half still names its RECEIVER, whose failure reaches it nested by design, and `MarkSelectedMemberOutput` still marks every member outcome. An own missing output that a consumer nests AFTER its subject's frame claimed it keeps that claim (`F() + 1` with an output-less `F` is still "Cannot call 'F' …"): `NestMissingOutput` sets the internal, equality-transparent `EvalError.MissingOutput.KeepsOwnBlame` when the own failure sits directly under a frame. A VALUE read that crosses a structural carrier keeps its terminal's blame the same way (`A = { P }` / `A` with an output-less `P` is still "Property 'P' …"; M4★, the carrier is a transparent reference to its terminal; `NestCarrierCrossing`), while `A = { { Q = 1 } }` / `A` — an anonymous row, not a reference — is the row's generic report. F3 parity uses the same slot: the hoisted `$deconstruct$N` source's one row IS the written right-hand side, so the synthetic frames' row loop and the deconstruction binder nest a bare own right-hand-side failure through `NestDeconstructionSourceFailure`, and `x, y = { }` / `x` keeps rendering exactly as `x = { }` / `x` (and `x()` as "Cannot call 'x' …"), while `x, y = {} + 1` / `x` is the operand's generic report like `x = {} + 1`. `IsNested` itself, and so every spread decision, is unchanged; kinds, codes, values, counts, caches and every own-case message and span are unchanged. F5 is deliberately NOT narrowed: the written-argument frame names the written argument whatever the origin (`F(1 + { })`, `sum(({ }, 1))`, `count({ {} })`, the parameter re-labeling of `F({ {} })`, the receiver text of `({} + 1).string`). Lean's twin is `withMissingOutputCtx`, which frames only `.missingOutput .own`; Lean has no call-frame rendering and no deconstruction, so `KeepsOwnBlame` is C#-only. Pinned by `NestedMissingOutputBlameTests` and Lean `CoreTests/OutputSemantics.lean` (`nestedMissingOutputIsNotThePropertys`).

Explicit selected-member invocations retain that route for an unclaimed nested missing output too (`MarkNestedMemberInvocationOutput`, shared by ordinary, async and planned member dispatch). Otherwise a declared member named `string` would be mistaken for the intrinsic: `Box.string()` with `string = {} + 1` would blame the output-bearing `Box`. These nested failures keep the generic report at the operand; existing own-case invocation wording is preserved, including the historical receiver wording of an own `Box.string()`.

Missing CALLABLE capability blames the current written invoking occurrence when known, falling back to the cell's original argument span when it is not. This is a fresh refusal at that use, not a failure originating inside the transported computation. A whole collector has no single original argument span, so its written slot still positions the refusal. This applies to ordinary, dot and fused callback paths and loop-callee projection (`Q06Q27FinalHostileReviewTests`); an actual projection failure passes through unchanged. META-DIAG-2 replaces the former original-span-first priority for these fresh capability refusals only; no computation cell or memoized failure is rewritten.

### FE-4a hostile-review compatibility and cycle repairs

Logical memberships remain per property. Exact owner-qualified sets share immutable final backing; nonempty per-property wrappers preserve `Property` record equality and hash behavior, including `with` copies, without copying the entries. Public names enumerate in ordinal order, captures by ordinal name then owner depth. Mutation is unsupported. Reference identity of backing is not semantic requirement identity. `LocalOnlyProperty` error equality remains content-based; its raw `ToString()` now intentionally prints bounded names instead of a concrete collection type. This is a reviewed presentation change, not a structured-error or language-semantics change.

Unions of two large bases with small variants retain the shared combined core (`RequirementSetInterner`), so reassociation does not recreate per-property wide arrays. Arbitrary independently written large sets still require their own distinct contents; this is not a universal compression claim. Shared output storage does not remove the logical cost of explicitly enumerating every membership.

A scope-free summary formerly carried an open lookup condition (the providers of the levels a reference escaped) until outer lexical lookup was known, and repeated cycles nested that same condition without bound (also reproduced on the pre-FE-4a parent); `SummarySeed.UnderMissingLexicalHead` reduced a repeated head inside an already selected provider. No summary carries a provider any more — the owning level's settlement selects it from completed structure — and that reduction was deleted with the open-candidate channel in November 2026 (EXPOSURE-OPEN-1, REMOVAL). The verdicts it preserved are the settlement's own (`OpenLevelSettlement`): one provider contributes its member's requirements, multiple providers contribute nothing, and bound-owner exclusions are preserved; `Fe4aHostileReviewTests.GeneratedLibrariesWithEveryOpenForm_MatchIndependentOracleInAnyDeclarationOrder` pins them from source. Structural lookup, outer shadowing, and runtime accessibility stay unchanged. The exposure worklist and summary worklist retain dependency validation and fair reader notification; instrumentation counts both kinds of notifications.

`Fe4aHostileReviewTests`, `RequiredAncestorRepresentationTests`, and the non-friend `RequiredAncestorPublicContractTests` pin the repairs. No executable Lean change or public signature change is required: these are C# representation, analysis-termination, and diagnostic-debug-text changes. V3 remains required. `ScopeVisibility.Symbols` and its retained editor snapshots were FE-4b (see SHARED SCOPE VISIBILITY above).

### EXPOSURE-OPEN-1 pending references, monotone summaries, and shared expansions

C# front-end representation, termination, and performance rules (October 23 2026; the open-candidate channel that change introduced was deleted on November 16 2026); NO semantic change and no Lean counterpart.

WHAT A SUMMARY CARRIES. The scope-free summary walk (`PropertyDependencyGraphBuilder`), which always runs in the completed structural context of the body it summarizes (ONE SUMMARY MODE, below), reports bare visible names and member paths. A relative `SummaryReference` can travel with a shared argument bundle. An anchored `PendingReference` carries a head, the member steps navigated after it, what the head denotes (`PendingSelection`: a lexical name, an inline-block head, or the open-target walks of a level), the completed structural context it was written in (`Origin`), whether it is an open-target path, and the owners whose parameters are bound on the way (`BoundOwners`). A reference names WHAT is read and from where. It carries no `open` provider and nothing a provider charges: the level expansion (`ExpandAtLevel`) resolves only what the level itself declares — a bare name, or a reference the completed structure proves to select a local member (`SummaryMemo.SelectsLocalMember`) — and anchors everything else at the level's completed context, unsettled (`AddEscaping`). Which declaration an escaping head selects is decided once, by the owning level's settlement (`PropertyExposureResolver.PendingResolution`), from the completed structure of the origin's chain: the owner walk first, then the opens of each consulted level together (`OpenLevelSettlement`, providers counted by `OpenProviderIdentity`), a sole provider charging its member and two or more charging nothing.

MONOTONE SETS. `PendingReferenceSet` holds one entry per reference (`SummaryReference.ResolutionKey`: origin, selection, head, member steps). A repeated reference keeps only the bound owners EVERY occurrence binds, which is (requirements − bound₁) ∪ (requirements − bound₂) without enumerating the call paths of a shared DAG. A reference's own content never changes, so an entry changes only by losing bound owners and a summary only grows with the member summaries it reads; every summary operation is a union of per-entry contributions, so the dependency-driven member solver (`SolveMemberSeeds`) changes a member only by growth and reaches the least fixed point any fair iteration reaches. Read recording and reader notification remain complete, and the exposure pass's cache across equation evaluations still validates every level summary a cached top-level settlement read.

SHARING. A level expansion shares nothing: each `ExpandAtLevel` call reads its level's member map as it stands and keeps no state (its per-call seed cache answered no lookup once the expansion stopped nesting, and was deleted on November 19 2026: KEY TEXT AND HOISTED ROWS, below). Nested exposure settlements share completed `(reference content, level, site, bound owners)` keys during ONE synchronous settlement, and a settlement that read no in-progress summary and does not depend on its site is kept for the whole resolution (SETTLEMENT SHARE, below). Neither publishes a result whose computation encountered a cycle cut (an expansion cuts no cycle: it reads only its level's member map and no summary in progress, so every expansion it computes is complete; its former cut epoch, `SummaryMemo.ExpansionCycleCuts`, lost its last writer in 04225cf7 and was deleted on November 18 2026), an exception publishes nothing, and all reads of the first computation remain in the enclosing equation's recording when a later reach reuses it. No partial cycle-cut result is published, and no unvalidated mutable-summary version is reused. Since November 22 2026 a partial result answers later meetings of its own exact key while its component is being settled, inside one top-level settlement (COMPONENT SETTLEMENTS, below).

KEYS. A reference's `ContentKey` is its resolution key plus its bound owners' ids; a seed's key serializes its own names and its references' keys. A resolution key has three length-prefixed parts: origin and selection, head, member steps. `SummaryKeyCharacters` counts the id-free length of the content text of every base seed the member solver compares (since November 20 2026 the solver compares contents directly and builds that text for an observer only), and `SettlementShare` weighs its entries by the length of their content keys with every process-wide id counted at its widest (LIVE OPEN WALKS, CONTENT-COMPARED EXPANSIONS AND STABLE SHARE WEIGHTS, below). Until November 19 2026 a reference key kept a fourth, always-empty part where the deleted channel had serialized carried providers, so that those counts stayed the measured ones; it was dropped then, and the counts were recalibrated (KEY TEXT AND HOISTED ROWS, below).

EXPLICIT STACK (OOS-22, October 24 2026). The level expansion ran as heap frames (`ExpansionFrame` with `RunExpansion`) while carrying an escaping name still settled an opened member's charge at the owner, once per link of an opened chain. An escaping step only anchors its reference now (`AddEscaping`), so nothing in an expansion nests, and since November 18 2026 `ExpandAtLevel` is one plain loop: it observes the token once when it starts and once before each name it carries outward, the checkpoints and order the frame driver had (one per frame pushed), and it keeps no state, so an exception publishes nothing. The exposure settlement that resolves the references is iterative (EXPOSURE SETTLEMENT RUNS ON AN EXPLICIT STACK, above). `ExposureOpenChainDepthProcessTests` (child process, 1 MiB thread) pins the 700-link witness and 2,000-link opened, dotted, and shadowed chains plus 1,000-link constant-terminal, mixed, root-read, and deferred-branch variants by their classification and run results; `InterruptedChainExpansion_LeavesNoPartialResult_AndTheRetryMatchesAFreshAnalysis` pins failure cleanup.

WHAT IS PINNED. `ExposureOpenChainComplexityTests` keeps the shapes that exposed the original defects, each with its exact classification and deterministic work counters at increasing sizes: reproducer A (a chain of N members opened from one another inside `O(p)`, read at the owner: 2N + 2 algorithm summaries, N + 2 member expansions, O(N) key characters, at most 8N + 16 settlement frames), its dotted, mixed, root-level and captured-terminal controls, K readers of one chain and two owners with same-named parameters, reproducer B and its minimal core (mutually reading libraries; bounded member evaluations), the scalable B family at n = 8 / 16 / 32 (polynomial bounds and doubling ratios), siblings opening each other and members reading around an open cycle (formerly a host-stack overflow), two-member diamonds (N + 2 member expansions, at most 7N² settlement frames), an expansion's read of the member map as it stands (`Expansion_ReadsTheMemberMapAsItStands_AtEveryCall`), and the reference set's intersection rule (`PendingReferenceSet_KeepsOneEntryPerReference_AndNarrowsBoundOwnersByIntersection`). `Fe4aHostileReviewTests.GeneratedLibrariesWithEveryOpenForm_MatchIndependentOracleInAnyDeclarationOrder` pins every open form against the independent `OpenLibraryOracle`, a plain-set least fixed point written without any of the analysis' machinery. Measured on November 16 2026 and again on November 19 2026 (the exposure pass alone, after the preceding stages; key characters before and after KEY TEXT AND HOISTED ROWS, below): reproducer A at N = 64 takes 130 algorithm summaries, 66 member expansions, 3,327 key characters (14,506 before) and 502 settlement frames; reproducer B takes 22 member evaluations, 17 member expansions, 1,012 key characters (3,079) and 80 settlement frames; the B family at n = 32 takes 128 member evaluations, 8,994 key characters (29,266) and 1,092 settlement frames. These bounds hold for the tested chain/diamond/library families; they are not a bound on exposure analysis of every program.

HISTORY. Before October 23 2026 an escaping reference carried, for each level with opens it escaped, that level's providers as open candidates, a resolved one holding its provider's charged seed — which held the next link's candidate, N deep for a chain of N links. Reproducer A then cost O(N³) characters and copies (N = 300: 9 s and 20 GB), because every enclosing link rebuilt and re-serialized the nesting; reproducer B never finished, because a candidate's seed grew during the member fixed point and the stale and the grown reference were two entries that circulated around a cycle of libraries; and members reading around a cycle of opens overflowed the host stack. EXPOSURE-OPEN-1 made candidates shared immutable nodes with cached deep facts and interned seed keys and united same-shape references; OOS-22 moved the level expansion and the candidate rewrites to explicit stacks. ALIAS-UNIFY / B3-2★ (04225cf7, October 30 2026) removed the last production construction of a candidate: open providers are selected by the settlement from completed static facts.

REMOVAL (November 16 2026; implementation only). Evidence before deleting: a temporary fail-loud probe — throwing and recording on any candidate construction, any `PendingReference` built with candidates, any same-shape seed merge, any incomplete `OpenProviderIdentity`, and any reference with candidates reaching settlement — ran under the full .NET suite: 51,607 tests, 4 failures, each a test that constructed a candidate directly, and no production firing; with those four test sections removed, the probe-instrumented exposure tests (283) passed with no firing. Deleted: `OpenCandidate` / `ResolvedOpenCandidate` / `UnresolvedOpenCandidate`, `PendingReference.Candidates` and the shape key with its seed union, `CandidateRewriteFrame`, the deep-fact interner (`SeedFact`, `SeedFacts`, `SeedFactInterner`), `SummarySeed.UnderMissingLexicalHead`, the memo's seed-key, provider-identity-key and united-candidate tables, the incomplete state of `OpenProviderIdentity` (`IsComplete`, `DeclaredWithin`, `EscapedThrough`, `CompletedAt`), and the four observation counters only that code incremented (`OpenCandidateSeedRebuilds`, `OpenCandidateKeyCharacters`, `OpenCandidateFactProbes`, `PendingReferenceSeedMerges`). A before/after snapshot of 656 programs (the chain, diamond, cycle and library families above with random libraries, the tracked `.kat` scenarios, and the tutorial's fenced blocks) through the exposure pass and through `Parser.Parse` is identical in every property's `Exposure`, `RequiredAncestorParameters` and `CaptureRequirements`, in diagnostics, and in every remaining observation counter; the four deleted counters were zero in every baseline record.

ONE SUMMARY MODE (November 16 2026; implementation only, no Lean counterpart and no semantic change). Until this date the summary channel had a second, contextless mode: `BuildSummaries`, `CreateWalkMemos` and `SummaryWalkMemos` took an optional completed context; with none, a gate (`HasStructuralSelectionQuestion`) chose between a standalone analysis made on the summary's behalf (`SummaryMemo.AnalysisContextOf`) and a walk with no context at all, whose summaries were kept in a node-keyed table of their own (`SymbolicAlgorithmSummaries`, with memo-wide transparent-seed and argument-bundle tables). In that walk an escaping name stayed a bare visible name and an escaping path an origin-less reference, and the settlement could only refuse a reference no context anchored (`Requirement settlement requires an explicit structural context`). Since ALIAS-UNIFY / B3-2★ (04225cf7) every exposure level carries its completed context, so no exposure run entered that mode. Evidence before deleting: a temporary fail-loud probe — writing to a per-process file and then throwing wherever a summary graph was requested without a caller's context (whichever way the gate decided), a walk-memo bundle was created without one, the contextless table was consulted, an algorithm or branch body was summarized without one, an exposure level had none, or the settlement met a reference it could not anchor — ran under the full .NET suite: 51,609 tests, 41 failures, each a test whose own frame called `BuildSummaries` (40) or `CreateWalkMemos` (1) without a context, and no firing below any exposure entry, across 160 test processes that built 6,455,164 exposure levels, every one with its context, and anchored 508 origin-less references at their level. The probe throws at a test's first firing, so what those 41 tests do afterwards was first exercised after the change, under the permanent check below.

Now `BuildSummaries(algorithm, completedContext, …)`, `CreateWalkMemos` and `SummaryWalkMemos` REQUIRE the context, and `ProcessScopeBody` refuses a level without one as an internal error. Deleted: the gate, the standalone-analysis fallback, the contextless table, the memo-wide transparent-seed and argument-bundle tables, and every null-context branch (the unanchored arms of the level expansion and of `AddEscaping`, and the null tests of the open-target, block-head, branch-body and argument-bundle walks). What stayed unanchored on purpose (until November 19 2026: KEY TEXT AND HOISTED ROWS, below): the rows of a hoisted deconstruction source are the declaring level's own written rows and are walked raw, so the bare names and paths they write reached that level's summary graph without an origin; the level solver settles a bare name on the chain first and anchors everything else at the level's context (`PendingResolution.ReferenceFrame`), and the settlement's refusal of a reference it cannot anchor was unchanged. The effect-free share (`IndependentAlgorithmSummaries`, `IndependentBranchSummaries`, `IndependentArgumentBundles`; § Shared-DAG guarantee) keys a computation that USED no context by its node: it is a cache of the one mode, not a second mode. A before/after snapshot of 683 programs (the corpus of the removal above, plus 24 sources with hoisted rows, clause families, block heads, dot fallbacks and aliases) and 14 host-built trees, through the exposure pass and through `Parser.Parse`, is byte-identical in every property's `Exposure`, `RequiredAncestorParameters` and `CaptureRequirements`, in diagnostics, and in every observation counter.

Tests. A test that reads a summary graph supplies the context a standalone analysis gives (`StandaloneSummaries.Build`: `AnalyzedStaticStructures.ForBoundAst`, and the shared-body census an exposure run gives its memo, without which a shared DAG is summarized once per path). All 41 tests were ported and none deleted: 27 pass with the assertions they had (three gained the assertions the anchored representation needs), and 14 are restated in the one mode's terms. A name written in a nested body is a lexical reference anchored there, which is the level solver's read edge (`StandaloneSummaries.SiblingReads`), never a bare sibling edge; a host-built dot edge reports its path and the member name it may fall back to as two references; pattern reads are counted over the walk alone, after the context is built. One pin was contextless-only: `Fe1HostileReviewTests` required ONE contribution for a body shared by every branch of a family (300 entries for 300 names under 200 literal branches). Exposure never had that: each literal branch is a completed context of its own and a family skips a repeated contribution by that context, so the exposure pass merges 60,000 entries for that tree, before and after this change (measured on both binaries), while the body is still summarized once. `SharedWideBranch_IsSummarizedOnce_AndContributesOncePerBranchContext` pinned both numbers through the summary channel and through `PropertyExposureResolver.Resolve` (on November 18 2026 the skip was keyed by the summary representative and the test, renamed `SharedWideBranch_IsSummarizedOnce_AndContributesOncePerFamily`, pins 300 again: DEAD GUARDS AND ONE CONTRIBUTION PER REPRESENTATIVE, below); `PropertyExposureResolverTests.Build_AnchorsEveryReference_HoistedSourceRowsAtTheirLevel` (formerly `Build_AnchorsWhatLeavesANestedBody_AndOnlyHoistedSourceRowsStayRaw`) pins the one mode and the hoisted rows, and `StandaloneStructureIntegrationTests.LegacyExposureAuthoritiesArePhysicallyAbsent` the deleted names.

DEAD GUARDS AND ONE CONTRIBUTION PER REPRESENTATIVE (November 18 2026; implementation only, no Lean counterpart and no semantic change). Three leftovers of the two removals above were proved dead before they were deleted: the level expansion's cycle-cut publication guard (`SummaryMemo.ExpansionCycleCuts`, which no code had incremented since 04225cf7, so the guard was always true) and the settlement's two bare-name arms (the visible-name loop of `PendingResolution.ResolveSeed` and the bare-name test of `TryResolveInPlace`: an expansion anchors every name it does not resolve, so no seed a settlement receives carries one). Evidence before deleting: a temporary fail-loud probe — writing its stack to a file, then throwing, on a non-zero cut epoch where the expansion read it, on a false publication guard, and on a bare name at either settlement site — ran under the full .NET phase: 52,616 tests passed (51,891 in `KatLang.Tests`) with no firing, in 183 processes that reached the expansion site 3,313,199 times, `ResolveSeed` 2,251,598 times and the in-place test 1,641,829 times; with the probe removed the tree was byte-identical to HEAD. The field, the publication guard and both bare-name arms were removed. Settlement reads requirements and pending references. The level solver still handles raw names from hoisted deconstruction rows. The level-expansion frame driver (`ExpansionFrame`, `RunExpansion`, `EscapingFrame`, the iterator forms of the expansion and of `AddEscaping`) is one plain loop with the same checkpoints (EXPLICIT STACK, above). `StandaloneStructureIntegrationTests.LegacyExposureAuthoritiesArePhysicallyAbsent` pins the deleted names.

FE-1. A family skipped a repeated branch contribution by each branch's own completed context, so a body shared by K literal branches — summarized ONCE, at its summary representative (`SummaryMemo.SummaryRegion`, then `SharedRegionContext`) — was cloned and merged K times: 60,000 entries for 300 names under 200 branches. The skip is now keyed by that representative (`BranchSummaryRepresentative`, which the family walk and the deferred branch's `BranchBodyContribution` share): branches with one representative receive one summary, so, union being idempotent, the family's seed is unchanged while the repeats are neither cloned nor merged. Measured (Release, the exposure pass of the host-built tree, median of 15 with tiering off): 300 × 200 from 60,000 to 300 entries, 33.3 to 25.7 ms and 27.6 to 16.8 MB allocated on the measuring thread. Peak process working set was 50.0 MiB in both runs. At 300 × 1,000, entries fell from 300,000 to 300, median time from 167 to 123 ms, allocated memory from 115.9 to 61.7 MB and peak process working set from 140.0 to 99.4 MiB. Parsed source is unaffected: every written branch has a body of its own, so no two branches of a parsed family share a representative. The baseline and candidate produced identical comparison records for 41,769 test-derived and generated inputs and 526,897 E-18 census probe programs covering the grid, its extension, the fixed cases and settlement families. The records include diagnostic text, digests of the elaborated tree and published exposure metadata (including owner depths), scalar traversal counters and per-kind cancellation counts. Twelve host-built shared-branch trees (one body under many literal branches, two families, alternating bodies, binder and literal branches over one body, a family-owned open, owner parameters read directly, through a local, a dotted path and a nested shared family) are identical in all of these and in their run results except `BranchSummaryContributionEntries`, which drops to one contribution per representative. `Fe1HostileReviewTests` pins one contribution per family (`SharedWideBranch_IsSummarizedOnce_AndContributesOncePerFamily`), one per representative (`SharedBranchBodies_ContributeOncePerSummaryRepresentative`) and the metadata of unshared twins (`SharedBranchBody_PublishesWhatItsUnsharedTwinPublishes`). `ExposureCompletedContextTests` pins the contract the removals rest on: every body an exposure run descends into — a property value, a block literal, a branch body, a loaded module's root — and a deferred branch body when it materializes is a level settled in the context `CompletedStructureContext.For` gives it.

KEY TEXT AND HOISTED ROWS (November 19 2026). A reference resolution key encodes the origin and selection, head, and member steps. Removing its empty provider field saves two characters and can also shorten an enclosing length prefix. Each level expansion resolves its input against the current member map and returns the resulting seed. The former `ExpandedSeeds` cache serialized a seed and stored a clone for an expansion that never nested. A `SummaryReference` can describe a relative path while an argument bundle is shared across owners. `BuildSummaries` applies Q1 selection, keeps the resulting input key for the level equation, and anchors any relative hoisted path at the declaring level. `PendingReference.Origin` is required. A bare name from those rows first consults the level’s chain. If that lookup misses, `ResolveVisibleName` constructs its reference at the same level. The equation input key preserves both visits when a nested read and a hoisted row select the same path. The graph sorts inputs by the former decimal length prefix and then by the current key. This preserves checkpoint order across digit boundaries such as 100 → 98. The former provider field contributes no text to any stored key.

The scratch prototype kept the previous anchoring and refusal checks. The baseline hoisted-row test observed an origin-less `Lib.V` at the settlement handoff in its parameterized owner. Witnesses in root, parameterized, nested, block, branch, loaded-module and deferred-branch contexts showed that the earlier handoff supplied the same completed context. The instrumented .NET phase passed 52,799 tests. It observed 3,302,434 seed-cache lookups with zero hits and 4,784,702 reference frames with an origin. All 4,393 newly anchored references observed at a frame carried the exact declaring context object that the previous handoff supplied.

Release comparison against 7931d2c5 covered 51,823 corpus programs (15,809 test-file literals, 25,078 generated owner cases and 10,936 generated hoisted-row cases) and 526,897 E-18 census probe programs. Diagnostics, elaborated-tree digests, published property metadata including owner depths, run outcomes, non-key traversal counters, and cancellation-checkpoint sequences agreed for every input. `SummaryKeyCharacters` fell from 19,038,558 to 6,831,184 across the corpus and from 523,329,700 to 169,383,051 across the census. A second comparison covered the preceding exposure task’s 41,769 programs, 3,664 tracked scenarios, tutorial and specification cases, explorer cases and random-library programs, plus 12 host-built trees and four ledger examples. Their non-key results agreed. All 48 identifier-length probes preserved the order of settlement frames and cancellation checkpoints.

The default share capacity is 524,288 units. Instrumented copies of both builds admitted all 214,828 corpus stores and 5,369,610 census stores. Corpus admitted weight was 436,617 → 435,563 and census weight was 12,625,991 → 12,513,442. Maximum weight retained in one resolution was 177 → 175 and 105 → 104, respectively. The lifted-150/captured-250 chain retained 9,473 → 9,456 units and used 3,188 frames. The literal-key share and footprint tests keep their thresholds. Reproducer A measured 3,562 / 7,210 / 14,506 key characters before and 831 / 1,663 / 3,327 after for 16 / 32 / 64 links. Its guard moved from 512N to 128N to retain similar headroom. The B family measured 7,256 / 14,578 / 29,266 before and 2,232 / 4,482 / 8,994 after. Diamonds measured 5,009 / 10,339 / 21,035 before and 1,241 / 2,565 / 5,221 after. The cubic coefficient moved from 512 to 160 and the quadratic coefficient from 1,000 to 250 in proportion to the measured character counts. The doubling factors remain 10 and 5. `PropertyExposureResolverTests` pins the path’s owner, the two level-equation inputs, the baseline checkpoint count, the order across a decimal length boundary and the bare-name route through an open.

LIVE OPEN WALKS, CONTENT-COMPARED EXPANSIONS AND STABLE SHARE WEIGHTS (November 20 2026, over 47f69650; C# implementation only — no classification, diagnostic or Lean rule changes). Three separate results.

Work bound. The route census listed three extension work families, all with the `rt-alg-call` head: `W|head=rt-alg-call|reader=param|place=outside|level=enc1|use=unused`, the same at `level=two`, and `W|head=rt-alg-call|reader=block|place=outside|level=enc2|use=unused`. Their open targets `L{i} = X{i}.Sub.Algorithm` cross a `Sub` whose value calls `Cap`, a member of `F`, so the walks of `F`'s opens read `Cap`'s summary while `F` is being solved. Such a settlement cannot enter the run's share, which keeps final results only, and C1 (`SharedOpenWalksLevel`) used the unbound walks only when they stood from every site, a test that includes having read no live summary. Every reader with bound owners therefore settled the unbound walks AND its own: 2M open-walk settlements of M + 1 targets each, every target a walk frame, the anchored `Sub` seed and the `Cap` reference (1,300 / 5,100 / 20,200 frames of each at M = 25 / 50 / 100). The summary checkpoints grew with those walks alone: `ReferenceTerminal` lookups from `OpenTargetMayCharge`, 2M(M + 1), and from `ChargeOpenCrossing`, 2M² + 1; the summary building and expansions stayed linear. A live summary is adopted as it stands whatever the bound owners, and no summary changes within one settlement, so it gives both C1 routes the same set: the route now needs the unbound walks to be whole and to answer every site (`AnswersEverySite`: no view at two versions, no site lookup below the level), not to have read nothing live. A level's unbound walks that read summaries of the level in progress are kept for the rest of that level's solve (`PendingResolution._liveOpenWalks`, exact key, site included) with those summaries as read; they are reused only while each still equals the current one and every view they consulted stands, and their live reads are recorded again in the reusing top-level settlement, so the level solver evaluates that reader again when one changes. Every frame records the names of the live summaries it read, merged into its parent and kept with its result (`Settled.LiveReads`; until November 25 2026 at most `MaxLiveReads` = 16, a result past that reused only inside its run: EXPOSURE RESIDUALS OF THE NOVEMBER 21 AUDIT, below, removed the cap). The three families now settle in 302 / 602 / 1,202 frames (the block readers 428 / 853 / 1,703) and 671 / 1,321 / 2,621 summary checkpoints (block 822 / 1,622 / 3,222), and their lines left `census-gaps.txt`. Over all 1,871 census W doubling families (5,613 programs) the published metadata was identical and only these three changed their work; over 51,137 corpus programs every output and counter was identical; over 526,897 census probe programs only 408 `rt-alg-call` cells changed, each by fewer frames and checkpoints, with less footprint work in 102 cells (for example 18 → 15 frames), with identical diagnostics, elaborated trees and metadata, and the per-reader seam published the same metadata for every one of them. `ExposureAccessCheckComplexityTests` pins the three families, the shape itself (one computation of the level's walks over every reader, none with bound owners) and `KeptOpenWalks_AreSettledAgainWhenASummaryTheyReadChanges`: `Cap` gains `w` after both readers settled, and a reuse without the check, or without recording its read, leaves a reader without `w`. The bookkeeping costs about 0.05% of the corpus's allocation.

Seed-content cache. `SolveMemberSeeds` reuses an expansion for every member whose base seed has equal content while the member seeds it read stand. Its key was the seed's serialized text. Measured on 47f69650 with a scratch probe on the production path: the corpus made 299,241 lookups (59,519 hits, 1,693 stale, 238,029 misses), its keys allocated 140.8 MB and the store path 69.8 MB, and the expansions the hits avoided would have allocated 13.3 MB; the census made 7,949,401 lookups (2,916,434 hits, none stale), keys 2.61 GB, stores 1.72 GB, avoided expansions 0.23 GB, so 0.84% of its 514.9 GB parse allocation bought a 0.05% saving. On the wide families of equal seeds (K members `P_i = v0`, or K readers of one opened or navigated member, K = 100 to 800 at W = 64, and the pinned 300 at 400 and 600 at 800) one key allocated about as much as the expansion it avoided (616 against 632 bytes) or more (about 1,000 against 500), and a family of distinct seeds paid its keys for no hit. Without the cache the bytes fell most (corpus −202 MB, census −4.14 GB), but every equal seed expanded again, which the pinned sharing (`Summaries_PropertiesOfAWideOwner_ShareOneParameterNameSet`, `OpenedOrNavigatedMember_IsSettledOncePerLevel`) excludes. The cache now compares contents directly (`SeedContent`: an order-independent hash computed once per member, then `SummarySeed.ContentEquals`); every existing measured work counter — `SummaryKeyCharacters` included, which an observer still measures through `SummarySeed.RecordKeyCharacters` — was identical on all 578,034 programs, and it saved −152 MB (−0.43%) on the corpus, −2.95 GB (−0.57%) on the census and up to 0.70 MB per wide family; time was unchanged within noise. `SummarySeed.ContentKey` remains the text the tests compare against.

Controlled cache measurement. On checkout baseline 47f69650 and the final implementation, a scratch Release probe ran the same nine programs (the three W IDs above at widths 25, 50 and 100). Bypassing only the member expansion cache increased member expansions in every row. For the parameterized reader at enc1, the counts rose from 129 / 254 / 504 to 156 / 306 / 606 on both versions. Bypass preserved frame visits, summary checkpoints, open-walk counts and published metadata in all nine rows on each version. At width 100 the baseline still visited 61,101 frames and took 42,620 checkpoints for that reader with bypass enabled. The final implementation took 1,202 frames and 2,621 checkpoints with either cache setting. Repeated settlement of the open walks accounts for the growth. Sharing equal seed expansions independently saves work.

With caching enabled, the table records direct production-path measurements after a warm-up parse of each input. Hits, misses and stale entries count lookups. Key bytes cover key construction. Lookup and store bytes include dictionary operations and read arrays. Total bytes cover the full parse on the measuring thread. Byte pairs give baseline → final.

| Inputs | Hits / misses / stale, both versions | Key bytes | Lookup and store bytes | Total bytes |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Nine W programs | 546 / 2,839 / 0 | 2,639,096 → 70,000 | 814,656 → 572,472 | 719,632,944 → 370,522,664 |
| 26 wide and control programs | 6,630 / 1,790 / 0 | 6,324,512 → 120,000 | 747,520 → 645,728 | 473,404,160 → 467,249,696 |
| First 200 programs of the regular corpus | 141 / 53 / 1 | 18,176 → 320 | 27,072 → 15,056 | 91,675,616 → 91,705,840 |

Stable share weights. The share's admission weight counted the decimal digits of the origin's requirement identity and of the bound owners' ids, both process-wide counters. In one process, the lifted-150/captured-250 chain before and after ten million unrelated owner identities and scope keys had been issued: retained weight 9,341 → 15,720 units; at a scratch capacity of 9,456 units, 2,324 → 1,110 kept settlements and 9,074 → 70,612 frames, with identical metadata. A reference key is now charged at the length of its text with every id written at 19 digits (`SummaryReference.StableContentKeyLength`, from its structure; never less than the text the key retains), and a bound-owner set structurally, one character per owner and separator (`BoundOwnerSet.StableKeyLength`: a set holds no text). The same experiment then gave identical admissions, frames and hits at every capacity; the chain retains 8,678 units, captured-N2000 31,990 (formerly 31,989), and the largest weight retained in one resolution is 214 over the corpus and 112 over the census, far below 2¹⁹. `FrontEndTraversalObservations.SettlementShareRetainedWeight` reports it; `ExposureSettlementShareTests.RetainedWeight_DoesNotDependOnIdsIssuedEarlier` pins the chain's 8,678 units across 200,000 unrelated ids, and `ReferenceKeyCharge_IsItsKeyWithEveryIdAtItsWidest` the charge against the key's own layout.

SETTLEMENT SHARE (November 10 2026, over 92abaec0; C# implementation only — no classification, diagnostic, or Lean rule changes). Why the chain was quadratic: every link's level settles its own member's reference by walking the links below it, and no nested result outlived its top-level settlement — the completed-settlement cache is cleared around each one because a settlement may read live equations, and its key held the SITE (the level a settlement resolves owner-qualified parameters from), which differs for every link. Attribution on the five chain shapes at N = 256 found about 97% of frames repeating a (content, level, bound owners) key under another site, and no exact site-qualified key repeating across top-level settlements, so a longer-lived cache with the old key would have saved nothing.

The rule (`PropertyExposureResolver.SettlementShare`, one per resolution on `ExposureRun`): a nested settlement is kept for the rest of the resolution, keyed by (reference content or seed, level, bound owners) WITHOUT its site, exactly when its whole computation — its nested frames and every result it reused included — (1) saw no cycle cut, (2) read no summary of the level in progress, and (3) made no site lookup that landed below its level or found no owner. Each frame carries that record (`Frame.Complete`): a nested frame's record is absorbed by its parent and a reused result brings its own, so a settlement never looks independent merely because what it depended on arrived through a cache. The entry also holds the completed-fact views it consulted with the versions it read, and a lookup answers only while each stands (`StaticLookupFacts.ViewVersion`, the anchor cache's rule; a requirement projection's version moves when a provisional view is admitted); a stale entry is dropped and computed again. The within-settlement caches, the level solver's validated top-level cache, the explicit stack and the cancellation checkpoints are unchanged, and the share is consulted before the within-settlement cache.

Why every summary is unchanged. (1) Which providers settle a reference is structural, so a cut-free settlement is the union over everything its reference reaches; a hit replaces a subtree by that subtree's own complete union, and every top-level settlement returns the set it returned before. A stored entry has no live read, so the reads recorded for the level solver's validation, its evaluation order and its notifications are the same. (2) A level is solved before any level below it is processed, so the only summaries still growing are those of the level in progress; whether a settlement reads them is structural (a lookup on the property scope), so a computation that read none of them reads only final summaries whenever it runs. (3) A settlement's site lies at or below its level on a chain through that level. A lookup that landed at or above the level found an owner on the level's own chain; from any other site below the level the walk first crosses levels nested inside the level's algorithm, none of which can be that owner (bodies are acyclic), and continues identically above — so every site resolves it to the same scope, and the binding-region adjustment is a function of that scope. A settlement that depends on its site is reused only inside its own top-level settlement, as before.
