(4) Keys hold identities — the level and seed objects, and the origin and owner ids inside a content key — never a spelling alone, so owners with same-named parameters stay apart. Cycle cuts remain uncached everywhere: only the opened-member rings reach a cut-only frame (in the reproducer B family every cycle passes through a level summary, which ends the walk), so they are the pinned witness of that rule. Since November 22 2026 a partial result is kept for its own key while its component is being settled, and is still published nowhere (COMPONENT SETTLEMENTS, below, whose entry sweeps witness the rule for keys met twice).

Bounds and limits. The chain tests pin the opened, dotted, mixed, root-level and captured-terminal shapes to at most 8N + 16 frames at their smaller sizes and to 16N in the sweep through 2,000 links. The share retains a bounded weight (`SettlementShare.DefaultCapacity` = 2¹⁹ units: one per entry, one per 64 characters of combined content key text — every process-wide id counted at 19 digits — and bound-owner charge — one character per owner and separator —, and one per recorded fact view). The tested chains keep a constant number of small entries per link. A program whose settlements never repeat a key stops adding entries at the cap — lookups still answer, and no result depends on a hit. A member chain whose links own parameters (lifted or written, read or not) used quadratic frames under this rule: N² in the measured root-level shapes (the lifted terminal, and the written `P{i} = { public A(x) = P{i-1}.A(1) + 1 }`) and N(N + 3) in the measured nested lifted shape. The cause is the bound owners, not lifting: every such link binds one more owner, so no exact key repeats. When a link reads TWO members below it, the bound owners depend on the path and the frames grow exponentially. Since SETTLEMENT FOOTPRINTS (below) these chains settle in linear frames (`ExposureSettlementShareTests.ExplicitParameterMemberChain_SettlesInLinearFrames`, `LiftedTerminalChain_SettlesInLinearFrames_AndBoundOwnerVisitsStayWithinThem`). Every settlement frame is charged one unit of static work to the operation that admitted its unit, and a program whose frames exceed that operation's work bound is refused with `StaticStructureLimitExceeded` (SETTLEMENT FRAME CHARGE, below).

Evidence. The research prototype of this rule (at d31dd374; it also kept site-dependent results per site, which the implementation does not) was measured before the implementation: frames on the five shapes from N = 16 to 2,000 of 6.0N to 8.0N (captured 4,006,000 → 15,994 at 2,000 links); Release `Parser.Parse` of the 5,000-link captured chain on a 1 MiB thread 59.8 s → 3.2 s (three processes each, independent rerun); and an identical before/after differential of every property's `Exposure`, `RequiredAncestorParameters`, `CaptureRequirements`, diagnostics and run results over 2,371 programs (1,412 generated chains, shadowed chains, two-activation programs, libraries and the B family; the 947-program EXPOSURE-OPEN-1 corpus; 12 open-cycle programs). Mutations on the prototype: sharing live-summary readers fails `RequiredAncestorRepresentationTests` (six stale-settlement cases) and the reproducer B tests; sharing cycle-cut results passes every earlier test and fails only `ExposureSettlementShareTests.OpenedMemberRing_CycleCutResultsAreNeverShared` (3/3, the accepted witness); sharing nothing fails the linear frame bounds. Full report: ignored `artifacts/validation/exposure-settlement-complexity/run-01/REPORT.md`. `ExposureSettlementShareTests` pins the five shapes at 16 to 2,000 links with their classification, the rings, two owners with same-named parameters, and the share's own rules (view staleness, site dependence, capacity). A chain test also advances the actual published fact view during settlement with a declaration-preserving retarget and checks that the old entries are discarded.

Historical implementation measurements (ed5ec2f2 against its parent 452a2b28, Release, 5,000-link captured chain, 1 MiB thread). Three fresh parses per build took 77.3 / 80.8 / 81.5 s before the share and 4.16 / 4.54 / 3.83 s with it. The isolated exposure probe counted 25,015,000 and 39,994 frames respectively. The logs are in the ignored `artifacts/validation/exposure-settlement-share-impl/measure/` directory.

BOUND OWNERS (November 10 2026, over ed5ec2f2). A settlement carries the algorithms whose own parameters it has already supplied. The walk adds the owners contributed by each `PendingReference.BoundOwners` and uses the accumulated set in its local completion, cycle and run-wide share keys. Previously each frame copied the accumulated set and sorted and joined its ids for the reference and seed keys. On lifted-terminal chains the quadratic number of frames repeatedly traversed sets whose sizes grew with the chain length.

`BoundOwnerSet` extends a persistent `ImmutableHashSet`, sharing unaffected nodes. Owners compare by reference. The set's count and order-independent fingerprint screen equality, followed by exact membership comparison when needed. Each insertion takes logarithmic work in the underlying set under ordinary hashing. Comparing separately built equal sets can traverse the full set. The accumulated sets have no interner. The separate summary-side keys in `PendingReference` still encode the owners contributed by that reference.

The share preserves its former admission charge. The set tracks the length of the former decimal id key incrementally, with no sorting, formatting or copying when the share weighs a key. One unit covers at most 32 retained bound-owner memberships before structural sharing, since each id contributes at least one digit and every adjacent pair contributes a separator. Charging one unit per two owners saturated the share early in a mixed program and forced the later captured chain to repeat its settlements. The mixed-program and weight-boundary tests pin the repaired behavior.

`ExposureSettlementShareTests` checks reference identity, exact equality on a forced fingerprint collision, frame and owner-visit bounds, allocation growth and capacity. The owner-visit counter counts owners offered to an extension and the size of a set whose exact membership comparison is entered. The former cubic work was in accumulating and serializing the walk's owner sets. The lifted-chain frame count stayed quadratic until SETTLEMENT FOOTPRINTS (below) made it linear.

Review measurements (Release scratch builds, three fresh processes per build and size). On the root lifted chain, the exposure pass at 1,000 links took 28.0–32.4 s on ed5ec2f2 and 3.1–4.3 s with persistent bound owners. At 2,000 links it took 258.7–329.8 s and 12.2–17.4 s respectively. Allocation at 2,000 links went from about 86,949 MiB to 6,656 MiB. Both builds used 1,000,000 and 4,000,000 frames respectively. The new counter recorded 500,499 and 2,000,999 bound-owner visits. The 5,000-link captured chain used 39,994 frames in both builds, with overlapping whole-parse timings. The three-build differential matched property metadata, diagnostics and typed run results on all 2,371 programs, with equal frame counts between ed5ec2f2 and the reviewed change. The logs are in the ignored `artifacts/validation/exposure-bound-owner-set/review/` directory.

SETTLEMENT FRAME CHARGE (November 16 2026, over 450be599; C#-only host resource policy — no classification, diagnostic kind, or Lean rule changes; owner decision of November 14 2026). Settlement frames were charged to no budget, so a source inside every static limit could spend unbounded CPU in the exposure pass. The rule (`PropertyExposureResolver.SettlementFrameWork` = 1): every settlement frame — what `RequiredAncestorSettlementFrames` counts, one per pending reference or seed settled, nested and reused ones included — costs ONE unit of static work on the ledger of the operation whose unit the exposure call completes and seals. It is charged before the frame is counted or built, through the existing `StaticQueryResourceBudget.Work`, with no token: the charge is no cancellation checkpoint (those stay one per frame pushed), depends on the frames alone, and changes no result of an admitted program. Past the operation's work bound the ledger fails and `StaticQueryLimitExceededException` reaches the entry's existing handler: one unpositioned `StaticStructureLimitExceeded` ("Static structure analysis exceeded its resource bound. The static operation exceeded its work bound (N). This bound scales with the size of the analyzed program (16,384 + 1,024 per node and edge) rather than with the host's static-structure envelope, so no envelope setting raises or lowers it.") in place of the tree, with the unit rejected unsealed, so nothing is published. Refusal is resource policy, never an inference answer.

The entries that run exposure. EAGER PARSE (`FrontEndPipeline.ResolveExposure` → `PropertyExposureResolver.ResolveCompilation`): the resolved root carries the compilation's unsealed unit, whose table holds the admission operation's ledger until exposure seals it. DEFERRED MATERIALIZATION (`PropertyExposureResolver.ElaborateDeferredBranch`): the resolved branch body carries the materialization operation's unsealed unit, so a deferred unit's frames are charged to its own operation, whose bounds were measured from the loaded body. Both REQUIRE that unit: a source root without one, or an unsealed unit without a ledger, is an internal error and never an uncharged run. STANDALONE ANALYSIS (`PropertyExposureResolver.Resolve` of a host-built tree, a declaration-preserving copy, or an already completed tree) has no operation: its facts are an analysis snapshot or a sealed table, and both released their producer's ledger when they sealed. No source reaches that entry.

The bound is the operation's work bound, 16,384 + 1,024F for F admitted nodes and edges (resolver design § 3), and it does NOT move with `SourceProcessingLimits.MaxStaticStructureBytes`, so its refusal explains that independence (`StaticStructureAdmission.WorkBoundSentence`, selected by `StaticQueryLimitExceededException.Bound`; the read bound, 65,536 + 4,096F, signature selection's fixed 2^62 enumeration limit, the fixed four refinements per query, a namespace certificate's fixed 32-bit record count, and the entry bound while its program part, 4,096 + 128F, is below the envelope's one entry per KiB have their own sentences too; resolver design § 3). Lowering the envelope far enough makes its entry or storage cap refuse the written chain before the frame charge does: the chain needs about 290 ledger slots and 16 entries per link, so it is refused from 441 links under 8 MiB and from 3,604 under 64 MiB, by the storage bound (measured, and exactly where (envelope / 64 − 3,460) / 290 puts it). A chain far past its envelope is refused before settlement begins (4,096 links under 8 or 64 MiB run no frame); one just past it is refused when the unit is retargeted, after its frames have run. Below about 361 MiB the storage cap is reached first; from there to the 1-GiB ceiling the frame charge is.

What it refused before SETTLEMENT FOOTPRINTS (below; Release scratch probe outside the repository, one process per run, 8-core laptop, November 16 2026; frame counts are exact and machine-independent, times are not). The written chain `P{i} = { public A(x) = P{i-1}.A(1) + 1 }` takes exactly D² frames (measured at 64 to 2,048 links and at 20,392), has F = 20D + 181, and charges D² + 98D + 1,068 units against a bound of 20,480D + 201,728 (both read from the ledger through the real pass sequence): by that arithmetic it is admitted through 20,391 links and refused from 20,392. The 20,392-link run was refused as predicted, by the work bound 417,829,888, after all 415,833,664 frames, 78 minutes and a 745-MiB peak working set (one run, partly beside a test run; there the unit's retargeting crosses the bound once its frames have used it up, and a frame itself does from 20,405 links); the 20,391-link side is the arithmetic's, not a run. The charge is therefore a deterministic backstop for that shape, not a time bound: a frame costs about 2 µs at 1,024 links and 8 to 10 µs beyond 5,000, a 4,096-link chain still takes about 90 s, and a host that needs a time bound sets `RunOptions.SourceProcessingCancellationToken`, which every frame observes. A link that reads TWO members below it is the shape the charge matters for, because its frames are exponential in the chain length: with each member calling the members of the two links below (`P{i} = { public A(x) = P{i-1}.A(1) + P{i-2}.A(1) }`) 16 / 24 / 25 / 26 links take 17,639 / 831,936 / 1,346,161 / 2,178,197 frames (×1.62 per link; 2.7 s and 250 MiB at 24), and with two members per link each calling both members below 16 / 17 / 18 links take 786,292 / 1,572,716 / 3,145,572 (×2 per link; 9.6 s and 427 MiB at 18), where the parameterless twins take 159 and 120 frames. Uncharged, a 1.8-KB source of 40 such links would need about 1.8 × 10⁹ frames (extrapolated from the measured ratio). Charged, the first shape is refused from 25 links (906,911 frames admitted, about 3 s and 270 MiB; 40 links: 4.3 s) and the second from 17 (4.3 s; 30 links: 6.3 s), and a refused run stops at its bound instead of growing with the program. Wider links reach it sooner: with three or four members per link, each calling every member of the link below, 10 and 8 links are admitted (531,342 and 582,448 frames, about 2 s and 210 MiB) and 11 and 9 are refused. These programs elaborated before, slowly; the ones the charge newly refuses took at least 4 s, and their cost multiplied with every further link. The structural repair the audit proposed — settlements reused across the owners they never read (the footprint), so that the share can serve these chains — is SETTLEMENT FOOTPRINTS (below): the written and branching chains now take 4D − 4 and 8D − 12 frames, and the feasible witness of a default-bound refusal is a branching chain whose first member reads the parameters of seventeen nested owners, one more than a footprint records.

`ExposureSettlementFrameChargeTests`: the ledger rises by exactly one unit at every frame, before it is counted, on five shapes (since November 25 2026 the ledger also takes the live-read checks and seed-part work settlement counts, EXPOSURE RESIDUALS OF THE NOVEMBER 21 AUDIT, below, and the test adds them); equal source charges equal work with or without a live token; a work bound with room for k frames admits exactly k and rejects the unit unsealed; the exact work of an elaboration is admitted with unchanged metadata and one unit less is refused; the 25-link branching chain was refused by its work bound through `Parser.Parse`, through the engine under a 4-MiB envelope (same bound), and inside a deferred unit at materialization (since SETTLEMENT FOOTPRINTS it is admitted in 8D − 12 frames, and the over-cap nested-owner chain of 24 links is the refused witness on all three routes); a lowered envelope's refusal of 4,096 written links runs no frame; and a completed tree is not charged while the source entry refuses it. Mutation: with the charge removed, 9 of those 13 tests fail. Logs: ignored `artifacts/validation/settlement-frame-charging/`.

`ExposureOpenChainComplexityTests` (reproducer A and its root-level, dotted, captured-terminal, and mixed controls at doubling sizes with exact counters; readers sharing one chain and same-named parameters of different owners; reproducer B verbatim and its minimal core; the scalable B family against the oracle; open cycles on a 1 MiB thread; the merge rule) and `Fe4aHostileReviewTests.GeneratedLibrariesWithEveryOpenForm_MatchIndependentOracleInAnyDeclarationOrder`; `RequiredAncestorRepresentationTests` (stale-settlement and stale-member-expansion cases) and `ExposureSettlementDepthTests` are unchanged.

WHOLE SETTLEMENTS (E-18 F1, November 15 2026; C# implementation only, no classification, diagnostic or Lean rule changes). A cycle cut is a reference met again while its own settlement is in progress; where it is met it contributes nothing. The former rule kept no settlement whose computation saw any cut, which also discarded the settlement the cut led back to. That one is not partial: when every cut inside a settlement reached that settlement itself or one nested in it, each cut reference is settled inside it, so its union is everything it reaches, exactly the set it has as a top-level settlement (which always returned a result with its own cuts). A frame therefore records the OUTERMOST settlement a cut inside it reached (`Frame.OutermostCut`, the stack depth of that settlement's frame, and since November 22 2026 its discovery index, merged upward as nested frames complete), and is kept, in the run and in the share under the unchanged conditions, when that is not an enclosing one (`Frame.IsWhole`). A settlement whose cut reached an enclosing settlement is still a partial union and is still published nowhere (since November 22 2026 its component keeps it for its own key while it is being settled: COMPONENT SETTLEMENTS, below): `ExposureSettlementShareTests.OpenedMemberRing_CycleCutResultsAreNeverShared` fails 3/3 when every settlement is treated as whole. Why it matters: the open walks of a level whose target path runs through the container that holds the opens (`open Lib.Box.Sub, …` written in `Box`; `open Lib.Mid.Sub, …` on `F` with `Mid`'s value read through those opens) reach themselves, so the level's one unbound settlement (C1, `SharedOpenWalksLevel`) never completed without a cut and every reader with bound owners walked the level again. The route census's work check then flagged 285 of 2,442 doubling families; with whole settlements none is flagged, every depth family settles each level once, and the `Lib.Mid.Sub` discharge witness costs 204 / 379 / 729 frames at 25 / 50 / 100 opens and readers (formerly 3,522 / 11,972 / 43,872). The published metadata and every outcome of the census's 26,102 valid cases are identical before and after (`full-observations.txt` byte for byte), and the per-reader seam (`OpenWalksPerReaderForTesting`) still publishes the same metadata. Two smaller changes of the same run keep the costs of the check-site rule (ownership-and-lookup.md § Open, exposure, and accessibility) where they were: the members a reference's open crossing selects are found once per reference and facts view (`SettlementShare.OpenCrossings`, completed facts only, revalidated against the views read), and a member an open-target walk selects whose seed names parameters only is resolved in place, without a frame of its own. `ExposureAccessCheckComplexityTests` (`CensusWorkFamily_*`, `CensusSelfCheckFamily_*`, `CensusDepthFamily_*`), `ExposureCensus.ExposureCensusTests` (`full` and `work` modes); evidence `artifacts/validation/e18-f1-closeout-20261115/`.

SETTLEMENT FOOTPRINTS (November 17 2026, over 0f4a50cc; C# implementation only — no classification, requirement, diagnostic kind or Lean rule changes). Why the bound-owner chains were superlinear: a member chain whose links own parameters binds one more owner at every link, so two walks below the same link — from different top-level settlements, or along different paths — carry different bound-owner sets, and the share, keyed by exact sets, never matched them. On the audit's witnesses every frame was stored and none was ever reused (D² frames on the written chain `P{i} = { public A(x) = P{i-1}.A(1) + 1 }`, exponentially many when each link reads two members below), although those walks read none of the owners that told their keys apart.

The rule (`PropertyExposureResolver.SettlementShare`, `PendingResolution.Frame`, `ISettlementReader`). A settlement reads its bound owners only through MEMBERSHIPS — a requirement's owner (`SummaryScope.TryResolveRequirement`), a parameter head's owner, and the level algorithms the open-walks route tests (`SharedOpenWalksLevel`) — and, on that route only, through whether there are NONE (its null shortcut and its skipped test). Each frame records those reads with their answers, present or absent, relative to its own bound owners: its FOOTPRINT, at most `SettlementShare.MaxFootprint` = 16 memberships. A nested frame's reads reach its parent except the memberships of owners the nested reference binds itself (present under any parent) and the emptiness read of a frame that binds any (never empty); a reused result brings its footprint; the level's one unbound open-walks settlement (C1) runs with no bound owners, so its reads are its own (leaking them into the reader trips the frame's consistency check). The share keeps a completed settlement — under the unchanged whole, live-read, site and view rules — as a CANDIDATE of its content and level (at most `SettlementShare.MaxCandidates` = 8 each) and answers a later key whose bound owners give every recorded read the recorded answer: exact `Contains` by owner reference, never a fingerprint, so same-named parameters of distinct owners stay apart. Exact keys remain for in-progress cycle cuts and the within-run caches, and for a settlement without a usable footprint: more than 16 memberships, a reused exact-only result, or a full candidate list.

Why every summary is unchanged. (1) Every decision of a settlement — what a requirement charges, where its lookup lands, which route a reference takes, which frames it opens — is a function of its key's other parts, the final summaries and fact views it read, and those answers. Under bound owners that give the same answers it makes the same decisions in every frame it reaches, transitively, and those frames correspond one to one with the same contributions (their bound owners extend the new ones by the same references' own owners); an owner it never read takes part in no decision, so it cannot expose a different dependency. (2) Where a frame meets a key in progress may differ under other owners, because two frames' bound-owner sets can become equal or unequal; that changes no whole settlement, whose union is everything its reference reaches however its repeats are cut (WHOLE SETTLEMENTS), and partial settlements are never in the share (COMPONENT SETTLEMENTS, below, keeps them for exact keys inside one top-level settlement, and its closing settlement's record covers every member), so a projected hit is never a partial union and cannot mask an enclosing cut: it replaces a subtree by that subtree's own whole union, exactly as an exact hit does. (3) A candidate is kept only when it stands from every site, read no live summary, and answers only while its views keep their versions (a stale candidate is dropped and recomputed). (4) The open-walks route is chosen by recorded reads, and C1's exactness makes both of its routes give the per-reader set.

THE OFF-CHAIN GAP (pre-existing; follow-up). Since the fail-closed entry was retired (73d6256e, Q1), a requirement whose owner is on no level of its site's chain charges nothing there and raises no landing, so its settlement is kept as site-independent although another site below its level can hold that owner and charge it. With exact keys such a settlement was already reused from other sites: for `K = { public M(z) = F.G + z }` and `F(x) = { public G = x + 1 ⏎ public H = K.M(1) ⏎ 0 }`, `H` is published `Exported` because `G`'s settlement from `K` answers the one from `F`, while the unshared settlement charges `x` (as it does for `H(y) = K.M(1) + y`, whose key differs) and the evaluator fails `G`'s check in `M`'s lexical context. Footprints do not extend this: a settlement with such a lookup, and every one that reached or reused it, answers its exact key only (`Settled.OwnerOffChain`), so every program keeps the metadata of the exact-key share. Recording the landing would make the result route-independent but charge `x` there; resolving a nested written path at its own site would follow Q2; either changes published metadata and is an owner decision.

Measured (C# Release scratch harness outside the repository, through the real pass sequence; frames and counters are exact and machine-independent, times are not). Written chain, literal or forwarded argument: exactly 4D − 4 frames, D − 2 share hits, 3D − 2 stores, 2D − 1 bound-owner visits, no footprint membership, one candidate compared per hit, at 16 to 4,096 links (D² before); the front end through exposure takes 1.1 s at 4,096 links. Branching chain: 8D − 12 frames at 8 to 1,024 links (17,639 at 16 and refused from 25 before). Lifted terminals: 6N − 9 frames at the root and 8N − 6 inside the owner (N² and N(N + 3) before). The opened, dotted, mixed, root-level and captured-terminal controls take the frames they took before. The branching chain inside k nested owners whose parameters its first member reads (`ExposureSettlementFootprintTests.NestedOwnerBranchingChain`): k = 2, 16D − 8 frames (248 at 16 links, 38,538 before); k = 16, 72D − 8 (1,144 at 16, 155,578 before) with about 15 footprint units per frame; k = 17, one past the footprint bound, falls back to exact keys and stays exponential, about ×1.6 per link (9,504 at 16, 151,624 at 22), and is refused from 24 by the default work bound — the residual superlinear shape, and the feasible witness of a default-bound refusal. Each frame retains at most 16 distinct memberships. Recording adds bounded work per existing membership query; merging visits at most 16 memberships from each nested frame or reused result, and each candidate comparison visits at most 16 (at most 8 candidates per lookup). A candidate's retained weight adds one unit per distinct membership. Removing the last stale candidate also removes its group, releasing the content key and list even when a replacement cannot fit.

Evidence. A differential of front-end diagnostics and a hash of the reflected elaborated tree (metadata included) over 41,620 programs — 15,458 literals extracted from every test file and 26,162 generated ones (the witness families at small sizes, re-entry and nested-read shapes, random owners, paths and opens) — is identical between 0f4a50cc and this change, and among the footprint, exact-key and unshared share modes (`PropertyExposureResolver.SettlementShareModeForTesting`) on the first 21,536; the unshared mode departs from both only on hand-written off-chain-gap programs. Mutations: ignoring footprints changes 5 corpus programs (the `Outer.Mid.X` probe and `ref-in-mid`); not propagating nested reads, or subtracting inherited owners as if a child bound them, changes no corpus program but every nested-read witness; dropping the off-chain rule changes 13 (re-entry programs); dropping the emptiness read changes nothing. Pinned by `ExposureSettlementFootprintTests` (the rules, those witnesses, the three modes over every chain shape, the rings and the specification corpora; each mutant above fails it, the unbound leak fails `ExposureAccessCheckComplexityTests`), `ExposureSettlementShareTests` and `ExposureSettlementFrameChargeTests` (linear frames, the tight-budget refusal at the ledger boundary, the over-cap refusal under the default and a lowered envelope and at deferred materialization). Logs: ignored `artifacts/validation/settlement-footprint/`.

COMPONENT SETTLEMENTS (November 22 2026, C# exposure implementation). In the November 21 audit, finding 5, each member of a small ring read the next two members. Once a walk cut back to a reference already in progress, its partial union was discarded at return. The same reference was then walked for each path to it. The original ring took 65, 399 and 2,533 frames at 4, 8 and 12 libraries. The audit’s 377-byte terminating witness reached the work limit at 465,224 frames on `7adaec1e`.

A cut identifies a reference by its content, level, check site and bound owners. The pre-change census found multiple bound-owner sets in 44 components and multiple levels in 30 of 78,375 components. A trial of a level’s unbound open walks can reset the bound owners. Another path to the same key can also observe new owner memberships, off-chain lookups, a different site landing or a live read. A partial result’s record alone cannot certify what its key depends on.

`PendingResolution` retains each partial settlement by its exact key in the current component. A repeat inherits the original cut’s discovery index. A frame that discards nested results invalidates partials that depend on them. A closing whole settlement that reused partials absorbs its members’ owner and site records before releasing them. Views and live reads propagate through the enclosing frames. Failure and cancellation release the partials on unwind.

On the first re-entry of a seed at the same level and check site, an operation with completed, non-provisional facts may collect equations. Each vertex is a seed, level and site. The ordinary reference walker at that vertex supplies its direct requirements and records an edge for each nested seed. Each discovery frame has the ordinary work charge and cancellation checkpoint. The collector declines on a discarded result, a reference cycle within the direct walk, an off-chain lookup, a live summary, or an edge inside a trial that resets the bound owners. The reachable views must agree and remain current. The combined owner footprint must be known and hold under each edge’s extension of the incoming bound owners. These checks cover every membership and emptiness decision the walk made. Inductively, another extension on a path keeps those answers, so all the collected equations add exactly the same direct terms. A failure to establish any condition leaves settlement to the exact walk.

All collected vertices are reachable from the entry. A reverse walk identifies those which also reach the entry. Exactly those vertices can be answered with the union of all reachable direct requirements. When requested, such a vertex gets the combined dependency record and must match the site, bound-owner footprint and current views. The solution is available only while the original seed is active. Only a whole settlement actually requested by the enclosing walk goes through the existing publication rules. Other vertices receive no cache entry from discovery.

`ExposureSettlementComponentTests` counts discovery in its frame bounds. Rings cover 8 through 64 members. All-to-all value and function components cover 4 through 32 members, with bounds linear in the number of written reads. At 4, 8 and 16 libraries, all-to-all functions take 74, 278 and 1,070 frames through dotted paths and 184, 692 and 2,668 through opens. The 377-byte witness returns 0 in 184 frames. Small cycles can cost more than the former walk because discovery itself performs work. These bounds apply to the tested families. Components for which the equations cannot certify the dependencies retain exact keyed settlement. The tests compare published metadata with the former walk, check captured inputs against independent reachability, evaluate multiple activations and interrupt the witness at every settlement frame. `ExposureSettlementFrameChargeTests` also checks discovery against the operation’s work ledger.

**Exposure allocation in deep, wide scopes (November 2026).** In the `StaticOwnerWalkTests.AliasChain` workload, the innermost of D nested blocks reads N root properties. Each enclosing level settles N escaping references against its own summaries. This takes D × N charged settlement frames. Declaring the names halfway down shortens that walk. Reading an enclosing owner's parameters directly takes zero settlement frames.

At depth 88 and width 2,048, a Release build of `775c0a03` allocated 699 MiB across the measured front-end stages. Detection accounted for 78 MiB, resolution for 30 MiB and exposure for 587 MiB. The editor allocated another 16.5 MiB. In a separate full-GC measurement, the live heap grew by 47 MiB from entry through exposure. At width 512, instrumentation attributed 60 MiB to summary construction and 81 MiB to level settlement, out of 143 MiB of exposure allocation. Most summary traffic came from copying pending-reference sets at each level.

The lookup closure now runs only on a completed-view cache miss. The summary builder sizes copies of distinct pending references once, narrows repeated keys on a separate path and returns previously built requirement levels directly. Settlement reuses the stack and empty read recordings within a solver, stores a frame's first view inline and starts share-candidate lists at one element. In the same 88 × 2,048 workload, exposure allocated 361 MiB, the staged front end 474 MiB and the editor 12.2 MiB. `ExposureDepthWidthTests` pins allocation growth, classification, owner depths, frame charges, cancellation and lowered-envelope refusal.

The 88 × 12,288 workload reaches the 1,048,576-entry limit during detection, before exposure. Shared audit `403c2ba1` admitted it with an interim owner-lookup shortcut. That audit's reviewer restored the metered walk before publication in `a33efa7e`. Width 10,000 completes on `775c0a03` and on this revision.
EXPOSURE RESIDUALS OF THE NOVEMBER 21 AUDIT (November 25 2026, over `01a0662c`; C# exposure implementation only — no classification, requirement, diagnostic kind, value or Lean rule changes). Three medium findings of the adversarial performance audit (baseline `8947d21e`) were measured again after COMPONENT SETTLEMENTS (`8221e5d1`), the open-walk reuse of `9e3ff233` and the later allocation work: their frame and element counts were exactly the audit's, so none of those changes had touched them. Frame counts and counters below are exact and machine-independent; allocations are the exposure pass alone (Release, unobserved, one thread).

LIVE READS (`exposure-live-open-walks-maxlivereads`). Why the walks were repeated: a frame recorded the names of the live summaries it read in a list of at most `MaxLiveReads` = 16, scanned at every read, so a settlement that read seventeen or more had unknown live reads and `_liveOpenWalks` could not keep it. The level's unbound open walks were then settled again by every reader with bound owners: M readers of M caps took M² + 6M + 1 frames (10,601 / 41,201 / 162,401 at 100 / 200 / 400), and with the caps fixed at seventeen each reader cost 22 frames where sixteen cost one. Now every frame keeps its live-read names as a canonical set of one interner per level solve (`PendingResolution._liveNameInterner`, created at the first live read): a read is one insertion along a trie path, a nested frame's or reused result's set is one memoized union (the set itself while the frame has read none), and there is no cap on the names a set holds (the interner's own bound follows the ledger paragraph below). The walks take 503 / 1,003 / 2,003 frames, seventeen caps cost what sixteen do plus one frame, and the pass allocates 6.3 / 16.4 / 35.8 MB where it allocated 18.2 / 63.7 / 223.2 MB. Each reuse still compares every summary the walks read (M × C comparisons in all), now charged as one unit of static work per summary (`SettlementLiveReadChecks`) instead of costing frames.

LIVE SETTLEMENTS (that finding's broader shapes). The cap was not the only limit: any settlement other than the unbound walks that read a live summary was reused only inside its own Run, because the run's share keeps final results only. A member listing W of the level's properties, read by K readers with bound owners, took K × W frames: 10,300 / 40,600 / 161,200 through a written path (`Lib.V[0]`) and 10,501 / 162,001 through an `open` (K = W = 100 / 200 / 400; 100 / 400 for the latter). Such a settlement is now kept for the rest of the level's solve (`PendingResolution.LiveSettlements`) under the share's rules: whole; answering every site at its level; no owner lookup off its site's chain, here or in anything it reached or reused (that settlement answers its exact key only, and keeping it would extend THE OFF-CHAIN GAP to keys the walk computes afresh); answered by footprint, at most `MaxCandidates` per content and level, within `DefaultCapacity` of retained weight. A later key takes it exactly when its bound owners satisfy the footprint, every view stands and every summary it read still equals the current one; its live reads are then recorded in the reusing top-level settlement, so the level solver evaluates that reader again when one changes. The argument is the share's plus the one `_liveOpenWalks` already rests on: which summaries a settlement reads is structural, none changes within a Run, and the share covers key, views and bound owners. The shapes take 400 / 800 / 1,600 and 601 / 2,401 frames, allocating 4.9 / 13.6 / 30.3 MB instead of 16.3 / 59.8 / 216.5 MB through the path.

SEED PARTS (`exposure-summary-seed-logical-width`). A navigated member whose seed holds its predecessors BY REFERENCE (a completed seed of more than `CopiedCompletedEntries` entries) was folded at every settlement, copying its whole logical width, and its names were canonicalized from scratch: K readers of a chain whose member i requires i names canonicalized K² / 2 + 3K / 2 elements (125,750 / 501,500 / 2,003,000 at 500 / 1,000 / 2,000), uncharged. Anchoring such a seed through D parameterless blocks between the library and the reader expanded, and so copied, it at every block: 157.5 / 544.3 / 1,963.6 MB at depth 32. Now `ResolveSeed` takes the parts of a seed through kept requirement sets when every part, transitively, holds required names only (`SettlementShare.SeedPartRequirements`, one canonical set per part and level for the resolution, built from the part's own names and its parts' sets; `RequiredNameParts` records each part's verdict once): the seed's owner-qualified parameters and pending references are then its own, so nothing is lost. `AnchorPublishedSeed` carries a seed through an intervening level unchanged when the level binds no parameter and the seed holds no visible name or pending reference, here or in any part (`NameAndReferenceFreeParts`): the expansion, and the settling at the prelude of a module root, give the same content. Examining a part and building a part's set charge one unit of static work per part examined and per name and held part (`SettlementSeedPartWork`). The chain canonicalizes 3,244 / 6,494 / 12,994 elements, allocates 24.7 / 51.0 / 103.6 MB instead of 30.0 / 76.7 / 209.1 MB, and the depth-32 shape 28.6 / 58.3 / 118.1 MB. Frames are unchanged.

The ledger charges the width of each admitted live snapshot before allocating its arrays, the width of each live-summary validation before comparing, and each entry the live-name interner creates (below). Declined entries allocate no snapshot. Per level, open-walk snapshots wider than sixteen names share a total allowance of `SettlementShare.DefaultCapacity` names. Each of the three seed tables keeps at most that many entries per exposure resolution. A full table uses ordinary settlement or anchoring for a part it cannot retain. The ledger also charges the widths of scanned part edges, including visits to known children. Wide snapshot and part walks poll for cancellation within a frame. `ExposureResidualReuseTests` compares saturated tables with ordinary exposure and checks the copied snapshot width.

THE LIVE-NAME INTERNER'S BOUND (review of November 25 2026, over `7de84650`). The interner of a level solve keeps every trie branch it builds and every union it memoizes until the solve ends, and it builds them for every frame that reads a live summary, before any snapshot is admitted and whether or not one is. Readers whose live read sets differ build different sets path by path. The measuring shape: `F` opens `M.N`, member `X{i}` lists K / 2 of `F`'s K properties starting at `Cap{i}` (distinct, overlapping windows), and reader `A{i}(z)` reads `X{i}`. Uncapped, the interner held 5,091 / 20,378 / 81,540 entries at K = 100 / 200 / 400 and allocated 0.84 / 3.52 / 14.81 MB (Release, instrumented scratch build), exactly as much with live settlements disabled or the share unshared; the same sets read in rotated order held 14,801 / 60,871. The shapes the reuse is for stay small: 416 / 932 / 2,064 entries for `liveb`, `pathl` and `memberl` at 100 / 200 / 400. So the interner is now bounded and accounted: every entry it creates (a name, a canonical branch, a memoized union) costs one unit of static work. Each completed insertion or union charges and counts its new entries before returning a set (`SettlementLiveNameEntries`). `SettlementLiveNamePeakEntries` includes the final operation's transient entries. Between operations the tables retain at most `SettlementShare.DefaultCapacity` entries. The final operation is itself bounded: insertion follows one finite key path, and union only combines branches of its two existing input tries, producing at most one result trie over their names. It checks cancellation at each previously uncombined branch pair. An operation that crosses the capacity hands out no set and releases the interner; sets already handed out stay valid, because nodes reference no table. From then on, for the rest of that solve, a frame that reads a live summary records its live reads as UNKNOWN (`Frame.LiveReadsKnown`: it read live and names none), so does every frame that absorbs or reuses one, and `KeepLiveOpenWalks` and LIVE SETTLEMENTS never keep such a result: it is the ordinary settlement, reused only inside its Run, as past the former sixteen-name cap (`SettlementLiveNameSaturations` counts such solves). The level's own record of what its top-level settlement read (`_levelReads`), which decides when the level solver evaluates a reader again, never depends on the interner and stays complete. Below the capacity the measured shapes keep their settlement frames, live-summary comparisons and canonicalized element counts. With the capacity lowered to 4,096, the K = 100 / 200 / 400 windows saturate at 4,097 entries, the interner allocates 0.49 / 0.49 / 0.50 MB, and frames and metadata are unchanged. `ExposureResidualReuseTests` pins the growth, the capacity (at most the capacity plus one operation's entries), saturation at every capacity from 1 to 16 and at powers of two to 4,096 over the live witnesses (including a snapshot identified at admission and again when its live summary has changed after that solve saturated, whose readers still require the new input), and cancellation while the interner grows; `ExposureSettlementFrameChargeTests` adds the entries to the exact per-frame charge.

Remaining quadratic terms, measured and charged rather than hidden by the frame counts. Each reuse of a kept live settlement compares every summary it read, and each kept one copies those names: `liveb` takes 503 / 1,003 / 2,003 frames but 9,900 / 39,800 / 159,600 live-summary comparisons and 10,400 / 40,800 / 161,600 snapshot names at M = 100 / 200 / 400 (`pathl` and `memberl`: the same comparisons, 10,200 / 40,400 / 160,800 names), where the former walk took 10,601 / 41,201 / 162,401 frames; each comparison and name is one unit of static work, as each frame is. Distinct overlapping read sets can reuse nothing, so there the reuse machinery is pure cost: the exposure pass allocates 33.3 / 127.6 / 505.6 MB against 31.1 / 118.9 / 469.3 MB before it (+7.1–7.7%; the rotated sets +7.7–8.2%, the three-name windows +3.5–3.9%), the interner and the snapshots of settlements no reader takes again. The navigated chain's frames are unchanged (1,501 / 3,001 / 6,001) while its canonicalized elements fell from 125,750 / 501,500 / 2,003,000 to 3,244 / 6,494 / 12,994 with 1,724 / 3,468 / 6,968 units of seed-part work. All of these counts are identical with and without the interner's bound.

Pre-review differential measurements. Over 792 corpus programs (the snapshot corpus of the November 16 harness with the audit families) and 131 residual-shape programs, every published classification, requirement list, capture position, diagnostic and run value is byte-identical to `01a0662c`; over 24,000 fuzz programs the same holds for every program `01a0662c` completes, and 10 erroneous programs it refused at the work bound now complete with the same other diagnostics; 3,000 random mutually reading libraries agree with an independent classification, value and activation-isolation oracle. `ExposureResidualReuseTests` compares production with the former walks through the seams `LiveSettlementsPerRunForTesting`, `SeedPartsFoldedForTesting` and `OpenWalksPerReaderForTesting` and with the unshared share, pins work against size, and holds the negative controls: a summary a kept settlement read changes (a settlement taken unchecked, or without recording its read, leaves a reader without the new input), bound owners a kept live settlement's footprint refuses, an off-chain lookup (kept, it would answer later readers in one frame), a part that holds paths, and intervening levels that bind a parameter or meet a seed holding paths. Production mutants that drop the staleness check, the re-recording, the footprint test, the off-chain exclusion, the part test, the parameter test or the path test each fail one of them. Two of those checks had no witness among the hand-written shapes and were found by mutation over the fuzz corpus, which the tests now hold verbatim: answering a kept live settlement whatever its footprint makes the front end throw ("A settlement read one bound owner with two answers") on 409 of the 24,000 fuzz programs (`g22_00156`), and carrying a seed that holds paths through a parameterless level changes the metadata of 35 (`f11_00254`: `M` would require `C`, `L` and `X` besides `x`).

THE OFF-CHAIN TERMINAL (`exposure-offchain-terminal-disables-footprints`): NOT REPAIRED, recorded for the owner decision of THE OFF-CHAIN GAP. A requirement whose owner is on no level of the site's chain makes its settlement, and every one that reaches or reuses it, `Settled.OwnerOffChain`, so the share keeps it for its exact key only: a written chain `P{i} = { public A(x) = P{i-1}.A(1) + 1 }` over `P0 = { public A(x) = F.G + x }` takes D² + 3D + 2 frames (10,302 / 40,602 / 161,202 at 100 / 200 / 400; 4,597 MB at 1,600), and the branching chain grows by about 1.6 per link (600 / 4,354 / 30,178 / 207,272 at 8 / 12 / 16 / 20). A second effect was found here: once any settlement of a resolution has looked up an owner off its chain, `SettlementShare.SawOffChainLookup` disables component equations for the rest of it, so an unrelated off-chain read declared before an all-to-all component takes the component from 184 / 692 / 1,520 frames to 831 / 29,091 / 229,287 (4 / 8 / 12 libraries). Both keep the exact-key share's metadata on purpose. Footprint reuse is exact only when the asking site's chain also lacks every off-chain owner, and a reused settlement skips the nested exact stores the exact walk makes; a later site that holds such an owner then computes the off-chain read afresh where the exact walk hands it the stored gap answer. So any reuse past the exact key changes the metadata of programs in which the gap shows, which is an owner decision (THE OFF-CHAIN GAP, above). Once the gap is decided — for example a share answer that must hold from the asking site, recording the off-chain owners and checking them against its chain — both residuals reduce to the ordinary footprint and component rules. The read stays a latent check: the front end charges nothing for it and accepts the program, and demanding it fails with `LocalOnlyProperty` (pinned).

Residual seed-width shapes, recorded with their bounds. A part that holds owner-qualified requirements is folded as before: output rows of an owner reading K chained library members under that owner as a bound owner allocate 119.8 / 403.3 MB at K = 1,000 / 2,000, with K² / 2 footprint memberships recorded (a part's set would also have to carry its memberships and site landing). The member solve still copies predecessors' own sets for members that read two or more of them (the audit's F4: 50.6 / 137.1 MB at 1,000 / 2,000).
